## Review: AI agent Phase 1

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-13. Branch: `feat/ai-agent`. **2 P1, 4 P2, 1 nit; no P0.** Fix the findings and request re-review before enabling Phase 2 tools.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, RTK guidance, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, Revision 2 R1/R2/R7/R8, both plan critiques and “Phase 1 — Implementation evidence” in `.agent/plans/ai-agent.md`. Reviewed the uncommitted tracked diff, all untracked `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/` modules, `commands/ai_agent.rs`, `aiAgent.ts` and its tests, plus adjacent provider/transport/state and installed Tauri channel code. No application source edits, staging or commit. Only this report appended. Additional HTTP/cancel-isolation fixtures appeared during review; inspected them and reran verification against the updated tree. Runtime findings below remain present.

### Findings

1. **P1 — `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/run.rs:131-136,177-196,268-273`: pre-canceled runs can still start work.** Both `tokio::select!` expressions use randomized branch polling, and `drive` has no cancellation check before entering `model.step`; `run_tool` similarly has no check before announcing/starting a tool. A ready canceled token does not guarantee its branch is polled first. Independent harness using the exact coordinator source observed **116 of 200 already-canceled runs polling the model**. The post-model check prevents an answer, but cannot undo an HTTP request or tool side effect already started. Check cancellation before each provider/tool invocation and use cancellation-first selection where both branches can be ready; recheck after tool awaits before accepting results/events. Add pre-cancel and simultaneous-ready model/tool regressions through `run_turn`, rather than only asserting that the registry token is canceled.

2. **P1 — `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/run.rs:133-136,268-272`: deadline/tool timeout drops futures without signaling operation cancellation or owning cleanup.** The 120-second timeout drops `drive`, and the 30-second timeout drops `tool.run`; neither cancels the token supplied to the tool. Harness confirmed a started tool's captured token remains **uncanceled after deadline failure**. An HTTP future can be dropped safely, but Phase 2 native DB operations/blocking workers can continue independently while the coordinator finalizes and releases its registry slot. Give each run/tool an owned cancellation scope; signal it on deadline, tool timeout and channel failure, and require an explicit abort/cleanup contract before releasing execution resources. A per-tool timeout should cancel that tool without unnecessarily canceling the permitted final answer. Add a worker/abort-handle fake proving no late operation survives termination. No DB tool is registered today; this is a blocking foundation defect for the requested Phase 2 safety review.

3. **P2 — `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/run.rs:181-203`: output budget is a retrospective threshold, not a hard reserved allowance.** Every request still receives 4096 tokens, including finalization; usage is added only when present. Seven 4096-token tool turns followed by the reserved final step produce **32768 tokens against the 32000 limit**, reproduced through the exact coordinator. Missing/partial usage records zero and bypasses accounting. Reserve finalization allowance inside the total budget, charge a conservative allowance when usage is absent, and clamp each request to its remaining allocation while respecting provider minimums. Use checked/saturating bounded accounting for provider-supplied counters. Add real-budget, absent-usage and near-exhaustion tests; cancellation/deadline must not trigger finalization.

4. **P2 — `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/wire.rs:184-201,196-204,335-340,474-487`; `run.rs:212-220`: malformed/refused envelopes are not reliably fail-closed.** Missing OpenAI `function.arguments`, Anthropic `tool_use.input` and Gemini `functionCall.args` are synthesized as `{}`; harness confirmed all three become runnable tool calls. OpenAI's `message.refusal` and refusal content parts are ignored: a nonempty refusal with `finish_reason: stop` is classified `EndTurn` (harness reproduction), and a refusal accompanying calls is not given precedence. Missing finish reasons can also promote calls to ToolUse. Duplicate IDs are discovered only while executing the batch, so the first duplicate may already run and replay receives two results for the same ID. Reject missing/wrong-shaped required wire fields, recognize explicit refusal independently of text/finish reason, and preflight batch IDs before any execution. Preserve intended per-call error results for syntactically present but invalid JSON/schema arguments. Add malformed-field, nonempty-refusal, missing-stop and duplicate-valid-call fixtures; existing tests cover nameless calls and duplicate IDs after an already-invalid call, which miss these cases. OpenAI documents both refusal representations in its [Chat Completions reference](https://developers.openai.com/api/reference/resources/chat/subresources/completions/methods/create).

5. **P2 — `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/wire.rs:127-134`: OpenAI reasoning models receive an incompatible token parameter.** Only Groq GPT-OSS uses `max_completion_tokens`; every OpenAI model receives `max_tokens`. The app suggests `o3` and `o4-mini` (`src/lib/ai-assistant/providers.ts:57-58`), but OpenAI explicitly says `max_tokens` is incompatible with o-series models. Those selections fail before the agent can answer; generic 400 is correctly not a fallback signal. Use the provider/model-appropriate completion-token parameter and add o-series payload fixtures, keeping Groq/Ollama contracts intact. This is newly copied into the agent adapter even though the legacy adapter has a similar existing limitation. Source: [OpenAI token parameter reference](https://developers.openai.com/api/reference/resources/chat/subresources/completions/methods/create).

6. **P2 — `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/model.rs:68-70,103-112`; `src-tauri/src/commands/ai_agent.rs:33-37,73-95`: R2 capability check/cache is incomplete.** Production only records `false` from one raw-body substring; it never sends a capability probe or records successful acceptance of `tools`. Because Phase 1 offers no tools, a successful run proves nothing about Ollama tool support. Known OpenAI-compatible structured tool-parameter rejections without that exact phrase become ordinary failures, while an unrelated 400 echoing that phrase can poison the negative cache. Saving config clears entries, but a still-running old snapshot can subsequently repopulate the same key after a credential/subpath change. Implement a provider-specific, structured acceptance/rejection check, cache positive acceptance for the declared TTL, and bind cache writes to the current configuration generation so stale runs cannot restore invalidated entries. Test explicit parameter rejection versus generic/auth/rate/network/refusal errors and in-flight save invalidation. Before Phase 2 fallback exists, carry whether any tool already ran so unsupported detection after execution cannot request legacy replay. Current UI performs no fallback, so no private-result replay occurs today.

**Nit — `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/run.rs:303-313`:** `cap` retains `max` bytes and then adds `\n[truncated]`, exceeding the stated 64 KiB result cap by 12 bytes (before OpenAI's error prefix). Reserve marker/envelope overhead and assert the final serialized tool-content bound.

### Reviewed behavior and remaining gates

- **Replay:** OpenAI-compatible assistant messages retain provider-native fields; Groq keeps GPT-OSS effort/token policy; Anthropic keeps block order, empty thinking, redacted thinking and signatures; its results are grouped before subsequent user text with `is_error`. Gemini retains original model parts/thought signatures and emits ordered function responses, excluding synthetic IDs and thought text. Replay is ephemeral and provider/model-bound; immutable provider snapshots keep a run on one destination. No source evidence of raw replay state reaching the frontend. Existing fixtures are useful, but Anthropic parallel results and a real provider round trip with tool history during tools-disabled finalization are not established. Current finalization simply omits tool declarations and adds no “answer with what you have” instruction; validate that exact wire request, rather than treating a scripted model returning an answer as provider compatibility evidence. Anthropic supports explicit `tool_choice: {type: none}` even with thinking: [tool definition/control docs](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/tool-use/define-tools).
- **Registry:** mutex-protected active-ID duplicate rejection, two-active-run limit, 60-second early-cancel retention, isolation and generation-safe guard removal are present. Generation currently protects registry removal, not event dispatch; no detached result callbacks exist in Phase 1. IDs may be reused after completion and accept non-UUID strings; Phase 2 should generate unique IDs and bind any worker/event callback to the run generation. Unknown/finished cancel becomes an early-cancel tombstone, so a late cancel can affect a reused ID. This is not a blocker if IDs are never reused as the approved UUID design intends.
- **Events/Channel:** Rust tagged/camelCase payloads match TypeScript; Channel is passed as a top-level command argument, owned by the spawned task, and dropped at completion. Installed Tauri sends an end marker on Rust drop and unregisters the JS callback, so local JS variable scope does not lose normal events. Tool event names come from the local registry; arguments, results and reasoning are absent. However `Channel::send().is_ok()` acknowledges Rust serialization/eval submission, not frontend listener liveness: JS unmount/removed callbacks need explicit `aiAgentCancel` ownership in Phase 2. Fake closed-sink tests do not establish real webview/unmount behavior. `ToolFinished`/terminal send failures are currently ignored; cover those and prevent callback exceptions from breaking channel ordering when wiring UI.
- **Response cap / legacy:** `send_json` now uses the shared 2 MiB reader for both agent and legacy chat. Declared lengths are rejected early; lengthless/chunked bodies are checked while reading; no truncation or partial JSON is parsed. Oversized success/error bodies now return `AI_PROVIDER_RESPONSE_TOO_LARGE` on the legacy path too: intentional R1 hardening, with changed error behavior rather than unchanged transport semantics. New loopback agent fixtures cover both length modes but cannot bind here; no independent HTTP cap round trip passed in this sandbox. A network chunk may allocate before the accumulated-body check, so this is a retained-body cap, not an absolute process-memory bound.
- **Privacy/redaction:** production transport/provider errors omit raw response messages, URLs and bodies; no new transcript/tool logging or analytics found. Provider error codes retain the existing sanitized-code contract. Future `AgentTool` errors must be vetted before entering model transcripts, and future `AgentModel` implementations must honor code-only errors because `run_turn` forwards their String directly. Phase 3 consent/provenance/revocation/native approval are deferred, not claimed verified.
- **Scope:** no tools registered, no UI callers, no legacy planner/session/Run/Insert changes. No hidden feature-setting gate was added; commands are dormant through lack of UI integration. Keep Phase 2 beta off by default and enforce its independent executor/privacy/UI gates. Live provider/DB/native-worker and desktop Channel behavior remain unverified.

### Independent verification

- `npm run build`: passed; existing externalization, dynamic-import, chunk-size and plugin timing warnings.
- `npm run test:run`: **117 files / 654 tests passed**. Focused agent/legacy wrapper and assistant tests also passed (**8 files / 51 tests**).
- Updated `cargo test --offline --locked ai::agent`: **31 passed / 4 failed / 1 ignored**; all four failures are new HTTP fixtures failing at socket bind (`model.rs:166`, PermissionDenied / Operation not permitted). Re-run with those four explicitly skipped: **31 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored**.
- Full app-store Rust suite: **224 passed / 12 failed / 2 ignored**; all 12 failures are socket-bind environment restrictions (4 agent HTTP, 3 legacy chat, 5 transport). Initial default full suite before the added fixtures: **223 passed / 8 socket-bind failures / 2 ignored**. Final full suites in **both flavors**, excluding the same 12 socket-dependent tests: **224 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored** each. Socket restrictions are limitations, not findings.
- `cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets` in both flavors: passed with pre-existing warnings outside agent code. `cargo fmt --all -- --check`: passed. `git diff --check`: passed before report append.
- Temporary harness in `/private/tmp/politedb-agent-review` imports the exact repository coordinator/protocol/wire modules; only model/provider/chat dependencies are minimal stubs. **5 reproductions passed**, asserting current defects: pre-cancel model polling, uncanceled tool token after deadline, 32768-token overshoot, missing arguments becoming runnable on all three wires, and ignored OpenAI refusal. No repository application source changed for these tests. These are regression demonstrations, not claims of a live HTTP/DB exploit or exact provider behavior.

Next: Claude fixes P1 and the Phase 1 P2 contract gaps, adds focused regressions, updates implementation evidence, then requests Codex re-review. Native DB interruption/cleanup proof remains a prerequisite for enabling Phase 2 executors.

## Re-review: AI agent Phase 1 round 2

**Final verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-13. Branch: `feat/ai-agent`. **Two remaining P2 findings; no new P0/P1.** This verdict supersedes the original Phase 1 review for the current working tree.

Read `AGENTS.md`, RTK guidance, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, approved Revision 2 and “Phase 1 — Review round 1 fixes” in `.agent/plans/ai-agent.md`. Re-reviewed all task-owned tracked changes and untracked `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/*`, `src-tauri/src/commands/ai_agent.rs`, `src/lib/tauri/aiAgent.ts` and its tests. Included concurrently-added HTTP fixtures, concurrent-run cancellation fixture and structured `tools_unsupported` branch. Only this report edited; no application source edits, staging or commit.

### Remaining actionable findings

1. **P2 — Output budget still exceeds its hard ceiling through real adapters and partial usage.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/run.rs:232–242,279–285`; `wire.rs:130,223–232,273–276,366–375,459–471`. Coordinator allowances now reserve finalization correctly for a model that honors them, but Anthropic always-thinking and Groq GPT-OSS raise smaller allowances to 4096 after budgeting. Six full turns consume 24576; the seventh coordinator allowance is 3328 but the wire sends 4096; finalization again sends 4096. The actual total is **32768 > 32000**. A temporary harness importing the exact coordinator/parser/builders reproduced this with reported usage. Separately, a present usage object missing its output counter becomes `Some(Usage { output_tokens: 0, ... })`, bypassing the new `None` fallback; a partial-usage harness reproduced eight 4096 allowances and the same excess. The existing budget test covers complete usage and entirely absent usage, but not either real-wire floor or partial usage. Gemini also still adds candidate/thought counters with plain `+` before the coordinator’s saturation. **Fix:** budget and charge the effective wire allowance, reserve provider minimums before choosing another tool step, and finalize earlier or stop when no permitted final allowance remains. Preserve missing/invalid output usage as unknown and conservatively charge the effective allowance; use saturating/checked arithmetic throughout parsing. Add adapter-backed floor, partial/invalid counter and near-exhaustion regressions. Merely documenting the floor does not satisfy the existing hard-budget finding; the plan describes Anthropic’s floor as a local heuristic.

2. **P2 — Capability generation is not captured atomically with the provider snapshot.** `src-tauri/src/commands/ai_agent.rs:56–77`. The provider lock is released after `snapshot_for_chat`, before `CapabilityCache::key` reads the generation. A save in that gap changes credentials or subpath while retaining provider id/model/base URL and bumps the generation; the old config snapshot then receives the _new_ generation. Its later capability result passes `record`’s generation check and can poison the current cache for 24 hours, including rejecting agent start with a stale negative. Existing cache regression captures its key before invalidation, so it misses this command ordering. A temporary test using the exact cache implementation reproduced `old snapshot → forget_provider → key(old snapshot) → record(false) → current key returns false`. **Fix:** capture the config/key snapshot, selected model and capability generation under the same provider lock used by save/delete; release it before network I/O. Add a barrier-controlled snapshot/save/key interleaving regression through the command/service boundary.

### Disposition of every round 1 fix

- **P1 pre-cancel — resolved.** Entry check, cancellation-first biased selection, checks before model/tool work and after tool completion prevent the reported ready-cancel polling and late tool-result events. Pre-canceled and concurrent-run isolation fixtures pass. The test named simultaneous-ready currently also exercises pre-cancel; code inspection confirms the biased branches.
- **P1 cancellation/cleanup — resolved for Phase 1’s cooperative tool contract.** Deadline/cancel/channel failure cancel the run token; tool timeout cancels its child token and permits finalization. Tracker-owned worker fixtures prove cooperative cleanup before return on deadline, timeout and cancellation. `ToolFinished` delivery failure now ends the run. Cleanup remains bounded by a five-second grace: tracker wait does not forcibly abort a noncooperative native worker. No production tool exists in Phase 1; Phase 2 must prove real executor interruption/resource cleanup and must not interpret these cooperative fakes as a hard native-worker guarantee.
- **P2 budget — partially resolved; finding 1 remains.** Final reserve, absent-envelope-usage charging and coordinator saturating accumulation are present, but effective adapter allowance and partial counters remain unaccounted for.
- **P2 malformed/refused envelopes — resolved.** Missing/wrong-shaped OpenAI arguments and Anthropic input fail closed; Gemini rejects present non-object args and intentionally permits omitted args for parameterless calls. Explicit OpenAI refusal wins over valid calls/text; absent stop reasons never authorize calls; duplicate IDs reject the batch before its first execution. Focused fixtures pass.
- **P2 OpenAI token parameter — resolved.** OpenAI requests use `max_completion_tokens`; Ollama and ordinary Groq retain `max_tokens`; Groq GPT-OSS retains its special settings. Payload fixtures pass.
- **P2 capability — partially resolved; finding 2 remains.** Positive acceptance hook, TTL, explicit structured tool-parameter rejection, generation-checked writes, save/delete invalidation and post-tool `AI_AGENT_TOOLS_REJECTED` handling are present. Generic 400/auth/rate errors do not downgrade. Reviewed concurrent structured branch matches the approved known-signal contract; no separate actionable defect found there. No tools are declared by production Phase 1, so live capability probing/cache population remains dormant until Phase 2.
- **Truncation nit — marker allocation fixed.** `cap` now includes its marker in the content bound. OpenAI’s error prefix adds framing bytes; do not describe the content cap as an exact bound on the complete serialized JSON envelope. No additional defect elevated for this framing overhead.
- **Finalization/replay — improved.** Tools remain declared with explicit calls-disabled settings on all three wires and a fixed final-answer instruction. Provider-native assistant replay and destination checks remain intact; no raw replay/tool output enters events. Hidden frontend opt-in is now present and off by default, with wrapper tests. No legacy UI/session/Run/Insert callers changed.

### Independent verification and limits

- `npm run build`: **passed**, with existing externalization/dynamic-import/chunk-size/plugin-timing warnings.
- `npm run test:run`: **117 files / 654 tests passed**.
- `cargo test --offline --locked`, default and `--features app-store`: **239 passed / 12 failed / 2 ignored each**. Every failure is socket bind `PermissionDenied: Operation not permitted`: four agent HTTP fixtures, three legacy chat fixtures and five transport fixtures. These are sandbox limits, not defects. The plan’s 249-pass figure was not reproduced; this tree contains 251 nonignored tests, of which 12 cannot bind here.
- `cargo test --offline --locked ai::agent -- --skip http_`: **46 passed / 1 ignored**. Includes round 1 regressions, structured unsupported detection, replay and cancellation isolation.
- `cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets`, both flavors: **passed**, with pre-existing warnings outside agent code. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check`: **passed**.
- `/private/tmp/politedb-agent-round2`: **three independent reproduction tests passed**, asserting the remaining adapter-floor overshoot, partial-usage bypass and snapshot/generation ordering defect. Coordinator/protocol/wire files are imported directly; cache code is copied verbatim. Provider/model/HTTP surroundings are minimal stubs; these are deterministic contract reproductions, not live provider or database tests.
- HTTP fixture implementation inspected, but no loopback request can run in this sandbox. Ignored live provider smoke not run; it still uses `allow_tools: true` on its second request and does not validate production calls-disabled finalization. Desktop Channel/unmount behavior, live provider compatibility and native DB interruption remain phase-gate limitations. No UI flow changed, so no desktop UI inspection performed.

Fix the two P2 findings, update evidence and request re-review before enabling Phase 2 tools.

## Re-review: AI agent Phase 1 round 3

**Final verdict: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-13. Branch: `feat/ai-agent`. Both round 2 P2 findings are resolved. No new actionable Phase 1 defects found. This verdict supersedes the round 2 verdict for the reviewed working tree; it does not verify Phase 2 executors or authorize enabling data tools.

Read `AGENTS.md`, RTK guidance, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, the prior Phase 1 reviews and “Phase 1 — Review round 2 fixes” in `.agent/plans/ai-agent.md`. Inspected the uncommitted tracked integration diff and untracked `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/*`, `src-tauri/src/commands/ai_agent.rs`, frontend wrapper and tests. Only this review report edited in the repository; no source edits, staging or commit.

### Finding disposition

1. **P2 output budget — resolved.** `run.rs:232–295` obtains the adapter floor through `AgentModel::min_output_tokens`, reserves at least that floor for finalization, refuses another tool step below the floor, and stops without a request if finalization cannot fit. `HttpModel` delegates to `wire::min_output_tokens`; Anthropic and Groq builders apply the same constants/predicates. Missing or invalid primary output counters now parse as unknown and charge the effective allowance. Gemini thought-counter addition and coordinator accumulation saturate. Existing floor/unknown-usage and partial-counter/overflow regressions pass. Independent harnesses importing the current coordinator, protocol and wire modules confirm **28,672 ≤ 32,000** actual wire tokens for both Anthropic always-thinking and Groq GPT-OSS, with complete usage and partial usage; final request forbids tools. The former eight-request 32,768-token reproductions no longer occur.
2. **P2 capability snapshot/generation race — resolved.** `start.rs:21–47` captures config, secret, selected model and capability key while holding `ai_providers_lock`; `ai_agent_start` uses this service. Provider save/delete hold the same lock during invalidation and store mutation, so neither can enter between config read and generation capture. Network I/O remains outside the lock. The barrier-controlled concurrent-save service test passes, as do explicit-model/blank-model selection and stale-generation cache-write regressions. Both orderings are covered by the service-lock inspection plus new-save snapshot and old-key invalidation tests; the old command ordering is removed.

### Regression assessment

- No production tools registered; direct IPC still reaches only a no-tool model turn. Hidden frontend opt-in remains off by default. Legacy UI/session/Run/Insert paths are unchanged.
- Cancellation priority, pre-cancel, concurrent-run isolation, deadline, cooperative worker cleanup, duplicate-call rejection and late tool-result suppression regressions pass.
- Provider-native replay, destination binding, malformed/refused/truncated call rejection, token parameters, unsupported-signal classification and capability invalidation regressions pass.
- Live smoke now sets `allow_tools: false` for its second request, matching finalization. No new provider protocol or library API introduced by these fixes.

### Independent verification and limits

- `npm run build`: **passed**; existing externalization, dynamic-import, chunk-size and plugin-timing warnings.
- `npm run test:run`: **117 files / 654 tests passed**.
- `cargo test --offline --locked`, default and `--features app-store`: **242 passed / 12 failed / 2 ignored each**. All 12 failures are loopback socket-bind `PermissionDenied: Operation not permitted` (four agent HTTP fixtures, three legacy chat fixtures, five transport fixtures). These are environment limits, not actionable defects; the implementer's 254-pass result cannot be reproduced in this sandbox.
- `cargo test --offline --locked ai::agent -- --skip http_`: **49 passed / 1 ignored**.
- `cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets`, both flavors: **passed**; warnings remain outside agent code.
- `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check`: **passed**.
- `/private/tmp/politedb-agent-round3`: **two adapter-backed harness tests passed**, each exercising Anthropic and Groq with current repository coordinator/parser/builders. Surrounding provider/model types and Anthropic model predicate are minimal stubs; these prove the reviewed budget contract, not live provider acceptance.
- Loopback HTTP fixture bodies inspected but execution blocked by socket restrictions. Ignored live provider smoke, desktop Channel/unmount behavior and live provider compatibility not exercised. Capability learning remains dormant on production Phase 1's no-tool path. Cooperative fake cleanup is not proof of native DB interruption; real executor protection, interruption and resource cleanup remain Phase 2/3 gates. No UI flow changed, so no desktop UI inspection performed.

## Review: AI agent Phase 2

**Final verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-13. Branch: `feat/ai-agent`. **2 P1 / 6 P2; no P0.** Phase 1 round 3 remains VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS for its protocol scope; this review does not supersede that verdict or approve Phase 3.

Read `AGENTS.md`, `/Users/lamtung/.codex/RTK.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, `.agent/plans/ai-agent.md` Revision 2 (especially R3/R6/R7), both plan reviews, all earlier Phase 1 reviews and “Phase 2 — Implementation evidence”. Reviewed `git diff HEAD` plus untracked catalog executors, `agent*.ts`, component and hook tests. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Only this report edited; no application source edits, staging or commit.

### Findings

1. **P1 — Cancellation does not retain ownership of the pooled database session.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/catalog/postgres.rs:37–86`; `catalog/mysql.rs:53–86`; `run.rs:175–176,387–398`. Both macros spawn a watcher but discard its join handle and release the client without waiting for cancellation to finish. On run cancellation the coordinator drops the tool future immediately, returning its client to the pool while the watcher still holds its cancel token/thread id. A subsequent editor query can acquire that same session before the watcher sends PostgreSQL CancelRequest or MySQL `KILL QUERY`. Even a normal completion can take the kill branch if the parent is canceled before the completed watcher's `run_cancel.is_cancelled()` check. MySQL makes the race larger by awaiting an unbounded checkout from the _same_ pool; with an exhausted pool it cannot interrupt the catalog query, and may acquire a killer only after the target session has been recycled. No server-side MySQL timeout bounds that case. MySQL `?`/`return Err` inside `$body` also skip explicit `ROLLBACK`; pool reset/recycler behavior is not tracker-owned proof of the advertised cleanup. PostgreSQL transaction-drop rollback mitigates transaction leakage, but does not synchronize its cancellation watcher. **Fix:** retain the target lease in a tracker-owned operation through query interruption and rollback/discard; use independently available, bounded MySQL cancellation capacity; disarm and join the watcher before session reuse on success; discard failed/canceled sessions. Bound acquisition, cancel transport and cleanup, and check cancellation again after checkout. Add barrier-controlled completion/cancel/reuse tests and pool-exhaustion tests, plus real slow-query cancellation evidence for PostgreSQL, MySQL and MariaDB. Never issue a late cancel against a released session. These are source-level lifecycle defects, not socket-bind limitations.

2. **P1 — New SQL previews advertise mutating SQL as read-only and enable Run.** `src/lib/ai-assistant/agent.ts:144–174`; integration at `hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.ts:299–303`. `sqlSafety` calls only the legacy frontend keyword heuristic; the R7-required Rust classification is absent. Independent execution of the current TypeScript functions classifies `PRAGMA user_version = 99`, `SELECT 1 INTO OUTFILE "/tmp/agent-output"` and `SELECT pg_advisory_lock(1)` as `read_only`. The preview therefore exposes Run; `AiAssistantMessageCard.tsx:133–150` repeats the same heuristic and sends the SQL to unrestricted `runSqlQuery`, so the PRAGMA can change SQLite state and OUTFILE can write a server file when permissions allow. Existing manual safety prompts remain, but the new label/Run eligibility is false. **Fix:** implement the approved backend classification integration and fail closed for unknown/side-effecting forms; do not equate the current Rust `Other` category with read-only. Keep Insert available for manual editor review, and enable assistant Run only for SQL positively classified as read-only. Add dialect-aware PRAGMA assignment, SELECT INTO/OUTFILE, locking/admin function and ordinary SELECT regressions. This concerns manual suggested-query execution, not enabling Phase 3 data tools.

3. **P2 — SQLite's “main only” description reads TEMP or attached metadata.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/catalog/sqlite.rs:170–179,195,223–224`. Existence is checked in `sqlite_master`, but `pragma_table_info(?1)`, `pragma_foreign_key_list(?1)`, `pragma_index_list(?1)` and `pragma_index_info(il.name)` omit the schema argument. SQLite resolves these names across schemas, with TEMP taking precedence. Independent SQLite reproduction: create `main.orders(main_id)` and `temp.orders(temp_secret)`; the exact existence lookup finds the main table, but the executor's table-info statement returns `temp_secret`, while `pragma_table_info('orders', 'main')` returns `main_id`. The response labels this other metadata as `main`, leaking identifiers outside the declared scope and generating incorrect SQL. **Fix:** explicitly pass `main` to every table-valued pragma, including index-info; add same-name TEMP/attached table and index fixtures. This can be fixed while preserving the shared connection and its memory/MAS lease behavior.

4. **P2 — SQLite can lose cancellation before its interrupt handle is armed.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/catalog/sqlite.rs:47–83`. The watcher exits after a single timeout/cancel and does nothing if `handle_slot` is empty. The worker checks cancellation before `try_lock`, then installs the handle and calls `work` without rechecking cancel or the deadline. Cancel can land between that check and handle installation; the watcher sees no handle and exits, then the worker executes without an interrupter. Likewise the lock can become available after the deadline: deadline is checked only on `WouldBlock`, so a later successful checkout starts work after the timeout watcher exited. Tracker ownership alone cannot stop that blocking worker. **Fix:** synchronize arming with terminal state, check both cancellation and deadline after acquiring the connection, and keep interruption effective for the whole owned operation. Add barrier-controlled pre-arm cancel and timeout/lock-release races, verifying no catalog work starts afterward and the editor connection remains usable. The existing busy-lock cancellation test covers only cancellation while the lock stays unavailable.

5. **P2 — R6 byte cap and authoritative in-memory history are not enforced.** `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.ts:151–176,193–205`. The trimming loop stops at one message, even when that message exceeds 2 MiB; it measures a session object rather than the complete persisted envelope. A reproduction importing the current function retains **2,097,272 bytes** of sessions for a **2,097,152-byte** cap, before envelope overhead. Larger legacy messages remain arbitrarily oversized; storage failures are swallowed. More fundamentally, `fitted` updates the module map/localStorage but never the hook's `sessions` state, so visible history still contains evicted sessions/messages and later mutations can resurrect them. This contradicts the claimed single list for memory/storage. **Fix:** fit the exact serialized envelope, define a fail-closed policy for one oversized message (including SQL/parts and legacy records), and apply eviction to the authoritative hook state before persistence. Cover UTF-8, envelope-only overflow, oversized single message, legacy reload, visible eviction and subsequent mutation/remount without resurrection.

6. **P2 — Agent routing drops conversation and established context.** `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.ts:282–304`; `src/lib/ai-assistant/agent.ts:49–56,117–126`; `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/mod.rs:37–56`. Legacy planning receives message history, reply-language state, active schema/table and target-table state. The agent branch sends only the new question, runtime connection and optional editor SQL; its backend transcript starts fresh each submit. A follow-up such as “now group it by month” cannot refer to the preceding generated query when it has not been inserted into the editor, and active-table/reply-language context is also lost. Persisting those messages does not make them available to the agent. Existing tests use only an isolated question and do not satisfy the R7 conversation/context regression gate. **Fix:** carry bounded user-visible conversation history and the selected context through the typed backend boundary; keep provider-native replay ephemeral and never reuse hidden reasoning from prior runs. Preserve reply-language/target context. Add multi-turn, SQL-chip off/on, active-table, session-switch and multiple-tab regression cases.

7. **P2 — Approved Phase 2 privacy controls are missing.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/catalog/postgres.rs:166–195`; `catalog/mysql.rs:177–202`; `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantSettingsDialog.tsx:476–493`. Comments/defaults are clipped but always sent; no setting or executor input can exclude them as R3 requires. Literal defaults and free-text comments can contain sensitive information. R6's UI disclosure that chat history is stored unencrypted is also absent (the only new plaintext note is a source comment). The beta description does not disclose metadata delivery to the selected provider. **Fix:** add the approved metadata exclusion setting and enforce it before tool-result serialization/provider dispatch, with sentinel regressions; show the required unencrypted-history disclosure and clear metadata/provider copy in settings. Phase 3 data consent remains deferred; this finding concerns already-approved Phase 2 controls.

8. **P2 — Step UI loses catalog truncation status.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/run.rs:408–412`; `catalog/tools.rs:123–133,169–180,215–221`; `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantMessageParts.tsx:554–558`. Catalog tools put `truncated: true` inside their JSON when row/list caps are reached, but `ToolFinished.truncated` reports only the coordinator's 64 KiB string truncation. A 501-table catalog whose JSON fits that byte cap is sent as partial to the model but recorded/displayed as an unqualified successful “Listed tables” step. The frontend never sees the raw JSON, so it cannot correct this. **Fix:** propagate typed tool-result metadata and combine semantic truncation with byte truncation before emitting/persisting the summary. Test schema/table/column/FK/index caps through the real coordinator-to-step boundary, not only a mock event already marked partial.

### Reviewed guarantees and gate disposition

- **SQL injection:** no model SQL enters catalog executors. PostgreSQL names are bound then resolved to an OID; MySQL and SQLite names/search terms are bound. Generated LIMIT constants and server-assigned MySQL thread ids are not tool text. Literal `%`/`_` searches use position/LOCATE/instr. Fixed statements do not select application rows. No injection path found in these builders. SQLite schema resolution defect above is distinct from injection.
- **Read-only entry:** PostgreSQL establishes a read-only transaction and local statement timeout; MySQL/MariaDB `START TRANSACTION READ ONLY` errors prevent the catalog body from running. SQLite uses fixed metadata SELECT/table-valued pragma statements and does not change the shared connection's query_only state. The documented shared-handle deviation preserves `:memory:` and avoids opening around the MAS lease; it does not justify schema ambiguity or lost cancellation. No data tools registered. Cleanup guarantees remain unverified and defective as described above.
- **Arguments/privacy:** object/type/unknown-field/length/control validation is present; unknown fields and DB error text are not echoed. Metadata errors are fixed safe reasons. Registered tool names only enter step events, with no args/results/provider replay. No new SQL/customer-data logging or analytics found in the reviewed paths. End-to-end trace sentinel coverage requested by R6 is not established by the current executor fixtures.
- **Binding/liveness:** the command resolves a runtime UUID in backend state; the model cannot select another connection/provider. Each executor is captured for that run, and every tool checks map membership. Closing before a later call returns a fixed safe tool error. This is membership checking, not a live DB probe or cancellation subscription for an already running operation; test actual disconnect/reconnect behavior along with cleanup fixes.
- **Fallback:** terminal-event fallback is restricted to known unsupported/disabled codes before `ToolStarted`; post-tool rejection fails instead of invoking legacy planning. No production command path was found that emits tool events and then rejects the start invocation. Off/unsupported-engine routes preserve legacy planning. Abort sends backend cancel with a fresh UUID; pre-abort starts nothing. Existing SQL-chip handling filters `currentSql` in `AiAssistantPanel.tsx:377` before passing it to this hook.
- **UI/persistence:** fixed step labels avoid identifiers; unknown labels are generic; summaries contain tool/ok/truncated/duration only. Copy text excludes step parts via the existing text/SQL path. Step progress is currently shown only after the final answer, since the submit hook does not supply `onSteps`; optional UX improvement, not an additional blocker. Clear/delete retain existing session behavior. Byte fitting and conversation compatibility require the fixes above.
- **Legacy:** all frontend regressions pass, but green mocked single-turn tests do not prove the new agent branch's multi-turn context, Run safety, persistence eviction or native cancellation. R7 Phase 2 gate therefore fails. Phase 3 consent, native approval, data tools and live read-only corpus remain deferred.

### Independent verification and limitations

- `npm run build`: **passed**; existing externalization/dynamic-import/chunk-size/plugin-timing warnings.
- `npm run test:run`: **121 files / 672 tests passed**.
- `cargo test --offline --locked ai::agent -- --skip http_`: **62 passed / 3 ignored**.
- Full `cargo test --offline --locked`, default and `--features app-store`: **255 passed / 12 failed / 4 ignored each**. All 12 failures are socket-bind `PermissionDenied: Operation not permitted` (four agent HTTP, three legacy chat, five transport fixtures). These are environment limits, not findings. The evidence's 267-pass count is consistent with running those twelve outside this restriction.
- `cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets`, default and app-store: passed; six and seven warnings respectively, outside new agent code. `cargo fmt --all -- --check`: passed. `git diff --check`: passed before report append.
- Independent Node harness transpiled the current byte-fit and agent safety functions without changing repository source; reproduced oversized retained history and the three false read-only classifications above. Independent Python SQLite harness executed the exact catalog lookup/table-info SQL and reproduced TEMP shadowing; this proves SQLite name-resolution semantics, not a desktop/MAS test. One initial Node extraction failed on a TypeScript annotation; rerun through the TypeScript transpiler succeeded.
- Ignored live PostgreSQL/MySQL/MariaDB fixtures inspected but not independently run; implementer's reported Docker smoke is evidence, not reviewer verification. No live slow-query cancel/timeout, real provider plus DB desktop round trip, app-store MAS lease, or native UI inspection performed. Current browser harness evidence uses mocked Channel events and cannot establish native cancellation or live provider compatibility. Socket-bind limitations do not excuse the source-level findings.

Next: Claude fixes P1 and approved Phase 2 P2 findings, adds the focused race/safety/context/persistence regressions, updates implementation evidence and requests Codex re-review. No source changes authorized by this review.

## Review: AI agent Phase 2 — round 2

**Final verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-13. Branch: `feat/ai-agent`. **2 P1 / 3 P2; no P0 or nits.** Phase 1's prior verdict remains unchanged; Phase 3 is not approved.

Read `AGENTS.md`, `/Users/lamtung/.codex/RTK.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, the previous “Review: AI agent Phase 2”, approved Revision 2 requirements, and Claude's “Phase 2 — Review fixes, round 2”. Reviewed `git diff HEAD` and the untracked catalog executors, `sql_safety.rs`, `agent*.ts`, component/hook tests. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Only this review report edited; no application source edits, staging or commit.

### Findings

1. **P1 — MariaDB executable comments bypass the new Rust safety check.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/sql_safety.rs:70–93`. The tokenizer rejects `/*!` and `/*+`, but strips `/*M! ... */` as ordinary comments, even for `mariadb`. Independent compilation of the current classifier returns **ReadOnly** for `SELECT /*M! GET_LOCK('agent_review', 0) + */ 1`; the exact current frontend `isReadOnlySql` also returns **true**. Consequently `sqlSafety` sets `read_only`, the preview enables Run, and `AiAssistantMessageCard.tsx:133–150` sends the SQL through unrestricted `runSqlQuery`. MariaDB executes the hidden `GET_LOCK`, acquiring a session lock that transaction rollback does not release. This is a concrete lexical bypass, not a Phase 3 data-tool concern. **Fix:** reject MariaDB executable comments, including versioned forms, before stripping comments; add Rust and actual frontend/backend eligibility regressions. Keep Insert available. Server semantics: [MariaDB comment syntax](https://mariadb.com/docs/server/reference/sql-statements/comment-syntax), [GET_LOCK](https://mariadb.com/docs/server/reference/sql-functions/secondary-functions/miscellaneous-functions/get_lock). Classification/eligibility reproduced locally; live MariaDB execution not independently run.

2. **P1 — Allowed function spellings and unchecked casts do not establish pure built-in identities.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/sql_safety.rs:507–530`. Unqualified allow-listed names are automatically trusted; casts/type names and ordinary operators are not resolved or restricted. Independent classifier and frontend runs both accept `SELECT lower(1)` and `SELECT CAST(1 AS public.side_effect_type)`. For a concrete PostgreSQL fixture, define `public.lower(integer)` as a VOLATILE PL/pgSQL function that performs `pg_advisory_lock($1::bigint)` and returns `$1::text`: the exact integer overload wins over built-in candidates, so `SELECT lower(1)` has a locking side effect despite its new `read_only` label. Custom cast functions/types can likewise execute code without an explicit function-call token. Rejecting `public.fn(...)` does not fix resolution of unqualified overloads. **Fix:** classify unknown unless trusted function/type/operator identities can be established for the actual execution connection; restrict unsupported casts/operators and indirect execution rather than claiming purity from token spelling. Add an adversarial overload/cast fixture through Run eligibility; retain normal reporting regressions. PostgreSQL resolution evidence: [function type resolution](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/typeconv-func.html), [CREATE FUNCTION](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/sql-createfunction.html). Acceptance reproduced; server-side overload effect is inferred from documented resolution and the supplied fixture, not claimed as a live test.

3. **P2 — SQLite unlocks the editor connection before disarming its interrupt watcher.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/catalog/sqlite.rs:85,95–121,145–150`. `_finish` is declared before `guard`; Rust drops locals in reverse declaration order, so the connection mutex guard is released before `Finish` clears `arm.handle` and sets `finished`. During cancel/timeout, the repeating watcher can acquire `arm` in that gap, see the stale handle, and interrupt an editor query that has acquired the shared connection. A thread deschedule after mutex unlock makes this more than a single instruction window. The pre-arm race is fixed, but the new disarm ordering introduces a session-reuse race. **Fix:** disarm under the arm mutex while still holding the connection guard on every exit, including unwinding; release the connection only afterward. Add a barrier-controlled completion/cancel/editor-reuse test that verifies no interrupt can occur after release. Existing tests only reuse the editor connection after tracker completion and miss this interleaving. Static lifecycle finding; no live editor interruption claimed.

4. **P2 — Oversized legacy session metadata defeats the byte cap and creates an effect loop.** `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.ts:82–86,234–245,313–316`. `normalizeSession` retains unbounded legacy titles, ids and context fields. When the envelope remains too large after replacing message text, fitting does not recheck/fail closed: it returns another new array with `changed: true` every time. Independent execution of the exact current function with a legacy session whose title is `MAX_PERSISTED_BYTES + 1` ASCII characters returns a **2,097,402-byte** envelope against the **2,097,152-byte** cap; fitting that result again still returns `changed: true`. A loaded legacy record reaches the persist effect, which continually calls `setSessions` and never reaches persistence. An oversized message id or target-table field has the same residual path. **Fix:** bound/normalize all envelope metadata and add a terminal fitting policy when even the minimal record cannot fit; guarantee cap compliance and idempotence before setting state. Add legacy metadata-only overflow and mounted-hook bounded-render tests. The new ordinary oversized-message and visible-eviction tests pass, but do not cover this case.

5. **P2 — Catalog cleanup can outlive the run and its concurrency slot.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/catalog/mod.rs:173–174`; `catalog/mysql.rs:105–107`; `run.rs:195–205`. The owned operation can spend **3 seconds interrupting + 3 seconds unwinding**, and MySQL/MariaDB can then spend **3 seconds disconnecting**. The coordinator waits only **5 seconds**, logs a timeout, emits Done and returns; `commands/ai_agent.rs` then drops the run guard. TaskTracker dropping does not abort or join remaining tasks. Under a slow/failed cancel transport, the target remains owned, which prevents the old late-kill/reuse defect, but database work/cleanup can continue after the run releases its slot. Repeated canceled runs can bypass the intended active-run bound. **Fix:** give catalog cleanup one bounded total deadline compatible with the coordinator, and retain the run guard until all tracked work has terminated/discarded its connection. Do not claim terminal completion solely because tracker wait timed out. Add slow/failed-interrupt and disconnect tests through the coordinator/registry boundary, asserting Done and slot reuse occur only after native cleanup. Existing immediate-interrupt helper and successful live-cancel fixtures do not cover this path.

### Disposition of the eight previous findings

| Previous finding                                                     | Code verification / disposition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1. PostgreSQL/MySQL/MariaDB session ownership, cancellation, cleanup | **Substantially fixed, residual lifecycle issue #5.** `owned` retains the lease if the tool future is dropped; `acquire` bounds checkout and checks cancel after checkout. Interrupt transport completes/times out before target release. PostgreSQL uses transaction rollback and removes interrupted clients with `Object::take`; installed deadpool client drop aborts its connection task. MySQL kill uses separate unpooled `Conn::new(opts)` within the interrupt deadline; interrupted target is disconnected. Work-body errors/early returns reach rollback. All fixed MySQL catalog SELECTs use `Bound::sql`; MySQL gets MAX_EXECUTION_TIME and MariaDB gets SET STATEMENT max_statement_time. Interrupted targets are not returned for editor reuse. Live fixtures inspected, not independently rerun. |
| 2. Rust safety classifier/frontend integration                       | **Integration fixed; fail-closed guarantee still fails (#1–#2).** New typed command registered in `main.rs`/CMD; errors return unknown; agent preview awaits both checks. Original PRAGMA assignment, OUTFILE and advisory-lock examples now fail closed. Actual Run eligibility remains bypassable as described above.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| 3. SQLite main-only pragmas                                          | **Fixed.** Table existence/listing use `main.sqlite_master`; table-info, FK-list, index-list and index-info explicitly name `main`. TEMP/attached shadow fixture passes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| 4. SQLite pre-arm cancellation/deadline race                         | **Original race fixed; new release race #3.** Arming and stopped state share a mutex; cancel/deadline rechecked after acquiring the connection; repeated interrupts cover stops between statements. Tests cover cancel before first statement and deadline before lock release.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 5. Byte-capped authoritative history                                 | **Ordinary cases fixed; residual overflow/render loop #4.** Exact envelope measured, message/SQL/parts overflow replaced, fitted list applied to hook state before memory/storage. UTF-8, visible eviction, later mutation and remount tests pass. Metadata-only overflow is not terminal or idempotent. Storage-quota failure still silently retains prior disk content; documented limitation, not evidence that legacy oversized disk content was removed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 6. Conversation/context forwarding                                   | **Fixed in reviewed paths.** Visible text and SQL forwarded through typed start DTO; backend bounds newest history and editor SQL, receives active schema/table, target and reply language. SQL chip filtering remains upstream. History excludes resultPreview rows, step parts and provider replay; context test covers a follow-up with SQL chip off. Dedicated real multi-tab/session-switch integration evidence remains absent.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| 7. Column-details privacy/disclosures                                | **Fixed.** Setting reaches MetadataPolicy; comments/defaults removed before result encoding when disabled, with sentinel tests. Settings disclose provider metadata delivery and unencrypted chat history. Frontend defaults column details on; backend missing input defaults off, as documented in fix notes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 8. Typed semantic truncation                                         | **Fixed.** ToolOutput carries catalog truncation; schema/table tools propagate cap flags, describe propagates column/FK/index cap flag; coordinator ORs semantic and byte caps into ToolFinished. Actual catalog-tool/coordinator test covers 501 tables; frontend shows partial. Dedicated through-coordinator fixtures for every description sub-cap were not added.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

### Regression/privacy checks

- Catalog SQL remains fixed and parameter-bound; no model-written SQL reaches metadata executors. No application-row tool registered. Read-only transaction setup failures prevent PostgreSQL/MySQL catalog work. SQLite retains the shared-handle/main-only design; interrupt release ordering still needs repair.
- Agent history takes text and visible SQL only; result rows and structured step details do not enter it. Step persistence contains tool/ok/truncated/duration only. Share/copy projection excludes agent steps and result previews. Final answer text/SQL may still contain disclosed metadata; plaintext history disclosure is present. No new raw SQL/customer-data logging or analytics found in these paths.
- Opt-out removes comments/defaults before provider-facing serialization. Backend executor errors remain fixed safe reasons. Connection ownership is captured at start and membership checked before each tool; active disconnect is not a subscription that cancels in-flight work.
- Off/unsupported routes still use legacy planning; post-tool unsupported terminal events do not fall back. Abort cancels backend run and suppresses final message; mounted panel cleanup cancels on unmount. No normal-history fitting render loop observed in tests; residual metadata loop above is independently reproduced.

### Independent verification and limitations

- `npm run build`: **passed**, existing browser externalization/dynamic-import/chunk-size/plugin-timing warnings.
- `npm run test:run`: **122 files / 683 tests passed**.
- `cargo test --offline --locked ai::agent -- --skip http_`: **78 passed / 5 ignored / 206 filtered out**.
- Full `cargo test --offline --locked`, default and `--features app-store`: **271 passed / 12 failed / 6 ignored each**. Every failure is socket-bind `PermissionDenied: Operation not permitted` in four agent HTTP, three legacy chat and five transport fixtures. **Environment limits, not findings.** Claude's 283-pass count is consistent with those twelve running elsewhere.
- `cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets`, both flavors: **passed**, 6/7 pre-existing warnings outside new agent code. `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check`: passed.
- Temporary `/tmp` harness compiled the current Rust classifier without serde-only annotations and transpiled the exact frontend heuristic/history fitter; reproduced executable-comment/identity acceptance and metadata overflow/non-idempotence without modifying source. Lifecycle findings are static interleaving/deadline analyses, not claimed live reproductions.
- Six live fixtures remain ignored in normal tests. No independent PostgreSQL/MySQL/MariaDB live run, provider-plus-DB desktop round trip, native UI/MAS lease verification, or real multi-tab/session-switch smoke performed. Implementer's reported Docker runs are recorded evidence, not reviewer execution. These limits do not explain the source-level findings.
