## Review: AI agent Phase 3

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-13. Branch: `feat/ai-agent`. **0 P0 / 4 P1 / 4 P2 / 1 nit.** Passing tests do not close the SQL identity race, disclosure provenance gaps or stale-consent acquisition described below.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `/Users/lamtung/.codex/RTK.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, Revision 2 R4/R5/R6/R7/R8 and relevant R1 limits, “Phase 3 — Implementation notes”, “Phase 3 — Implementation evidence”, and prior Phase 2 findings/fix dispositions in this report. Reviewed `git diff HEAD` and the untracked consent, data, query guard, command, consent-list and test files specified in the handoff; ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Independent read-only passes covered SQL, consent/lifecycle and frontend/history. Only this report changed; no source edits, staging or commit. Findings are established from code paths; adversarial live PostgreSQL/native UI reproductions were not executed independently.

### Findings

1. **P1 — PostgreSQL identity validation still finishes before the execution transaction begins.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/data/tools.rs:111–137`, `data/postgres.rs:42–44`, `catalog/postgres.rs:137`. `only_builtin_code` validates on a catalog transaction that ends before native approval; the data executor then acquires another session/transaction. While approval is pending, another database session can create `public.lower(integer)` containing `pg_advisory_lock`, and an already accepted `SELECT lower(7)` can resolve to that new function. Read-only transactions do not prohibit all external/session effects; discarding the session clears a lock afterwards but does not prevent its execution, and cannot reverse filesystem/network effects from privileged code. This is the validate-then-run limitation explicitly deferred by the Phase 2 round-10 review, now on the autonomous execution path. Move identity validation and execution into an owned boundary that prevents relevant function/operator/type/view/relation identities changing through planning and execution; a second unprotected check alone is insufficient. Add concurrent-DDL tests during approval and for both query and EXPLAIN. Disable affected automatic execution until that boundary holds.

2. **P1 — Replaying history to the same provider ID bypasses endpoint/target consent invalidation.** `src/lib/ai-assistant/agent.ts:61–70`, `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.ts:290–305`, `src-tauri/src/commands/ai_agent_data.rs:119`. `messagesForProvider` always admits an answer whose `dataProviderId` equals the selected ID; status is checked only for foreign IDs. Read data with provider A, edit A's endpoint or kind, then ask a follow-up: its old data-bearing answer is included in the initial request to the replacement destination despite the invalid grant. Profile-target edits have the same missing history binding. The backend starts provenance at false and receives role/text history without provenance, so its initial request gate does not protect this replay. Bind stored history to source profile/target and destination revision, validate current consent before every disclosure including the first request, and enforce this through the backend boundary. Cover endpoint/kind edits, target edits, reload and legacy fallback.

3. **P1 — Derived answers and private tool failures lose data provenance.** `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.ts:358–362`, `src/lib/ai-assistant/agent.ts:53–55`. Tagging depends only on a successful data tool in the current turn. After A reads rows, a follow-up that summarizes those rows without another tool produces an untagged answer; switching to unconsented B filters the original answer but forwards the summary. Legacy answers likewise do not inherit provenance from admitted history. Backend provenance also covers potentially private query errors, whereas frontend `readData` ignores failed calls. Carry provenance transitively from admitted history and all potentially private outcomes through answers, SQL, persistence and fallback; do not infer privacy from successful step summaries. Add multi-turn and failed-query disclosure regressions.

4. **P1 — Acquisition uses stale consent after catalog/native waits.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/data/tools.rs:103–140`, `src-tauri/src/commands/profile/store_cmds.rs:14–37`. `admit` checks connection liveness and consent once, awaits catalog validation and authentication, then checks cancellation only. Profile update/delete does not cancel bound runs. Change the profile target or delete it while approval is pending, then approve: the query still reads the old runtime target although the binding/grant is no longer valid. The next provider gate may stop disclosure but cannot undo acquisition. Recheck connection generation/liveness, profile binding and consent immediately after waits and at the execution boundary, and cancel affected runs on profile edit/delete. Add approval-wait and pool-acquisition-wait regressions.

5. **P2 — Native approval is not explicitly dismissed on run cancellation or tool timeout.** `src-tauri/src/ai/adapters.rs:75–85`, `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/run.rs:458–470`, `src-tauri/src/platform/macos.rs:75,115–133`. Cancellation drops the approval future; the 30-second tool timeout drops it after cleanup grace. `AuthContext` has no invalidating Drop, and `invalidate()` executes only when the authentication future's own 60-second timeout completes. Dropping that future therefore skips its explicit dismissal path, and the prompt is not owned by the tool tracker. Give approval an explicit cancellation-aware/RAII invalidation path and verify that cancel, timeout, revoke and unmount dismiss it before Done. Actual native prompt persistence was not exercised; the missing cleanup path is visible statically.

6. **P2 — Revocation purges only currently open tab scopes.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AiDataAccessList.tsx:30–36`, `src/stores/screen.ts:314–339`, `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.ts:303–312`. Closing a tab removes its profile association but leaves its chat cache and `politedb.ai.chat.<tabId>` storage. Revoke afterwards and the closed tab is absent from the enumerated scopes, so its plaintext answers/SQL remain on disk, contrary to R6. Persist scope-to-profile ownership and purge all matching persisted/cached scopes, including closed tabs; add close-then-revoke and restart coverage.

7. **P2 — A pending Allow operation can start a new run after close/unmount.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantPanel.tsx:431–439,535–548`. The Allow handler awaits the grant, then unconditionally calls `submitQuestion`. Closing/unmounting during that await cancels existing requests, but a later successful grant starts a fresh request after cleanup. Guard the continuation with mounted state and an invalidated request generation, also covering connection/provider changes; test delayed grant completion after close/unmount.

8. **P2 — Row/cell limits do not bound acquisition allocation.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/data/postgres.rs:44–55`, `data/sqlite.rs:110–114,150–159`, `data/mod.rs:91–102`. PostgreSQL materializes complete `row_to_json` strings with `tx.query`, then parses complete JSON before clipping; SQLite copies full text before clipping. An allowed query such as `SELECT repeat('x', 1000000000)` can allocate/transfer enormous values before the 500-character and later 64-KiB result caps apply. Column counts and nested JSON collections are also uncapped. R1 specifies 50 columns and caps while reading; a row LIMIT is not a byte limit. Bound columns/values/aggregate result bytes at acquisition, avoid whole-result materialization, and add oversized-value/column/nested-JSON tests. Where the driver inherently allocates one value, document and constrain that residual explicitly rather than claiming a hard memory ceiling.

9. **Nit — Failed grants leave the consent card saying Allowed.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantMessageCard.tsx:247–257`, `AiAssistantPanel.tsx:535–547`. Card state changes before saving consent; save failure adds an error but never restores pending, so the card falsely reports success and cannot retry. Use a pending/saving state and mark Allowed only after a successful grant. Settings also say Touch ID although native device-owner authentication permits password; match the existing contract in copy.

### Reviewed guarantees and phase disposition

- Guard requires one parsed query, walks nested statements/set bodies, rejects writes/INTO/locking and disallowed calls, renders the parsed tree and applies the lexical classifier to that rendering. Data execution receives the rendered SQL; generated samples quote catalog-resolved identifiers. No additional stable lexical bypass established in the reviewed corpus.
- PostgreSQL establishes read-only transaction and statement timeout with failure propagation. Data sessions are removed from the pool on every outcome; tracked interruption retains ownership through cleanup. Query wraps the validated SQL in row_to_json plus LIMIT; EXPLAIN uses FORMAT JSON without ANALYZE. These protections do not close finding 1.
- SQLite uses a separate READ_ONLY connection, query_only and statement readonly check, with an interrupt watcher and tracked blocking work. Real-file read/write/cancel tests pass. Memory/URI paths are excluded. No stable SQLite write bypass established; this does not certify every virtual-table or filesystem behavior.
- Grants use backend runtime/profile bindings, target and endpoint fingerprints, atomic private-file replacement and fail-closed missing/corrupt loading. Grant creation rejects edited-profile mismatch. Explicit revoke deletes grants and cancels matching active runs; delete removes grants. Disclosure is checked before each provider request after current-run acquisition. Findings 2–4 identify gaps beyond those tested cases.
- Missing/unknown mode becomes lock; lock offers no data tools. Non-macOS safe/production offers none. Native approval requires true; denial stops the run without another provider request. Showing the first 200 characters of the rendered underlying SQL is explicitly allowed by R5, so truncation alone is not a finding. UI-supplied safety mode remains the accepted R5 residual.
- Steps remain summary-only and share/export omits them; raw tool output/provider envelopes are not persisted as steps. Reviewed additions do not add raw SQL/data logging. Short raw DB diagnostics are consent-gated during the current run as planned; their derived-answer provenance still needs finding 3 fixed. No independent end-to-end trace-mode sensitive-sentinel run performed.
- **R7 Phase 3 gate is not satisfied.** Four-provider live smoke and visual/native/MAS checks are explicitly still open in implementation evidence; Claude's security review and live PG16.3 results are implementer evidence, not independent confirmation. Existing Phase 2 verdict remains unchanged. Separate SQLite execution addresses its shared-connection data-path residual; PostgreSQL validate/execute race remains actionable in this phase.

### Independent verification and limitations

- `npm run build`: passed in frontend review pass; existing Vite externalization/dynamic-import/chunk warnings.
- `npm run test:run`: **123 files / 696 tests passed**. Focused assistant pass: 17 files / 128 tests, overlapping the full suite.
- `cargo test --offline --locked ai::agent -- --skip http_`: **99 passed / 8 ignored / 210 filtered out**, default and app-store flavors. Includes real-file SQLite write/cancellation tests, guard, consent, approval fake and coordinator tests.
- `cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets`, both flavors: passed; 6 existing warnings in export, MySQL driver and credential-identity test paths; app-store build emits its existing Tauri CLI/private-api notice.
- `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check`: passed before report append.
- Full Rust/socket-bound tests were not rerun. Socket-bind and Docker-permission failures remain environment limits, not findings. Ignored live DB/provider fixtures, desktop UI, native authentication and MAS file lease were not independently exercised. No user credentials requested or provider requests made. MAS SQLite lease and analytics remain explicitly deferred in implementation evidence.

Required handoff: fix P1 findings and the applicable P2 lifecycle/persistence/resource gaps; record targeted regression evidence, then request Codex re-review. No source changes authorized or performed by this review.

## Review: AI agent Phase 3 — round 2

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-13. Branch: `feat/ai-agent`. **0 P0 / 3 P1 / 4 P2.** Several fixes close their reported cases, but SQL identity protection, history provenance, and continuation/purge lifecycle still have concrete gaps.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `/Users/lamtung/.codex/RTK.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, Revision 2 R4–R8, the previous “Review: AI agent Phase 3”, and Claude's “Phase 3 — Review fixes, round 2”. Reviewed `git diff HEAD` plus the untracked consent, query guard, data executors, data commands, consent UI and tests. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Only this review file was edited; no application source edits, staging or commit. Supplemental probes lived outside the repository in `/private/tmp`.

### Findings

1. **P1 — Relation locks do not pin the schema identity used by the later SQL.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/data/postgres.rs:173–205,222–236`. `prepare` retains schema/relation names and locks the original relation, then checks `builtin_only`; execution reparses those names in a later statement. After that check, a schema owner with database CREATE permission can rename `s` to `s_old`, create a replacement `s`, and create `s.t` as a view containing `pg_catalog.pg_try_advisory_lock(777)`. The lock on the original `s_old.t` does not lock the replacement relation, and the pinned function search path does not constrain a view's stored expression. An accepted `SELECT * FROM s.t` can therefore execute the replacement view. This is a static inference from application code and PostgreSQL 16's [RenameSchema implementation](https://github.com/postgres/postgres/blob/REL_16_STABLE/src/backend/commands/schemacmds.c#L230-L281), which changes the namespace catalog without locking its contained tables, and [relation lookup implementation](https://github.com/postgres/postgres/blob/REL_16_STABLE/src/backend/catalog/namespace.c#L309-L371), which resolves schema names anew and locks relation OIDs. It was not reproduced live here. Protect namespace-to-relation identity through planning/execution, not only the original table; add a synchronized two-session schema-rename/replacement regression. Keep affected automatic execution disabled until this boundary is demonstrated. EXPLAIN uses the same preparation boundary and needs corresponding coverage, though the example's advisory lock specifically demonstrates the query path.

2. **P1 — Transitive data taint disappears when the original tagged message is evicted.** `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.ts:292,360–363`, `hooks/useAiChatSession.ts:199–207,242–249`, `src/lib/ai-assistant/agent.ts:63–74`. Answers that summarize admitted private history without a new data tool remain untagged; the fix infers their taint only from an earlier retained `dataRead` message. The 80-message limit or 2-MiB eviction removes that marker while retaining derived summaries. `historyHasData` then becomes false, the backend's first-request gate stays open, and the legacy path's `withoutData` also retains the summaries for an unconsented provider. An independent probe using the actual transpiled helpers returned `beforeDataIndex: 0`, `retainedMessages: 80`, `afterDataIndex: -1`, `withoutConsentRetained: 80`. Persist inherited provenance on every derived answer/SQL or maintain authoritative session provenance that survives eviction and reload; cover both agent and legacy/fallback output, byte eviction, and provider switching after eviction.

3. **P1 — History consent still has no binding to the source target that produced it.** `src/types.ts:267`, `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.ts:292–305,347–348`, `src-tauri/src/commands/ai_agent_data.rs:80–91,133–148`. Stored messages carry only `dataRead`; the backend receives role/text plus a boolean and checks the current runtime connection's binding. Read target X, retain its chat, edit that profile to target Y, reconnect the existing tab, and grant Y to provider B. The gate validates Y/B, then sends X's old answers to B although B never had consent for X. Reconnection updates the tab's runtime identity without changing its chat scope (`src/hooks/useEnsureRuntimeConnection.ts:105–119`). The legacy status/truncation path has the same substitution. Endpoint edits without a new grant now fail closed, but granting a new endpoint can similarly admit history whose source target is absent from the consent check. Persist the source profile/target fingerprint with data and inherited provenance, pass it through history, and check every actual source against the destination grant; alternatively purge incompatible history when the target changes. Add X→Y/reconnect/regrant/reload regressions, including legacy fallback. Checking only the current connection cannot satisfy R5's provenance-set contract.

4. **P2 — Purge deletes ownership needed for a subsequent revoke.** `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.ts:335–344`, `src/screens/connection/ConnectionScreen.tsx:703–740`. First revoke removes the scope's owner entry even while its tab remains open. Re-allow and chat again without changing the connection/editor context: persistence writes new answers but never restores ownership; the registration effect's dependencies need not change. Close that tab and revoke again: neither open-tab enumeration nor the owner registry includes it, leaving its new plaintext history on disk. The independent ownership probe confirms the owner list becomes empty after purge. Preserve ownership for scopes that can be reused, or register ownership whenever history is created/persisted. Add revoke→regrant→chat→close→revoke and restart coverage. The existing single close-then-revoke test does not cover this cycle.

5. **P2 — Pending Allow has no saving state or invalidation generation.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantMessageCard.tsx:253–267`, `AiAssistantMessageParts.tsx:586–602`, `AiAssistantPanel.tsx:547–575`. Both consent buttons remain enabled while the grant is pending, and the Allow promise resolves only after the retried question finishes. Two Allow clicks issue two grants/continuations; Not now changes the card to declined but does not invalidate the earlier continuation, which subsequently starts a run and overwrites it with Allowed. Temporary UI probes against the actual card confirmed both outcomes. Mounted/connection/provider equality guards close the simple unmount and permanent-target-change cases, but do not invalidate Cancel, a new chat/session, or a target/provider change followed by a change back. Add a saving state and one owned operation generation, invalidate it on decline/cancel/close/session/context changes, and check it before retrying. Test delayed grant completion for these cases. Failed saves now remain retryable, but the requested guarded continuation/saving contract is incomplete.

6. **P2 — Runtime disconnect does not cancel a run already past `admit`.** `src-tauri/src/commands/connection/lifecycle.rs:395–399`, `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/catalog/postgres.rs:78–94`, `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/data/sqlite.rs:80–98`. The post-approval liveness/consent checks happen before pool acquisition or the SQLite blocking-worker queue. `connection_remove` removes the binding/runtime but never calls `ai_agent_runs.cancel_on_connections`. A queued executor retains its cloned PostgreSQL pool or SQLite path, has an uncancelled token, and can acquire/read after disconnect; PostgreSQL close only drops one pool clone (`engines/runtime/connection_ops.rs:104`). Its disclosure gate also retains a captured profile binding and does not require the runtime still to exist. Cancel connection-bound agent runs before disconnect and recheck the authoritative binding/liveness at the executor boundary after acquisition. Add queued-acquisition/worker and running-query disconnect regressions. Profile edit/delete and explicit revoke cancellation are present, so this finding concerns the adjacent disconnect path.

7. **P2 — Global CTE-name collection skips real relations outside the CTE's scope.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/data/postgres.rs:51–63,110–114`. `CteNames` collects every CTE alias in the whole statement, and `Relations` treats any matching unqualified table as a CTE everywhere. With `public.t` on the normal search path, valid nonrecursive SQL `WITH t AS (SELECT * FROM t) SELECT * FROM t` uses the real table inside its CTE definition. That table is skipped, never resolved/locked/qualified, and the pinned `pg_catalog, pg_temp` path makes execution fail or select a different same-named relation. A nested CTE can similarly hide an unrelated outer table. Track CTE visibility by query scope and definition order, including the different recursive rule; test self-named nonrecursive CTEs, nested shadowing, and sibling visibility. No side-effect exploit is asserted for this scope regression.

### Disposition of all nine previous findings

| Previous item                        | Round 2 verification                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1 — PostgreSQL identity race         | Same-transaction `prepare`, relation resolution, ACCESS SHARE locks, pinned search path and `builtin_only` are present for query and EXPLAIN. Qualified/quoted cast and typed-string rejection is present with guard tests. Original public-function-overload window is addressed; namespace replacement remains finding 1, and relation coverage has finding 7. Not closed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| 2 — Same-provider history consent    | `historyHasData` marks the backend gate before the first request; no-connection tainted history fails; lock/no-data-tool mode has `history_gate`; changed endpoint/kind or profile target without a new matching grant fails. Historical source identity is still missing (finding 3). Partially fixed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| 3 — Derived/private-error provenance | Any finished data-tool step, including failure, tags the answer. Tail-based taint and legacy truncation work while the first marker remains. Inherited taint is not persisted through derived answers and eviction (finding 2). Partially fixed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| 4 — Stale consent after waits        | `admit` rechecks liveness, bound consent and cancellation after catalog/native waits. Approval races cancellation. `profile_update`, update-mode `profile_save`/`profile_save_and_connect`, and `profile_remove` cancel profile-bound runs. Disconnect/queued execution still lacks the required execution-boundary lifecycle protection (finding 6). Partially fixed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 5 — Native prompt dismissal          | `AuthContext::drop` calls `LAContext.invalidate`; the named `_context` binding survives the wait and drops on cancellation/timeout/completion. Static cleanup gap fixed. Actual native dismissal remains unverified.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| 6 — Closed-tab purge                 | Persisted ownership plus open-tab enumeration closes the first close-then-revoke case, including loading the owner registry after restart. Ownership loss on reuse remains finding 4. Partially fixed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 7 — Allow after unmount              | Mounted ref and latest connection/provider checks block the reported simple unmount case. Missing operation/session generation and duplicate continuation protection remain finding 5. Partially fixed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| 8 — Acquisition bounds               | PostgreSQL query values are clipped server-side before transfer, including oversized nested JSON converted to clipped text; at most 50 columns. SQLite takes a bounded borrowed-byte prefix before copying text and at most 50 column names/values. Long-cell/wide-row tests exist; SQLite tests passed independently. Specific full-cell-copy/transfer gap fixed. These are not a hard process-memory or 64-KiB acquisition ceiling: the database still evaluates full values, SQLite holds its native value buffer, PostgreSQL collects capped rows before the coordinator result cap, and EXPLAIN clipping remains after plan materialization. Live PG transfer bounds were not independently rerun. |
| 9 — False Allowed/copy               | Failed grant returns false and leaves the card pending; Allowed follows successful saving. Touch ID/password copy matches device-owner authentication. Those reported cases fixed; missing saving state creates finding 5.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

### Independent verification and limitations

- `npm run build`: passed. Existing Vite browser externalization, dynamic-import and large-chunk warnings.
- `npm run test:run`: **123 files / 697 tests passed**.
- `cargo test --offline --locked ai::agent -- --skip http_`: **102 passed / 8 ignored / 210 filtered out**, independently in default and `--features app-store` flavors. Includes real-file SQLite read-only, cancellation and long-cell/wide-row tests.
- `cargo test --offline --locked commands::ai_agent`: **3 passed / 317 filtered out**, independently in both flavors; includes consent/history-gate tests.
- `cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets`, both flavors: passed. Six existing export/MySQL/credential-identity warnings; app-store also emits its existing Tauri/private-api notice. No new changed-path warning established.
- `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check`: passed before report append.
- Source-helper eviction/ownership probes reproduced findings 2 and 4's state transitions. Temporary Vitest card suite: **7 passed**, consisting of five existing copied tests plus two explicit probes that assert the observed duplicate-Allow and decline-overwrite bugs; these passing probes demonstrate failures, not remediation. No repository tests were modified.
- Schema-replacement, historical-target substitution, queued disconnect and CTE-scope findings are established by static paths; their live desktop/PostgreSQL reproductions were not run. PostgreSQL server executable was unavailable locally. No Docker/socket permission workaround attempted; the known socket-bind and Docker-permission failures remain environment limits, not findings.
- Ignored live PostgreSQL/provider fixtures, four-provider smoke, desktop visual checks, native authentication dismissal and MAS lease were not independently exercised. No provider requests or user credentials used. R7's open live/native/MAS evidence and the documented analytics deferral remain unchanged; passing unit tests do not satisfy those phase gates.

Required handoff: fix the three P1 paths and four P2 regressions, add the specified focused regressions, update implementation evidence, then request re-review. No application source changes performed.

## Review: AI agent Phase 3 — round 3

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-13. Branch: `feat/ai-agent`. **0 P0 / 3 P1 / 0 P2.** Most round-2 cases are addressed, but planning-time SQL dispatch, partial-success profile mutations and stale Allow safety context still block code readiness.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `/Users/lamtung/.codex/RTK.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, both previous Phase 3 reviews, R4–R8 and “Phase 3 — Review fixes, round 3” in `.agent/plans/ai-agent.md`. Reviewed `git diff HEAD` and untracked application files, following changes into profile persistence/reconnection, floating-panel context, provider disclosure, session ownership and database execution. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Only this report edited; no application source edits, staging, commit or credential use. Findings below are code-path conclusions; the new adversarial PostgreSQL and desktop scenarios were not reproduced live independently.

### Findings

1. **P1 — The relation gate protects execution, but not replacement-view code evaluated during planning.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/data/postgres.rs:245–249,302–316,334–348`. After `builtin_only` returns and before the later statement is parsed/planned, the same ordinary schema owner used in the rename fixture can rename `s` to `s_old`, create replacement `s`, create an `IMMUTABLE` PL/pgSQL function in it, and create `s.t` as `SELECT s.probe() AS id`. For example, `probe()` can `PERFORM pg_catalog.pg_advisory_lock(999); RETURN 1;`. The function and view did not exist during the catalog check; the view's stored function identity bypasses the pinned search path. PostgreSQL can fold that constant function call while planning `SELECT id FROM s.t`, before evaluating the outer `to_regclass(...) = old_oid` gate. EXPLAIN is likewise exposed: omitting ANALYZE does not prohibit planning-time function execution, and it does not even carry the relation gate. This needs schema ownership/database CREATE and ordinary trusted-language function privileges, **not superuser catalog modification**. The plan's assertion that planning folds only built-ins is invalid once the replacement view introduces code after the check. PostgreSQL documents both immutable constant folding and the ability of an immutable procedural function to call volatile functions: [PostgreSQL 16 volatility](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/16/xfunc-volatility.html), [CREATE FUNCTION privileges](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/16/sql-createfunction.html). **Fix:** protect checked relation/code identity through rewrite and planning as well as execution, or disable automatic PostgreSQL data execution where that boundary cannot be guaranteed. Extend the synchronized rename test with a newly created immutable function/replacement view for query and EXPLAIN. Observe a sentinel during planning/while the owned session remains alive; a lock count after session discard can miss an executed effect. Do not treat an arbitrary error as evidence that the replacement code never ran.

2. **P1 — A profile mutation that succeeds before a later failure emits no purge event.** `src/lib/tauri/profile.ts:618–624,786–788`; `src-tauri/src/commands/profile/connect.rs:71–107`; `src-tauri/src/commands/profile/store_cmds.rs:32–42`. `profile_save_and_connect` writes the edited profile and cancels runs before trying to connect. If connection creation fails, the frontend await rejects before `announceProfileChanged`, although the saved target has already changed. Read X, edit to Y with Save-and-connect while Y is temporarily unavailable, then reconnect the same tab after Y becomes available and grant Y/provider B: X's retained answers still have only `dataRead`, and the history gate checks Y/B, admitting X's data. A later successful save would purge it, but a reconnect does not. Delete has the parallel partial-success gap: profile removal succeeds, then consent-file replacement fails, so the frontend skips the deletion purge and retains plaintext chats. **Fix:** notify/purge at the committed profile-mutation boundary, independently of subsequent connection/consent cleanup failure, or carry and validate source-target provenance on every history disclosure. Add save-success/connect-failure → reconnect/regrant/reload and delete-success/revoke-write-failure regressions. The current test manually dispatches the event and does not verify these command/wrapper paths.

3. **P1 — Pending Allow retries using the safety mode captured before the grant wait.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantPanel.tsx:437–439,552–577`; `hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.ts:253–256,364`. Generation changes cover session, runtime connection and provider ID, but exclude `querySafetyMode`. While Allow is pending in default mode, change the same tab to lock or safe/production. `ConnectionScreen.tsx:705–740` updates the floating panel's context without changing the runtime/provider/session. The old handler passes its generation check and calls its captured `submitQuestion`, which sends the old default mode to the backend. Data tools can therefore run despite the new lock setting, or without the newly required native approval. This is an ordinary UI stale-continuation path, distinct from the approved residual that IPC supplies safety mode. **Fix:** invalidate pending Allow when safety mode and other authority-bearing context changes, and use current context only after verifying the owned operation remains valid. Add an actual delayed-grant panel regression for default → lock and default → production, together with cancel/session/provider/unmount cases.

### Round-2 finding disposition

| Round-2 item                | Round-3 assessment                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| --------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1 — OID identity gate/race  | OIDs are recorded, and the **executed query** includes `prepared.gate()`; it is not merely a preflight check. The test-only pause and two-session schema rename/replacement fixture are present in `agent_pg_live_data`, plus a false-gate subquery test. Claude records a live PG16.3 pass and a gate→true mutation failure. Those are implementer evidence; no persistent mutation fixture or independent live rerun here. The volatile-view runtime case is addressed; planning-time replacement code remains finding 1. |
| 2 — Inherited taint         | Fixed for the reported eviction path: the new question and every finalized derived answer/SQL inherit `dataRead`, including agent, direct and legacy/fallback output. New data-tool failures also count as data. Later retained marked messages therefore survive message/byte eviction and reload. The new hook regression passes.                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| 3 — Source-target history   | Successful save/update/delete wrappers dispatch `politedb:profile-changed`, and the module listener purges matching owned scopes with data, including closed/persisted scopes. This is the permitted purge alternative. Partial-success failures leave finding 2; source binding is not fully closed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| 4 — Ownership after purge   | Fixed: `purgeAiChatScopes` retains owner entries. The reused closed-scope second-revoke regression passes, and the owner registry remains persisted across reload.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| 5 — Allow saving/generation | Saving disables both buttons; success alone marks Allowed; failures remain retryable; the promise ends after saving rather than after the retried run. Generation and mounted checks address cancel, session/runtime/provider changes and unmount. Current floating Close unmounts the panel, so its mounted guard applies. Missing delayed-continuation coverage is a verification gap; stale safety context is concrete finding 3.                                                                                        |
| 6 — Disconnect              | Fixed for the reported queued/running path: `connection_remove` calls `cancel_on_connections` before removing binding/runtime and closing resources. PostgreSQL acquisition/owned interruption and SQLite post-open cancellation receive that token. Registry isolation and acquisition/cancellation tests pass; an actual desktop disconnect fixture was not run.                                                                                                                                                          |
| 7 — CTE shadow              | Fixed conservatively: every collected WITH name is checked with `to_regclass` under the original path, and existing-relation collisions fail closed before rewriting. This intentionally rejects otherwise valid self/nested-shadow SQL rather than modelling scope. The accepted/rejected live CTE cases exist in the ignored PostgreSQL fixture; no independent live rerun.                                                                                                                                               |

### Residuals versus readiness and R7 gates

- **Superuser changes to built-in catalogs:** documented trust-boundary limitation, not an additional readiness blocker by itself. Finding 1 uses ordinary user-created objects and cannot be assigned to this residual.
- **Delayed-continuation panel test:** absence alone would warrant a verification limitation rather than inventing a runtime bug. Finding 3 supplies a concrete reason that this regression now belongs in the remediation, not merely a deferred test.
- **Live provider smoke:** OpenAI, Anthropic Opus 5.5, Gemini and Ollama are explicit R7 Phase 3 gate items. They require the user's configured providers/keys and are still open. Code review cannot waive them or represent fixture/unit success as completion of R7. They are separate from the code defects above.
- **Native prompt/visual checks:** RAII `AuthContext::drop` invalidation remains present and fixes the static dismissal gap. Actual cancel/timeout/revoke/unmount dismissal requires a desktop/native authentication session; record as unverified until exercised, without declaring another source defect from absence of that evidence.
- **MAS SQLite lease:** no lease integration on this branch. Normal-file read-only execution is covered, but sandbox/security-scoped access is not validated. This limits app-store readiness and needs lease integration or explicit gating before claiming app-store external-file support; it does not establish a SQL-write/disclosure exploit in the reviewed non-MAS path.
- **Analytics:** approved R6 enum/opt-in analytics remain deferred. No new telemetry leak was established; missing optional instrumentation alone does not block SQL/consent code readiness. Record the scope deviation explicitly rather than claiming R6 analytics completion.
- **Socket-bind and Docker permissions:** environment limits, not findings. No bypass attempted. R7 activation/release gates remain open independently of this **CHANGES REQUIRED** code verdict.

### Independent verification

- `npm run build`: passed; existing externalization/dynamic-import/chunk warnings.
- `npm run test:run`: **123 files / 701 tests passed**.
- `cargo test --offline --locked ai::agent -- --skip http_`: **102 passed / 8 ignored / 210 filtered out**, default and app-store flavors.
- `cargo test --offline --locked commands::ai_agent`: **3 passed / 317 filtered out**, both flavors.
- `cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets`: passed in both flavors; six existing export/MySQL/credential-identity warnings, plus the app-store build notice. No changed-path warning established.
- `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check`: passed before append.
- Ignored live PostgreSQL/provider tests and the claimed gate mutation were not rerun independently. `psql` is installed, but no local PostgreSQL server executable was found. No native/UI/MAS smoke, provider requests or user credentials used. New findings are static path analysis supported by PostgreSQL's documented planner semantics, not claimed live reproductions.

Required handoff: fix the three P1 paths and add their focused regressions; re-review code separately from the user-assisted R7/native/MAS evidence. No source files modified.

## Review: AI agent Phase 3 — round 4

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-13. Branch: `feat/ai-agent`. **0 P0 / 2 P1 / 0 P2.** Round-3 findings 2 and 3 are fixed for their reported paths; two adjacent disclosure paths remain. Round-3 finding 1 is a human-accepted residual, not a readiness finding in this review.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `/Users/lamtung/.codex/RTK.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, all three previous Phase 3 reviews, R1/R4–R8, and Claude's “Phase 3 — Review fixes, round 4”. Reviewed `git diff HEAD` plus untracked application files, following target mutations, consent/history, fallback, SQL execution and cancellation into their existing callers. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Only this report edited; no application source changes, staging, commit, credentials or provider requests.

### Findings

1. **P1 — Import can replace a saved target without purging its old data history.** `src/lib/tauri/profile.ts:835–840`, `src-tauri/src/commands/profile/import.rs:35`, `src-tauri/src/profiles/store.rs:133–156`. PoliteDB import preserves incoming profile IDs and `profile_upsert_many` overwrites an existing profile with the same ID. Unlike the fixed save/update/delete paths, the import command neither cancels profile-bound runs nor emits an invalidation, and its frontend wrapper never announces `PROFILE_CHANGED_EVENT`. Read X in profile P, import P with target Y, disconnect/reconnect the same tab, then allow Y for provider B. The tab's scope and X's `dataRead` history remain; `history_gate`/`bound_consent` now validate Y/B, and the first request forwards X's answers although B never had consent for X. This is the same historical-source substitution that the purge alternative must prevent, through an ordinary supported import action. An independent execution of the actual transpiled import wrapper and chat ownership/purge helpers, with mocked IPC/storage, produced **0 profile-change events and retained the private X sentinel**. Backend overwrite and same-tab reconnect are static code-path evidence; no live desktop disclosure was attempted. **Fix:** include import/upsert in the committed target-mutation boundary: invalidate/cancel affected bindings, apply the approved import consent policy, and purge incompatible owned chats even across partial-success failures; alternatively validate persistent source-target provenance at disclosure. Add existing-ID X→Y import → reconnect/regrant/reload coverage, including closed scopes and legacy fallback. External imports share the upsert helper; their current parsers generate fresh IDs, so the demonstrated overwrite is specifically PoliteDB import.

2. **P1 — Pre-tool fallback reuses consent checked before an endpoint change.** `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.ts:305–318,414–450,464`, `src-tauri/src/commands/ai.rs:54–68,147–160`. The hook computes `history` from one data-access status check before awaiting `runAgentTurn`. If that attempt returns `AgentFallback`, the legacy planner receives that same captured history without another consent check. With a data-bearing chat and a valid grant for provider P at endpoint A, start a turn; while A's pre-tool request is pending, save P with endpoint B. Provider save invalidates capability caching but does not cancel the run. If A then returns the supported “tools unsupported” signal, fallback calls `ai_chat_complete`, which resolves P's **current B config** and sends A-consented data to B. B has no matching grant, and the legacy command has no provenance/consent gate. This uses no successful current-turn tool, so the existing no-fallback-after-tools rule does not close it. An independent execution of the actual transpiled submit hook with fake ports changed consent from true to false during the pending pre-tool fallback: **1 status check; private history still passed to the legacy planner**. Endpoint resolution is confirmed statically; no external request was made. Purging/resetting visible chat also cannot erase history already captured by this continuation. **Fix:** authorize every data-bearing legacy disclosure at the backend request boundary against its source target and the exact destination snapshot, or prohibit private-history fallback and use only `withoutData(messages)` there. Cancel/invalidate pending continuations on relevant changes as defense in depth. Add delayed unsupported-response tests for same-ID endpoint/kind edits, revoke and profile mutation; assert that the legacy provider receives no private sentinel without current matching consent. A frontend-only recheck reduces the window but does not bind authorization to the config that `ai_chat_complete` actually resolves.

### Round-3 disposition and earlier fixes

| Item                                             | Round-4 assessment                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Round 3 #1 — Planning-time replacement-view code | **Accepted by the human on 2026-10-13; not re-raised.** Round-4 notes explicitly identify schema ownership/database CREATE, a replacement view over an IMMUTABLE function, and planning-time folding. The execution OID gate and session discard remain present. Documentation qualifications are recorded below.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Round 3 #2 — Partial-success save/delete         | **Fixed for those paths.** `profileSaveCore` announces every update in `finally`, outside the inner save/connect/keychain flow; `profileRemove` announces in `finally` even when consent cleanup fails. The new removal wrapper test passes. An additional source-helper probe exercised the actual update-mode save-and-connect wrapper with rejected connect IPC and the real purge listener: one change event, private history removed. The repository still has no dedicated save-success/connect-failure → reconnect/regrant/reload integration test. Import remains the separate finding 1.                                       |
| Round 3 #3 — Pending Allow safety context        | **Fixed.** Generation dependencies include `querySafetyMode`; continuation calls the latest `submitQuestionRef.current` only while mounted and with matching generation. All four new panel tests pass: unchanged retries, default→lock stops, default→production stops, unmount stops. Existing card saving/success/failure tests pass. The reported dependency-removal mutation was not independently performed because this review does not modify source. Delayed cancel/session/provider/change-back panel cases still lack explicit regressions, although their generation/cancel guards remain in code.                          |
| Inherited/failed-tool taint                      | Every later question and finalized answer inherits `dataRead`; failed data-tool summaries count as data. Eviction/reload no longer relies exclusively on the original marker. Current full frontend regressions pass.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Ownership and repeated purge                     | Owner entries survive purge; owned closed scopes remain discoverable for a second revoke. Profile-change and repeat-revoke tests pass. This does not cover import or already captured fallback history.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Acquisition/disconnect/native cleanup            | Post-wait liveness/consent checks and cancellation are present; explicit revoke, save/update/delete and disconnect cancel bound runs. Pool acquisition checks cancellation; SQLite checks after open. Native `AuthContext::drop` invalidates the prompt. Import is not part of this cancellation coverage. Actual desktop/native timing remains unverified.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| SQL and acquisition limits                       | Parser rendering, nested-write/locking/function rejection, PostgreSQL same-transaction catalog check/search-path pinning/relation locks/OID execution gate and always-discarded data sessions remain. SQLite uses a separate read-only/query-only connection and interrupt watcher. Server-side PostgreSQL cell/column clipping and bounded SQLite text copying remain; real-file SQLite regressions pass. No additional SQL side-effect path established outside the accepted residual. These caps still do not establish a hard process-memory ceiling or prevent full database evaluation; EXPLAIN clipping follows materialization. |

### Accepted residual documentation

The human's acceptance stands. Its precise scope is **planning-time replacement-object code, for both query and EXPLAIN**, before an execution-time identity gate can protect it. Read-only transactions and session discard constrain ordinary writes/session lifetime but do **not** promise prevention or rollback of external/filesystem/network effects from code executed during planning. This review records that limitation without asking to disable the accepted feature.

The round-4 note's “~1 ms” / “sub-millisecond” window is not a measured or enforced bound in code; scheduling and database latency can widen it. Treat those timings as illustrative, not a safety guarantee. The assertion that the attacker “opens no capability” is the human's risk rationale, not an independently established equivalence between user-clicked SQL and autonomous acquisition. The earlier round-3 statement “planning folds only ... built-in[s]” is superseded by the accepted residual. Follow-up evidence should use this qualified description; no new blocker is assigned to the accepted path.

### Code readiness versus user-assisted gates

- **Code readiness:** blocked by the two concrete disclosure findings above. Green tests and acceptance of the planning residual do not close them.
- **R7 live providers:** OpenAI, Anthropic Opus 5.5, Gemini and Ollama smoke tests remain open and require the user's configured providers/keys. Fixtures and this review do not satisfy that gate.
- **Visual UI/native prompt:** consent/settings visual checks and actual cancel/timeout/revoke/unmount authentication dismissal remain unverified; unit tests and RAII inspection provide code evidence only.
- **MAS lease:** lease integration is absent. External-file SQLite app-store support needs that integration or explicit gating and user-assisted validation before claiming readiness. Non-MAS real-file tests do not prove sandbox access.
- **Analytics:** approved R6 opt-in enum instrumentation remains deferred; record the scope deviation. No new telemetry disclosure established. This is separate from the two code findings.
- **Environment:** socket-bind and Docker-permission failures are environment limits, not defects; no workaround attempted. Ignored PostgreSQL/live-provider fixtures and Claude's recorded live race/mutation checks were not independently rerun. Claude's security-review evidence remains implementer evidence.

### Independent verification

- `npm run build`: passed; existing browser externalization, dynamic-import and chunk warnings.
- `npm run test:run`: **125 files / 706 tests passed**, including both new round-4 test files and earlier assistant/history regressions.
- `cargo test --offline --locked ai::agent -- --skip http_`: **102 passed / 8 ignored / 210 filtered out**, default and app-store flavors.
- `cargo test --offline --locked commands::ai_agent`: **3 passed / 317 filtered out**, both flavors.
- `cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets`: passed in both flavors; six existing export/MySQL/credential-identity warnings, plus the app-store build notice.
- `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check`: passed before append.
- Three in-memory source probes, with actual TypeScript transpilation and mocked boundaries: import retains private history without announcing change; rejected update save-and-connect announces change and purges history; pre-tool fallback retains previously admitted private history after consent invalidation. They establish frontend state transitions, not live native/backend/provider end-to-end execution. No source or repository test files were changed.

Required handoff: close import/upsert target invalidation and private-history fallback disclosure, add focused regressions, then re-review code. Keep the accepted planning residual and the separate user-assisted R7/native/MAS/analytics obligations explicit.

## Review: AI agent Phase 3 — round 5

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-13. Branch: `feat/ai-agent`. **0 P0 / 1 P1 / 0 P2.** The round-4 fallback disclosure path is closed. Import now invalidates history and cancels runs, but its best-effort consent removal leaves a concrete stale-consent failure path.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `/Users/lamtung/.codex/RTK.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, all four previous Phase 3 reviews, approved R4–R8 requirements and Claude's “Phase 3 — Review fixes, round 5” in `.agent/plans/ai-agent.md`. Reviewed `git diff HEAD` plus untracked consent, guard, data executors, commands, UI and regression files. Followed import/upsert, history/legacy/fallback routing, backend provider snapshots, acquisition, cancellation, persistence and native approval. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Only this report edited; no application source/test edits, staging or commit. Supplemental failure probe is outside the repository in `/private/tmp`.

### Findings

1. **P1 — Import silently retains consent when revocation cannot be persisted.** `src-tauri/src/commands/profile/import.rs:37–58`, especially `48–51`; `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/consent.rs:172–181`. Import commits the profile upsert first, then discards every `revoke_profile` error with `let _ =`, cancels existing runs and returns success. Atomic consent replacement deliberately preserves the old readable file on failure. Give profile P/provider A a grant, make the AI consent directory unwritable while the separate profiles directory remains writable, and import P with the same target (an ordinary restore or label-only replacement). The import succeeds, but a subsequent run on P still passes `bound_consent` and can read/send rows without another Allow. Cancellation stops only existing runs; frontend chat purge does not remove the backend grant. A changed target initially fails fingerprint matching, but restoring the original target also revives the retained grant. This violates R5's explicit “import never carries consent” requirement.

   An independent scratch Rust executable included the actual repository `consent.rs`, with minimal DTO stubs and a `ConsentFiles` port that preserves its prior file on failed save. It produced **`revoke_error="AI_DATA_ACCESS_WRITE_FAILED"` and `same_target_still_granted=true`**. This establishes the helper's failure semantics; the import's ignored error/success and next-run acceptance are static code-path evidence. No live desktop/provider disclosure was attempted.

   **Fix:** make import revocation fail closed rather than best effort. Prefer durably removing grants for all incoming IDs before committing the profile replacement; abort import when that cannot be done. If retaining post-commit cleanup, invalidation must remain authoritative despite a failed write, survive restart, cancel all affected runs, and preserve committed-ID chat notifications on partial failure. Merely replacing `let _ =` with `?` after the upsert does not close stale consent and would additionally skip the wrapper's `importedIds` purge notification. Add failed-write coverage for same-ID/same-target import, changed-target→restore, multiple imported IDs and subsequent run/reload; assert no stale grant can authorize acquisition/disclosure.

### Round-4 disposition and earlier fixes

| Item                                                     | Round-5 assessment                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| -------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Round 4 #1 — Import replaces target without invalidation | **Reported successful-import history substitution fixed.** Backend captures incoming IDs before upsert, returns `importedIds` with matching serialization, attempts grant removal, and cancels bound runs for every ID. `profileImport` announces each returned ID; the existing profile-change listener purges data-bearing owned chats, open or closed. New wrapper regression passes. External import parsers use fresh IDs. Durable consent removal is incomplete on failure: finding 1.                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Round 4 #2 — Stale private history in pre-tool fallback  | **Fixed.** Hook computes `history = withoutData(messages)` unconditionally and supplies it to direct app replies, `chatReply`, legacy planning and post-agent fallback, regardless of frontend consent status. Only `runAgentTurn` receives full `agentHistory(messages)` with `historyHasData`. Backend marks `ConsentGate` before the first request, including the no-data-tool `history_gate` case, and checks it before every model step against the exact provider config snapshot used by `HttpModel`. Fallback cannot reuse private history after endpoint/kind/consent changes. New granted-legacy and pre-tool-fallback sentinel assertions pass.                              |
| Partial-success save/delete                              | Update-mode `profileSaveCore` and `profileRemove` retain `finally` notifications, independent of connect/cleanup rejection; module listener retains the purge alternative to source-target history binding. No regression established in those fixed paths.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Inherited/failed-tool provenance                         | Failed data-tool steps count as data; later questions and finalized derived answers/SQL inherit `dataRead`. Retained later messages continue to carry the marker across eviction/reload. Legacy/fallback now exclude the marked tail entirely.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Ownership and repeated purge                             | Owner registry remains persisted after purge; closed/reused scopes remain discoverable for subsequent revoke/profile change. Existing history/ownership regressions pass.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Allow continuation                                       | Saving disables both buttons, success alone marks Allowed, failed saves stay retryable. Generation includes session, runtime connection, provider and `querySafetyMode`; Cancel invalidates it; continuation checks mount/generation and uses latest submit ref. Delayed unchanged/lock/production/unmount tests pass. Additional delayed cancel/session/provider/change-back desktop cases remain unexercised, without a new concrete defect established.                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Acquisition, disconnect and native cleanup               | Post-catalog/native-wait liveness/consent/cancel checks remain. Save/update/delete, disconnect, successful revoke and now import cancel bound runs. PostgreSQL acquisition checks cancellation and retains owned interruption/cleanup; SQLite checks cancellation after opening. `AuthContext::drop` retains prompt invalidation. Actual native prompt behavior remains a user-assisted check.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| SQL execution and caps                                   | Parser-rendered SQL, nested-write/locking/call/type restrictions, PostgreSQL same-transaction builtin checks, relation qualification/locks, pinned search path, execution OID gate and discarded data sessions remain. SQLite retains separate READ_ONLY/query_only execution and tracked interrupts. PostgreSQL clips cells/columns before transfer; SQLite clips borrowed text before copying and limits columns. Focused real-file SQLite and guard tests pass. No additional concrete SQL side-effect path established outside the accepted residual. Limits do not establish a hard process-memory ceiling; server evaluation and plan materialization remain qualified as before. |

### Accepted residual and user-assisted gates

- **Round 3 #1:** human-accepted planning-time replacement-view residual remains accepted for both query and EXPLAIN; not re-raised. Prior round-4 qualifications still apply: execution OID gates do not stop planner-folded code; read-only/session discard do not promise rollback of external effects; the claimed sub-millisecond interval is not an enforced bound.
- **Code readiness:** blocked by finding 1, independently of the following gate items.
- **R7 live providers:** OpenAI, Anthropic Opus 5.5, Gemini and Ollama smoke tests still require the user's configured providers/keys and desktop session. Unit fixtures do not satisfy this gate.
- **Visual UI/native prompt:** consent/settings presentation and actual cancel/timeout/revoke/unmount prompt dismissal remain unverified. Passing panel/card tests and RAII inspection are code evidence only.
- **MAS lease:** lease integration is absent on this branch. App-store external-file SQLite support needs integration or explicit gating plus user-assisted sandbox validation; app-store compilation does not prove file access.
- **Analytics:** approved R6 opt-in fixed-enum instrumentation remains deferred. Record the scope deviation; no new telemetry disclosure established. This is separate from code consent readiness and live-provider validation.
- **Environment limits:** socket-bind and Docker-permission failures are not source findings. No workaround attempted. Ignored live PostgreSQL/provider fixtures and Claude's live race/mutation/security-review results were not independently rerun; implementer evidence remains distinct from reviewer evidence.

- `cargo test --offline --locked ai::agent -- --skip http_`: **102 passed / 8 ignored / 210 filtered out**, default and `--features app-store` flavors. Includes real-file SQLite read-only/cancel/bounds, guard, consent, approval-fake and coordinator checks.
