- Scratch failure-port probe using actual `consent.rs`: stale grant persists after failed revoke, as described above. DTO stubs/storage injection are not an AppHandle import integration test. No repository test/source files changed, no user credentials accessed and no provider requests made.

Required handoff: close failed import revocation with fail-closed durable invalidation and focused failure regressions, then re-review. Keep the accepted planning residual and separate user-assisted gates explicit.

## Review: AI agent Phase 3 — round 6

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-13. Branch: `feat/ai-agent`. **0 P0 / 1 P1 / 0 P2.** Round-5 failed-write path is fixed; an unreadable existing consent file still permits import to commit without durable revocation.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `/Users/lamtung/.codex/RTK.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, all five prior Phase 3 review sections, approved R4–R8 and Claude's “Phase 3 — Review fixes, round 6” in `.agent/plans/ai-agent.md`. Reviewed `git diff HEAD` plus untracked application files, including consent/data/query guard, commands and frontend regressions; ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Read-only supplemental SQL and disclosure review passes confirmed the finding and earlier protections. Only this report modified; no source/test edits, staging, commit, credentials or provider requests. Scratch probe lives in `/private/tmp`.

### Findings

1. **P1 — Import skips durable revocation when an existing consent file is temporarily unreadable.** `src-tauri/src/ai/adapters.rs:48–51`, `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/consent.rs:192–199`, `src-tauri/src/commands/profile/import.rs:40–49`. `AppConsentFiles::load` converts missing, corrupt and unreadable files to `None`. `revoke_then` removes grants only inside `if let Some(...)`; on `None` it performs no write and invokes `profile_upsert_many`. Give P/provider A a grant, temporarily make the existing consent file unreadable while profiles remain writable, then import the same ID/target. Import succeeds and the original grant file remains unchanged. Restore read access: `bound_consent` loads the old matching grant and a new run can acquire/disclose rows without another Allow. A changed target followed by restoring the original target likewise revives the grant. This violates R5 “import never carries consent,” through a read-failure variant of the filesystem-failure threat already covered by round 5. Cancellation and frontend history purge do not revoke that durable grant.

   Independent scratch Rust probe copied the **unchanged repository `revoke_then` function** and supplied minimal DTOs plus a `ConsentFiles` port retaining a valid file while `load` returns `None`, then restoring reads. Result: **`import_ok=true committed=true grant_restored_without_allow=true`**. This demonstrates the helper's control flow and retained file; adapter error-to-None mapping and subsequent grant acceptance are static path evidence. It is not a native AppHandle/permission integration or live provider reproduction.

   **Fix:** distinguish absent consent from failed reads and abort import on read errors, or require a successful durable replacement of the consent store when loading returns `None` before committing profiles. Preserve unrelated grants when readable. Add unreadable-existing-file → same-ID/same-target import → restored-read/restart coverage, changed-target→restore and multiple granted IDs; assert failed revocation cannot commit import and stale grants cannot authorize a later run. The current failed-save test never exercises `load == None`.

### Round-5 disposition and earlier fixes

| Item                                  | Round-6 assessment                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Round 5 P1 — Failed consent write     | **Fixed for the reported write-failure path.** Import holds `ai_consent_lock` across `revoke_then` and upsert. The helper removes all incoming IDs in one consent write; `files.save(&file)?` prevents the commit closure on failure. Atomic replacement preserves the old file, but the profile is not replaced. Successful revocation precedes profile persistence; a later profile-write failure leaves consent removed, which is conservative. The new failing-save regression passes in both flavors. Read failures remain finding 1.                                           |
| Import cancellation/history purge     | Successful import still returns `importedIds`, cancels profile-bound runs and triggers wrapper profile-change notifications. Owned open/closed chats are purged. Failed pre-commit revocation returns before import, so no committed-ID notification is needed for that failure.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Legacy/pre-tool fallback              | All direct/legacy/fallback routes use `withoutData(messages)` regardless of frontend consent. Full history goes only to the agent with `historyHasData`; backend gates its first and subsequent provider requests against the same captured destination config used by `HttpModel`. The stale endpoint fallback path remains closed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Inherited/failed-tool provenance      | Later questions and finalized answers/SQL inherit `dataRead`; failed data-tool steps also count. Markers survive eviction/reload without depending on the first private message.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Mutation/purge ownership              | Save/update/delete retain `finally` notifications for partial failures. Purge preserves persistent ownership for closed/reused scopes; fresh session IDs prevent late finalizers repopulating the purged session.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Allow lifecycle                       | Saving disables both buttons; success alone marks Allowed, failure remains retryable. Generation includes safety mode/session/runtime/provider, Cancel invalidates it, and continuation checks mounted/generation state and calls the current submit ref. Existing delayed unchanged/lock/production/unmount tests pass.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Acquisition/disconnect/native cleanup | Post-catalog/native-wait liveness, consent and cancellation checks remain. Profile mutations, successful revoke/import and disconnect cancel bound runs. PostgreSQL owned interruption and SQLite post-open cancellation remain; `AuthContext::drop` invalidates the native prompt. Actual native dismissal remains unverified.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| SQL safety and output bounds          | Single-query parser rendering, nested-write/locking/function/type restrictions remain. PostgreSQL qualifies/locks relations, pins search path, checks builtin dispatch in the execution transaction, uses the query OID gate and discards data sessions. SQLite uses a separate non-URI READ_ONLY/query_only connection, readonly statement check and tracked interruption. Cell/column bounds remain. No additional concrete SQL side-effect path established outside the accepted residual; output caps do not promise bounded server evaluation or a hard process-memory ceiling. |

### Code readiness versus user-assisted gates

- **Code readiness:** blocked only by finding 1 established in this review. Passing tests do not cover temporarily unreadable consent recovery.
- **Accepted round 3 #1:** human-accepted planning-time replacement-view residual for query and EXPLAIN remains accepted and is not re-raised. Prior qualifications still apply; execution gates/session discard do not undo external planning effects.
- **R7 live provider smoke:** OpenAI, Anthropic Opus 5.5, Gemini and Ollama still need the user's configured providers/keys and desktop session. Fixture/unit passes do not satisfy this gate. Ignored live PostgreSQL/provider fixtures and Claude's live race/mutation/security-review evidence were not independently rerun.
- **Visual UI/native prompt:** actual consent/settings presentation and cancel/timeout/revoke/unmount prompt dismissal remain user-assisted checks; static RAII and panel tests are code evidence only.
- **MAS lease:** absent on this branch. External-file SQLite app-store support needs lease integration or explicit gating plus sandbox validation. Compilation alone does not verify file access.
- **Analytics:** approved R6 opt-in fixed-enum instrumentation remains deferred, a scope deviation separate from SQL/consent readiness. No additional telemetry disclosure established.
- **Environment limits:** socket-bind and Docker-permission failures remain environment limits, not findings; no bypass attempted. This review did not claim those suites or native/live gates passed.

## Review: AI agent Phase 3 — round 7

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-13. Branch: `feat/ai-agent`. **0 P0 / 1 P1 / 0 P2.** Round-7 handles errors returned by the consent port, but its production filesystem reader still reports some unreadable existing files as absent. Round-6 P1 is therefore only partially fixed.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `/Users/lamtung/.codex/RTK.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, all six prior “Review: AI agent Phase 3” sections, approved R4–R8 and Claude's “Phase 3 — Review fixes, round 7” in `.agent/plans/ai-agent.md`. Reviewed the uncommitted `git diff HEAD` plus untracked application files, tracing consent storage through import/revoke/delete, profile bindings, data executors, provider request gates, history/fallback and Allow lifecycle. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Only this report edited; no application source/test edits, staging, commit, provider requests or credentials. Scratch probes are in `/private/tmp`.

### Finding

1. **P1 — Filesystem permission errors still masquerade as missing consent, so revocation can succeed without removing the grant.** `src-tauri/src/ai/adapters.rs:48–51`, `src-tauri/src/file_storage.rs:61–65`, `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/consent.rs:54–56`. `AppConsentFiles::read` delegates to `json_read_if_exists`, whose `read_bytes_if_exists` first calls `Path::exists()`. That boolean returns false on metadata errors, including PermissionDenied when the `ai` directory lacks search permission. The reader consequently returns `Ok(None)` rather than Err. `remove_grants` treats that result as successful revocation without writing; `revoke_then` commits import, and `revoke_profile` also returns success.

   Give profile P/provider A consent, temporarily remove search permission from the AI directory while the separate profile store remains writable, and import the same ID/target. Import commits, leaving the old grant untouched. Restore directory access: the old matching grant authorizes a subsequent acquisition/disclosure without another Allow. Explicit revoke has the same false-success recovery path, including when a previously displayed Revoke button is clicked after permissions change. Failed-read grant checks are conservative only while access remains broken; cancellation and frontend history purge do not durably revoke the grant.

   Independent scratch executable includes the **current repository `consent.rs`**, uses minimal provider/profile DTOs, and a real-file port with the exact current `read_bytes_if_exists`/JSON-reader control flow. With directory mode 000, results were **`import=true ok=true committed=true grant_restored_without_allow=true`** and **`import=false ok=true committed=false grant_restored_without_allow=true`** after restoring mode 0700. A separate Rust probe confirmed **`exists=false read=Ok(None)`**. This is a real permission/recovery reproduction of the helper chain, not an AppHandle/native import or live provider integration test. The new `Flaky` fixture returns Err directly and therefore cannot detect the production reader's misclassification.

   **Fix:** make the consent reader distinguish a genuinely absent file from all other filesystem failures. Read directly and return None only for NotFound; propagate PermissionDenied and other errors into the tri-state port so empty replacement must succeed or import/revoke fails. Avoid an existence precheck that suppresses errors. Add real-filesystem regressions for inaccessible-parent → same-ID/same-target import and explicit revoke → restored access, alongside the existing unreadable-file and failed-write cases. Do not change unrelated readers unless their compatibility has been assessed.

### Earlier-fix disposition

| Item                                         | Round-7 assessment                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| -------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Round 6 P1 — Unreadable store                | **Partially fixed.** Actual file-read and parse errors now reach Err; `remove_grants` replaces the file with an empty version-1 store, or propagates write failure before import commit. New regression passes in both flavors. Metadata permission errors remain the finding above.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Round 5 — Failed import write                | Still fixed: one consent write precedes `profile_upsert_many` under `ai_consent_lock`; failed write prevents commit. Successful revocation followed by profile-write failure leaves consent conservatively removed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Import/history and partial-success mutations | Successful import returns `importedIds`, cancels bound runs and announces profile changes. Save/update/delete wrappers retain finally notifications, so committed edits followed by connect/cleanup failure purge owned data chats. Closed/reused ownership survives purge; fresh session IDs reject late finalizers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| History and fallback disclosure              | Full history goes only to the agent with `historyHasData`; first and later request gates check the captured destination config used by `HttpModel`. Direct/legacy/pre-tool fallback always receive `withoutData(messages)`. Inherited answer/question/SQL markers and failed data-tool provenance remain; eviction no longer depends on the original marker.                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Allow continuation                           | Saving disables both buttons; only saved consent marks Allowed. Generation includes session/runtime/provider/safety mode, Cancel invalidates it, and continuation checks mount/generation before using the current submit ref. Delayed unchanged/lock/production/unmount regressions pass.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Acquisition and cleanup                      | Post-catalog/native-wait liveness, consent and cancellation checks remain. Profile mutation/disconnect/successful revoke cancel bound runs. PostgreSQL pool acquisition/owned interruption and SQLite post-open cancellation remain. Native `AuthContext::drop` invalidates the prompt; actual dismissal is still unverified.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| SQL safety and caps                          | Parser-rendered single queries reject nested writes/locking/disallowed functions/types. PostgreSQL relation qualification/locks, builtin checks inside execution transaction, pinned search path, execution OID gate and session discard remain. SQLite uses separate non-URI READ_ONLY/query_only execution with readonly check and tracked interruption. Cell/column clipping remains. No additional concrete SQL side-effect path established outside the accepted residual. Caps do not bound server evaluation or promise a hard process-memory ceiling. |

### Code readiness versus user-assisted gates

- **Code readiness:** blocked by the remaining P1 filesystem-read/revocation path above. The round-7 port-level fix is insufficient end to end.
- **Accepted round 3 #1:** human-accepted planning-time replacement-view residual for query and EXPLAIN stays accepted; not re-raised or counted as a finding.
- **R7 live provider smoke:** OpenAI, Anthropic Opus 5.5, Gemini and Ollama still need the user's configured providers/keys and desktop session. Fixtures do not satisfy this gate. Ignored live PostgreSQL/provider fixtures and Claude's live race/mutation/security-review evidence were not independently rerun.
- **Visual UI/native prompt:** consent/settings presentation and actual cancel/timeout/revoke/unmount prompt dismissal remain user-assisted checks. Panel/card tests and RAII inspection are code evidence only.
- **MAS lease:** absent on this branch; external-file SQLite app-store support needs lease integration or explicit gating plus sandbox validation. Passing app-store compilation does not establish file access.
- **Analytics:** approved R6 opt-in fixed-enum instrumentation remains deferred, a scope deviation separate from SQL/consent readiness. No additional telemetry disclosure found.
- **Environment limits:** socket-bind and Docker-permission failures are environment limits, not findings. No bypass attempted; no claim that those suites or live/native gates passed.

### Independent verification

- `npm run test:run`: **125 files / 707 tests passed**.
- `cargo test --offline --locked ai::agent -- --skip http_`: **104 passed / 8 ignored / 210 filtered out**, default and `--features app-store`; includes new unreadable-port regression, prior failed-write test, real-file SQLite protection/cancel/bounds, guard, approval-fake and coordinator tests.
- `cargo test --offline --locked commands::ai_agent`: **3 passed / 319 filtered out**, both flavors.

- `cargo fmt --all -- --check` and `git diff --check`: passed before report append.
- Real-directory permission/recovery scratch probes reproduced retained consent after successful import/revoke, as detailed above. Full Rust/socket-bound suites, desktop/native/MAS checks and live provider requests were not run.

## Review: AI agent Phase 3 — round 8

**Verdict: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-13. Branch: `feat/ai-agent`. **0 P0 / 0 P1 / 0 P2 findings.** Round-7 strict-consent-read P1 is fixed. No additional concrete SQL side-effect, unconsented-disclosure or stale-consent path established in the reviewed code. This is code readiness with the accepted residual and verification limits below, not completion of the R7 activation/release gate.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `/Users/lamtung/.codex/RTK.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, all seven previous “Review: AI agent Phase 3” sections, approved R4–R8 and Claude's “Phase 3 — Review fixes, round 8” in `.agent/plans/ai-agent.md`. Reviewed uncommitted `git diff HEAD` and untracked consent, data executors, query guard, commands, consent UI and regression files. Traced SQL dispatch, profile mutations, acquisition/disclosure gates, history/fallback, purge ownership, Allow continuation and cancellation. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Only this report edited; no application source/test edits, staging, commit, credentials or provider requests.

### Findings and round-7 disposition

No new actionable findings.

**Round-7 P1 — fixed:** `src-tauri/src/file_storage.rs:209` reads directly with `fs::read`, without the error-suppressing `Path::exists()` precheck. Only `NotFound` can produce `None`; the parent-directory check rejects inaccessible-parent errors. Other read errors, empty/invalid JSON and parse errors propagate. `AppConsentFiles::read` now uses this strict reader and maps failure to `AI_DATA_ACCESS_UNREADABLE` (`src-tauri/src/ai/adapters.rs:48`). Unrelated compatibility readers remain unchanged.

The end-to-end control flow now fails closed: `remove_grants` handles read failure by durably replacing the store with an empty version-1 file; a replacement failure propagates. `revoke_then` cannot call the profile commit closure before successful removal/replacement. Explicit revoke cannot falsely succeed through the reported mode-000 directory path. The real-filesystem strict-read regression passes independently in both build flavors under UID 501, covering missing file, inaccessible AI directory and unreadable file. Existing unreadable-store recovery and failed-save tests also pass: successful replacement prevents consent revival when reads recover; failed replacement prevents import commit. These are production-reader and service-port tests plus call-chain inspection, not a native AppHandle import/revoke integration reproduction. Profile deletion still commits before cleanup and may return a cleanup error; its frontend finally notification purges history, as previously reviewed. Do not interpret a rejected removal as an atomic rollback of the profile deletion.

### Earlier-fix confirmation

| Area                     | Round-8 assessment                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Durable import consent   | All incoming IDs are revoked in one write before profile upsert under `ai_consent_lock`; write/read failure cannot silently carry grants into an import. Successful import returns `importedIds`, cancels bound runs and announces profile changes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| History/disclosure       | Inherited question/answer/SQL provenance and failed data-tool marking remain. Full history goes only to the agent; first and subsequent provider requests check consent against the captured provider config used by the transport, including lock/no-data-tool history. Direct, legacy and pre-tool fallback use `withoutData(messages)` unconditionally.                                                                                                                     |
| Target mutation/purge    | Save/update/delete wrappers retain finally notifications for partial failures; import announces committed IDs. Persistent ownership covers closed/reused scopes and survives purge. Fresh session IDs prevent old finalizers from repopulating the purged session.                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Allow lifecycle          | Saving disables both buttons; successful persistence alone marks Allowed; failures remain retryable. Generation covers session/runtime/provider/safety mode and Cancel; continuation checks mount/generation and uses the current submit ref. Delayed unchanged, lock, production and unmount regressions pass.                                                                                                                                                                |
| Acquisition/cancellation | Consent/liveness/cancel checks remain after catalog/native waits. Profile mutation, disconnect and successful revoke/import cancel bound runs; pool acquisition and post-open SQLite work honor cancellation. Native authentication has RAII invalidation on dropped waits.                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| SQL execution            | Parser-rendered single queries reject nested writes, locking, unsafe calls and qualified/quoted types. PostgreSQL retains same-transaction builtin dispatch checks, relation qualification/locks, CTE collision rejection, pinned search path, execution OID gate, read-only transaction/timeout and discarded data sessions. SQLite retains separate non-URI READ_ONLY/query_only execution, readonly statement check and tracked interruption. Cell/column clipping remains. |

**Accepted round 3 #1:** the human-accepted planning-time replacement-view residual for query and EXPLAIN remains accepted and is not re-raised or counted as a finding. Execution gates and session discard do not prevent or undo external planning effects. Output caps also do not promise bounded database evaluation or a hard process-memory ceiling; EXPLAIN clipping follows materialization.

Live PostgreSQL/provider fixtures and Claude's recorded live race/mutation/security-review results were not independently rerun; implementer evidence remains distinct from reviewer evidence.

## Review: legacy-webkit-storage Phase 1

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-14.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, RTK instructions and `.agent/plans/legacy-webkit-storage.md` (IMPLEMENTED), then reviewed the complete uncommitted diff on `fix/legacy-webkit-storage` against `b74100b557f9b46ab9526c7231267be6f006f818`, plus all five requested untracked paths. `.claude/launch.json` excluded. Application code unchanged; only this review appended. No real stores under `~/Library/WebKit` accessed, no ignored real-store tests run, and no real user values printed.

### Findings, ranked

1. **P1 — `src/main.tsx:26`: unavailable localStorage can abort desktop startup.** `window.localStorage` is evaluated while constructing the arguments, outside the helper's try/catch. When the browser storage getter throws `SecurityError`, `boot()` rejects before theme setup, persistent restore and `render()`, leaving the app blank. The existing unavailable-storage test only supplies an object whose methods throw; it does not exercise this getter. Synthetic execution of the exact call shape confirms rejection before the helper guard. Acquire storage inside a guarded startup path (or inject a guarded accessor), skip migration on failure, and test that boot continues to render. This violates R4's requirement that migration failure never prevent normal startup.

2. **P2 — `src/lib/legacyWebStorage.ts:81-85`: a failed write permanently completes an incomplete import.** The catch ends the entire entry loop, then writes the success marker. A single large chat exceeding remaining localStorage quota can prevent smaller settings/groups/pins later in the response from being attempted, and later launches never retry even after storage becomes available. A synthetic throwing `setItem` reproduced `first=done`, marker present, later group absent, and only one backend read across two invocations. Handle failures per entry so later small entries still get a chance; route incomplete imports through the bounded failure/attempt policy rather than marking them successful. Preserve already written values and arrange the reload needed for module-time readers after partial success. Add first-entry failure and partial-write regressions, including recovery on a later launch. R3 specifies no completion marker on error before the three-attempt give-up limit.

3. **P2 — `src-tauri/src/legacy_web_storage/webkit.rs:47-48,58-59`: parent symlinks bypass the source-selection boundary.** `symlink_metadata` checks only the final database component. A symlinked `inner/LocalStorage` directory or the entire flat-layout directory therefore resolves to an outside regular database and is accepted. A temporary Rust harness importing the exact production module returned one imported entry for each synthetic symlink case. The existing regression covers a symlinked hash folder and a symlinked database file, leaving these parent cases uncovered. Check each traversed storage-directory component before reading/copying (including the flat root), and add these two fixtures. R1 requires discovery without following symlinks; this is a boundary violation, not evidence that the original database is written.

4. **P2 — `src-tauri/src/legacy_web_storage/mod.rs:388-392`: real-store smoke test logs raw storage keys.** Although ignored by default, this checked-in test explicitly reads the actual legacy store and prints every restored key when run as documented. Dynamic chat/model key suffixes contain persisted identifiers; R1 permits counts/error kinds only and explicitly prohibits logging keys. Remove the key listing and retain counts or use synthetic fixtures for key assertions. This finding comes from source inspection; the real-store test was not executed.

### Independent verification

- `cargo test --offline --locked legacy_web_storage`: **15 passed / 1 ignored**. Synthetic WebKit/WAL and Chromium/snappy fixtures pass; real-store smoke stays ignored.
- `npm run test:run -- src/lib/legacyWebStorage.test.ts`: **8 passed**.
- `npm run test:run`: **117 files / 659 tests passed**.
- `npm run build`: **passed**, including TypeScript. Browser externalization, ineffective dynamic-import, large-chunk and plugin-timing warnings emitted in existing modules.
- `cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets` and the same command with `--features app-store`: **passed**, with warnings reported in existing unchanged code; no new legacy-storage diagnostic reported.
- `cargo test --offline --locked`: **208 passed / 8 failed / 2 ignored**. All eight failures are unchanged AI chat/transport loopback tests whose socket bind returns `PermissionDenied: Operation not permitted` (`src/ai/chat.rs:393`, `src/ai/transport.rs:96,159`). Full-suite verification is limited by sandbox networking; focused migration tests pass.
- Temporary harnesses under `/private/tmp/politedb-storage-review` execute the transpiled production TypeScript helper and import the production Rust module directly. They reproduce findings 1–3 using in-memory storage and throwaway SQLite files only; no repository application/test edits required.
- `git diff --check`: **passed** before report append.

### Scope and remaining limitations

- Exact allowlist amendment, frontend fill-only checks, timeout handling, backend fixed error mapping, private scratch cleanup, App Store early return, Windows-only pinned production dependency plus cross-platform dev dependency, and unchanged `data_migration.rs` reviewed. No new source write path found in either reader. Dependency review covers manifest/lock changes and exercised parser behavior; no external vulnerability audit performed.
- Provider selection already falls back selected → enabled default → first enabled; restored legacy chat snapshots use the existing parser. `chatOwners`/`dataRead` purge behavior is absent on this branch, matching the plan's stated limitation; integration with the unmerged agent branch is not verified.
- Windows/Linux target builds and real upgrade runs not performed. Synthesized fixtures do not establish real WebView2/WebKitGTK interoperability. The plan records the user's acceptance of missing real Windows/Linux probes; this is a limitation rather than a new required finding.
- No desktop launch/manual upgrade performed: it would access the real user stores. The implementer's recorded macOS smoke/manual evidence was read, not independently repeated.
- Non-Tauri boot skips the migration call but does not set the web marker requested by R3; no native read/reload occurs, so recorded as a minor plan deviation rather than a separate blocking defect. The planned reader trait is represented by a cfg-dispatched enum; platform branching remains centralized.

Next: Claude fixes findings, records focused regressions, and requests Codex re-review. Data migration also requires Claude's security-review pass under `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` before handoff is complete. No staging or commit performed.

## Re-review: legacy-webkit-storage Phase 1 round 2

**Verdict: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-14. All four prior findings resolved; no new P0/P1/P2 findings.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, RTK instructions, the Phase 1 review and the plan's `Fixes for Codex review round 1`. Reviewed the current uncommitted diff against `b74100b557f9b46ab9526c7231267be6f006f818` on `fix/legacy-webkit-storage`, plus all requested untracked reader, command, frontend helper/test and wrapper files. `.claude/launch.json` excluded. Only this report appended; no application-code edits, staging or commit. No real stores under `~/Library/WebKit` accessed or printed; ignored real-store smoke test not run.

### Prior finding dispositions

1. **P1 resolved — `src/main.tsx:29`, `src/lib/legacyWebStorage.ts:66`: throwing storage getter.** Startup passes an accessor; acquisition and marker lookup now execute inside the helper's guard. A `SecurityError` returns `done` without a backend read, allowing boot to reach its existing setup/render path. Added regression exercises a throwing accessor as well as throwing storage methods. Theme/font/density and module-time shortcut reads retain their existing guards. Actual full boot/render with a throwing getter was not exercised; helper behavior and call-site wiring verified separately.
2. **P2 resolved — `src/lib/legacyWebStorage.ts:84-109`: incomplete import marked successful.** Each entry has its own guard; failed entries no longer suppress later writes. Incomplete imports count toward the three-attempt give-up policy without a success marker. Partial writes return `restored`, so module-time readers reload from restored values; subsequent imports skip those values. Tests verify first-entry failure with a later successful write, recovery on the next invocation, three incomplete attempts, preservation of existing values, and no repeated reload when the marker alone is unwritable. Marker-write failure also records an attempt. If storage cannot persist either marker or counter, attempts cannot be durably bounded; no-write invocations still request no reload.
3. **P2 resolved — `src-tauri/src/legacy_web_storage/mod.rs:148-159,196-209`, `src-tauri/src/legacy_web_storage/webkit.rs:54-56`: parent symlink boundary.** Both reader variants now validate the identifier root and every directory down to their source layout; WebKit additionally rejects symlinked `inner/LocalStorage`. Synthetic fixtures reject the two reported cases, symlinked general/root directories, and a symlinked Chromium leveldb directory. Regular-store and WAL fixtures still pass. Checks are metadata-based; concurrent hostile directory replacement is not tested or made atomic by this fix.
4. **P2 resolved — `src-tauri/src/legacy_web_storage/mod.rs:412-428`: smoke test logs storage keys.** Source inspection confirms only entry/skipped counts are printed; raw-key loop removed. Test remains ignored and was not executed.

The minor non-Tauri R3 deviation is also resolved: boot supplies an empty read function outside Tauri, setting the marker through the same helper without native invocation. This wiring was checked statically; no dedicated non-Tauri boot integration test exists.

### Independent verification

- `npm run test:run -- src/lib/legacyWebStorage.test.ts`: **1 file / 10 tests passed**.
- `cargo test --offline --locked legacy_web_storage`: **17 passed / 1 ignored**. Tests use throwaway SQLite/WAL and LevelDB/snappy stores, including source-preservation and symlink regressions.
- `npm run test:run`: **117 files / 661 tests passed** in this run; no frontend failure reproduced.
- `npm run build`: **passed**, including TypeScript. Existing browser externalization, ineffective dynamic-import, chunk-size and plugin-timing warnings remain in unrelated modules.
- `cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets` and the same command with `--features app-store`: **passed**. Reported warnings concern unchanged MySQL, credential-identity and export code; none reported in the new migration modules.
- `git diff --check`: **passed** before report append.

### Remaining limitations and handoff

No desktop launch/manual upgrade, real-store smoke, Windows/Linux target build, real WebView2/WebKitGTK probe, or full Rust suite rerun. The focused Rust suite and both macOS Clippy flavors pass; previous full-suite loopback restrictions and accepted Windows/Linux interoperability limitations remain. Synthetic fixtures establish the tested reader behavior, not real upgrade compatibility. Integration with `chatOwners`/`dataRead` on the unmerged agent branch remains unverified. No external dependency vulnerability audit performed.

Codex re-review is complete within this scope. Claude's security-review pass remains required by `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` for data migration before handoff is complete; this verdict does not attest that pass occurred. Commit only when requested by the human.

## Review: keychain-service-rename Phase 1

**Verdict: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-14.

Reviewed commit `3dd0bec34e832a89ecebadce94af14547edf0f8b` on `fix/keychain-service-rename`, against `main` / `b74100b557f9b46ab9526c7231267be6f006f818`. Read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, RTK guidance and `.agent/plans/keychain-service-rename.md` first. Reviewed `git show 3dd0bec` and the complete base-to-commit diff, including ignored native-test code; traced production keychain entry points and the unchanged adapter's storage fallback. Application sources match the reviewed commit. Only this review appended; unrelated `.claude/launch.json` preserved. No application edits, staging or commit.

### Findings, ranked

- **P0: none found.**
- **P1: none found.**
- **P2 — Missing regression for the synchronization boundary.** `src-tauri/src/security/secrets.rs:28`; test setup `src-tauri/src/security/credential_store.rs:342`. All store tests instantiate `CredentialStore` directly with a `RefCell` fake, bypassing `MAC_STORE_LOCK`. The existing migration hooks exercise add/reread races, but none proves the R7 case: delete waiting while get has read an old value and is about to add it. Removing or shortening the production lock would leave the entire current suite green. Add a deterministic threaded test using a thread-safe fake and barriers/channels through the same synchronization boundary: pause get after the previous/legacy read, start delete, finish migration, then assert delete completes with all profile namespaces absent and no subsequent resurrection. Cover both migration sources and logical aliases of the same credential. This is a nonblocking coverage gap; inspection found the present lock correctly spans each complete production store operation.

### Migration and compatibility assessment

- Read order is current `PoliteDB` → previous `com.politehq.politedb` → legacy `politedb` aliases. Only genuine not-found advances the chain. Previous reads disable storage upgrade; add failure retains the source and returns its usable value. `AlreadyExists` returns the authoritative current reread or its error without retry/re-add.
- Previous retirement occurs only after this call reports `Added` and a reread equals the copied value. Failed verification or deletion retains the previous item; messages contain no account or secret. Successful set writes the current namespace before best-effort previous cleanup, as allowed by R6. Legacy aliases retain their existing policy.
- Delete attempts both branded services and every recognized legacy alias, retaining the first error while continuing cleanup. List deduplicates logical credentials and surfaces previous-service errors. Unsupported previous accounts remain ignored. Custom UUID-shaped physical accounts remain unmapped under `politedb`; `_database` support is not reintroduced. Logical SecretRefs, profile/export formats and non-macOS storage remain unchanged.
- All production get/set/delete/list/optional-read calls enter `mac_store_call`; no production native-adapter bypass found. The mutex is process-wide and held through migration and retirement. Its broader coverage of unmapped keys is a documented R7 deviation: an ACL prompt can now delay AI/custom-key operations too. `keychain_delete_if_present` still consists of two separately locked operations; the lock does not provide cross-process or external Keychain Access synchronization.

### Verification run

Commands below used the required `rtk` prefix; Rust commands ran in `src-tauri/`.

- `cargo test --locked security::credential_store::tests::`: **42 passed**, 0 failed; fake keychain only.
- `cargo test --locked security::credential_identity::tests::`: **16 passed**, 0 failed; pure identity/parser tests.
- `cargo clippy --locked --all-targets`: **passed**, 0 errors; RTK reports 6 warning occurrences in MySQL driver formatting, an unchanged identity-test formatting line, and export MSRV compatibility. None is on added/modified lines. No baseline Clippy run performed.
- `npm run build`: **passed**, TypeScript and Vite. Browser externalization, ineffective dynamic imports, large chunks and plugin timing warnings remain outside this diff.
- `git diff b74100b 3dd0bec --check`: **passed**. Final working-tree whitespace/status check recorded after append.

### Limits and release gates

- **No real keychain items were read, printed, created or deleted.** Neither ignored native smoke test ran; no desktop connection/manual migration, `security` command or native probe ran. The author's reported native smoke and Phase 0 file-keychain results are recorded evidence, not independently verified results from this review.
- The fake matches service names exactly; it cannot prove that native `PoliteDB` and `politedb` lookup, uniqueness, listing and deletion are distinct. The plan reports file-keychain success, but data-protection case sensitivity remains unverified. The Phase 0 statement covering every existing DMG install is broader than this review can establish from one installed build. This verdict does **not** clear the MAS/data-protection release gate: retain the signed/provisioned native probe before shipping that path, and choose a distinct service string if it fails. SQLite storage alone is not evidence of API comparison semantics.
- Signed-app ACL prompts, access-group behavior, actual file-to-data-protection transitions and Windows/Linux execution were not verified. Downgrade credential loss after approved D2a retirement remains intentional.
- Workflow requires Claude's security review after Codex review. The plan records one before this review; Claude should perform the required post-review pass, including these limits, before release handoff.

## Review: AI agent markdown and activity (669c1ff)

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-16. **1 P1, 2 P2; no P0 found.**

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, RTK guidance and `.agent/plans/ai-agent.md`, especially R5/R6. Reviewed `git show 669c1ff` on `feat/ai-agent` and traced rendering, request cancellation, session updates, persistence and history. Current HEAD is `d7c92d6`; the five application files named in this request have no changes between `669c1ff` and HEAD. Verification ran on this working tree. No application code changed; temporary review probes were removed. Unrelated `.claude/launch.json` preserved.

### Findings, ranked

1. **P1 — Unclosed link markers can freeze the UI with quadratic parsing work.** `src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.ts:89`. For each `[` the anchored regex scans and backtracks over the entire remaining suffix looking for `]`, then the outer loop advances only one character. `parseMarkdown("[".repeat(n))` measured **119 ms / 471 ms / 2023 ms / 7499 ms** at **16k / 32k / 64k / 128k** characters in a local Node probe of the exact transpiled source. Parsing runs synchronously inside `MarkdownText`'s render-time `useMemo`, so an untrusted answer or restored answer can block the desktop UI, including cancellation. The 2 MiB session-storage cap is not a parser work bound; provider token allowances are not a frontend character/work bound either. **Fix:** avoid rescanning failed link candidates (use a bounded single-pass scan or equivalent), impose explicit input/work/node limits with a plain-text fallback, and add adversarial scaling regressions. Test large ordinary text as well as unmatched brackets/markers.

2. **P2 — Queued activity can overwrite the finalized answer's authoritative text.** `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.ts:359` (new caller); underlying scheduling at `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.ts:578` and `:596`. `onActivity` schedules a requestAnimationFrame update. If the answer and `done` arrive before that frame (or the webview delays frames), finalization writes the answer and sets `streaming: false`, but the pending frame subsequently replaces `message.text` with e.g. `Listing tables…` without checking streaming state. A deterministic probe through the real session hook reproduced final text becoming the activity label while `parts[0].text` remained the answer. Rendering can therefore appear correct while persisted text, copying and later `agentHistory` lose the final explanation. Sequence guards at callback time do not invalidate an already queued frame. **Fix:** invalidate pending updates for that message on finalization and apply frames only to still-streaming messages; add a real-session regression for activity → finalize → frame. This shared scheduling hazard existed for legacy delta callers; the commit introduces it into the agent activity path.

3. **P2 — First-match emphasis closing corrupts ordinary nested formatting.** `src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.ts:75` (also `:58`). `*italic **bold** text*` parses as two sibling emphasis nodes plus literal `* text*`, rather than outer emphasis containing strong text; `***both***` produces strong text `*both` plus a trailing literal `*`. The nearest `indexOf` delimiter closes inside a different delimiter run, and recursion cannot recover the nesting. This is a common model-output form within the advertised strong/emphasis subset. **Fix:** match delimiter runs with nesting-aware rules and retain unmatched runs literally; add fixtures for both nesting directions, triple markers, inline code containing markers and unclosed markers. `**bold *italic* text**` currently works and must remain covered.

### Security, compatibility and lifecycle assessment

- No HTML injection or clickable-link/remote-image creation found in the reviewed renderer. Model values are text children; element names and attributes are locally fixed. Markdown links become spans with label/hostname only; HTML, autolinks, JavaScript/data URLs, Markdown images and HTML inside code remain inert in DOM probes. `new URL` only parses the destination. Unsupported link/image syntax may remain literal text but does not create a network-capable element.
- Both assistant paths (text parts and legacy messages without parts) use Markdown; both user paths remain literal text with `whitespace-pre-wrap`. Probes covered panel and floating presentations. SQL preview behavior stayed covered by existing message-card tests. No change to consent or executable SQL policy in this commit.
- Controlled hook probes confirmed activity callbacks after explicit cancel and from an older request cannot call the streaming updater: `requestSeqRef` guards hold. Per-run backend callbacks are scoped to their own channel. `runAgentTurn.onEvent` itself does not ignore events after settlement; current UI sequence guards cover cancel/supersession, but a settled guard would improve its callback contract. The queued-frame finding above is separate and reproduced without malformed backend events.
- R6: activity labels are transient streaming text and existing persistence excludes streaming messages. No new telemetry, tool arguments/results or dynamic error logging added. The finalization race can nevertheless persist an activity label as final answer text. Analytics remains explicitly deferred in the plan; this review does not clear that or earlier release gates.

### Verification run

All shell verification used `rtk` after reading RTK guidance.

- `npm run build`: **passed** (TypeScript + Vite). Warnings: browser-externalized `node:zlib`, ineffective dynamic imports, large chunks and plugin timing, outside the reviewed change.
- `npm run test:run -- src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/MarkdownText.test.tsx src/lib/ai-assistant/agent.test.ts`: **3 files, 19 tests passed**.
- Focused message-card, submit-hook and session byte/eviction tests plus temporary DOM/lifecycle probes: **6 files, 44 tests passed**. This includes a temporary copy of the 10 submit tests, so the count includes repeated existing tests; four added probes cover inert hostile markup/resources, role/presentation rendering and cancel/new-request activity suppression.
- Separate temporary real-session RAF reproduction: **1 test passed**, asserting the observed corruption (not asserting correct behavior). Final answer text changed to `Listing tables…` after the pending frame, while its answer part remained intact.
- Direct parser probes reproduced nested-emphasis failures, literal unclosed `**` preservation, snake_case preservation and the unmatched-bracket timing sequence above. These probes used the installed TypeScript transpiler and exact current parser source; timings are Node/V8 measurements, not native WebKit measurements.
- Temporary probe files removed; final `git diff --check` and status checked after report append.

### Limits and handoff

No native desktop/manual visual inspection, live provider calls, browser network capture, full frontend suite or Rust suite run. DOM probes establish absence of resource-capable elements for tested payloads, not a native-webview security audit. Pure parser benchmarks establish the algorithmic issue; exact native freeze durations remain unmeasured. No Context7 lookup needed for this code review/business-logic scope. Claude should fix the P1 and the two concrete P2 regressions, add permanent focused tests, record verification and request re-review. Earlier provider/native-auth/MAS release limitations remain unchanged. No commit, push or application edits.

## Re-review: AI agent markdown and activity round 2

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-16. **1 P1, 2 P2; no P0 found.**

Scope: read `AGENTS.md` first, then `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, RTK guidance, the prior review `AI agent markdown and activity (669c1ff)` and plan section `Review fixes: markdown and activity (669c1ff), round 1`. Reviewed the current uncommitted diff on `feat/ai-agent` (HEAD `d7c92d6`), including the untracked `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.streaming.test.ts`. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Only this report was appended; no application/test code, plan, dependency, or Git history changes made.

### Disposition of prior findings

1. **P1, quadratic unmatched-link parsing — specific reproduction resolved; overall work-bound requirement incomplete.** The remembered `]` search removes the repeated suffix regex. Current `parseMarkdown("[".repeat(n))` took approximately 1.4 / 2.8 ms at 64k / 128k after warm-up. Inline loops charge a shared budget, recursive paths have depth guards, and oversized Markdown takes a plain-text path. However, the block scanner bypasses that budget and still has a quadratic fence regex (finding 1). The 200k cap does not make that path responsive.
2. **P2, activity frame overwrites final answer — resolved.** `useAiChatSession.ts:588` applies a frame only to a still-streaming message; `:603` drops a pending update for the finalized ID. The untracked real-hook regression tests pass for activity → finalize → frame, a late update after finalization, and ordinary streaming updates. The queued RAF can remain scheduled safely because it reads the current pending ref and checks message state. No new authoritative message state was introduced.
3. **P2, nested emphasis — reported fixtures resolved; delimiter handling remains incomplete.** Both original nesting directions, `***both***`, code containing markers, and unclosed markers now pass. Valid mixed-length closing/opening runs still fail (finding 2). Do not treat the new fixtures as complete coverage of the advertised nesting support.

### Findings, ranked

1. **P1 — Fence recognition still performs quadratic work outside the new budget.** `src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.ts:262` (cap at `:244`). Reproduction: `parseMarkdown("```" + " ".repeat(n) + "!")`. The two whitespace repetitions around the optional language token can repartition the same whitespace suffix quadratically when the final `!` prevents a match. Exact-source Node/V8 probe measured **44.5 / 163.9 / 665.8 / 2665.4 ms** for **8k / 16k / 32k / 64k** spaces. All are below `MAX_MARKDOWN_CHARS`; the inline budget cannot interrupt regex execution. Rendering remains synchronous, so an answer or restored answer can freeze the UI. This regex predates the rewrite, but remains an unaddressed parser work path under the prior P1 fix requirement. **Fix:** recognize fences with a linear scan or non-overlapping validation steps; add adversarial `parseMarkdown` scaling regressions, including near-miss fences. Keep work/render limits effective before expensive block recognition. Existing hostile-input tests mostly call `parseInline`, so they miss this path.

2. **P2 — Nested underscore closing runs and split triple runs remain incorrectly parsed.** `src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.ts:76-78`, `:109-113`, `:187-198`. `__a _b___` and `_a __b___` both return a single literal text node instead of nested strong/emphasis. For underscores, `canClose` tests `s[k + len]` in the middle of a longer closing run; the remaining `_` is classified as a word character, rejecting a legitimate closer. Separately, `***a** b*` stays wholly literal although it represents emphasis containing strong `a` followed by ` b`: `findCloser` requires a closer at least as long as the original opener and cannot split a triple opener across two closers. These are remaining correctness gaps in the rewritten nesting algorithm, not a request for unsupported Markdown features. **Fix:** consume delimiter runs with remaining opener/closer counts and apply underscore word-boundary checks outside the whole run; cover these fixtures and retain the now-passing star/code/snake_case cases.

3. **P2 — Emphasis matching treats link destinations as visible delimiter text.** `src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.ts:97-119` (link construction at `:157-179`). `*x [label](https://example.com/a*b) y*` becomes an emphasis node containing literal `x [label](https://example.com/a`, followed by literal `b) y*`; the link and its domain presentation disappear. The same failure occurs with strong emphasis and `a**b`. The outer delimiter search skips code spans but scans through links, closing inside their opaque destinations before `parseRange` can build the link node. This interaction also existed in the old parser; it remains newly identified within the rewritten parser's supported emphasis/link combination. **Fix:** recognize complete link spans while locating emphasis closers, keeping destinations opaque, or tokenize those spans before delimiter resolution; add links containing `*`/`**` inside outer emphasis to regression coverage. No HTML execution is implied by this finding: the exposed destination remains inert text.

### Depth, text preservation, and injection assessment

- `parseRange` and `findCloser` both enforce `MAX_NESTING = 16`, with one shared work budget and a caught `WorkLimit`. Deep mixed-marker input (10,000 repeated opener pairs plus closing pairs, 120,003 characters) completed in approximately 7.6 ms and returned the entire original text as plain text. No stack overflow reproduced.
- Five direct assertions checked exact flattened text preservation for 128k unmatched brackets, repeated unmatched single/double-star openers, deep mixed-marker fallback, and a 240k-character oversized multiline answer. All passed. The permanent hostile-input test only asserts a nonempty flattened result despite its “Nothing is lost” comment; it would not detect partial text loss. Add exact fallback assertions to retain this guarantee.
- No new HTML/attribute injection sink found. Renderer remains fixed JSX with text children; links are spans, destinations only reach `new URL(...).hostname`, and the existing DOM test confirms HTML remains literal with no `img` or `a`. Unsupported syntax and malformed links can degrade presentation, as above, but no executable markup or resource-capable element was found. This is source inspection plus the existing DOM regression, not a new exhaustive hostile-DOM or native-webview audit.
- The size fallback is a formatting cutoff, not a constant node cap: `plainInline` still emits a break node per newline. This review does not establish a total DOM-node/render-time bound for arbitrarily large supplied text.

### Verification run

- `rtk npm run build`: **passed**, including TypeScript. Existing warnings: browser-externalized `node:zlib`, ineffective dynamic imports, large chunks, plugin timings.
- `rtk npm run test:run -- src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/MarkdownText.test.tsx src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantMessageCard.test.tsx src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.streaming.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.bytes.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.evict.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.agent.test.ts src/lib/ai-assistant/agent.test.ts`: **8 files, 56 tests passed**, including both untracked streaming tests. An earlier narrower invocation passed 4 files / 19 tests; two mistyped file filters matched nothing, so the corrected invocation above supplies the adjacent submit/eviction evidence.
- `rtk proxy node --input-type=module` probes loaded the exact working-tree parser through the installed TypeScript transpiler. Reproduced all three findings, measured bracket/fence scaling, exercised deep nesting, and passed five exact text-preservation assertions. Probes were in-memory; no temporary source files remain.
- `rtk git diff --check`: **passed** before and after report append. Working-tree scope checked; pre-existing edits and untracked files preserved.

Limits: no full frontend suite, native/manual visual run, provider/DB calls, browser network capture, or Rust checks. Native WebKit timing remains unmeasured; Node scaling demonstrates the fence algorithm's failure, not an exact native freeze duration. No Context7 lookup needed for this code-review/business-logic scope. Earlier provider/native-auth/MAS and analytics release gates are outside this re-review. Claude should fix the P1 and these P2 cases, record focused verification, and request re-review. No commit or push.

## Re-review: AI agent markdown and activity round 3

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-16. **1 P2; no P0/P1 found.**

Scope: read `AGENTS.md` first, then `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, RTK guidance, round 2 of this review and plan section `Review fixes: markdown and activity, round 2`. Reviewed the current uncommitted diff on `feat/ai-agent`, including untracked `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.streaming.test.ts`. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Only this review section was appended; application code, tests, plan and Git history were not modified.

### Disposition of prior findings

1. **Round 2 P1, quadratic fence recognition — resolved.** `src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.ts:328` uses trim/prefix/token validation without overlapping whitespace repetitions; closing fences use trim/equality. Near-miss fence probes at 16k / 32k / 64k / 128k spaces took **0.76 / 0.29 / 0.63 / 0.97 ms**. Heading/list tails now accept Unicode line separators without forcing whitespace backtracking. Permanent block near-miss tests pass. The original unmatched-bracket case also remains covered and passes.
2. **Round 2 P2, nested underscore/split triple runs — partially resolved.** Whole-run underscore boundary checks and range boundaries fix `__a _b___` and `_a __b___`. Both isolated split-triple fixtures (`***a** b*`, `***a* b**`) pass, as do original nesting/triple/code cases. However, split resolution is only attempted when no later full triple closer exists. Composition with another ordinary formatted span remains broken; see finding 1 below. This prior correctness requirement is not fully resolved.
3. **Round 2 P2, emphasis closes inside link destinations — resolved.** `markdown.ts:137` skips complete links through the same `linkEnd` used by construction. Both permanent single/double-star destination fixtures pass; a repeated-links-within-emphasis probe scales without the prior corruption. Destinations remain opaque and inert.
4. **Original P2, queued activity overwrites final text — remains resolved.** `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.ts:588` checks streaming state, and finalization at `:603` clears a matching pending update. Both untracked real-hook tests pass, covering finalize-before-frame, late activity after finalization, and normal streaming updates. No parallel authoritative state was introduced.
5. **Round 2 text-preservation coverage request — resolved.** Permanent hostile-input assertions now require exact flattened equality, rather than nonempty output. Six additional direct equality assertions passed for unmatched brackets, single/double-star exhaustion, deep mixed markers, newline-heavy fallback and oversized multiline input.

### Findings, ranked

1. **P2 — A later triple closer bypasses an earlier valid split closure and merges separate formatted spans.** `src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.ts:213` (fallback only at `:227`; matching at `:149`). `parseInline("***a** b* then ***c***")` returns one `strong(em(text("a** b* then ***c")))` node. Expected: `em(strong("a"), " b")`, literal `" then "`, then `strong(em("c"))`. Likewise, `***a* b** then ***c***` collapses into one triple-formatted span. The same split fixtures pass when used alone: adding unrelated subsequent formatting changes their interpretation. `findCloser` ignores the earlier partial closures while searching for a run of at least three, then succeeds at the final `***`; `parseRange` therefore never tries `splitTriple`. **Fix:** resolve partial opener/closer counts in source order (or compare eligible split and full closures before choosing), preserving separate subsequent spans. Add composition fixtures for both split directions, underscores, and following formatted text; retain existing budget/depth/fallback tests. This is a remaining supported-emphasis correctness defect, not an injection finding or a request for additional Markdown syntax.

### Work bounds, recursion, text and injection assessment

- Inspected all block recognizers and inline scanning paths, including cached character searches, link-destination scanning, delimiter runs, recursive closer search and split fallback. No additional super-linear path or catastrophic regex backtracking was established. Destination scans and recursive loops charge the shared budget; depth guards precede recursion; fallback preserves original inline source. Block regexes have no remaining overlapping unbounded repetitions that reproduce the former fence failure. This is inspection plus adversarial probes, not a formal complexity proof for every input/engine.
- At 16k / 32k / 64k / 128k size parameters, numeric near-miss lines took **0.19 / 0.35 / 0.65 / 1.25 ms**; list continuation chains **1.53 / 1.59 / 1.94 / 4.17 ms**; repeated links inside emphasis **1.84 / 2.63 / 2.98 / 6.12 ms**; repeated `[` followed by an unterminated destination **1.96 / 3.21 / 6.16 / 11.29 ms**. Deep mixed markers fell back in under 0.1 ms in this probe, with no stack overflow. Sizes are construction parameters; each family's actual length differs slightly.
- No new literal/fallback text loss reproduced. Formatting and link destinations intentionally remove syntax from displayed output; the P2 above incorrectly retains internal marker syntax and spreads formatting across spans.
- Renderer inspection and the existing `MarkdownText` DOM tests still show fixed JSX/text children, span-only links, no HTML insertion, and no image/link resource creation. `new URL` only extracts a hostname. No injection sink found in this diff.
- The 200k cutoff remains a formatting cutoff, not a hard DOM-node limit: oversized plain text still produces a node per newline. No constant render-time/node ceiling is claimed for arbitrary oversized input, consistent with round 2's limitation.

### Verification run

- `rtk npm run build`: **passed**, TypeScript and Vite. Warnings remain for browser-externalized `node:zlib`, ineffective dynamic imports, large chunks and plugin timings; no warning identified on the reviewed parser/session change.
- `rtk npm run test:run -- src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/MarkdownText.test.tsx src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantMessageCard.test.tsx src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.streaming.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.bytes.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.evict.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.agent.test.ts src/lib/ai-assistant/agent.test.ts`: **8 files, 57 tests passed**, including both untracked session tests. Earlier narrower invocation: 4 files / 20 passed; two nonexistent submit/eviction filters matched nothing, corrected in this final command.
- In-memory Node probes transpiled the working-tree parser with the installed TypeScript compiler, retaining native Error subclass semantics (`ES2022` target). Six exact preservation assertions passed, scaling results above were collected, and both split-composition defects reproduced. An initial probe used TypeScript's default ES5 target and failed `instanceof WorkLimit` due to downlevel Error subclassing; that harness result was discarded and corrected. The project targets ES2020 and the actual build/tests pass; this harness artifact is not an application finding.
- `rtk git diff --check`: passed before append; repeated after append. No temporary probe files created.

Limits: no full frontend suite, native/manual UI inspection, live provider/DB calls, browser network capture or Rust checks. Timings are Node/V8, not native WebKit. No Context7 lookup needed for this code-review/business-logic scope. Existing provider/native-auth/MAS/analytics release gates remain outside this re-review. Claude should fix the remaining P2 composition case, record verification and request re-review. No commit or push.

## Re-review: AI agent markdown and activity round 4

**Verdict: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-16. **No new P0/P1/P2 findings.**

Scope: read `AGENTS.md` first, then `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, RTK guidance, round 3 of this review and plan section `Review fixes: markdown and activity, round 3`. Reviewed the current uncommitted `git diff` on `feat/ai-agent` (HEAD `d7c92d6`), plus untracked `src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.streaming.test.ts`. `.claude/launch.json` excluded. Only this section appended; application code, tests, plan and Git history unchanged by reviewer.

### Disposition

- **Round 3 P2 resolved — split closure versus later triple closure.** `src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.ts:213-221` computes both full and split readings, selecting the split when its end precedes the full end. `splitTriple` at `:264-295` compares both inner/outer orders; `buildSplit` at `:298-319` constructs the selected ranges without consuming intervening text. Both reported examples (`***a** b* then ***c***`, `***a* b** then ***c***`) now produce separate spans. Permanent fixtures pass, including underscore composition and consecutive full triples. Independent structural probes also passed both split directions for both `*` and `_`, followed by a full triple span.
- **Earlier fixes remain resolved within tested scope.** Nested emphasis, opaque link destinations, fence near misses, unmatched brackets and exact literal/fallback preservation tests pass. Both untracked real-session tests pass: queued activity cannot overwrite final text, late updates remain inert and normal streaming updates still apply. The production finalization clear and frame-time streaming guard remain in place.

### New findings, ranked

- **P0: none found.**
- **P1: none found.**
- **P2: none found.**

### Correctness, work bounds, recursion, text and injection

- Reviewed inline range construction, full/split selection, cached searches, delimiter/link scans and all block recognizers. No new out-of-range consumption, text-loss path, unbounded recursive call chain or reproducible super-linear freeze established. Full and split searches share the same budget; recursive closer/range entry points retain depth guards. Additional searches may exhaust the budget, but fallback returns the original inline source rather than partial parsed output. This is inspection and adversarial evidence, not a formal complexity proof.
- In-memory probes passed **20,000 seeded non-delimiter text-preservation checks** over mixed stars, underscores, letters, spaces and newlines: flattened output after removing delimiter characters exactly matched the input with those characters removed. This checks preservation of ordinary content, not conformance of every random delimiter arrangement. Separate exact-equality checks covered oversized multiline, deep mixed markers and repeated backticks.
- At construction sizes 16k / 32k / 64k / 128k, Node/V8 times in ms were: repeated triple openers **0.90 / 2.19 / 2.97 / 5.75**; repeated split spans **1.07 / 1.77 / 3.46 / 6.55**; unmatched brackets **0.90 / 0.76 / 1.39 / 1.83**; unmatched brackets followed by an unterminated destination **2.36 / 3.27 / 5.80 / 11.42**; near-miss fences **1.21 / 0.29 / 0.54 / 0.93**; deep mixed markers **0.03 / 0.02 / 0.02 / 0.05**. Actual lengths vary by construction; deep cases use fallback. No stack overflow reproduced.
- Injection assessment: parser returns data only. Inspected `MarkdownText.tsx`: fixed JSX elements, text children, no HTML insertion, dynamic URL attributes or resource-capable link/image elements. Links remain spans containing label/domain; `new URL` only extracts the hostname. Existing hostile HTML/link DOM regression passes. No new injection sink found.

### Verification run

- `rtk npm run test:run -- src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/MarkdownText.test.tsx src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantMessageCard.test.tsx src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.streaming.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.bytes.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiChatSession.evict.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.agent.test.ts src/lib/ai-assistant/agent.test.ts`: **8 files / 57 tests passed**.

### Limitations

The plan's documented formatting fallback remains: repeated valid split triples can exhaust the work budget and display literally. Text is preserved; formatted output is not guaranteed for every input below 200k characters.
