## Review: ollama-native-context Phase 1

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-16. No P0/P1 found; four P2 findings below require correction against the approved Phase 1 contracts.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, RTK guidance and `.agent/plans/ollama-native-context.md`, including Revisions 4–6 and Phase 1 implementation evidence. Reviewed the uncommitted task diff on `feat/ollama-native-context` plus untracked `ai/agent/prepare.rs`, `ai/agent/wire_ollama.rs`, `ai/generations.rs` and `ai/ollama.rs`. `.claude/launch.json` excluded. Unrelated edits to `src/lib/politedbBackend/ai.ts` and its test appeared during review; excluded from this task review. Only this review section was written by reviewer; application code and plan unchanged. No credentials, saved provider files, real user data or database operations used.

### Findings, ranked

1. **P2 — Malformed versions pass the native gate and can expose arbitrary provider text in settings.** `src-tauri/src/ai/ollama.rs:54` discards everything after the first `+`; `:59` only checks whether a prerelease is empty; `:65` allows leading-zero core components. Exact extracted Rust functions return `Some(true)` for `0.12.6+`, `00.12.6`, `0.12.7-rc..1`, and `0.12.6+synthetic\nPRIVATE_PROVIDER_TEXT` (actual newline in the probe). A2′ row 5/C1 require malformed SemVer to return unreachable with no conversation send. Instead `identify` accepts Native and retains the original string; `server_status` at `ollama.rs:304` forwards it, and `OllamaStatusLine` at `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantSettingsDialog.tsx:861` renders it. This is a provider-text containment failure, not HTML execution. **Fix:** validate the entire SemVer grammar before precedence comparison or status publication, including core/prerelease numeric leading-zero rules and nonempty ASCII prerelease/build identifiers. Add routing and status fixtures for malformed suffixes, multiple `+`, whitespace/newlines, invalid identifiers and sensitive-looking suffixes; preserve valid build metadata and boundary prerelease behavior.

2. **P2 — Generic tools detection overrides a recognized native context error, allowing false capability learning and fallback.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/model.rs:120` runs `tools_unsupported` before the native classifier at `:123`. For HTTP 400 `{"error":"the input length exceeds the context length: does not support tools"}`, the approved D2 classifier returns `AI_AGENT_CONTEXT_FULL`, but the preceding suffix matcher also returns true, so `step_native` returns `AI_AGENT_TOOLS_UNSUPPORTED`. `ChannelSink::send` (`commands/ai_agent.rs:64`) then caches a native negative; when no tool ran, `src/lib/ai-assistant/agent.ts:188` permits legacy fallback. Thus an appended suffix changes a context failure into a capability downgrade and potentially resends via the legacy wire. This overlap was reproduced with the exact extracted classifier functions, using synthetic text only. **Fix:** give recognized native context/load/model errors precedence, or make the classifiers mutually exclusive. Add native HTTP fixtures with overlapping sensitive-looking suffixes and assert context-full does not record unsupported or trigger fallback; retain genuine tools-unsupported behavior.

3. **P2 — Removing the default model leaves the previous model's context status authoritative.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantSettingsDialog.tsx:306` changes `defaultModel` through `updateDraft` without clearing/canceling/re-querying status, unlike the explicit selection path at `:285`. With models A and B, open A's native status and remove A: B becomes default while the line still shows A's trained maximum/effective context. A pending A response is also still accepted because its token is unchanged. Removing the sole model can leave native context copy instead of `native_no_model`; adding the first model via `addModels` (`:250`) can leave the inverse stale state. C2/C5 require default-model changes to invalidate and re-query. **Fix:** route all actual default-model transitions through one status-invalidation/re-query path, including removal and first addition. Add resolved and delayed-response tests for removal with a fallback model, removal of the last model, and adding the first model. Current tests only cover clicking another model's default button.

4. **P2 — Required native transport and command-lifecycle regression gates are absent.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/model.rs:445` constructs HTTP fixtures with `HttpModel::new`, which selects the compatibility wire. None of those HTTP tests call native `HttpModel::step`; native JSON/replay and error tests exercise builder/parser/classifier helpers in isolation. Consequently A1/A5/D2's actual native outbound URL/body and bounded HTTP classifier fixtures do not exist, and would not catch finding 2. Preparation tests at `ai/agent/prepare.rs:448` onward separately test a pending preparation deadline, terminal emission, and routing cancellation, but do not cover the promised preparation-plus-model shared deadline, metadata cancellation, connection-close/consent-revoke during preparation with zero chat sends, or run-slot release through the command path. No discovery/status command integration tests establish cancellation during each probe and ownership cleanup on command exits. **Fix:** add the approved injected-transport/local-server native round-trip fixtures (including replay, flags/clamp on every step, final tools omission, malformed/oversized responses, classifier positives/negatives and prefix/IPv6 destinations) and focused lifecycle tests covering these interleavings. A channel-closed `emit_end` unit test alone does not verify closure during preparation. This finding concerns missing committed gates, separate from this review environment's socket restrictions.

### Contract assessment

- Native request builder sets `truncate:false`, `shift:false`, snapshotted `num_ctx` and `num_predict`, omits `think`, and omits tools on final steps. Parser validates the entire call batch, including C3 ID types/256-byte bounds, before exposing calls. Raw assistant replay is preserved; result IDs are all-or-none and internal IDs remain local. No additional parser/replay defect found by inspection.
- Trained maximum validation and clamping, fixed native error codes, existing `length` behavior and frontend EN/VI error copy are present. SemVer validation and error dispatch have the defects above. This is not a clean containment sign-off.
- Shared provider generations, generations-before-cache publication, per-wire/destination capability keys, cache-owned lookup tasks, canceled-waiter detachment, matching in-flight cleanup and token-aware cancel-registry guards appear consistent with the ownership contracts. No additional race established. Existing static OpenAI/Anthropic/Gemini/Groq serialization paths remain materially unchanged by the diff.
- One preparation deadline, biased cancellation and single terminal helpers are implemented; full command-level lifecycle guarantees remain insufficiently tested as detailed above. Settings field validation/persistence are present; model-derived status has the stale-state defect above.

### Verification run

- `rtk npm run build`: **passed**, TypeScript and Vite. Warnings include browser-externalized `node:zlib`, ineffective dynamic import and large chunks; none identified on the reviewed context changes.
- `rtk npm run test:run`: **129 files / 744 tests passed** at the time of execution.
- `rtk cargo test --locked` from `src-tauri`: **368 passed / 23 failed / 15 ignored**. Inspected all 23 failures: each failed on loopback socket creation/bind with `PermissionDenied: Operation not permitted`, including existing transport/compatibility fixtures and new routing/preparation fixtures. Rust compiled successfully, and non-socket tests passed; the full suite is **not** reported as passing. No escalation is available under this session's permission policy.
- `rtk cargo check --locked --features app-store`: **passed**. Warning: building outside Tauri CLI leaves `macos-private-api` enabled; this is a compilation check, not an App Store artifact verification.
- Standalone `/tmp` Rust probes compiled exact extracted working-tree version and error-classifier functions; reproduced findings 1 and 2. No application/test source edits needed.
- `rtk git diff --check`: **passed** before report append; repeated afterwards.

Limitations: no native desktop settings inspection, real-provider generation, independent `/api/ps` allocation check, old-version runtime, or live database smoke. Loopback restrictions blocked local HTTP fixture execution, so implementation evidence's Ollama 0.40.4/qwen3:4b smoke remains implementer-reported and was not independently reproduced. No full app-store test suite or clippy rerun. No Context7 lookup needed for this code-review/business-logic task. Fix findings and complete the missing approved regression gates before re-review; no commit or push performed.

## Re-review: ollama-native-context Phase 1 round 2

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-17. No P0/P1 found. Prior findings 1–3 resolved; finding 4 partially resolved, with one new P2 defect in its replacement regression gate.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md` first, then `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` and RTK guidance; reviewed the Phase 1 plan, revisions, prior review and `Fixes for Codex review round 1`. Reviewed current task-owned uncommitted changes on `feat/ollama-native-context`, including untracked `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/prepare.rs`, `wire_ollama.rs`, `src-tauri/src/ai/generations.rs` and `ollama.rs`. Excluded `.claude/launch.json` and unrelated `src/lib/politedbBackend/ai.ts` / `ai.test.ts`. Only this section appended; no application/test edits, staging or commit. No real user data, saved providers, credentials or live provider requests used.

### Prior finding disposition

1. **P2 SemVer validation / provider-text containment — resolved.** `src-tauri/src/ai/ollama.rs:55-114` validates the complete bounded string: three numeric core components without leading zeros, nonempty ASCII prerelease/build identifiers, prerelease numeric leading-zero rules, and no extra `+`, whitespace or newline. Build metadata does not affect precedence; a prerelease of the minimum remains too old. Invalid versions route to unreachable before status publication. Grammar/precedence unit tests passed; HTTP routing/status fixtures remain blocked locally by socket permissions. This validates the intended grammar and containment boundary, not arbitrary server honesty about its version.
2. **P2 native classifier order — resolved.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/model.rs:120-132` checks recognized context/load/model errors before generic tools rejection. Context plus a tools-unsupported suffix now returns context-full before any capability downgrade. Genuine tools rejection remains supported. The new actual native HTTP overlap fixture is at `:808`; frontend fallback-code tests passed. HTTP fixture execution was blocked locally, so the transport-level assertion was inspected rather than independently passed.
3. **P2 default-model status transitions — resolved.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantSettingsDialog.tsx:250-255`, `:295-305` and `:318-324` route first addition, explicit selection and default removal through invalidation/cancellation/re-query. The new request explicitly overrides the model in the draft snapshot, avoiding dependence on asynchronous state updates. Focused dialog tests passed, including late old-model response, fallback model, removal of the last model and first addition.
4. **P2 missing transport/lifecycle gates — partially resolved.** Actual native `HttpModel::step` fixtures now cover two requests, prefix/IPv6 destinations, replay, flags/context size, final tools omission and bounded error handling. The command calls `prepare::drive_turn`, whose tests use a real run registry and cover preparation deadline, metadata cancel/connection close, failure terminals and slot release. Both discovery and status call the same `run_cancellable`; status tests exercise cancellation during version and show probes. However, the round-trip gate can falsely pass without executing (new P2 below), and the promised combined preparation-plus-delayed-model deadline remains untested: `prepare.rs:658-670` substitutes a callback that only records `left`; `:693-728` checks budget subtraction, never `run_turn` timing/terminal behavior. Consent revoke during preparation and a closed sink through `drive_turn` also lack the requested integrated cases; the old closed `emit_end` unit test remains the only closure fixture. Complete these focused gates before closing finding 4. No production deadline/cancellation defect established by inspection.

### Findings, ranked

- **P0: none found.**
- **P1: none found.**
- **P2 (new) — Mandatory native round-trip fixtures silently pass when IPv4 binding fails.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/model.rs:722-730`: `native_server` converts every bind error to `None`, and the caller unconditionally `continue`s for both mandatory IPv4 cases as well as optional IPv6. Reproduced here: `cargo test --locked native_round_trip_over_http_keeps_flags_and_size_on_every_step` reports **1 passed** in 0.01 s while IPv4 loopback binding is denied (the neighboring native error fixture fails on the same bind). Thus no URL, JSON, replay or context assertion runs, yet the new A1/A3/A5 gate is green. **Fix:** require IPv4 listener creation to succeed (or use injected transport); restrict any optional skip to the IPv6 case, report that skip explicitly, and assert mandatory cases executed.
- **P2 (retained, prior finding 4) — Complete the lifecycle regression gates.** `src-tauri/src/ai/agent/prepare.rs:658-670,693-728`: the model callback returns immediately, so the test cannot catch resetting the model to a fresh full deadline or duplicate/missing model-phase terminals. Exercise `drive_turn` with actual `run_turn` and a delayed synthetic model against one short total deadline; assert elapsed bound, terminal sequence and slot release. Add consent-revoke and failed-sink cases through the shared production task path. These are approved regression requirements still missing, separate from environmental inability to run socket fixtures.

### Ownership and behavior assessment

- `drive_turn` owns `RunGuard` across preparation and model execution; early outcomes emit only their own terminal, success delegates terminal ownership to `run_turn`, and scope drop releases the slot on unwind/abort. Cancellation is checked before ready-model invocation; preparation uses biased cancellation. Remaining time is passed into the existing bounded `run_turn`; no new functional deadline or terminal regression found.
- `run_cancellable` registers before polling validation/network work, immediately installs token-aware `FinishGuard`, and gives cancellation priority. Dropping the future, success, error and unwind release only the matching registration; supersession/early-cancel semantics remain as approved in Revision 6 D1. Registry ownership tests passed. Status/discovery share the helper, but this is not a full Tauri IPC integration test.
- Native replay/parser, generation-aware cache publication, wire-specific capability keys and fixed error copy remain consistent by inspection. No additional P0/P1/P2 application defect established in the fixes.

### Verification run

- `rtk npm run test:run -- src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantSettingsDialog.test.tsx src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/formatAssistantRequestError.test.ts src/lib/ai-assistant/agent.test.ts src/lib/ai-assistant/providers.test.ts`: **4 files / 57 tests passed**.
- `rtk npm run build`: **passed**, TypeScript and Vite. Existing warnings: browser-externalized `node:zlib`, ineffective dynamic import and large chunks.
- `rtk cargo test --locked` from `src-tauri`: **369 passed / 28 failed / 15 ignored**. All 28 failures occur on denied loopback socket binds (including `native_errors_map_to_fixed_codes_over_http`, whose bind error becomes `None` then unwraps). Rust compilation and non-socket tests passed; full suite did not pass. The native round-trip test's apparent pass is invalid evidence, as detailed above.
- `rtk cargo test --locked native_round_trip_over_http_keeps_flags_and_size_on_every_step`: **reports 1 passed / 411 filtered out**, but all three cases skip on denied binds; reproduced false-positive gate.
- `rtk cargo check --locked --features app-store`: **passed**. Existing warning: outside Tauri CLI, `macos-private-api` stays enabled; this is not an App Store artifact check.
- `rtk git diff --check`: **passed** before append; repeated after append.

Limitations: socket policy prevents independent native HTTP/routing/status/lifecycle fixture execution; no escalation available. No full frontend suite, app-store Rust test suite, clippy, native UI inspection, real Ollama generation, allocation check or database smoke rerun. Implementer-reported successful full suites/live evidence remain attributed to Claude. No Context7 lookup needed for this code-review/business-logic scope. ## Review: AI agent always on (uncommitted, feat/ai-agent)

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-19. One P2 stale consent reference; no P0/P1 or consent bypass found.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md` first, then `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` and RTK guidance. Reviewed the current uncommitted task diff against HEAD `736103d` on `feat/ai-agent`, using the “Agent always on (2026-10-19 ...)” Status entry in `.agent/plans/ai-agent.md` as the requirement. Excluded `.claude/launch.json` and `src/lib/politedbBackend/ai.ts` / `ai.test.ts`. Only this section appended; no code, tests, plans, staging or commit changed.

### Findings, ranked

- **P0: none found.**
- **P1: none found.**
- **P2 — Consent card still promises revocation in the removed settings list.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantMessageParts.tsx:608-609`, exposed by the removal in `AiAssistantSettingsDialog.tsx:569`: every consent request still says “You can revoke this in AI settings.” The Data access list was the only production mount of the revoke control; after this change that destination has no such action. A user granting ongoing access receives incorrect instructions for withdrawing it. **Fix:** update that sentence to reflect the current UI, preserving the card's Allow/Not now behavior, explicit per-connection grant, backend checks and native approval. Do not restore the list or auto-grant access. Add a focused assertion so the obsolete revocation instruction cannot return. The deliberately hidden list and temporarily unavailable revoke UI are explicitly accepted scope, not separate findings.

### Behavior and consent assessment

- Removing both opt-in checks makes supported connected engines enter the agent without reading the old preference. `hasConcreteDatabaseContext` recognizes a runtime connection even before metadata loads. Existing direct app-context replies remain local shortcuts. Unsupported engines/no connection retain legacy paths; provider/model/engine fallback codes remain, and runtime errors still surface instead of silently falling back. Removing `AI_AGENT_DISABLED` is consistent with removal of its only producer.
- The submit hook always passes `includeColumnDetails: true`. The lower-level runner/wrapper still permit omission/false for callers; the production submit path supplies true. Both legacy keys are removed at the next `aiAgentStart`, before IPC, with storage exceptions contained; this does not modify consent storage or create grants.
- Legacy history filtering remains `withoutData(messages)` before direct replies, chat replies, planning and agent fallback. Full agent history carries `historyHasData`, allowing the backend gate to require consent before the first provider request. Existing tests exercise filtered fallback even when a grant exists.
- No grant is issued by agent startup or settings. The panel still calls `aiAgentDataAccessGrant` only from the consent-card Allow handler; Not now does not grant. Failed saves remain pending, and connection/provider/generation guards prevent retrying in a changed context.
- Rust consent/approval code is unchanged. Inspected command wiring, `ConsentGate`, the coordinator's check before each provider request and `DataAccess::admit`: data reads require bound per-connection/provider consent, safe/production requires native approval, and consent/aliveness are rechecked after approval. Lock mode offers no data tools. No newly introduced bypass found by inspection.
- Generic agent errors no longer advise disabling a removed setting; English/Vietnamese fixed copy remains contained. No live references to removed opt-in helpers/constants or `AI_AGENT_DISABLED` remain in `src/` or `src-tauri/`. `AiDataAccessList` and its list/revoke wrappers/tests are intentionally retained but have no production mount, as recorded in the approved scope.

### Verification and test adequacy

- `rtk npm run test:run -- src/components/ai-assistant src/lib/ai-assistant src/lib/tauri/aiAgent.test.ts`: **22 files / 170 tests passed**. Includes settings, panel legacy fallback, agent runner, submit orchestration, request-error formatting, consent-card grant/decline/save races, panel grant lifecycle and chat-history filtering.
- `rtk npm run build`: **passed** (TypeScript and Vite). Warnings: browser-externalized `node:zlib`, ineffective dynamic imports and chunks over 500 kB.
- `rtk git diff --check`: **passed** before append; repeated after append.
- Changed tests cover no opt-in, old absent/false keys removed on startup, always-included column details, unsupported-engine/no-connection routing and preserved filtered legacy history. Panel tests now explicitly simulate a model without tool support rather than relying on default agent-off state. Existing consent tests pass unchanged.
- Small nonblocking coverage gaps: settings tests do not explicitly assert the Data access list/agent description are absent; key cleanup does not cover stored `"1"` or unavailable storage; generic agent-error copy lacks a direct English/Vietnamese assertion rejecting the removed-toggle advice. Source inspection supports these paths. The stale revocation sentence above currently escapes the consent-card tests.

Limitations: no native desktop/browser visual inspection, live provider/database run or Touch ID/password prompt interaction. Frontend tests use mocked IPC and cannot independently prove native enforcement; native paths were inspected, not rerun, because this scoped diff changes no Rust. Existing release smoke gates remain open. No Context7 lookup needed for this code-review scope. Build necessarily includes the current working tree, including the excluded backend-client files, without reviewing those unrelated changes. No commit performed.

## Review: pro-gating Phase 1

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-19. No P0; two P1 and two P2 findings below.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` and referenced RTK guidance first. Reviewed committed base `983f40e` using `git diff origin/main...HEAD`, all staged/unstaged changes using `git diff HEAD` and `git status`, and untracked implementation/tests. Revision 5 of `.agent/plans/pro-gating.md` and its Phase 1 implementation evidence are authoritative. Covered V1/V2, W1/W2/W4 capture/W5/W6, B1/B2/B3/B5/B6, R4/R6/R7/R9, V7 and cleanup D. D1–D8, D2b/D2c/D2d/D9 remain settled; unsigned state and client device identity are accepted v1 limits. Phases 2–3 and release approval are outside this review.

### Findings, ranked

1. **P1 — Expiry normalization loses the distinction between unlimited and malformed, and retains obsolete deadlines on refresh.** `src-tauri/src/license.rs:767` and `:274`. `normalize_api_state` uses `pick_string([payload.expires_at, previous.expires_at])`: an explicit `expiresAt: null` on an active Pro refresh retains the old expiry. If that deadline has passed and the trial is exhausted, an authoritative unlimited renewal is finalized as expired and remains locked. Conversely, `expiresAt: ""` or whitespace is dropped to `None`; `paid_expiry` also explicitly treats an empty persisted string as unlimited. An active paid response with this present-invalid expiry therefore grants unlimited access, contrary to W5. **Fix:** make the response's expiry authoritative, preserve present-invalid strings (or an explicit validity discriminator), and grant unlimited access only for absent/null expiry. If missing and null need distinct transport handling, represent their presence explicitly; never inherit a finite deadline for explicit null. Add raw-response → normalize → save/reload tests for null following an expired/malformed deadline, missing expiry, empty/whitespace expiry, malformed expiry, and a valid trial alongside each invalid paid expiry.

2. **P1 — License writes still delete the authoritative file before replacement.** `src-tauri/src/license.rs:588`, via `src-tauri/src/file_storage.rs:53`. The new locked writer calls `json_write_atomic`, whose implementation removes the existing destination and then renames the temporary file. A rename failure or crash between those steps loses credentials and the original trial/legacy evidence; the next read creates a fresh trial. The process mutex prevents interleaving in-process readers but cannot make this operation atomic or preserve state on failure. This also affects W4's newly automatic capture write during load. **Fix:** use the existing `storage::json_replace_preserving` replacement path for license writes, under the same file lock; retain the original bytes/revision on failure. Add a failed-replacement regression asserting the old credentials and original legacy/trial start survive, plus a successful-write revision assertion. No general storage refactor is needed.

3. **P2 — An old state version is reset rather than migrated.** `src-tauri/src/license.rs:568`. The single decoder filters out every version other than 1, then constructs `inactive_at(now)`. An otherwise decodable older-version file consequently loses its credentials, original trial start and migration provenance; an exhausted trial becomes a new 14-day local trial and that new start is captured. W4/V3 explicitly require old-version migration before capture. The test helper at `:1468` removes new fields but leaves `version` at 1, so it does not exercise this requirement. **Fix:** migrate supported older versions in the decoder while preserving credentials and original seconds/ms trial timestamps; handle unsupported future versions explicitly rather than silently resetting them. Add older-version → load, clear-before-load and activation-before-load cases, including an exhausted trial and restart.

4. **P2 — App Store agent access still depends on license-file readability.** `src-tauri/src/license.rs:670`. `require_pro` always loads the license state before reaching the distribution bypass in `entitlement`. A corrupt/unreadable state file or an unwritable fresh license directory therefore returns `AI_AGENT_PRO_REQUIRED` from both agent start and consent grant on the App Store flavor, although that flavor's unconditional grant and frontend remain unlocked. This is an error-path regression from adding the native agent gates, not a request for App Store billing. **Fix:** return success immediately from the native gate when `!Distribution::current().gates_pro_features()`, before license I/O; keep Direct failures closed. Test both native entry points with missing/unwritable or malformed storage in the App Store flavor, and preserve Direct denial.

### Behavior, concurrency and privacy assessment

- The recorded fixture deviation is reasonable: TypeScript consumes native `pro_access`, `pro_access_until` and `paid_license`, rather than duplicating paid-plan classification. Gateway headers obtain a fresh native snapshot before mapping paid access to `ultimate`; trial-only access remains `free`. No additional shared-JSON requirement is raised.
- Positive paid-tier classification, response-owned plan/name, paid/trial union, exact deadline denial, malformed nonblank expiry persistence and normal App Store finalization are covered. The expiry cases in finding 1 escape the current fixtures.
- Operation serialization consistently uses the async mutex before credential reads/HTTP/commit; clear/save share it. File read-modify-write and sequence assignment occur under the synchronous mutex, without an await or recursive public-helper locking. Activation merges device-owned fields from the current disk state. Legacy capture and its load write share one file critical section. No additional in-process lost-update/deadlock found by inspection; durable replacement remains finding 2.
- HTTP uses 5 s connect, 15 s attempt and 30 s aggregate deadlines, with capped body consumption. Transport/malformed errors use fixed messages, and HTTP failures retain typed status/Retry-After. No newly added logging of license keys, device identifiers or raw bodies found. Derived entitlement inputs are recomputed, and caller-supplied trial/legacy values cannot replace the on-disk device fields. Accepted unsigned-file limits are unchanged.
- Both native agent gates precede provider/profile/database work. Free submissions use the existing basic-chat path and filtered history. Allow checks access at click time; generation and action-time checks prevent resubmission after downgrade/context changes. Grant remains explicit; upgrade does not automatically grant or retry. Existing consent, read-only tools and native approval flow remain intact.
- Raw EXPLAIN stays free through direct/stream/restored result rendering; Pro analyzer/report handlers retain privacy confirmation. Inspector collection is gated and pending results invalidated on loss. PNG/Mermaid paths and table SQL import remain ungated; SVG, database backup/restore and active masking are gated. All-Keep masking remains passthrough, with no unmasked fallback for rejected active masking. Ordinary connection health checks remain unchanged. Badges, shared upgrade feature list and overlay cleanup match Phase 1 scope.

### Verification and test adequacy

- `VITE_POLITEDB_API_BASE=http://localhost:4000/api npm run test:run`: **134 files / 794 tests passed**, including all focused changed suites and untracked tests. The gateway file also passed separately (2 tests). An initial run with a different API base had only the known hard-coded localhost expectation failure; the complete rerun with the expected base passed.
- `npx tsc --noEmit`: **passed**. `npm run build`: **passed** (also runs tsc). Warnings include browser-externalized `node:zlib`, ineffective dynamic imports, chunk sizes and plugin timing.
- `cargo test --locked` and `cargo test --locked --features app-store`: each **382 passed / 37 failed / 15 ignored**. Socket fixtures fail on denied loopback binds (`PermissionDenied`, OS error 1); two dependent native HTTP assertions fail after the unavailable fixtures. These are environmental, not product findings. The five license HTTP/concurrency tests cannot run past their bind. Nineteen non-socket license tests pass in each flavor. The preliminary `cargo test --lib` runs zero tests; the reported counts come from the full binary suites.
- `cargo clippy --locked --all-targets`, default and `--features app-store`: **passed** with six unique warnings in unchanged export/MySQL/credential-identity code (five production warnings plus one test warning). App Store also emits its existing outside-Tauri-CLI build warning; this is not artifact validation.
- Current tests meaningfully cover Free gates, inspector downgrade/late results, historical Allow cards and grant races, native snapshot sequencing, stale frontend publication, expiry rereads, PNG/SVG, active masking versus All-Keep, backup versus table import, and basic-chat filtering. Add regressions described in findings. Remaining planned coverage gaps include deterministic concurrent legacy load/mutation, clear→reactivate and refresh/deactivate ordering, a queued activation after HTTP timeout, and actual timer/focus-driven expiry notifying multiple subscribers and invalidating a pending Allow (current component cases manually flip entitlement). Frontend App Store agent/grant/SVG integration is not exercised by the default-mode suite.
- `git diff --check`: passed before this append; repeated afterward.

Limitations: socket-dependent timeout/HTTP/credential-ordering behavior was inspected but could not be independently executed here. No desktop visual inspection, live provider/database/native-approval smoke or App Store artifact run. No Context7 lookup needed for this business-logic/code-review task. D9's later phases and separate security-review/release gates remain open. Only this new review section appended; no application code, tests or plan edited, no staging or commit.

## Re-review: pro-gating Phase 1 round 2

**Verdict: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-19. All four prior findings resolved by inspection; their non-socket regression tests pass. No new P0/P1/P2 issue found in the fixes or their integration.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` and referenced RTK guidance first. Re-reviewed `## Review: pro-gating Phase 1` against `Fixes for Codex review "pro-gating Phase 1"` in `.agent/plans/pro-gating.md:762-803`. HEAD remains `983f40e91e46c6fe6269ae0c98f2d433de1fdece`; reviewed `git diff origin/main...HEAD` against `origin/main` (`57de05f`), plus staged/unstaged changes and all untracked plan, implementation and test files. Included the staged overlay deletion. D1–D8, D2b, D2c, D2d and D9 remain settled; unsigned state and client device id remain accepted v1 limits. Phases 2–3 and release approval are out of scope.

### Disposition of the four findings

| Prior finding                             | Result       | File:line evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ----------------------------------------- | ------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| P1 — expiry normalization                 | **Resolved** | `src-tauri/src/license.rs:788-790` takes expiry exclusively from the response; missing/null becomes `None`, while blank/whitespace remains a present invalid string. `:276-282` grants unlimited only for `None`; malformed or blank values cannot grant paid access. Trial union remains independent at `:342-356`. Tests at `:1581-1615` cover an unlimited renewal after an expired deadline, blank/whitespace/malformed values and a future date; `:1618-1635` covers invalid paid expiry with a valid trial through commit/reload. Existing malformed save/reload coverage remains at `:1465-1486`. These tests passed in both flavors.                                                         |
| P1 — remove-before-replace license writes | **Resolved** | `src-tauri/src/license.rs:594-602` now calls `json_replace_private` and returns the incremented state only after success. The existing helper at `src-tauri/src/file_storage.rs:166-204` creates a unique private sibling, writes and syncs it, then replaces the destination without unlinking the old file; error cleanup removes only the temporary sibling. Unix replacement is rename-over-existing (`:130-134`); Windows uses `MoveFileExW` with replacement/write-through (`:136-155`). License regression `src-tauri/src/license.rs:1640-1666` passes in both flavors: failed clear preserves key, revision and original trial start, successful initial write has revision 1 and mode 0600. |
| P2 — old version reset                    | **Resolved** | `src-tauri/src/license.rs:570-590` rejects newer versions without resetting and migrates decodable older versions while retaining state, normalizing timestamps and capturing original legacy provenance. `:236-242` sets the current version; `:619-627` persists migration, and `:647-652` applies the same decoder to mutation entry paths while carrying device-owned fields. Tests at `:1679-1709` cover version 0 with an exhausted trial, load/clear/credential-commit before first load, retained key on load and subsequent reload; `:1713-1721` verifies newer-version load/clear errors leave bytes unchanged. Both pass in both flavors.                                                 |
| P2 — App Store gate depends on storage    | **Resolved** | `src-tauri/src/license.rs:683-699` passes the loader as a closure and returns before invoking it for ungated distributions. Both native entry points use this gate before provider/profile/database work: `src-tauri/src/commands/ai_agent.rs:88` and `src-tauri/src/commands/ai_agent_data.rs:232`. Regression `src-tauri/src/license.rs:1555-1577` passes in both flavors: App Store's loader panics if invoked, while Direct denies free or unreadable state and permits Pro. Thus malformed/missing/unwritable license storage cannot affect either App Store entitlement check.                                                                                                                 |

### Regression assessment and evidence limits

- Rechecked operation-lock/file-lock ordering, migration/capture writes, revision/snapshot publication, response normalization, both native agent gates and frontend action guards. No new lost-update, recursive-lock, stale-entitlement or privacy regression found. Existing consent checks, filtered basic-chat fallback, raw EXPLAIN access, PNG/Mermaid, table import and All-Keep export behavior remain intact.
- New frontend timer and focus tests (`src/stores/license.test.ts:99-125`) pass. They exercise subscriber notification and lifecycle reload rather than manually calling reload alone. Pending-Allow downgrade tests remain component-level; there is still no single timer-to-pending-Allow integration test.
- Regression coverage is narrower than some original requests: expiry tests construct typed payloads rather than separately parsing raw missing/null JSON and persisting every matrix case; failed-write regression fails temporary-file creation through directory permissions rather than injecting a rename failure; version migration uses the shared credential-commit path rather than an actual HTTP activation; App Store gate coverage uses the shared lazy loader rather than invoking both complete Tauri commands. These are test-evidence limits, not unresolved defects: the shared implementation paths establish the requested behavior by inspection. Windows replacement and App Store packaging were not executed.
- Added clear/reactivate and queued refresh/deactivate tests exist at `src-tauri/src/license.rs:1925-1987`. The timeout test at `:1991-2039` also attempts another activation after timeout; it proves a later lock acquisition, not a successful activation already queued during the original stall. All seven license HTTP tests are blocked by loopback restrictions here, so their runtime behavior remains unverified in this environment.

### Verification

- `VITE_POLITEDB_API_BASE=http://localhost:4000/api npm run test:run`: **134 files / 796 tests passed**.
- `npx tsc --noEmit`: **passed**. `npm run build`: **passed**, including its TypeScript check. Non-fatal build warnings concern browser-externalized `node:zlib`, ineffective dynamic imports, chunk sizes and plugin timing.
- `cargo test --locked` and `cargo test --locked --features app-store`: each **387 passed / 39 failed / 15 ignored**. All **24 non-socket license tests passed** in each flavor, including the four fixes. Seven license HTTP/concurrency fixtures fail at `src-tauri/src/license.rs:1761` with denied loopback bind (`PermissionDenied`, OS error 1); the remaining socket failures and two dependent native HTTP assertions have the same environmental cause as the prior review. These are not product findings.
- `cargo clippy --locked --all-targets`, default and `--features app-store`: **passed**. Six unique existing warnings: three MSRV warnings in unchanged export code, two useless-format warnings in unchanged MySQL code and one in unchanged credential-identity tests. App Store additionally emits the existing outside-Tauri-CLI build warning.
- `git diff --check`: **passed** before and after append.

Limitations: no desktop visual inspection, live provider/database/approval smoke or App Store artifact validation. ## Review: pro-gating Phase 2 (backend trial endpoint)

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-19. Two P2 findings; no P0/P1 found.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` and RTK guidance first. Reviewed Revision 5 of `.agent/plans/pro-gating.md`, particularly A2 backend, R5, V6, R8, timestamp-domain amendment and Phase 2 implementation evidence. Inspected `git -C ../politedb-backend diff HEAD`, status, all four untracked implementation/test files, and adjacent routing, ORM, license, error and rate-limit paths. Backend branch: `feat/trial-endpoint`; changes remain uncommitted. Backend treated as read-only. Settled decisions D1–D8, D2b/D2c/D2d and D9 remain unchanged; client-supplied identity remains an accepted v1 limit.

### Findings

1. **P2 — Accepted device-name length exceeds Postgres storage.** `../politedb-backend/src/validation/device.ts:6` accepts 256 characters, but `../politedb-backend/src/migrations/Migration20261019000000AddTrials.ts:7` creates `device_name varchar(255)`; `../politedb-backend/src/db/entities.ts:138` also uses the default string length. An ASCII name of exactly 256 characters passes validation but fails the trial insert with `503 TRIAL_UNAVAILABLE`, including repeat requests because the insert precedes conflict recovery. SQLite does not enforce this varchar limit. The shared schema also permits this name through activation, whose local activation column remains varchar(255), allowing an upstream activation followed by a local persistence failure. **Fix:** preserve the approved 256-character input contract by explicitly sizing both Trial and Activation device-name properties/columns to accommodate it, updating the new migration and adding an upgrade migration for the existing activation column. Add Postgres cases for 255/256-character names on first/repeat trial requests and activation, plus rejection at 257; schema-diff assertions must remain empty. A read-only validation probe confirmed that 256 ASCII characters are accepted.

2. **P2 — Revision 5 timestamp domain is not enforced.** `../politedb-backend/src/services/trial-service.ts:27-36,49-50,63-64` checks the legacy lower bound and future relationship, but neither applies the required exclusive `2100-01-01` upper bound nor validates the computed expiry as a safe integer in `[2026-01-01, 2100-01-01)`. Revision 5 explicitly requires these backend checks (`.agent/plans/pro-gating.md:630`). Under an out-of-domain server clock, the endpoint can persist/return a window that Phase 3 must reject; a start just below the upper bound can also produce an out-of-domain expiry. This is a contract gap, not an outage at today's clock. **Fix:** centralize the domain bounds, validate server time, legacy candidates and computed expiry before insertion, retain the approved ignore behavior for invalid legacy dates, and return a fixed non-404 AppError when no valid server window can be produced. Test exact lower/upper boundaries, expiry crossing the upper boundary, and unsafe values through the service boundary. Read-only probes confirmed acceptance at the upper bound and an expiry crossing it.

### Correctness, compatibility and privacy assessment

- Find-or-create follows V6: awaited autocommitted insert, `onConflict(['product','deviceId']).ignore()`, fresh manager read, and metadata-only native update. Installed MikroORM 7.2.5 maps conflict properties to database field names; SQLite concurrent-create test passes. Existing dates and createdAt are not overwritten. Postgres unique arbitration is structurally correct; independent runtime verification remains limited below.
- Legacy dates affect only insertion; valid earlier starts shorten the 14-day window, including exhausted trials. Future/pre-2026 dates are ignored. Installed Zod 4 `.int()` rejects unsafe integers before `newTrialStart`; the helper also guards safe integers. Thus unsafe input gets INVALID_PAYLOAD rather than being ignored over HTTP, despite the broader wording in Claude's evidence. No unsafe date reaches storage through that path. Missing upper/expiry checks are finding 2.
- Products are trimmed/lowercased and checked against the normalized env allowlist. Separate allowed products have separate composite identities by inspection. Response is exactly camelCase epoch-ms `{ trialStartedAt, trialExpiresAt }`, with no `now`, matching D2c and Phase 3. Unsupported products/payloads are 400, DB failures 503, limiter failures 429; trial business errors do not use 404.
- Trial DB errors are discarded and replaced by a fixed AppError. `app.ts:89-95` returns before console/report sinks; fresh-manager lookup and metadata-update failures are inside the same catch. No new device/payload/SQL-parameter logging found. Production ORM config does not enable query debugging. HTTP privacy assertion could not execute here because its fixture requires a socket.
- `licenseRateLimit` is reused, sharing the 60/minute IP budget with license operations. Existing Render trust-proxy bypass and per-process counters remain documented deployment limits, not new findings. Configure TRUST_PROXY and perform deployed smoke checks before Phase 3 release.
- Shared bounds intentionally tighten activate/validate/deactivate inputs. Current desktop-generated IDs are 35-character hashes; OS/architecture strings fit the new limits. No concrete regression for those normal clients found. Previously accepted custom device IDs of 129–255 characters now receive 400 even when an activation already exists, and deactivation also validates an optional supplied device. Tests do not establish compatibility for such legacy/custom callers; retain awareness of this boundary change and add targeted validation/deactivation regressions. Do not truncate or normalize identity silently.
- Migration and Trial entity otherwise agree: columns, timestamp types, primary key and composite unique constraint; both entity and explicit migration registration are present. Fresh-schema, legacy-schema and previous-schema upgrade tests assert no remaining schema diff.

### Verification and test adequacy

- `npm --prefix ../politedb-backend run build -- --noEmit`: **passed**; no dist output generated.
- Trial suite, with `--configLoader runner --no-cache --no-fsModuleCache`: **7 service tests passed / 7 HTTP tests blocked** by `listen EPERM: operation not permitted 127.0.0.1`. Service cases include creation, repeat metadata, expiry, normalized identity, legacy migration and SQLite races. HTTP failures are environmental; they do not establish response/privacy behavior at runtime here.
- License suite with the same read-only test flags: **15/15 passed**. Postgres suite: **5 skipped**, TEST_POSTGRES_URL unset. Claude reports 77/77 against throwaway Postgres in the plan; this review did not independently reproduce that evidence. V6 Postgres migration/race execution remains required before deploy approval, especially after fixes.
- Coverage gaps: no second allowed-product case, exact field-length matrix, legacy upper/expiry-domain cases, shared license-boundary regression, rate-limit response test, or console/report spies around duplicate inserts and read/update-stage failures. Existing failure test throws only from getEm; existing races use identical timestamps and payloads, so they do not prove every losing caller returns distinct winning legacy dates. Add focused regressions alongside the fixes; use different valid starts/metadata for a deterministic winner-preservation case.
- Backend `git diff HEAD --check`: passed. Backend status still contains the same seven tracked modifications and four untracked paths. No application code, tests, plan, staging or commit changed by this review; only this section appended. Separate security review and deployment/live smoke prerequisites remain open.

## Re-review: pro-gating Phase 2 round 2

**Verdict: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-19. Both prior P2 findings resolved; no new P0/P1/P2 found.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` and referenced RTK guidance first. Inspected sibling backend `feat/trial-endpoint`, HEAD `31c2c37c1048a6b75f9db2c97b8379e39e30e7bc`, using `git diff HEAD`, status and all untracked files: seven tracked modifications and four untracked files. Backend remained read-only. Settled decisions remain closed, including the recorded 255-character deviation; no code, tests, plan, staging or commit changed.

### Prior findings

1. **P2 — device-name storage mismatch: resolved.** `../politedb-backend/src/validation/device.ts:7-12` now rejects names over 255 before trial insertion or Polar activation. Trial uses this schema at `src/services/trial-service.ts:21-25`; activate/validate/deactivate share it at `src/services/polar-license-key.ts:10-31`. The bound fits both `src/migrations/Migration20261019000000AddTrials.ts:7` and the existing activation column at `src/migrations/Migration20261015000000Initial.ts:25`; entity mappings remain consistent (`src/db/entities.ts:101,139`). This is a correct, safe implementation of the recorded deviation; widening columns is unnecessary. Direct read-only probe accepted 255 and rejected 256. HTTP regressions cover those boundaries (`test/trial.test.ts:176-192`); Postgres first/repeat coverage uses 255 (`test/migrations.pg.test.ts:130-136`). Runtime limitations for those tests are below.

2. **P2 — timestamp domain: resolved.** `../politedb-backend/src/services/trial-service.ts:12-18` centralizes inclusive 2026/exclusive 2100 bounds and safe-integer validation. `:34-46` ignores invalid/future legacy candidates, validates server time, and checks computed expiry. `:62-65` rejects an invalid window before any DB operation with fixed `503 TRIAL_UNAVAILABLE` (`:49-50`), never 404. Tests cover lower/upper bounds, crossing expiry and no insertion on failure (`test/trial.test.ts:99-105,117-135`). Additional direct probes confirmed rejection below the lower bound, at the upper bound, with expiry exactly at the upper bound, and for unsafe/fractional server values; expiry one millisecond below the upper bound succeeds, and an out-of-domain legacy candidate is ignored. All arithmetic stays safely within the bounded integer domain.

### Regression and privacy review

- Winner preservation remains intact: awaited conflict-ignoring insert, fresh-manager read, metadata-only update and response from the winning row (`../politedb-backend/src/services/trial-service.ts:68-106`). Different-legacy-start race and separate allowed-product regressions pass (`test/trial.test.ts:107-115,138-148`). Postgres still checks migrations/schema agreement and separate-connection races (`test/migrations.pg.test.ts:58-86,104-127`).
- DB insert/read/update failures remain inside the sanitized catch (`src/services/trial-service.ts:68-109`); AppError returns before console/report sinks (`src/app.ts:89-95`). Added HTTP failure test exercises failure acquiring the post-insert manager (`test/trial.test.ts:195-209`); it does not inject an actual read-query or native-update failure. Those paths are covered structurally by the same catch, not independently executed here. No new sensitive logging found.
- Shared license bounds remain intentional and documented. Validation happens before upstream calls; normal desktop identifiers and metadata still fit. Route mounting and the existing shared limiter remain unchanged in behavior (`src/routes/license.ts:36-41`, `src/routes/index.ts:10`, `src/middleware/rate-limit.ts:12-32`). No settled product or deployment decision reopened.

### Verification and limitations

- `npm run build -- --noEmit` in the backend: **passed**, without generating dist output. `git diff HEAD --check`: **passed**.
- Read-only Vitest run: `vitest run test/trial.test.ts test/license.test.ts test/migrations.pg.test.ts --configLoader runner --no-cache --no-fsModuleCache`: **26 passed** (11 trial service tests, 15 license tests), **11 HTTP tests blocked** by sandbox `listen EPERM: operation not permitted 127.0.0.1`, **6 Postgres tests skipped** because `TEST_POSTGRES_URL` is unset. No backend test/cache files written.
- Direct validation/window probes passed using `node --import tsx --input-type=module`; the initial tsx CLI probe was blocked by its IPC socket, then rerun without that socket.
- Claude reports **86/86 against throwaway Postgres** in the fix section. This is implementer-reported evidence, not independently reproduced here. ## Review: pro-gating Phase 3

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED** (2026-10-19). No P0/P1 found; three P2 correctness/compatibility issues below.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` and referenced RTK guidance first. Reviewed `git diff HEAD`, status and all three untracked source/test files over app HEAD `98571ec09d65496d033832b5b1dbf3f1df7e4ec0`. Revision 5 is authoritative, including V1 trial, V3–V5, W3–W4 and settled D2b/D2c/D2d/D9. Checked the Phase 3 implementation evidence and read-only backend `feat/trial-endpoint` at `8b1290bb3cb677d763180b721be667da102e553e` (clean worktree), especially `src/services/trial-service.ts`, shared device validation and route/error handling. Settled product decisions and accepted unsigned-state/device-identity limits remain closed. Only this section was appended; no application code, tests, plan, staging or commit changed.

### Findings

1. **P2 — Malformed successful deactivation responses now clear credentials.** `src-tauri/src/license.rs:1119-1124`. The shared POST helper now returns raw text; activate and refresh restore `parse_api_payload`, but deactivate discards that text and immediately calls `clear_at`. An empty body, truncated JSON or plain text returned with HTTP 200 therefore clears the local key/token and paid entitlement, whereas Phase 1 returned `Malformed` and preserved them. This violates the retained failure-preserves-state contract. **Fix:** parse the successful deactivation payload before clearing, as activate/refresh do; attach the current snapshot on parse failure. Add a regression seeded with paid credentials and a known server trial: malformed 2xx must preserve file bytes/revision, credentials and trial; valid deactivation must still clear credentials and retain the trial.

2. **P2 — Long Retry-After overflows the wake-up timer and loses automatic retries.** `src/stores/license.ts:275-279` and `:347-348`. Native code accepts both integer seconds and HTTP dates without a short maximum, and `failureDelay` passes the resulting delay directly to `setTimeout`. A valid 30-day delay (`2592000000` ms) exceeds the signed 32-bit timer limit and fires almost immediately. The callback clears the timer, then `requestTrialSync` returns because `now < nextAttemptAt`; no timer is rearmed. With no subsequent focus/online/load event, syncing never resumes at the promised deadline. **Evidence:** an installed-jsdom probe fired that delay after 1 ms; an in-memory transpilation of the actual scheduler with a mocked 429 reproduced one request followed by `timerArmed: false` after the early wake. **Fix:** clamp each wake-up to a safe interval (the expiry scheduler already uses 24 h), retain the absolute deadline, and rearm when a wake occurs before it. Do not shorten the server's Retry-After. Test a delay above `2^31-1`, intermediate wakes and an eventual event-free retry.

3. **P2 — Active Free/unknown licenses hide server-trial status and offline feedback.** `src/components/license/TrialStatusNote.tsx:27-28`, `src/components/modal/LicenseDialog.tsx:141`, and `src/components/settings/LicenseKeySettings.tsx:88`. `status === "active"` is treated as paid when suppressing the note, and both consumers render the note only in their inactive branch. Under settled D1, an active license with `plan: "free"`, an unknown tier or malformed paid expiry can have `paid_license === false`; its Pro access can come solely from the server trial. Those users see neither remaining/ended/not-activated trial text nor sync-unavailable feedback. An executable helper probe with an active Free state, known trial and unavailable sync returned `null`. **Fix:** use native `paid_license` for paid suppression and render the note outside the activation-status branch in both screens. Add helper and rendered-consumer cases for active Free/unknown tiers with running, ended and absent trials, including offline feedback; retain suppression for genuine paid access and App Store.

### Confirmed behavior and coverage

- Native trial entitlement uses only `trial`, validates integer timestamp decoding, the inclusive 2026/exclusive 2100 domain, positive window length and checked maximum 31-day duration, and requires `started_at <= now < expires_at`. Legacy local dates grant nothing. Paid/trial union, exact expiry boundaries and distribution bypass remain intact.
- Trial sync returns before HTTP for App Store. Direct sync acquires the operation lock before reading, validates before its success write, then stores the server window and retires all three legacy fields together. `carry_device_fields` preserves the server trial and migration provenance across activate/refresh/deactivate/clear/save; caller-supplied trial values cannot replace disk-owned fields. All mutations share the operation lock; file critical sections contain no await. Atomic first-read capture remains under the file lock. Fresh-file initialization/legacy-capture writes are the approved W4 exceptions to read-only load, including loads used to attach failure snapshots; failed HTTP does not replace a known trial or credentials.
- Backend request/response shapes match: `{ product, device, legacyStartedAt? }` and camelCase epoch-ms `{ trialStartedAt, trialExpiresAt }`, without server `now`. Legacy start is sent only before retirement. URL candidates reach `/api/v1/trial` after the missing license-prefixed route; business 400/429/503 statuses remain typed, 404 candidates fall back, and Retry-After seconds/date parsing is retained. Typed success/rejection transport differs from W3's suggested outcome envelope but supplies the scheduler's required status/delay/current-snapshot information; no additional issue raised for that representation.
- Scheduler single-flight, six-hour success cadence, ten-minute focus minimum, bounded failure backoff, short Retry-After handling, 400 session suppression, network-online bypass and independence from expiry reloads are covered and pass. Listener installation is guarded once per module; reloads do not multiply listeners/timers. No explicit window-unload disposer is implemented (W3 asks for one); browser destruction releases these window-owned resources, so this is not counted as a separate runtime leak finding.
- No new logging of device identifiers, keys or raw bodies found. Free-feature gates and Phase 1 action guards were not changed by this diff; the complete frontend suite, including existing gate/expiry/agent/free-feature regressions, passes.
- Coverage still falls short of the complete V3/W4 integration matrix: active/exhausted legacy provenance through activate → refresh → deactivate/clear → reload → failed/malformed sync → delayed success is tested in pieces, not as one request-body lifecycle. New HTTP fixtures also accept the first `/licenses/trial` path rather than independently exercising an actual 404-to-`/trial` contract round trip. Add those focused regressions with the fixes; inspection supports the shared carry/decoder behavior, but this review does not claim executed end-to-end evidence for those combinations.

### Verification and limitations

- `VITE_POLITEDB_API_BASE=http://localhost:4000/api npm run test:run`: **136 files / 809 tests passed**.
- `npx tsc --noEmit`: **passed**.
- `npm run build`: **passed**; warnings concern existing browser externalization of `node:zlib`, mixed static/dynamic imports and large chunks.
- `cargo test --locked` and `cargo test --locked --features app-store`, from `src-tauri/`: each **389 passed / 43 failed / 15 ignored**. Socket-dependent tests fail under sandbox-denied loopback binding (`PermissionDenied: Operation not permitted`), including the new sync HTTP/concurrency fixtures. These are environmental, not counted as product findings; their successful execution outside this sandbox remains unverified here.
- `cargo clippy --locked --all-targets`, default and `--features app-store`: **passed** with warnings in unchanged export/MSRV, MySQL formatting and credential-identity test code. App Store additionally emits the existing build-script warning about using its dedicated Tauri build script for distributable artifacts.
- `git diff --check`: **passed** before review append. Supplemental scheduler/presentation probes ran from memory without modifying source/tests.
- No live native UI or deployed endpoint smoke run. Local server binding is restricted, and the Phase 2 endpoint is not deployed per handoff. D9 release gates remain: resolve findings, rerun affected regressions, complete deployed backend smoke checks and the separate license-sensitive security review. This verdict is not release approval.

## Re-review: pro-gating Phase 3 round 2

**Verdict: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS** (2026-10-19). All three prior P2 findings are resolved by inspection; no new P0/P1/P2 introduced by the fixes found. Native HTTP regression execution remains limited by denied loopback binding.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` and referenced RTK guidance first. Re-reviewed `## Review: pro-gating Phase 3` against `Fixes for Codex review "pro-gating Phase 3"` in `.agent/plans/pro-gating.md:934-961`. Inspected `git status`, `git diff HEAD` over app HEAD `98571ec09d65496d033832b5b1dbf3f1df7e4ec0`, and all four untracked source/test files. Backend `../politedb-backend` remained read-only, clean at `8b1290bb3cb677d763180b721be667da102e553e`; checked its trial request/response contract. Settled decisions and accepted v1 limits remain closed. Only this section appended; no code, tests, plan, staging or commit changed.

### Prior findings resolved

1. **P2 — Malformed successful deactivation response: resolved.** `src-tauri/src/license.rs:1119-1127` parses the successful body through `parse_api_payload` before `clear_at`; parse failures attach the current snapshot and cannot reach the credential-clearing write. Existing operation locking and disk-owned trial preservation remain intact. Regression `src-tauri/src/license.rs:2290-2339` activates a paid license with a known server trial, checks unchanged file bytes and retained paid state after plain-text/empty 200 replies, then checks valid deactivation clears the key and preserves trial access. This test compiled in both flavors but could not execute beyond its denied listener bind here.

2. **P2 — Long Retry-After timer overflow: resolved.** `src/stores/license.ts:271-289` caps each wait at the 24-hour bound defined at `:25`, retains the absolute `nextAttemptAt`, and re-arms on intermediate wakes. The 429 delay remains unchanged at `:291-303` and the deadline is set at `:330`; no early server request is introduced. Regression `src/stores/license.trialSync.test.ts:175-190` passes: a 30-day delay survives 29 days of intermediate wakes and retries automatically at the deadline without a lifecycle event. Existing short-delay, backoff, single-flight, online and expiry-reload tests also pass.

3. **P2 — Active non-paid licenses hide trial/offline status: resolved.** `src/components/license/TrialStatusNote.tsx:26-32` suppresses the note using native `paid_license`, independent of activation status or plan spelling. Both inactive and active branches render it: `src/components/modal/LicenseDialog.tsx:159-170` and `src/components/settings/LicenseKeySettings.tsx:104-105,133-135`. Thus active Free/unknown/malformed-paid-expiry snapshots with `paid_license: false` use the same running/ended/absent trial and unavailable-feedback paths. Helper coverage at `src/components/license/TrialStatusNote.test.ts:59-96` and rendered Settings coverage at `src/components/settings/LicenseKeySettings.trial.test.tsx:43-75` pass, including paid suppression. Coverage is narrower than the requested full consumer matrix: no rendered LicenseDialog test, explicit unknown-tier fixture or rendered ended-trial fixture was added; those paths were verified by source inspection rather than claimed as executed UI evidence.

### Additional regression review

- The new fallback fixture at `src-tauri/src/license.rs:2342-2378` requires `/api/v1/licenses/trial` to return 404 before `/api/v1/trial` succeeds. The exhausted-legacy lifecycle fixture at `:2381-2457` carries the original legacy start through activation, refresh, deactivation, reload, 503 and malformed sync, then delayed success and retirement. Both compile; both hit the environmental bind failure before exercising HTTP here. The lifecycle fixture covers an exhausted legacy trial; a running legacy trial and explicit standalone clear are covered separately, not by this one full matrix.
- Reviewed shared POST parsing/fallback, operation/file locks, trial carry/retirement, scheduler deadlines and both presentation branches for regressions from the fixes. No new P0/P1/P2 found. No new sensitive logging or backend changes.
