## Review: pro-gating UI follow-ups (a122a98..06d1374)

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED** (2026-10-19). No P0/P1 found; three P2 findings below.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` and referenced RTK guidance first. Reviewed `git log a122a98..HEAD` and the complete `git diff a122a98..HEAD` on `feat/pro-gating`, ending at `06d1374`: `ad1ea89`, `518de06`, `68e809e`, `0a0e8e2`, `06d1374`. Revision 5 and “UI adjustments after local testing” in `.agent/plans/pro-gating.md` are authoritative; settled product decisions remain closed. Only this section appended. No code, tests, plan, staging or commit changed; unrelated untracked `.claude/launch.json` preserved.

### Findings

1. **P2 — Manual can still execute the agent when storage is unavailable.** `src/lib/ai-assistant/assistantMode.ts:21-25`, with `src/components/ai-assistant/AssistantModePicker.tsx:43-47`. `setAssistantMode` swallows storage failures without retaining a session value, but the picker unconditionally displays the requested mode. Submission independently calls `getAssistantMode` (`hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.ts:389`), which returns the old persisted value or defaults to Agent. With Pro, selecting Manual after a failed write therefore displays Manual while the next eligible turn runs the agent; Free users can still receive the Agent upgrade hint. **Evidence:** an in-memory transpilation of the actual module with a throwing `localStorage.setItem` returned `agent` immediately after `setAssistantMode("manual")`. **Fix:** give mode one authoritative session representation, initialized from persistence, and make both picker and submit consume it; storage writes should be best-effort persistence. Add regressions for denied reads/writes and a previously stored Agent value: choosing Manual must prevent `runAgentTurn` and upgrade hints while keeping the displayed mode consistent.

2. **P2 — Switching to Manual leaves an existing Agent upgrade hint visible.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AssistantModePicker.tsx:43-47`; affected render at `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantPanel.tsx:567-569`. Reproduce on Free: submit with stored Agent to reveal the hint, then choose Manual in the same chat. The picker changes only its own state and storage. The panel still renders `agentHintVisible && !hasPro`; that flag resets only on chat change or explicit dismissal. The hint also remains after subsequent Manual submissions. This violates the follow-up requirement that Manual shows no upgrade hint; the new submit test verifies only that a fresh callback is not invoked. **Fix:** subscribe the panel to the authoritative mode and suppress the hint while Manual is selected, preserving existing per-chat/device dismissal semantics. Add a rendered panel regression covering Agent hint → Manual selection → Manual submit, rather than only a fresh Manual hook test.

3. **P2 — Mode picker does not expose its selected or expanded state to assistive technology.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AssistantModePicker.tsx:65-82,88-96`. The fixed accessible name “Assistant mode” overrides the visible Agent/Manual label; selection is represented only by an unlabelled checkmark. The trigger advertises a menu but supplies neither expanded state nor a menu/menuitem selection model. Screen-reader users cannot determine the current choice, including when switching to Manual for query-control reasons. **Fix:** include the active mode in the accessible trigger name, add `aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`, and use a coherent selected-state model (for example `menuitemradio` with `aria-checked` and matching menu keyboard/focus handling, or an accessible radio group). Support Escape dismissal and focus restoration. Add accessible-role/state and keyboard regressions using the real Popover, which current picker tests replace with a div.

### Confirmed behavior and regression assessment

- SVG checks `requirePro("er-diagram-export")` when selected, before any save dialog or write. PNG and JSON remain free; PNG uses the shared SVG builder internally without gating it. Export cancellation, object-URL cleanup, native file writes and existing error/success presentation survive the move into `useDiagramExport`. JSON contains schema metadata, not row data; serialization uses `JSON.stringify`. Existing SVG escaping remains intact.
- Masking enable and non-Keep rule changes retain action-time Pro checks. Export retains the existing pipeline entitlement check and fails closed for active masking after downgrade; no unmasked fallback was introduced. Turning masking off resets every selected rule, including internal omit, to Keep. Selection synchronization drops deselected rules, so reselecting a column cannot resurrect a hidden mask. All-Keep export and ordinary column selection retain Free semantics. Closing/reloading export options clears the toggle/policy state.
- With functioning storage, Manual bypasses the agent branch for both Free and Pro, and creates no new upgrade hint. Agent eligibility still requires a supported engine/database context and current Pro access; legacy basic-chat fallback, provider/model fallback, consent and native agent/grant guards are unchanged. Findings 1–2 concern the newly introduced mode state and existing-hint integration, not entitlement bypass.
- Shared Checkbox now calls the consumer before restoring the rendered controlled value. Current consumers read `checked` synchronously or toggle parent state; accepted changes re-render with the new value, refused changes return to the old value, and uncontrolled inputs are left alone. No concrete regression found in existing settings, import/export, table-filter/count, clone or destructive-option callers. Dedicated shared-component coverage remains missing for accepted true/false transitions, refused changes, uncontrolled inputs, indeterminate state and disabled/keyboard behavior; the new masking test covers only refusal, and passing consumer tests are not a complete app-wide Checkbox interaction matrix.
- Native dialog mapping is correct by source inspection for the actual 2.6.0 JS and Rust plugin: custom button results serialize as their label string; standard Yes/Ok/No and Cancel are also handled. Checked installed rfd macOS result mapping and plugin Linux custom-label mapping; cancellation/unknown results map to cancel rather than opening pricing or license entry. An in-memory probe of the actual wrapper confirmed custom primary/secondary/cancel labels, standard Yes/No/Ok and unknown fallback. Context7 official plugin documentation corroborates the result contract: https://github.com/tauri-apps/plugins-workspace/blob/v2/plugins/dialog/src/models.rs . Actual OS close/Escape behavior was not exercised.
- The host consumes requests immediately and its synchronous ref guard suppresses overlapping native prompts; `finally` releases the guard. Primary opens license entry; secondary opens the fixed pricing URL only when external purchase is configured. Host tests verify those routes and single-flight, but mock the entire wrapper: they do not cover its two-button branch, actual plugin invocation, close/Escape, rejection recovery or non-purchase distribution behavior.
- Diagram props correctly carry current metadata and filename source through the dialog into the export hook. Ignoring formatting, canvas geometry, virtualization, relationship rendering and zoom/pan logic are retained; the new inner relative wrapper retains overlay positioning. Export Popover anchors to a DOM div around the non-ref-forwarding Button; assistant picker anchors directly to a native button. No anchoring defect found by inspection, but both new menu test suites mock Popover and therefore cannot validate real positioning, portal focus, clipping or viewport constraints. New diagram header also lacks executed narrow-window/long-name layout evidence.
- Global dark-theme remaps cover the new white/neutral/slate surfaces and text; explicit dark variants address the changed banners and trial note. No confirmed dark-mode regression found by source inspection. No new sensitive logging, telemetry, database writes or dynamic external destinations introduced. Toolbar badge removal preserves existing action gates and Pro tooltips.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx tsc --noEmit`: **passed**.
- Full `npm run test:run`: **passed**. A separately captured full `npx vitest run` also passed: **140 files / 827 tests**, 15.01 seconds. Repeat captured the complete summary after the initial combined tool output was truncated.
- `npm run build`: **passed**. Existing warnings: `node:zlib` browser externalization, mixed static/dynamic imports, and large chunks.
- `git diff --check a122a98..HEAD`: **passed** before append. Supplemental probes used in-memory transpilation; no source/test fixtures changed.
- No live native UI, screen-reader, real Popover layout or native dialog close/Escape smoke run. Unit tests substitute native dialogs, canvas/image APIs and Popover; source/probe evidence does not establish platform UI behavior. Add the focused regressions above and manually verify both menus, keyboard navigation, light/dark appearance and native dialog outcomes before clearing these findings.

## Re-review: pro-gating UI follow-ups round 2

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED** (2026-10-19). Two previous P2 findings resolved; accessibility P2 partially resolved. No new P0/P1 or separate P2 found in the fixes.

Scope: read AGENTS.md, .agent/WORKFLOW.md and referenced RTK guidance first. Reviewed git diff HEAD and relevant untracked files on feat/pro-gating (HEAD 06d1374), against “Review: pro-gating UI follow-ups (a122a98..06d1374)” and “Fixes for Codex review "pro-gating UI follow-ups"” in .agent/plans/pro-gating.md. Ignored .claude/launch.json; settled decisions remain closed. Only this section appended; existing review content, code, tests and plan preserved. No staging or commit.

### Previous finding disposition

1. **P2 — storage failure / Manual execution: RESOLVED by implementation.** src/lib/ai-assistant/assistantMode.ts:14-39 initializes one session value from best-effort storage, updates it before persistence, and notifies subscribers even after a failed write. Picker subscribes at src/components/ai-assistant/AssistantModePicker.tsx:41; shared hook reads/subscribes at src/components/ai-assistant/useAssistantMode.ts:9-14; submission reads the same getter at src/components/ai-assistant/hooks/useAiAssistantSubmit.ts:389-398. Manual excludes both agent execution and the locked-agent hint callback. Denied reads or stale persisted Agent cannot override a later Manual session choice. AssistantModePicker.test.tsx:64-77 verifies throwing writes and matching getter/display. Coverage limitation: no explicit denied-read + previously persisted Agent submission regression; existing Free/Pro Manual hook regressions pass.
2. **P2 — existing hint after choosing Manual: RESOLVED.** src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantPanel.tsx:415,570-572 subscribes to mode and suppresses the hint in Manual, preserving chat/device dismissal state at :403-411. Rendered regression at AiAssistantPanel.allow.test.tsx:265-277 shows an existing hint disappearing on mode change. Subsequent Manual submission cannot re-enable its render condition. Coverage limitation: test uses the shared setter and mocks submission rather than driving picker → Manual submit end to end.
3. **P2 — accessible picker state / keyboard handling: PARTIALLY RESOLVED.** Trigger names current mode and exposes expanded/controlled state at AssistantModePicker.tsx:140-143; menu/items expose roles and checked state at :92-113. Arrow/Home/End and Escape handlers plus focus restoration exist at :55-78. Initial focus still fails with the real application wrapper's delayed mounting; picker tests still mock that wrapper at AssistantModePicker.test.tsx:13-20.

### Remaining finding

**P2 — initial menu focus runs before its items mount.** src/components/ai-assistant/AssistantModePicker.tsx:49-53. On first open, or after the previous close transition completes, the effect attempts focus while item refs are null. The real src/components/common/Popover.tsx:38-49,75-76 initially keeps content unmounted, then schedules mounted=true from its own effect. The picker effect depends only on open and never retries when items mount. Keyboard opening therefore leaves focus on the trigger; Arrow/Escape handlers live only on the menu (AssistantModePicker.tsx:97), so the advertised keyboard flow is unavailable until focus moves there separately. **Fix:** focus the selected item when menu content actually mounts, retain Escape restoration, and add a regression using the real application Popover lifecycle, including reopening after full close.

**Evidence:** a temporary /tmp Vitest config transformed the existing picker suite in memory, removing its application-Popover mock and retaining actual Popover.tsx. Only react-tiny-popover was replaced by a minimal conditional renderer honoring isOpen and rendering content; application mounting effects remained unchanged. Result: **3 passed / 2 failed**. Selected-item focus assertion received document.body; first ArrowDown focused Agent instead of expected Manual because initial focus never occurred. No repository test was edited. This isolates the wrapper/picker lifecycle defect, not third-party positioning. An earlier attempt with the actual third-party renderer failed with jsdom InvalidCharacterError ("[object Object]" did not match the QName production), supplying no real-renderer behavioral evidence.

### Regression assessment and verification

- Session subscriptions unsubscribe on unmount; submission retains Pro/context/engine checks. Hint suppression preserves dismissal state. No new entitlement bypass, data write, sensitive logging or external destination found.
- Added Checkbox tests cover accepted/refused transitions both ways, uncontrolled input, indeterminate and disabled attributes. Added dialog-wrapper tests cover button shapes, custom/standard results, cancel/unknown and browser fallback. Neither changes production behavior; native close/Escape and browser disabled/keyboard interactions remain outside their evidence.
- npx tsc --noEmit: **passed**, exit 0.
- Full npm run test:run: **passed**, **142 files / 840 tests**, 19.29 seconds. Supplemental lifecycle probe above exposes a gap hidden by the passing mocked suite.
- npm run build: **passed**, exit 0. Warnings remain for browser externalization, mixed static/dynamic imports and large chunks.
- git diff --check HEAD: **passed** before append.
- No live desktop/browser, screen-reader or native-dialog smoke run. Real positioning, close-animation focus behavior, light/dark and platform dialog close/Escape remain unverified. These limits do not reopen settled decisions.

## Review: lost-key portal link (dbaefce)

**Verdict: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS** (2026-10-19). No P0/P1 or confirmed functional regression found. P2 coverage follow-up below is non-blocking.

Scope: read AGENTS.md and .agent/WORKFLOW.md first, then referenced RTK guidance. Reviewed `git show dbaefce` against parent `f886bbc` on `feat/pro-gating`; HEAD is `dbaefcebad0465bcf80d15a21273a10245310f6b`. Inspected both screens, distribution configuration, native command policy, shared opener and adjacent tests. Only this section appended; code, tests, plan and existing review content preserved. Unrelated `.claude/launch.json` left untouched. No staging or commit.

### Findings (ranked)

- **P2 — recovery coverage is incomplete (non-blocking).** `src/components/settings/LicenseKeySettings.lostKey.test.tsx:31-39` checks only the Direct Settings portal call with the entire opener mocked. No LicenseDialog recovery test exists; the new App Store branches at `src/components/modal/LicenseDialog.tsx:74-78` and `src/components/settings/LicenseKeySettings.tsx:55-59` are not exercised. **Concrete fix:** add rendered tests for both screens with Direct/App Store features explicitly selected; assert the exact portal URL and unchanged activation view on Direct, no portal/pricing call and visible support instructions on App Store, dialog Back returning to the prior active/inactive view, close/reopen reset, and Settings recovery reset on reactivation. Exercise each mail action with native-opener rejection and assert the supplied fallback navigates to the static encoded mailto; separately verify policy rejection cannot invoke either browser fallback. Existing helper tests cover injected fallback success/failure/policy suppression but not either screen's mailto wiring or default window.open behavior. Add a default-fallback assertion for `_blank`, `noopener,noreferrer`, and explicit Rust policy assertions accepting the exact portal URL on Direct and rejecting it on App Store.

### Correctness and regression assessment

- `src/lib/license/proAccess.ts:28` defines one fixed `https://polar.sh/politedb/portal` destination. Both handlers call `openExternalUrl` only with `externalPurchase=true`; otherwise they retain the previous `setLostKeyHelp(true)` behavior. Direct clicks leave the existing form and typed key intact. No license activation, refresh, deactivation or entitlement logic changed.
- Native enforcement remains authoritative: `src-tauri/src/commands/license.rs:69-70` checks `Distribution::current().allows_external_url` before opening. `src-tauri/src/distribution.rs:40-44,69-94` accepts the portal on Direct and rejects its host on App Store; mailto remains allowed. App Store frontend mode disables external purchase (`src/lib/distribution/index.ts:31-36`); `tauri.appstore.conf.json` and `scripts/tauri-appstore.sh` pair that mode with the Rust feature. No allowlist expansion or policy bypass introduced.
- `src/lib/tauri/license.ts:170-187` opens through the backend, suppresses fallback for `FEATURE_UNAVAILABLE_IN_DISTRIBUTION`, and uses `window.open(url, "_blank", "noopener,noreferrer")` on other failures. Both retained mail handlers supply `window.location.href` instead (`LicenseDialog.tsx:82-92`, `LicenseKeySettings.tsx:63-71`). Thus policy denial cannot fall through to either fallback; ordinary opener failure preserves the existing browser/mail behavior. The helper cannot report whether a fallback actually opens a browser; that pre-existing limitation remains.
- App Store dialog recovery still replaces content, hides Lost license key?, and exposes Back (`LicenseDialog.tsx:109-158,224-235`); Back preserves the active/inactive branch and input, while opening/reset effects clear recovery state. Settings still adds inline support help and resets it when its active effect runs (`LicenseKeySettings.tsx:36-44,190-233`); it has no Back button before or after this commit. Direct no longer enters email help, as requested.
- Upgrade prompt now uses exactly `See plans & pricing` (`ProUpgradeDialogHost.tsx:31-35`), retaining the external-purchase gate, pricing destination, primary activation action, cancellation and single-flight behavior. Updated host and system-dialog tests pass.
- Privacy: portal and pricing URLs are static, with no email, license key, token, device identifier or account query parameters. Recovery mailto includes only the fixed support inbox and generic encoded subject/body; it does not interpolate stored customer data. No new logging, analytics or sensitive persistence introduced.

## Review: subscription-renewal Phase 1 (backend)

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-20 against the approved plan's Design R1–R5, backend Tests and Phase 1 implementation evidence. Scope: complete uncommitted backend diff plus untracked `src/services/polar-renewal.ts` and `test/polar-renewal.test.ts`, branch `feat/subscription-renewal`, HEAD `c0a50f4f8a1e8573b042260d9e42739680f270cc`. Paths below are relative to `politedb-backend`. Read app `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK guidance and `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`. Only this section appended; earlier reviews preserved. No application edits, commits or external network calls. No P0/P1 identified; the following P2s concern the approved date contract and required regression evidence.

### Findings

1. **P2 — Validate the selected date, rather than falling through malformed dates.** `src/services/polar-renewal.ts:110-113,130,142,148`: parsing each candidate before applying `??` conflates an absent preferred date with a present but malformed one. For example, `trial_end: "soon"` with a valid future `current_period_end` produces a dated First payment row; R3 selects `trial_end ?? current_period_end` first, then drops an unparseable selected date and preserves `trialing` with null `renewsAt`. Likewise malformed `ends_at` can substitute the period end (even incorrectly trigger `ended` when that period is past), and malformed `ended_at` can display an unrelated fallback end date. **Fix:** apply R3's nullish selection to raw timestamp fields before parsing, retain the known status when the selected timestamp is invalid, and add DTO plus email assertions with malformed preferred fields and valid fallback fields. `test/polar-renewal.test.ts:120-133` currently makes the fallback null or malformed too, hiding this difference; trial equal-to-now and the required map-to-email missing/malformed/equal/past matrix are also absent.

2. **P2 — Deadline tests do not advance the resolver clock with fake timers.** `test/polar-renewal.test.ts:35-37,311-342`, `src/services/polar-renewal.ts:37-45`: the hanging/late-completion tests call `vi.useFakeTimers()` without injecting `() => Date.now()` afterward. The resolver holds a previously captured Date.now function; cleanup also captures the fake function before restoring real timers. Advancing timers therefore does not reliably advance the clock used by the second-step floor. The late-completion test checks only the already-resolved result and absence of unhandled rejection; it can pass while an unnecessary subscription call starts after the virtual deadline. The late-rejection test never checks that the late error itself was categorized (its timeout assertion is already satisfied by the race timer). **Fix:** bind the injected clock to fake time in every fake-timer case, restore real timers before resetting the clock, assert no second call after late grant completion, assert the distinct late rejection category, and check timer cleanup/unhandled rejection after both steps. Add an exact 300 ms boundary assertion, not only the current 200 ms remaining case.

3. **P2 — Complete the approved caller/isolation regression matrix.** `test/license.test.ts:343-393`, `test/polar-service.test.ts:163-179,384-394,428-461`, `test/polar-renewal.test.ts:224-244`: validate has 11 s/11.8 s/13 s coverage, but activate has only 11.8 s, and webhook/resend each have only 11.8 s. There is no grant-versus-admin-resend race while renewal is pending; the existing race is webhook-versus-webhook after reaching the send. Privacy sentinels observe only resolver console.log/warn/error, never `reportError` or caller error boundaries. The email-error test reads null only after successful send, which unconditionally clears `emailError`, so it cannot establish that enrichment never wrote an error. **Fix:** add all three mandatory-work durations for all four callers; race grant and resend while a deferred renewal lookup holds the claim; assert only the winner enriches/sends; capture caller reporting/logging with sensitive SDK errors; and spy on failure recording or inspect stored error state before the send clears it. Add successful validate-with-failed-enrichment coverage. These are missing assertions, not observed production failures.

### Contract checks

- **R1/R2:** additive fields and null fallback are separate from key expiry/entitlement. Exact key/customer/benefit/type filters, no `is_granted`, one-match requirement, null subscription handling, pagination rejection and subscription id/customer checks match the plan (`src/services/polar-renewal.ts:164-195`). Trusted identity comes from Polar responses, not request identifiers. Validate's 404 body is unchanged and returns before enrichment (`src/services/polar-license-key.ts:452-502`).
- **R3:** first-match status precedence matches terminal → paused → scheduled pause → incomplete → scheduled end → delinquent → trial → active. Active/trial status survives an unusable renewal date. UTC normalization works. Preferred malformed-date handling needs finding 1.
- **R4:** all three timestamp captures are first statements, before mandatory work. Activation enriches after license/device persistence; validate enriches only after successful Polar validation. Both SDK calls receive decreasing seconds-based timeouts and enforce the <=300 ms floor. The actual caught work promise is raced; caller-level finally clears the timer without creating a discarded rejecting promise. Late SDK rejection remains caught. Although cleanup is on the outer race rather than the plan's work.finally, it provides the required timer ownership and no unhandled rejection path was identified. Failure output is a static category; raw SDK errors do not reach caller logging/reporting in inspected code. No DB writes or entitlement decisions occur in the resolver.
- **R5/fallback work:** enrichment stays inside the email claim, after key/optional benefit lookup and before send. It has no email-error writes; existing mandatory failure/retry/release and successful-send clearing remain intact. `emailExpiry` removes fabricated annual expiry, preserves the real expiry, and routes malformed expiry to Unknown. `renewalRow` uses the specified copy, key expiry precedence and UTC dates; HTML escapes row values and labels come from static literals (`src/services/license-email.ts:64-132`). No Manage subscription button added.
- **Installed SDK:** checked local `node_modules/@polar-sh/sdk/dist/2026-10/services/benefit_grants.mjs`, `services/subscriptions.mjs`, `models-P8aaHkKR.d.mts` and `base-Cir88A-B.mjs`. List returns direct `{ items, pagination }`, get accepts `(id, requestOptions)`, timestamps are strings, grant properties expose optional `license_key_id`, and timeout overrides are seconds (runtime converts to milliseconds). Fixtures have the relevant real field names/shapes; no wrapper/camelCase/Date-object mismatch found. Fixtures are partial/untyped and tsc includes only src, so passing tsc does not validate complete test fixtures. This is distinct from findings 1–3. No online documentation or live Polar call used.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx --no-install tsc --noEmit -p .`: passed.
- `npm run lint`: passed.
- `npx --no-install prettier --check src test`: passed.
- `git diff --check`: passed.
- `npx --no-install vitest run`: 140 passed, 6 skipped, 48 failed; 3 files passed, 1 skipped, 5 failed. All 48 failures report `listen EPERM: operation not permitted 127.0.0.1`; HTTP/admin integration execution is blocked by this sandbox, not demonstrated application failures. Resolver/email-render and direct service assertions ran, but the gaps above prevent treating green assertions as complete plan verification.
- PostgreSQL-only tests were skipped without TEST_POSTGRES_URL; local tests used in-memory SQLite. No production database, SMTP delivery, live Polar scopes/data, or deployment verification. Prior implementation claims of 194/194 and sandbox smoke are recorded in the plan but were not independently reproduced here. Run the complete suite with PostgreSQL and permitted loopback after addressing findings.

## Re-review: subscription-renewal Phase 1 (backend) round 1

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-20. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK guidance, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, approved plan and its Phase 1 fixes subsection, and the previous backend review. Re-read the complete uncommitted application diff, changed tests, and both untracked renewal files in `politedb-backend`, branch `feat/subscription-renewal`, HEAD `c0a50f4f8a1e8573b042260d9e42739680f270cc`. Paths below are relative to that backend. Only this section appended; no application edits, commits or external network calls. Accepted plan decisions remain closed.

### Disposition of previous findings

- **P2-1 — Runtime defect resolved.** `src/services/polar-renewal.ts:114-115,132-134,151-154` selects raw fields with `??` before parsing. Malformed preferred timestamps now preserve the selected status and produce null dates instead of falling through. `test/polar-renewal.test.ts:131-159` covers malformed trial/end/ended fields with usable fallback fields, including a past period end that must not force `ended`, and trial equal-to-now. `:161-169` exercises map-to-email status rows for active/trialing with missing, malformed, equal and past dates. Email status rendering is separately covered in `test/license-email.test.ts`. These checks passed. The exact malformed-preferred/valid-fallback cases are asserted at DTO level rather than each being passed into `renewalRow`; the mapper assertions plus shared row tests establish the corrected behavior without another blocking finding.
- **P2-2 — Clock defect and most assertions resolved; requested second-step coverage still open.** `src/services/polar-renewal.ts:37-47` reads `Date.now()` dynamically; `test/polar-renewal.test.ts:36-39,365-370` binds fake time explicitly and restores real timers before resetting the clock. `:354-363` checks exactly 300/301 ms. `:372-403` distinguishes late `upstream` rejection from the timer's `timeout`, forbids a subscription call after late grants, and checks timer cleanup/unhandled rejection; `:406-411` checks cleanup on timely success. These tests passed. Remaining requirement below concerns a timeout while the second SDK call is already pending.
- **P2-3 — Duration, failure isolation and privacy checks added; requested cross-caller race still open.** `test/license.test.ts:339-350,389-449` adds successful validation with failed enrichment and all three mandatory-work durations for activate/validate. `test/polar-service.test.ts:432-480` adds 11 s/11.8 s/13 s for webhook/resend. `:512-525` reads the prior SMTP error inside the send mock, before successful-send cleanup, so enrichment cannot hide a write behind that cleanup. `test/license.test.ts:353-373` captures caller reporting/logging and HTTP validate output; `test/polar-service.test.ts:528-544` captures webhook reporting/logging with sensitive SDK errors. Direct service checks passed; HTTP assertions could not execute under loopback restrictions. The race at `:482-509` still exercises two resends, not grant versus resend.

### Remaining findings (existing P2 scope)

1. **P2-2 — Exercise timeout and late settlement during the subscription step.** `test/polar-renewal.test.ts:372-411`: both deferred timeout cases stall `benefitGrants.list`; `subscriptions.get` is immediate in every fake-timer case. The original requirement to check cleanup/unhandled rejection after both steps remains unverified. **Fix:** let grants finish in time, hold `subscriptions.get` deferred, advance to the absolute deadline, assert null plus zero owned timers, then settle/reject the subscription and assert unchanged result, the distinct static late-error category on rejection, and no unhandled rejection. Reuse the explicit fake-time clock and cleanup already added. No production deadline defect identified.

2. **P2-3 — Race the actual grant path against admin resend while enrichment holds the claim.** `test/polar-service.test.ts:482-503`: the webhook is fully awaited at `:483`; both concurrent requests at `:494-503` target `/resend`. This proves resend-versus-resend exclusion but bypasses webhook delivery upsert/prechecks while the claim is held. **Fix:** seed an unsent delivery, start one actual grant/webhook invocation or resend and defer its renewal lookup, then invoke the other path for the same grant while pending; assert only the claim winner enriches and sends, and release/await both. Keep the existing two-resend case if useful. This is the previously requested regression case, not scope expansion or an observed duplicate-send failure.

### New findings and regression assessment

No new P0–P3 production correctness, security or privacy finding identified. The remaining findings above retain their original identifiers. Raw-field selection preserves status precedence and UTC normalization. The clock change preserves injected-clock behavior while removing stale captured timer functions. Renewal remains optional, display-only and without DB writes; activation persistence precedes enrichment, validation's 404 response remains unchanged, and email lookup remains inside the existing claim. Static error categories prevent SDK bodies reaching caller error handling. Mandatory failure recording, successful-send clearing, entitlement and key-expiry precedence remain intact. No accepted architecture decision reopened.

### Verification and limitations

- Backend `npx --no-install tsc --noEmit -p .`, `npm run lint`, `npx --no-install prettier --check src test`, and `git diff --check`: **passed**.
- Focused `vitest run test/polar-renewal.test.ts test/license-email.test.ts`: **90/90 passed**, 2 files.
- Full `vitest run` with `TEST_POSTGRES_URL` unset: **157 passed, 6 skipped, 52 failed**, 2 files passed, 1 skipped, 6 failed. Of the failures, 51 report `listen EPERM: operation not permitted 127.0.0.1`; the race test additionally times out waiting for enrichment because its first HTTP request cannot listen, with the same EPERM reported as an unhandled rejection. These are sandbox execution limitations, not demonstrated application failures. In particular, HTTP privacy, admin resend durations and concurrent HTTP claim behavior were not independently verified.
- PostgreSQL integration tests skipped; no database server, live Polar, SMTP or deployment checks attempted. The plan's 215/215 PostgreSQL result is implementer evidence, not reproduced here. Vite emits a CommonJS/native-config-loader compatibility warning; checks otherwise complete as described.

## Review: subscription-renewal Phase 2 (app)

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-20 against the Goal table, R1, R6, Tests and Phase 2 implementation evidence in `.agent/plans/subscription-renewal.md`. App branch: `feat/subscription-renewal`; scope: uncommitted application diff plus untracked `src/lib/license/renewal.ts` and `renewal.test.ts`. Backend contract inspected read-only on its `feat/subscription-renewal` branch. `.agent/*` and `.claude/launch.json` excluded as implementation review targets. Earlier review sections preserved.

### Findings

1. **P2 — `src/lib/license/renewal.ts:101`: blank key expiry bypasses precedence and misrepresents an invalid key as a subscription.** `normalize_api_state` deliberately preserves a present blank expiry as `Some("")` (`src-tauri/src/license.rs:834`), and the existing `the_response_decides_the_expiry` regression proves blank/unreadable expiry is invalid, not unlimited. But `if (state?.expires_at)` skips `""`. Thus `{ expires_at: "", subscription_status: "active", renews_at: <future ISO> }` renders “Renews on”; without subscription fields it renders “Renews with your subscription”. Both violate the Goal's “Expires at: Unknown” row for unparseable key expiry. This can reach both active-license screens because an invalid expiry does not itself rewrite `status` to expired. **Fix:** distinguish null/undefined from a present string before parsing; preserve the Unknown row for empty/whitespace/malformed expiry. Add regression cases with and without subscription fields to prove expiry precedence and no fallback. Leave entitlement logic unchanged.

2. **P3 — `src/lib/license/renewal.test.ts:4` and `src/components/modal/LicenseDialog.lostKey.test.tsx:157`: calendar boundaries and both-flavor renewal rendering lack explicit regression coverage.** Unit fixtures use local midday plus fixed 24-hour offsets in October; they never exercise local midnight with a different UTC date, spring/fall DST transitions, or a non-hour offset. Dated-row assertions only require a nonempty value, so an incorrect displayed date could pass. New dialog renewal cases use Direct only, and Settings renewal tests do not parameterize distribution; existing App Store recovery tests do not assert renewal output. **Fix:** add deterministic boundary fixtures with exact expected local dates/day wording under representative timezones (including DST), and assert shared renewal/status/fallback output in both screens under Direct and App Store policies. These are coverage gaps, not an identified ordinary-DST arithmetic or flavor-gating defect.

### Contract and regression assessment

- Backend `src/services/polar-renewal.ts:241` returns exactly camelCase `subscriptionStatus`, `renewsAt`, `endsAt`, each nullable. Rust `LicenseApiResponse` accepts those aliases and snake_case fields as `Option<String>`; serialized `LicenseState` exposes the snake_case fields consumed by TS. The eight TS status literals match the backend union.
- `normalize_api_state` reads all three renewal fields from this response only; absent, null and blank clear previous values. The backend validate-404 branch returns a successful inactive/revoked body without renewal fields, so normalization clears them. Its null activation token still follows the pre-existing carry-over rule. Transport/server failures retain the prior snapshot.
- New fields default to None in the authoritative inactive constructor; other constructors inherit that state or explicitly initialize them. Older state files deserialize via defaults; migration/version behavior remains intact. `LICENSE_STATE_VERSION`, `entitlement`, `paid_license`, `pro_access`, `pro_access_until` and `apply_local_expiry` behavior are unchanged. No renewal field feeds access decisions.
- Apart from finding 1, presentation matches key-expiry precedence, all known-status labels, missing/malformed/equal/past date status words, stale renewal handling, ended dates and unknown-status fallback. Local-midnight rounding accommodates ordinary 23/25-hour DST days by inspection; boundary tests remain absent as described above.
- Both screens consume `renewalPresentation` and its shared tone map; former expiry/date-decision logic and all `formatDaysUntil` references are removed. Layout markup remains screen-specific. No Manage subscription button was introduced or requested.
- Direct/App Store purchase and recovery policies remain unchanged: Direct retains the existing Polar portal entry; App Store retains email recovery, external-purchase restrictions and ungated Pro policy. Renewal presentation introduces no navigation or entitlement side effects.
- New Rust tests cover aliases, payload-only clearing, entitlement independence, old-state loading and lifecycle persistence/failure behavior. The alias test currently supplies null `endsAt`/`ends_at`, so it does not independently prove a non-null end-date alias; the annotations are correct by inspection. The lifecycle mock correctly models the backend's 404 response body as HTTP success, not an app-facing HTTP 404.

- Focused `cargo test renewal_fields_`: **4 passed, 1 failed** (450 filtered). Parsing, payload clearing, entitlement independence and old-state loading passed; lifecycle case stops at loopback server creation (`src-tauri/src/license.rs:2055`) with PermissionDenied.

### Dispositions

- **Previous P2 — CLOSED.** `src/lib/license/renewal.ts:105` now checks `state?.expires_at != null`, preserving precedence for every present string. Blank, whitespace and malformed expiry return exactly `Expires at: Unknown`, with no subscription date, relative detail or fallback. `src/lib/license/renewal.test.ts:140-162` asserts the complete row for all three invalid inputs both with and without active subscription fields. Null/absent expiry still reaches subscription presentation. Rust expiry/entitlement logic is unchanged.
- **Previous P3 — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.** `src/lib/license/renewal.test.ts:175-225` adds exact local-date and day-wording assertions for Ho Chi Minh City midnight, Los Angeles's previous local date, New York spring/fall DST and Kolkata's half-hour offset. These tests really change the timezone: installed Vitest defaults to child-process `forks` (`node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/coverage.DM_a_rWm.js:180`), and the unchanged 27-test file passes even with its parent forced to `TZ=UTC`. In UTC alone the Ho Chi Minh City fixture would say `today` instead of `in 1 day`, and Los Angeles would cross to October 21 instead of remaining October 20. A native Node v24.16.0 probe also confirms runtime TZ changes: offsets are -420 (Ho Chi Minh City), 480/420 (Los Angeles), 300/240 (New York) and -330 (Kolkata) at winter/summer fixtures. No worker-thread limitation is attributed to this default run. However, absent-TZ restoration is incorrect, as detailed below.
- **Flavor coverage — mostly addressed within previous P3.** Settings tests at `src/components/settings/LicenseKeySettings.lostKey.test.tsx:147-185` now check dated renewal, status-only payment copy and fallback under both policies. Dialog tests at `src/components/modal/LicenseDialog.lostKey.test.tsx:157-180` now check dated cancellation and fallback under both policies, but still omit the previously requested status-only assertion. Both flavors' existing purchase/recovery tests pass.
- **Non-null end-date alias gap — CLOSED.** `src-tauri/src/license.rs:1812-1830` asserts a real non-null date for both `endsAt` and `ends_at`, along with canceled status and absent renewal date. This new test passes under default and `app-store` features.

### Remaining findings

1. **P3 — `src/lib/license/renewal.test.ts:169-170`: cleanup does not restore an initially absent TZ.** `originalTz` is undefined when the shell has no TZ, including this review environment. Native `process.env.TZ = originalTz` then stores the literal string `"undefined"`; it does not remove the variable. A read-only Node probe reproduced `hadTZ: false` before this sequence and `hadTZ: true, restored: "undefined"` afterwards. Thus cleanup can change the effective timezone for later work in that worker, despite all current assertions passing. **Fix:** delete `process.env.TZ` when the saved value is undefined; otherwise assign the saved string. Verify both initially absent and explicitly set TZ are restored. This completes the requested timezone-test isolation; no application change is needed.

2. **P3 — `src/components/modal/LicenseDialog.lostKey.test.tsx:172`: status-only flavor coverage remains incomplete.** The parameterized test moves directly from dated cancellation to unknown fallback. Unlike Settings, neither Dialog flavor asserts a known status with no usable date, although the fix subsection claims both screens cover status words. **Fix:** add a status-only fixture and assert its label/value in the existing Direct/App Store test, resetting the stable mocked store between renders as already done. This closes the previous P3 request; no scope growth or observed production rendering defect is implied.

### Regression assessment

No new P0–P2 production correctness, security or privacy finding identified. Shared presentation still supplies both screens; key expiry wins; known-status, stale/missing/invalid-date and fallback paths remain intact. Rust renewal fields still come only from each response, clear on absent/null payloads, default for old persisted states, and never feed entitlement or local-expiry decisions. State version and existing access/deactivation/recovery policy remain unchanged. The remaining changes requested concern test cleanup and the previously specified Dialog status coverage, not renewal architecture or entitlement.

These do not demonstrate a renewal regression.

Alias parsing (including non-null end dates), payload clearing, entitlement independence and old-state loading pass. The same lifecycle test is blocked by loopback binding under both feature sets. ## Review: ER diagram routing (shared layout)

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-20 on `fix/license-key-input`. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK guidance and `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`. Reviewed the uncommitted diffs for `DiagramCanvas.tsx`, `diagramExport.ts`, `diagramExport.test.ts` and the complete untracked `diagramLayout.ts` / `diagramLayout.test.ts`. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Earlier review sections retained. No application edits, commits or network calls.

### Findings

1. **P2 — `src/components/diagram/diagramLayout.ts:430`, `src/components/diagram/diagramLayout.ts:457`: lane allocation can superimpose overlapping relations.** Internal gutters have only five slots, allocated by a global counter modulo five without checking occupied vertical intervals; the right outer gutter has only one slot. A read-only probe using the real layout builder and six same-column relations spanning the same two cards returned vertical lanes `[340, 346, 334, 352, 328, 340]`: the sixth shares the first's complete vertical span. Horizontal allocation can collide even before exhausting capacity: in a synthetic three-column layout with free gap `[128,156]` and six distinct endpoint-row pairs whose desired crossing is 156, actual crossing lanes were `[156,156,151,156,146,156]`. Clamping offsets onto the boundary collapses four runs onto one y. Shared strokes make dense relations ambiguous and prevent independent hover selection along their shared spans. **Fix:** allocate actual available coordinates against occupied intervals, reuse lanes only for disjoint spans, and provide an explicit overflow strategy (for example, widen congested gutters/gaps in the shared layout). For gaps, enumerate in-bounds lanes rather than clamping multiple offsets to the same coordinate. Add adversarial saturation and boundary-desired fixtures that assert overlapping spans remain distinguishable; the current `diagramLayout.test.ts:202-219` assertion only proves that at least two x values exist anywhere.

2. **P2 — `src/components/diagram/diagramLayout.ts:316`, `src/components/diagram/diagramLayout.ts:426`: corner rounding consumes space reserved for cardinality marks.** Stub clearance is measured before rounding. A generated one-table layout with a self-reference from its first row to its second produces points `(312,88) → (328,88) → (328,116) → (312,116)`, but its path starts `M 312 88 L 320 88 Q 328 88 328 96`: only 8 px remain straight. The one-to-one circle is centered 9 px outside the edge, extending to 12.5 px, so the path bends through the marker instead of preserving its horizontal attachment stub. The same issue affects short internal lanes; the minimum allocated edge distance is 16 px. This occurs in both canvas and export, and the outer margin offers no alternative lane. **Fix:** reserve the marker envelope plus stroke clearance after rounding, either by limiting endpoint-corner trim or by allocating longer stubs and increasing shared gutters/outer bounds as needed. Test actual rounded endpoint geometry for both cardinalities, outer-margin loops and the shortest internal lanes; checking raw lengths against 12 px does not establish this invariant.

3. **P3 — `src/components/diagram/diagramLayout.test.ts:85-118`, `src/components/diagram/diagramExport.test.ts:73`: the invariant tests leave important outputs unchecked.** The seeded fixtures test unrounded `points`, not the emitted `path`, marker geometry or layout bounds; they never assert target `endY`. A path containing an unrelated curve, an end attached to the wrong target row, or the marker-clearance defect above can therefore pass. The SVG end-circle regression uses only two different-column tables, where old `fromRight` logic already selects the correct side; it would not catch reverting the new same-column entry-side fix. The checker sanity test covers only one horizontal crossing, while its bounding-box predicate is valid as a segment/card oracle only after orthogonality has been established. **Fix:** add an independent expected-row assertion, explicit same-row self-loop allowance, finite/in-bounds checks, rounded-path/marker-envelope checks, and a same-column one-to-one SVG fixture with exact marker coordinates. Exercise the checker with horizontal/vertical crossings, non-crossings, edge touches and diagonal rejection. Add explicit empty-layout, narrow-free-gap and saturated-lane fixtures; keep the reproducible seeded cases as broader coverage.

### Correctness and regression assessment

- **Original card-crossing causes addressed by inspection and passing point tests.** Canvas and SVG now use the same layout and router. SVG emits relations before cards. Standard generated layouts have 56 px gutters and 48 px card spacing; vertical lanes remain outside cards, intermediate horizontal runs use free gaps, and same-column relations leave/enter the right edges rather than crossing their column. No straight-segment/card-interior violation found in the four seeded cases (12/20, 60/120, 150/300, 150/600 tables/relations). The checker tests every card, including source and target, and is a valid interior-overlap check for these orthogonal segments; its 0.5 px inset intentionally ignores near-edge penetration below that threshold.
- **Rounded corners avoid cards under the generated geometry.** A corner remains within the convex hull of its trimmed legs. Gutter clearance is at least 16 px and cross-column gap clearance is at least 10 px, against maximum trim 10 px; source/target corners turn away from their own cards. This supports card avoidance, but does not supply the straight marker space in finding 2. No arbitrary custom-layout safety guarantee is inferred: the router assumes aligned fixed-width columns and adequate free space.
- **Boundary cases inspected.** Empty layouts retain 640×400 bounds and return null for absent endpoints; one-table layouts use a bounded right-margin loop. Missing source or target tables are skipped without shifting original relation indices. Same-row self-references move the target attachment down 12 px, still within a normal 28 px row (including the last row); different-row self-references preserve target row centers. Ordinary one-column loops and all generated paths stay inside outer bounds. Very different column heights retain valid top/bottom free gaps, although choosing the midpoint of a large terminal gap can create long detours. Sub-spacing gaps yield a single clamped horizontal lane; they are safe only when the supplied layout still provides the router's card clearance, and cannot imply distinct lanes for concurrent runs.
- **Canvas integration preserved by inspection.** Relation indices, table-hover activation, marker hit targets and path-based hover handlers remain wired as before. `segments` now cover every simplified leg and continue feeding the relation spatial index and viewport culling. Rounded corners/marker envelopes extend beyond those thin rectangles, but the unchanged 420 px overscan is much larger than these local offsets; no newly demonstrated visible-viewport omission. Shared-span hover ambiguity is covered by finding 1. Zoom/detail behavior and Pro export gating are unchanged; no entitlement logic changed.
- **Determinism and scale.** Same ordered input yields stable routing; lane counters are local to each call, shorter spans sort first, and original indices break ties. Layout ordering retains the previous canvas's locale-sensitive name/key comparisons and input-order ties, so cross-locale or input-permutation identity is not promised. Routing sorts relations and scans free gaps per intermediate column, with generated column count capped at 12; it does not perform an all-cards collision search per segment. The 150-table/600-relation fixture passed. No production-size interaction benchmark or memory profile was run; spatial-index cost still grows with long routed legs.
- **SVG validity/escaping inspected.** Output retains the XML declaration, SVG namespace, finite generated coordinates, valid path commands and numeric relation IDs. Table names, column names and types pass through the existing XML escaper; user strings are not inserted into path attributes or IDs. Relation labels are not emitted into this SVG. No new escaping defect identified. Existing tests check substrings, not XML parsing or rasterized output; no standalone renderer validation was performed.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`: **passed**.
- `npx vitest run src/components/diagram`: **33/33 passed**, 7 files.
- `npm run test:run`: **927/927 passed**, 150 files.
- Read-only, in-memory Node/TypeScript probes executed the actual router to confirm the outer-margin rounded path and crowded gutter/gap coordinates quoted above. Synthetic gap fixtures isolate allocation behavior; they are not claimed to be output of the layout builder. No application or test files were written by these probes.
- No emitting build, browser/desktop session, real customer schema, SVG renderer, live hover/scroll check or performance benchmark run. Visual and interaction assessments above are static; passing tests do not close the findings.
- Only this section appended to `.agent/REVIEW.md`; earlier sections preserved. No application changes, staging or commits; no network calls.

## Re-review: ER diagram routing round 1

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-20 on `fix/license-key-input`. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK guidance, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, and the previous shared-layout review. Re-inspected the three tracked application/test diffs and both complete untracked layout files. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Scope remains routing correctness and the stated invariants.

### Dispositions of previous findings

- **P2 lane allocation — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.** `diagramLayout.ts:471-495` allocates logical gutter lanes against occupied vertical intervals, reusing a lane only for disjoint spans with 4 px clearance. Capacity exhaustion records demand and overflow instead of silently wrapping. `:509-565` enumerates actual crossing coordinates, avoiding other owners' crossings in the same and adjacent columns. `:725-754` reroutes after widening. Existing crowded-gutter and seeded inner-segment tests pass. However, opposite endpoint stubs can still superimpose even when `overflow` is false; finding 1 below keeps this correctness request open.
- **P2 rounding consumes marker space — CLOSED.** `diagramLayout.ts:329-350` limits the first/last corner trims by endpoint length minus `MARK_CLEARANCE`; `:578` supplies 14 px. Generated gutters retain at least 14 px edge clearance, including widened one-table margins and last-column left-gutter loops. Thus rounding retains the 12.5 px circle envelope plus stroke space. Emitted-path endpoint assertions run for seeded diagrams, self-references and saturation cases; same-column SVG tests check both left/right circle offsets. This correction does not increase corner radius or reduce the existing 10 px clearance from intermediate cards.
- **P3 unchecked outputs — MOSTLY RESOLVED, with one relevant blind spot.** `diagramLayout.test.ts:105-184` independently checks target/source rows, the explicit same-row self-loop offset, finite/in-bounds points, orthogonality/card avoidance and emitted rounded endpoint lengths. Empty/missing-endpoint, deterministic, fixed-layout overflow and widened saturation cases are present. `diagramExport.test.ts:93-161` exercises same-column one-to-one markers on both sides. The segment oracle now covers vertical/horizontal crossings and edge/non-crossings; diagonals are separately rejected by `assertRoutes`. However, `assertDistinguishable` omits all end stubs rather than allowing only legitimate common-endpoint fan-out, hiding finding 1. Path interiors are still assessed through orthogonal points and corner-clearance reasoning, rather than an independent full curve/card oracle; no separate defect established there.

### Remaining finding

1. **P2 — `src/components/diagram/diagramLayout.ts:636`, `src/components/diagram/diagramLayout.ts:641`, `src/components/diagram/diagramLayout.test.ts:199`: opposite endpoint stubs can share a rendered stroke between unrelated attachment points while overflow is false.** Gutter allocation checks only vertical spans; same-column loops never register their horizontal endpoint runs with the crossing allocator. A deterministic fixture can use four tables `public.a/b/c/d`, each with five rows `c0..c4`, and eight ordered relations: first `a.c0 → c.c1..c4`, then `b.c0 → d.c1..c4`. All tables have degree four, so layout places `a,c` left and `b,d` right. All eight vertical spans overlap and widening produces a 77 px gutter, left card edge 312, right card edge 389 and center 350.5. Relation index 3 takes x=362.5; index 4 takes x=338.5. Their source stubs both lie at y=88, despite attaching to different cards. After the actual 10 px corner trims, index 3 emits a horizontal line from x=312 to 352.5 and index 4 from x=389 to 348.5: a **4 px shared rendered segment**. Vertical lanes are distinct, demand fits, and `overflow` is false. This is not legitimate fan-out from a shared endpoint; canvas hit paths and SVG strokes coincide there. **Fix:** include endpoint horizontal runs in occupancy decisions, coordinating opposite-side attachments so their runs do not overlap (or route a separate safe detour), while retaining marker clearance and card avoidance. Extend the non-overlap checker to include endpoint runs, exempting only overlap attributable to the same attachment point, and add this fixture against emitted paths. This fixture is derived directly from the implementation; no new probe or test file was executed/written during this review.

### Regression assessment

- **Widening/caps:** `gutterRoomFor` accounts for the center-out odd lane count; its extra pixel makes measured vertical demand fit the allocator's floor-based capacity. `gapRoomFor` supplies the requested count inside an ordinary inter-card gap. Spacing grows monotonically and is capped at 640/480; execution is bounded to the initial route plus four reroutes. This is a bounded attempt, not a convergence proof. `overflow` correctly remains exposed on unresolved allocation, but its documentation at `:722` overstates that this necessarily means widest spacing: the pass limit can terminate sooner. Neighbor blockers are not included in successful per-gap demand counts, so horizontal demand is a heuristic for rerouting, not an exact capacity proof. No additional demonstrated production failure from that heuristic identified. Existing overflow coverage exercises a fixed layout; neither actual capped `layoutDiagram` output nor exhaustion of all four reroutes has a dedicated regression test.
- **Crossing ownership:** owner is the original relation index. Excluding that owner lets one relation cross adjacent columns on one horizontal run; other owners in neighboring columns remain blockers, covering horizontal crossings that share a gutter. This rule does not account for endpoint runs, as finding 1 demonstrates. Successful crossings can move to another free gap; no assumption that they must stay in the nearest gap is needed.
- **Canvas/export integration:** both consume `layoutDiagram`; missing endpoints retain null slots and original relation indices. Marker sides, table-hover activation, path hit handlers, zoom/detail behavior and Pro gating remain intact by inspection. Every simplified leg feeds the existing spatial index and viewport culling. Corner/marker offsets are small relative to existing overscan; no new viewport omission identified. Neither consumer uses the overflow flag to change rendering; capped/unresolved cases consequently retain shared lanes as the documented fallback. No entitlement changes identified.
- **Determinism/performance:** local allocation state, stable relation-index ties and fixed iteration bounds preserve repeatability for identical ordered input; the deterministic test passes. Locale-sensitive name ordering remains inherited behavior. Routing scans occupied lane intervals and neighboring crossings; each gap request also enumerates/sorts coordinates in candidate gaps, including potentially tall terminal gaps. Widening can repeat both layout and routing five times. The 150-table/600-relation fixture passes, but no interaction benchmark or larger-schema latency/memory bound was established. No speculative performance finding raised.
- **SVG:** relations precede cards, both marker directions use target entry side, and existing XML escaping remains in place. No new escaping/JSON-export regression identified.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`: **passed**.
- `npx vitest run src/components/diagram`: **45/45 passed**, 7 files.
- `npm run test:run`: **939/939 passed**, 150 files.
- Commands executed through the repository's `rtk proxy` wrapper. No network calls, emitting build, browser/desktop inspection, rendered-SVG inspection, custom executable probes or performance benchmark performed. Geometry evidence for finding 1 is static arithmetic from the actual allocator, layout ordering and rounding code; existing tests do not exercise that fixture.
- Only `.agent/REVIEW.md` edited. Earlier sections retained; no application/test changes, staging or commits. Remaining finding stays within the previous non-superposition invariant.

## Re-review: ER diagram routing round 2

Status: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS

Reviewed 2026-10-20 on `fix/license-key-input`. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK guidance, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, and round 1. Re-reviewed the complete tracked diffs for `DiagramCanvas.tsx`, `diagramExport.ts`, and `diagramExport.test.ts`, plus both untracked `diagramLayout` files. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Scope: routing correctness and existing invariants only.

### Previous finding disposition

- **Round-1 finding 1, P2 opposite endpoint-stub overlap — CLOSED.** `diagramLayout.ts:503-641` now allocates a passage against both vertical occupancy and horizontal runs. `:533-539` rejects opposite-side stubs on rows less than 4 px apart unless the low-side lane stays at least 6 px left of the high-side lane. `:566-570` records both endpoint runs and each jog. The same check applies to gutter portions of column crossings through `:825-828`, not only card endpoints. The exact four-table/eight-relation fixture is present at `diagramLayout.test.ts:350-373`; it passes with `overflow === false`, card/bounds/marker assertions, and the strengthened non-superposition oracle. `assertDistinguishable` at `:192-248` includes end runs and exempts only pairs sharing an attachment point, including either attachment of a one-segment route. Removing the distinct-first-x assertion is correct: disjoint vertical spans may reuse a lane.

### New findings

None. No new P0-P3 correctness issue established in the reviewed scope.

### Regression assessment

- **Side conventions and predicates:** low means the gutter's left boundary, high its right. Rightward crossings enter low/exit high; leftward crossings reverse those sides (`diagramLayout.ts:792-828`). Same-column loops use low on the right of a column and high on the left of the last column (`:769-787`). The opposite-stub inequalities are symmetric. Stub-versus-jog and jog-versus-stub checks both compare the actual horizontal intervals; jog-versus-jog checks reject positive-length overlap on nearby rows (`:530-557`). Perpendicular intersections remain permitted. Same-side fan-out remains permitted; in generated layouts its common row and boundary identify the same card attachment, while intermediate crossings use card-free gap rows.
- **Jog bounds and card clearance:** candidate rows stay strictly inside `(5, height - 5)` and at least 4 px from this passage's entry/exit (`:590-600`). Jog rows need not fall in inter-card gaps: every jog stays horizontally between two lanes inside one gutter. Available lanes retain at least 14 px from both gutter boundaries, so jogs and their vertical legs remain outside cards even when a jog row aligns with a card. Only column-crossing runs require gap allocation, which retains the existing 10 px card margin. Searching 48 candidate rows can conservatively trigger overflow/widening; it does not silently authorize an overlapping passage.
- **Two-lane occupancy and widening:** both vertical spans are reserved and both horizontal endpoints use their respective lane coordinates (`:560-571`). Demand uses the maximum occupied lane index plus one, accounting for the full center-out capacity required by either lane rather than counting passages. Failed passage allocation still marks overflow and requests additional capacity. The existing capped fallback deliberately shares rendered lanes; its virtual reservations are not a proof of separation, and separation is asserted only for successful non-overflow layouts. Widening remains monotonic, capped, and limited to four reroutes; no convergence guarantee is implied.
- **Rounded geometry and markers:** internal corners trim by at most half of each adjoining leg and 10 px; endpoint corners additionally preserve 14 px of straight marker space (`:322-357`, `:719`). Jog rounding stays within the gutter, and rounding at a column crossing stays within the existing gap/card clearance. Short jogs reduce corner trims rather than consuming endpoint marker clearance. Seeded, saturation, self-loop and previous-finding fixtures exercise emitted endpoint lengths; same-column export tests cover marker sides. No new marker-clearance defect identified.
- **Integration and determinism:** canvas and SVG still consume the same `layoutDiagram` output; original relation indices, null missing-endpoint slots, segment-based culling/hit integration, marker sides and Pro gating are preserved by inspection. Allocation state is local, candidate iteration order fixed, and relation-index ties explicit. Identical ordered-input determinism test passes; inherited locale-sensitive table ordering remains unchanged.
- **Performance:** the jog search is bounded to 48 rows with nested lane-pair searches and occupancy scans; widening can repeat routing five times total. No new unbounded loop identified for generated layouts. Existing 150-table/300- and 600-relation fixtures pass. The supplied approximately 73/186/520 ms measurements were not independently reproduced; they are not treated as verified interaction-latency guarantees or worst-case bounds.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`: **passed**.
- `npx vitest run src/components/diagram`: **46/46 passed**, 7 files.
- `npm run test:run`: **940/940 passed**, 150 files.
- Commands executed through `rtk proxy`; no network calls. No emitting build, custom executable probes, browser/desktop inspection, SVG rasterization, independent performance benchmark or memory profile. Curve/card clearance above is static geometric reasoning supported by point/endpoint tests, not an independent rendered-curve collision oracle. Tests do not exhaust all schemas, cap/pass-limit outcomes or jog candidate combinations.
- Only this section appended to `.agent/REVIEW.md`; earlier sections preserved. No application/test changes, staging or commits.

## Review: ER diagram edge bundling

Status: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS

Reviewed 2026-10-20 on `fix/license-key-input`, HEAD `f3bc981`. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK guidance, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, and previous ER diagram routing reviews. Scope: only the uncommitted changes in `src/components/diagram/diagramLayout.ts` and `diagramLayout.test.ts`, against HEAD. Ignored untracked `diagram-harness.html`, `src/dev/`, and `.claude/launch.json`. No P0–P2 implementation defect established.

### Findings

1. **P3 — `src/components/diagram/diagramLayout.test.ts:205-210`, `:251`: the oracle's whole-route exemption is broader than the actual bundle contract.** A shared source does not imply a shared bundle: with `A → B`, `A → C`, `X → B`, `Y → B`, the first relation chooses target B (three uses versus A's two), while the second chooses source A. Nevertheless, `bundled()` exempts every pair of their segments. Executed the actual oracle in memory with two routes starting at `(0,0)`: `[(0,0),(20,0),(20,100),(60,100)]` and `[(0,0),(30,0),(30,20),(20,20),(20,80),(80,80)]`. It accepts their coincident internal vertical span `x=20, y=20..80`, regardless of bundle assignment. This is a demonstrated false negative in the test helper, **not a reproduced production-router failure**. **Fix:** derive expected bundle identities independently from fixture relations/ports, allow whole-route overlap only within that selected bundle, and retain the existing common-attachment exception for endpoint stubs across bundles. Add a mixed source/target hub fixture and a negative oracle test using the coordinates above. The new source-hub test at `:432-457` also needs a target-hub counterpart: it currently constructs `hub → table`, so it does not directly exercise the strictly-greater target-use branch or final-trunk reuse.

### Assessment of requested risks

- **Different bundles / stubs / jogs:** successful allocation still filters out only the current bundle in vertical occupancy (`diagramLayout.ts:537-543`), checks both endpoint stubs against other-bundle stubs/jogs (`:548-567`), and checks jogs symmetrically (`:569-584`). Remembering a preferred trunk changes candidate order, not those acceptance checks. Every new passage records its complete vertical intervals, endpoint runs and optional jog (`:586-602`); extending a bundle therefore does not erase another bundle's reservations. Perpendicular line intersections remain allowed, as before. Same-side endpoint stubs from different bundles can share their common attachment run; this is intentional endpoint fan-out, not evidence that their internal lanes may merge.
- **Crossing-row reuse:** the immediate return in `takeGapY` reuses a row that was already reserved for this bundle in this column. Later allocations in either neighboring column inspect this reservation and cannot successfully take its row for another bundle. Reservations never move or disappear during a routing pass. Thus a newly added neighboring reservation cannot invalidate the reused row in a non-overflow result. Gutter passage checks still run when that crossing is reused. Another bundle's overflow fallback can collide; overflow never promised separation.
- **Oracle and visibility:** equality of a start/end coordinate is a useful test for legitimate shared endpoint stubs, but is not selected-bundle identity (finding 1). In successful routing, the mixed-bundle probes found no internal collinear overlap beyond those common endpoint runs. Shared strokes naturally have one visible stroke and overlapping hit areas; a shared trunk cannot identify an individual relation by geometry alone. Existing individual branches/endpoints and relation hover remain available, but pointer interaction on a shared trunk selects according to existing rendering/hit ordering. This is an interaction limitation of intentional bundling, not a newly established different-bundle routing defect. No browser interaction verified.
- **Cards, orthogonality, rounding and markers:** reusing a crossing row does not change its column's static free gaps. Reusing a vertical lane does not change its gutter bounds. Passage assembly still joins matching row coordinates with horizontal legs, and a jog remains entirely inside its gutter. Endpoint trimming still preserves `MARK_CLEARANCE`; bundle metadata does not alter rounding or marker positioning. Same-row self-loops still offset their target by 12 px; their bundle port is computed from the logical row before this offset. Accordingly, physical endpoint equality alone is also not a complete independent model of bundle identity. No new card-interior, diagonal, bounds or marker-clearance failure established; existing point/endpoint tests pass.
- **Demand / widening / overflow:** successful shared passages record lane indices actually used, so trunk reuse does not incorrectly count one lane per relation. New crossings reserve/count one row per bundle, matching actual shared-row use. Overflow still records virtual lane demand while drawing a bounded fallback on the last physical lane; different bundles may overlap there, and virtual reservations are not a collision proof for the returned geometry. This fallback predates bundling and remains signaled by `overflow`. Widening still reroutes from fresh allocation state, with four passes and existing caps. Neighbor blockers and jog-search failures make demand a heuristic, not a convergence guarantee. The rewritten distinct-row overflow test verifies reporting and card avoidance, not distinguishability after exhaustion; its `>=15` demand assertion is weaker than the former exact count. No new demand-accounting failure reproduced.
- **Determinism:** port counts are collected before allocation; source wins ties; relation span/index ordering, lane/jog iteration and first matching crossing remain deterministic for identical ordered inputs. State is local to each route call. Existing deterministic test passes. Inherited locale-sensitive table sorting/input-order dependence is unchanged.
- **Test strength:** 47 diagram tests retain geometry, emitted endpoint clearance, crowded-gutter, opposite-stub, seeded and overflow coverage. The new hub test verifies unchanged width and first-lane reuse for a source hub. It does not directly establish target-hub bundling, mixed bundle identity, reuse after neighboring-column allocations, actual cap/pass-limit behavior, or rendered shared-trunk interaction. Finding 1 is non-blocking because production checks and independent probes did not reproduce the illegal merge that this weakened oracle would miss.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx vitest run src/components/diagram`: **47/47 passed**, 7 files.
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`: **passed**.
- Read-only in-memory TypeScript/Node probes ran the actual router on **120 deterministic synthetic schemas**, each with 32 tables, six rows per table and 85 relations mixing source and target hubs. All returned `overflow === false`. Compared collinear point segments across independently assigned different bundles, allowing only common endpoint runs: **zero unexpected overlaps**, including **1,318 pairs** with a shared source or target but different selected bundles. Probe bundle identity accounts for the logical self-loop row before its physical target offset; an initial physical-endpoint-only classifier incorrectly classified that case and was corrected. These fixtures are extra evidence, not exhaustive proof or committed regression coverage.
- A separate **112-table / 56-relation incoming target-hub** fixture returned `overflow === false` and retained width **4040**, equal to its initial layout. This isolates target-hub reuse; it is not the supplied real 112-table/147-relation schema.
- Executed the existing oracle on the synthetic overlapping routes quoted in finding 1; it accepted them, confirming the test blind spot.
- Commands ran through `rtk proxy`; no network calls. No full-suite run, emitting build, browser/desktop inspection, SVG rasterization, customer-schema execution, performance benchmark or memory profile. Card/curve and marker conclusions combine existing tests with static geometry reasoning; no independent rendered-curve collision checker was run.
- Only this section appended to `.agent/REVIEW.md`. Application/test files and temporary harness files left untouched; no staging or commits.

## Re-review: ER diagram edge bundling round 2

Status: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS

Reviewed 2026-10-20, HEAD `f3bc981`. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK guidance, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, and the previous edge-bundling review. Reviewed `git diff HEAD -- src/components/diagram/diagramLayout.ts src/components/diagram/diagramLayout.test.ts`, including staged and unstaged changes. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`, `diagram-harness.html`, and `src/dev/` as requested.

### Previous finding disposition

- **Previous P3 test-oracle exemption — CLOSED.** `diagramLayout.test.ts:193-225` derives expected bundle identities from fixture relations, logical card rows and attachment sides, counts source and target ports separately, and selects target only when strictly busier (source wins ties). It reads no allocator bundle identifiers, reservations or preferred lanes. `:241-242`, `:281-282` exempt whole-route overlap only for equal non-null expected bundles; the separate common-attachment exemption remains limited to endpoint runs, including both attachments of a single-segment route.
- `diagramLayout.test.ts:291-321` uses the previous review's exact overlapping coordinates: different bundles sharing a start must throw; one bundle may share the internal vertical span. The target-hub counterpart at `:526-554` checks no overflow, unchanged width, geometry, distinguishability and shared last lane per entry side. The mixed source/target fixture at `:556-574` exercises both selection roles; an independent execution confirmed two pairs sharing a physical start while belonging to different expected bundles.

### New P0-P3 findings

None. No new actionable defect or regression established in the reviewed two-file diff.

### Oracle and regression assessment

- **Logical self-loop rows:** expected target identity uses `expectedRowY`, never the physically shifted `endY`. This agrees with production port selection before `SELF_LOOP_DROP` (`diagramLayout.ts:802-863`). A read-only execution of the actual helper on three self-relations targeting one row confirmed that a same-row loop ending 12 px lower still shares the expected target bundle with loops arriving at the unshifted row.
- **Side derivation:** source side comes from actual `startX` against the card's right edge rather than copying production column-direction branches. Target side comes from `enterFrom`, which `assertRoutes` separately checks against the target edge. Boolean source-side and string target-side encodings are consistent within their separately namespaced port domains. This is an independent model of bundle selection for the validated attachment geometry, not a separate oracle for every side-metadata mutation. Existing same-column/last-column tests explicitly check routing sides.
- **Null routes:** the helper returns null identities without dereferencing missing cards and excludes those slots from port counts, matching production's missing-endpoint exclusion. Null identities never authorize overlap. A read-only mixed valid/missing-endpoint probe confirmed this. For valid-endpoint fixtures, `assertRoutes` requires every route before the bundle oracle runs, so an incorrectly missing route cannot silently weaken those checks.
- **Allocation:** different-bundle vertical spans, stubs, jogs and neighboring crossings remain blockers; preferred trunk reuse changes candidate order, not acceptance. Reused crossing reservations remain present for later bundles. Demand, bounded widening, overflow fallback, rounded geometry and marker clearance retain the previously reviewed behavior. Separation remains asserted for successful non-overflow layouts; overflow can intentionally share lanes.
- **Mutation evidence:** Claude reported 10 failures after forcing every relation into one production bundle. This is consistent with the revised independent oracle, but that mutation was not rerun here because application/test files were to remain untouched.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`: **passed**.

The probe's initial assertion adapter hit a cross-VM array-prototype mismatch; normalizing both assertion values corrected the adapter, with no repository changes.
