## Review: ER diagram WebKit clipping

Status: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS

Reviewed 2026-10-20 on `fix/diagram-webkit-clipping`, HEAD `9cbb30f`; merge-base with main is `3711200d97ccf2e5e62f49c35f3ed642d4767460`. Scope: uncommitted `git diff` for `DiagramCanvas.tsx` and `DiagramCanvas.test.tsx`, with router/export/CSS inspection. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK instructions and `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`. Ignored untracked `.claude/launch.json`.

### P0-P3 findings

- **P3 — `src/components/diagram/DiagramCanvas.tsx:703-706`: removing the mask exposes endpoint paint beyond the routed centerline.** Hover either endpoint table of a normal one-to-many relation: active particle paths at `:839-843` use 4.8 px round strokes plus a 3 px drop shadow (`src/styles.css:875-881`), so a particle approaching the card edge can extend 2.4 logical px into the card, with additional shadow. Previously the black card mask suppressed this. SVG is above cards (`DiagramCanvas.tsx:583`, z-100 versus card z-1 at `:136`), so card backgrounds cannot occlude it; 275% zoom magnifies the overlap. Crow-foot round caps also extend up to 0.675 px past the target edge (`:854-863`); idle crow strokes extend up to 0.425 px. This is a small border/endpoint visual regression, not a route through card contents or a blocker by itself. If clean card-edge occlusion remains required, trim visible animation endpoints by their painted radius or place visible relations below opaque cards while retaining the separate hit layer above; add rendered hover coverage. Do not restore a large animated mask merely to hide this.
- **P3 — `src/components/diagram/DiagramCanvas.tsx:703-705`, `DiagramCanvas.test.tsx:81`: hypothesis is stated as an established WebKit defect.** The new two-table DOM test establishes mask absence, not buffer clamping or per-frame mask rebuilding. Reword comment/test rationale as a workaround for suspected mask compositing until before/after WKWebView evidence exists; record OS/WebKit version, viewport, zoom, DPR and fixture size with that evidence.

No P0-P2 defect established.

### Safety and dependencies

- Router invariants: `diagramLayout.ts:664-679` returns a physical gutter lane on overflow rather than the out-of-bounds virtual lane used for demand accounting. `:735-745` shares a coordinate in an existing free gap. Overflow can merge relations but does not inherently put them through cards. The no-gap `desired` fallback is unsafe for arbitrary custom packed layouts, but normal `layoutDiagram` constructs positive card gaps and outer margins and only widens them (`:941-979`); no production-reachable card crossing established. The fixed-layout overflow regression already calls `assertRoutes` (`diagramLayout.test.ts:580-602`). Capped widening is not a universal separation proof, but caps do not remove card gaps.
- Same-column/self-loop routes remain beside the card; same-row self-loops offset the return by 12 px (`diagramLayout.ts:856-884`). Endpoint rows remain away from rounded card corners, including last-row self-loops; no new corner-interior crossing established. Rounded path curves stay in the local bend region, with gutter clearance 14 px, crossing clearance 10 px and radius 10 px (`:79-87`, `:322-365`). Existing tests check orthogonal point segments and endpoint stubs, not every rasterized curve pixel.
- Visible tick centers are 5 px outward, circle centers 9 px outward with radius 3.5, crow-foot branches lie outward and end on the card edge (`DiagramCanvas.tsx:86-108`). Ticks/circles do not reach the card interior at normal stroke widths. Edge coverage previously suppressed by the expanded 1 px mask is now visible; normal path butt caps stop at the edge, while crow caps and animated round particles have the minor overlap above. Card shadows/hover rings can also be painted over in the gutter; this is distinct from interior routing safety.
- Transparent hit paths/marks (`DiagramCanvas.tsx:590-702`) were already outside the masked group. Their 4 px paths, 10 px crow strokes, 12 px ticks and radius-10 circles can cover a small card-edge strip and intercept hover, but mask removal does not create or enlarge these hit areas. Visible paths remain pointer-events none.
- Search of diagram sources and `src/styles.css` found no surviving `diagram-rel-mask` reference or CSS mask dependency. SVG export independently calls `layoutDiagram`, draws relations first, cards afterward (`diagramExport.ts:33-36`, `:109-110`); it never used this mask. SVG/PNG export geometry therefore remains unchanged, with card occlusion differing from the canvas at endpoint paint.

### WebKit hypothesis and remaining causes

Mask removal plausibly reduces offscreen compositing/raster work for animated relations and eliminates that mask as a potential clipping boundary. Static inspection cannot prove that WebKit clamps this buffer or rebuilds it every frame; no browser documentation claim or measured root cause is asserted here. The old mask white rectangle followed the overscanned SVG viewport rather than the entire diagram, so total diagram height alone does not establish an oversized mask. Its default region and full-path object bounds also warrant inspection in a retained before-version if the cutoff persists.

Other unchanged suspects, not established new defects:

- `DiagramCanvas.tsx:539-552`: scaled overflow-hidden wrapper plus CSS `zoom` selected by capability test (`:73-84`); transform fallback uses `translate3d`/scale. Capability support does not establish correct WebKit compositing at fractional zoom/DPR. Check zoom 100%, min/max and fractional steps.
- `DiagramCanvas.tsx:582-587`: SVG still explicitly clips to its own positioned width/height/viewBox. A bad or stale SVG box could cut relations while HTML cards continue. Mask removal does not remove this clip or outer wrapper clips. `diagramViewport.ts:95-116` clamps the SVG to layout bounds, intentionally excluding paint outside those bounds.
- `diagramViewport.ts:22-44`: 420 logical px overscan versus 120 px position buckets normally covers the less-than-120 px bucket lag. `DiagramCanvas.tsx:293-324` coalesces scroll/resize updates into one rAF; fast asynchronous scrolling/main-thread stalls can outrun overscan until JS catches up. This can cause transient missing SVG regions, not by itself explain a stationary fixed-height cutoff. Shared card/relation culling is not proof of identical clipping: only SVG has the additional SVG viewport clip. No culling change is needed based on this diff.
- `src/styles.css:790-793`, `:866-892`: dashoffset animation, two filtered animated paths per active relation, round 4.8 px particles and drop shadows remain. Hovering a high-degree table activates all its visible relations (`DiagramCanvas.tsx:380-411`); expensive SVG paint/filter work can still stutter without a mask. Long individual routes retain full path geometry despite viewport-sized SVG clipping. No rAF loop drives the stationary animation; it is CSS-driven. Reduced-motion rules disable animation/filter (`styles.css:895-905`) and provide a useful diagnostic comparison.

### Tests, verification and limitations

- `npx vitest run src/components/diagram`: **52/52 passed, 7 files**.
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`: **passed**.
- `npm run build`: **passed**; warnings about browser-externalized `node:zlib`, ineffective dynamic imports and large chunks concern unchanged modules/build structure.
- New test asserts at least one idle relation exists and neither a mask element nor a mask attribute is rendered (`DiagramCanvas.test.tsx:81-106`). Useful structural guard against restoring this mask; it does not test active hover paths, endpoint painted bounds, zoom/scroll clipping, large fixtures, browser rasterization or frame time. Existing layout tests strengthen centerline safety, including self-loops and an overflow fixture, but cannot prove mask-free raster safety.
- No native WKWebView run, browser screenshot, before/after reproduction, SVG raster collision test or performance trace performed. Therefore clipping/stutter resolution and visual acceptability of endpoint bleed remain unverified. Recommended final validation: reproduce original large schema in the actual macOS Tauri build, scroll through the previous cutoff at multiple zoom/DPR settings, hover a high-degree table, compare filters/animation disabled, and inspect endpoints in light/dark themes.
- Only this review section intentionally appended; no application/test edits, staging or commits. Earlier review sections preserved.

## Re-review: ER diagram WebKit clipping round 2

Status: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS

Reviewed 2026-10-20 on `fix/diagram-webkit-clipping`. Scope: updated uncommitted diff for `src/components/diagram/DiagramCanvas.tsx` and `src/components/diagram/DiagramCanvas.test.tsx`, with shared CSS, viewport, and export dependency inspection. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK instructions, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, and previous WebKit clipping review. Ignored untracked `.claude/launch.json`.

### Previous finding disposition

- **P3-1 endpoint paint bleeding over cards — CLOSED by structural/static verification.** `DiagramCanvas.tsx:578` puts all visible strokes, marks and animated paths in the z-0 SVG before cards (`:749`); the cards remain positioned z-1 (`:136`). Endpoint round caps and particle/shadow paint now go behind card backgrounds/borders. Transparent hit geometry remains separately above cards at `:759`. No mask restored. Pixel-level confirmation remains a limitation below.
- **P3-2 unproven WebKit cause stated as fact — CLOSED.** `DiagramCanvas.tsx:573-576` explicitly calls the mask a suspected cause. `DiagramCanvas.test.tsx:81-117` asserts structural behavior (mask absence, z-0 visible SVG preceding a z-1 card), without claiming proof of a WebKit root cause.

### New P0-P3 findings

None. No new actionable defect established in this diff.

### Stacking, appearance and interaction assessment

- **Both zoom modes:** `DiagramCanvas.tsx:543-552` applies zoom/transform to the common positioned surface containing both SVGs and cards. Transform fallback (`translate3d` plus scale, including scale 1) establishes a stacking context. CSS zoom alone does not need to establish a stacking context for this ordering: the positioned surface paints its dotted background before its nonnegative positioned descendants, and the visible z-0 SVG paints before z-1 card contexts and z-100 hit SVG. No negative z-index can send relations behind the surface background. DOM order reinforces the intended ordering; it is not the sole protection. Both SVGs use identical svgBox position, dimensions and viewBox, so there is no separate coordinate transform or viewport to drift.
- **Opaque cards in both themes:** card root uses plain `bg-white`, not an alpha variant (`DiagramCanvas.tsx:136`). Global dark override is solid `#242424` (`src/styles.css:211-215`); the nearby 0.88-alpha override applies only to slash-opacity classes, absent here. Header dark fill is also opaque (`:225-229`). Row divider alpha does not make the root background translucent. Hover/active rules (`:819-825`, `:844-850`) change border color and shadow only, with no opacity or z-index change. Root layout/paint containment and clipping remain unchanged.
- **Markers:** circles are centered 9 px outside cards with radius 3.5; their nearest active stroke edge is 4.825 px outside. Tick centers are 5 px outside with nearest active stroke edge 4.325 px outside (`DiagramCanvas.tsx:48-59`, `:95-108`). Both remain beyond the 2 px hover ring. Shadows are translucent, so can dim nearby strokes but do not erase these marks; stronger dark shadows warrant visual acceptance testing. Crow branches end exactly at the card edge: border and hover ring now cover the cap and last part of each branch, while the outward 5 px branch extent remains visible. This is the intended card-edge occlusion, not evidence of a missing cardinality marker. Rounded corners do not introduce a new route crossing; routing/attachment geometry is unchanged.
- **Hit behavior:** z-100 hit paths/circles/ticks/crows retain their previous transparent paint, dimensions, titles and enter/leave handlers (`DiagramCanvas.tsx:759-879`). Parent SVG remains pointer-events none, descendants explicitly opt into stroke/fill hit testing. Visible SVG and its painted descendants remain noninteractive. Hit shapes can still intercept a narrow card-edge strip and switch table hover to relation hover; that pre-existing behavior is neither enlarged nor newly introduced. Splitting the visible layer does not change event ancestry through the surface or relation indices. At low detail, hit targets/idle marks remain gated by showDetails as before; active relations retain the previous rendering rules.
- **Cost:** one additional viewport-sized SVG root and its positioning/viewBox attributes; visible and hit geometry are partitioned, not duplicated anew. Existing relation traversals, active filtered paths, viewport culling and CSS animation remain. Removal of the mask also removes its per-card rectangles. No new per-frame JS or geometry computation identified. Separate SVGs may have compositor overhead; net speed/memory improvement is not established without measurement.
- **Consumers/export:** source search found no production dependency on first-SVG order or a surviving mask reference. The new test deliberately finds the visible SVG through its idle path. SVG export independently builds geometry and emits relations before cards (`diagramExport.ts:41-110`); PNG consumes that generated SVG (`useDiagramExport.ts:79`), rather than serializing a canvas SVG. Exports therefore remain complete and unaffected by the split.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx vitest run src/components/diagram` through `rtk proxy`: **52/52 passed, 7 files** (includes canvas, export, routing and viewport coverage).
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` through `rtk proxy`: **passed**.
- No emitting build rerun in this review; previous review recorded a passing build. Current validation is the requested no-emit check and focused tests.
- Structural test verifies idle-layer class/DOM placement and mask absence; it does not exercise actual stacking pixels, both browser zoom branches, active endpoint animations, hit testing through overlapping cards, shadows, or dark-theme rasterization. No native WKWebView/browser visual run, screenshot, SVG raster collision test, large-schema before/after reproduction, or performance trace performed. Thus original WebKit clipping/stutter resolution and shadow/ring appearance remain unverified. Validate the original schema in macOS Tauri at fractional/min/max zoom and both themes, including high-degree table hover.
- Existing SVG viewport/outer wrapper clips, asynchronous scroll overscan lag, and filtered-animation cost remain the previous review's diagnostic limitations; no new regression established here.
- Only this section appended to `.agent/REVIEW.md`; earlier content preserved. No other file intentionally modified, no staging or commits.

## Review: connection form UI, tag dropdown, empty credential defaults

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed staged diff (`git diff --cached`) against HEAD/main `707911d0d552a2243ea89df2317cff16e16e3f19` on 2026-10-21. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK instructions, and `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`. Included user UI polish and Claude changes; ignored untracked `.claude/launch.json` and formatting-only staged review/feature-map changes. Application files had no unstaged differences during review.

### P0-P3 findings

- **P1 — `src/components/modal/ConnectionModal.tsx:294`: search ref now targets a component instance and crashes the picker effect.** Opening New Connection renders `<Input ref={searchRef}>`; `src/components/form/Input.tsx:4` is a plain Preact function component without ref forwarding. Its ref is an `Input` component instance, not the native input. `searchRef.current?.focus()` at `ConnectionModal.tsx:162` throws `TypeError: ref.current?.focus is not a function`, aborting the same effect before initial engine selection is established. Reproduced using the actual Input component with its left adornment in Preact/JSDOM. Forward the ref to the native input (or expose an explicit input-ref prop), and cover opening/searching the picker with a regression test.
- **P2 — `src/components/common/TagInput.tsx:74`: combobox consumes IME candidate-navigation keys.** During composition, ArrowUp/ArrowDown now call `preventDefault()` and change the active tag option instead of leaving candidate navigation to the IME. Enter also executes tag selection without checking composition state; reproducing an in-progress `ni` composition with `isComposing: true` prevents ArrowDown and commits `["ni"]` on Enter. The old component already lacked an Enter composition guard, but interception of candidate arrows and suggestion selection are newly introduced. Ignore combobox actions while composing; cover composition start/update/end and composing Arrow/Enter events, including the native WebKit confirmation sequence where practical.
- **P2 — `src/components/common/TagInput.tsx:55`: duplicate typed text can select a different tag on Enter.** With existing tag `d`, type `d` again: `canCreate` is false because the tag already exists, while matches are `dev` and `production`; `onInput` highlights index 0 and Enter at line 86 adds `dev`. Reproduced callback result: `["d", "dev"]`. Previously Enter normalized the typed text and duplicate addition was a no-op. Keep typed Enter behavior unless the user explicitly navigates/selects another option; also prioritize an exact suggestion over longer substring matches when suppressing Create Tag. Add duplicate-prefix and exact-match-order regression cases.

No P0 or additional P3 finding established.

### Behavioral compatibility assessment

- **Removed defaults/helpers:** repository search found no remaining `defaultCredentialUser` / `defaultCredentialDatabase` references. Only form initialization consumed them; import/export and backend profile serialization do not call them. Existing nonempty saved values still win. Saved empty users now remain empty rather than being silently replaced with `root`; mandatory-user engines then disable Test/Save/Connect through `hasRequiredConnectionFields`, consistently with backend profile validation. This follows the requested empty-default behavior, rather than establishing a separate regression.
- **Build/test/connect:** `buildConnectionInput` preserves entered users and trims database strings; shared footer gates required fields. PostgreSQL/MySQL/MariaDB/SQL Server/Oracle/ClickHouse require users; Snowflake additionally requires database and warehouse. PostgreSQL maps empty database to `postgres`; SQL Server maps it to `master`; MySQL/MariaDB pass the empty string as `db_name(Some(...))`; ClickHouse HTTP omits an empty database, while native configuration receives it as an empty default database. Oracle constructs `//host:port/` when database is empty, so success depends on server default-service configuration; there is no application service fallback. Oracle's database-optional UI and that connector behavior predate this patch. Empty database is now the initial state intentionally; no live-server compatibility claim is made here.
- **Redis:** existing builder maps empty database to index 0 and does not emit the form user at all. Redis ACL-user omission and failure to initialize database from saved `redis.db` are pre-existing paths, not introduced by removal of `root`; they remain outside these findings. Mongo/Cassandra optional credentials retain their existing null conversion. SSH's independent `root` fallback is unchanged.
- **Profiles:** native import/export preserves profile inputs independently of frontend form defaults; normal save/connect validation remains in place. Editing a profile no longer manufactures root user/database for empty stored fields. No DTO/schema change or secret-reference migration is introduced. Imported profiles are not rewritten by these removed helpers.
- **Escape/focus/click:** TagInput stops native propagation on Escape while a visible list exists. The shared Dialog listener is document bubble phase, so it is blocked; no connection-form capture-phase Escape closer was found. New-connection form uses OverlayModal, which has no Escape listener; the engine-picker element handler belongs to a separate render branch. A closed/empty list permits Escape to propagate as before. Prevented option mousedown retains input focus through the subsequent click; blur closes the list. Existing tests cover these bubble/click cases, not real IME or touch ordering.
- **ARIA/shared UI/theme:** combobox has an accessible name, expanded state, listbox relationship and active-option IDs while displayed; list options stay out of the tab sequence. Field retains its prop interface, and form Input now merges both class/className. No additional shared styling/prop regression established. Global dark-theme CSS remaps the new list's white/slate backgrounds, text and borders; missing local `dark:` classes alone is not a defect. Existing Field labels remain visual divs, not newly broken label associations. The Enter tip is still present in staged TagInput lines 200-202, contrary to the handoff description.
- **Layout limitation:** list is absolute with z-50 inside OverlayScrollArea's overflow viewport, itself inside an overflow-hidden dialog. Z-index cannot escape that clip; short windows, many selected tags, and scrolled form positions need browser/native validation. No measured clipping defect is claimed without layout evidence. Keyboard activation also has no explicit scroll-into-view for long custom suggestion lists (the connection form currently supplies only four).

### Verification and limitations

- `npx vitest run src/components` via `rtk proxy`: **52 files, 377 tests passed**.
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` via `rtk proxy`: **passed**.
- Additional in-memory Preact/JSDOM probes against actual staged source reproduced the Input ref exception, duplicate-prefix wrong tag, and composing ArrowDown/Enter behavior. No test/application source files were created or modified for probes. Vite module loading emitted a sandbox `EPERM` warning when attempting its HMR listener on port 24678; module loading and the probes still completed successfully. These are DOM event probes, not native IME validation.
- No native app/browser visual run, screenshot, screen-reader session, live database connection, Rust test run, or emitting production build. Requested no-emit check and component tests passed, but they do not establish native IME ordering, clipping, touch selection, pixel-level dark mode, or server-specific empty-database behavior. Existing tests do not catch the findings above.
- Only this section appended to `.agent/REVIEW.md`; no application edits, staging, commits, or changes to the index.

### Previous finding disposition

Backspace can insert `local` before the selected `dev` without changing the selected identity; Enter resolves the current option by that identity (`:109`). None. No new actionable defect or regression established in the reviewed scope.

### Identity and compatibility checks

- **Collision scheme:** `optionKey` at `TagInput.tsx:10` is injective over `(create, tag)`: different kinds have distinct fixed prefixes (`create:` versus `tag:`); within one kind, equal keys require equal complete tag strings. Colons or prefix-like text inside a tag cannot bridge those prefixes. Suggestions are normalized and deduplicated (`:50-53`), and at most one Create option exists (`:64`); the same rendered list therefore has unique option keys. A create-to-suggestion transition for the same tag intentionally changes identity and safely falls back to the typed match.
- **Disappearing option:** static trace of `:72-79,109-110` confirms a missing key produces `activeIndex === -1`, disables explicit navigation, and highlights the typed match or nothing. Enter then uses typed input; empty input adds nothing. A surviving key retains its tag through insertion, removal and reorder. Missing-option fallback is assessed statically; no new dedicated runtime probe was run in this review.
- **Navigation/reset/ARIA:** arrows compute from current highlight and save the resulting key (`:96-104`); mouseenter uses the same key (`:200`). Choose, blur, handled Escape and input clear navigation (`:85,119,169,175`). List keys use the same identity (`:190`). ARIA IDs remain positional but are regenerated with the derived current highlight (`:161-164,191-193`), so reorder does not leave the active-descendant pointing at the old index.
- **Remaining diff:** inspected shared Input/Field changes, connection form spacing and labels, tag styling/icon/casing, empty credential defaults and removed helper exports/callers. ## Review: keychain card redesign

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-21 on `feat/connection-form-polish`, against HEAD `707911d0d552a2243ea89df2317cff16e16e3f19`. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK guidance, and `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`. Scope: complete tracked working-tree diff and untracked `src/lib/connection/profileSummary.ts`. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. No application edits or staging.

### P0-P3 findings

- **P2 — `src/screens/main/KeychainSection.tsx:262-264`: A-z/Z-a sorts hidden identifiers instead of displayed names.** With connection Alpha assigned UUID `ffff…` and Zulu assigned UUID `0000…`, A-z renders Zulu before Alpha. Provider IDs also need not match provider labels. The comparator is unchanged, but replacing the visible key-derived headings with owner names makes the existing sort control (`topBar.config.tsx:89-100`) visibly misleading. Compare display titles, with raw key as deterministic tie-breaker; alternatively explicitly label the control as sorting key IDs. Preserve raw keys for selection/actions.
- **P3 — `src/screens/main/KeychainCard.tsx:120`: raw identity is available only through a pointer tooltip on a non-focusable div.** Two orphaned database keys now have identical visible title/badge and no detail; a keyboard or screen-reader user cannot distinguish their raw identities from the card text before acting. Focusing the outer button does not reliably expose the descendant's native title tooltip. Provide the full key through an accessible description on the focusable card and a keyboard-accessible way to inspect it. Existing Copy name remains a workaround. Badge as ordinary text is appropriate; no special role needed.
- **P3 — `src/screens/main/KeychainCard.tsx:162`: violet AI icon lacks a dark-mode color.** `text-violet-600` remains the light-theme violet on the icon surface mapped to `#181818` by `styles.css:226-230`; unlike the slate text/badge colors, no violet override exists. This gives roughly 2.8:1 contrast, below the usual 3:1 graphical contrast target. Use an existing theme-aware icon color or a lighter dark-theme violet. Badge remains a redundant text identifier, so this is a visual polish issue rather than loss of the key type.

No P0/P1 finding established. P2 sort mismatch needs disposition; P3 items are nonblocking recommendations.

### Behavior, privacy, and edge cases

- **Extraction:** compared the extracted helper text against HEAD. `firstNonEmpty`, `formatConnectionTarget`, `EngineInput`, `getEngineInput`, and the complete `buildSubtitle` body are unchanged; only the exported function name/comment/import boundary changes. `ConnectionCard` still consumes the same `{ engine, subtitle, hasSsh }`. No change to target formatting, engine fallback, SSH indicator, or database filename handling established.
- **Privacy:** display reads profile/provider metadata, not secret values. No new secret fetch, log, analytics event, persistence, or network send is introduced. Hosts, database names, account identifiers and SSH usernames/targets are sensitive metadata but intentionally shown locally; AGENTS prohibits logging/sending them, not this requested screen display. ConnectionCard already exposes the shared connection summary. Passwords, API tokens, SSH private keys/passphrases and Google service-account JSON/path are not selected for detail. Existing explicitly opened secret editor behavior is outside this redesign.
- **Files/engines:** SQLite and DuckDB database paths pass through `formatConnectionDatabaseDisplay`, which strips both Unix and Windows directory components. Google Sheets uses `spreadsheet_id`, suppresses host, and does not select a credentials file path; the formatter only basenames SQLite/DuckDB, not arbitrary Sheets strings. Turso URL/account/host fields are displayed verbatim as in ConnectionCard; this is metadata rendering, not a sanitizer for credentials manually embedded in such fields. Unknown engine strings fall back to their raw engine badge and DbIcon's generic database fallback; no matching input gives empty detail. Missing `input` is guarded. Valid empty/whitespace labels use provider-kind or unnamed-connection fallbacks; malformed null labels/models remain outside typed contracts.
- **Unused detection:** profile UUID comparison is case-insensitive in both directions. AI lookup first honors exact `apiKeyRef`, then exact case-sensitive provider ID, matching backend `key_ref(id)`/save behavior in `ai/provider_store.rs:44,67`. Thus a null ref can still match a live provider ID; a differing provider ref can also match by ID. This fallback predates the diff and backend canonicalizes saved refs from IDs. Connection unused means no matching owner profile, not proof that a surviving profile currently needs that secret: a stale SSH key whose profile no longer has SSH is still marked used. Owner-existence semantics are unchanged, not newly introduced validation of actual secret consumption. Failed or pending owner-list loads can temporarily label keys unused; existing loading behavior remains.
- **Identity/search:** raw key remains map/list key, selected identity and action argument. Search includes raw key, title, badge and detail, case-insensitively. New host/model/type searches follow directly from those fields; no secret-value search introduced. Generic keys retain last path-segment title behavior; redundant single-segment detail is intentionally suppressed.
- **Accessibility/theme:** Enter/Space selection and Shift+F10/ContextMenu handlers remain unchanged; the existing keyboard-menu test passes. Title/badge/detail become button text; DbIcon also contributes an engine alt, potentially repeating the badge. Slate badge background/border/text and icon ring have global dark overrides; violet is the exception above. Actual focus behavior, clipping, screen-reader announcements and rendered theme contrast were not exercised in a native session.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx vitest run src/lib src/screens` via `rtk proxy`: **83 files, 489 tests passed**. Updated display tests cover label/model fallbacks, orphaned owner IDs, uppercase key UUID, SSH target and duplicate connection names; card tests cover context menus on title/badge/detail and Shift+F10.
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` via `rtk proxy`: **passed**.
- `git diff --check`: **passed** before this append.
- No new test files or runtime probes created. Existing tests do not independently cover extracted summary across every engine, missing input, custom refs, filename privacy, search/sort ordering, or native tooltip accessibility; those conclusions use source inspection.
- No emitting production build, native/browser visual session, live keychain/database, screen reader, touch session or Rust checks. Requested test/type-check commands passed; these do not establish pixel-level dark-mode rendering or native accessibility behavior. Workflow calls for Claude's second security-review pass for keychain scope; not performed here.
- Only this section appended to `.agent/REVIEW.md`; no other file modified, no staging, commits or git identity changes.

Identically named orphan cards therefore have distinct text-derived accessible names. `:162` supplies an empty decorative image alt, removing repeated engine announcements. Compiling actual stylesheet in memory with installed Tailwind emitted the violet rule under `&:where(.dark, .dark *)`. Native rendered contrast remains unmeasured.

### New P0-P3 findings

None. No new actionable defect established in reviewed scope.

### Regression and edge-case checks

- **Accessible name:** sr-only content participates in the button's name; this is not an `aria-describedby` description. It appends `Key name: <full key>` after visible title/badge/detail, so UUID-based names become longer. For a generic single-segment key, visible title already equals raw key, so that string is repeated once. The existing descendant menu button also contributes its label to content-derived naming. These are verbosity tradeoffs, not evidence of missing identity, changed actions, or an unusable name. Exact full-name role queries would need updating; current title/badge/detail text queries and `Open menu` role query remain valid and pass. No native screen-reader session or computed-accessible-name assertion was run; new test checks title/textContent/empty alt, not actual announcements. Extremely long arbitrary custom keys have no new truncation in accessible text; usability at that extreme remains unverified.
- **Comparator reversal/ties:** descending swaps operands for the entire comparator, including raw-key fallback; reversing tie order is consistent and preserves comparator symmetry. Additional in-memory execution of actual transpiled helper confirmed exact ascending/descending reversal for 1,000 and 10,000 unique keys; equal display fields resolve raw keys `a,b,c` versus `c,b,a`. Input array remains unchanged. Current committed-source tests cover badge/detail ties but do not actually reach equal-display raw-key fallback, descending ties, or numeric ordering; the raw-key fallback/reversal probe supplements that gap. Locale order depends on runtime locale, as with the previous comparator.
- **Performance:** comparator does two map lookups and allocates two three-field arrays per comparison; no profile/provider scan or `keychainDisplay` call occurs inside sorting. Expected sort cost is O(n log n) comparisons with O(1) map access. A one-shot Node probe with synthetic mixed numeric titles measured approximately 30 ms for 1,000 keys and 294 ms for 10,000 keys ascending. Repeated localeCompare options/array allocation could warrant a reusable Intl.Collator/precomputed tuples if large-list latency becomes a requirement; no supported workload or native UI latency regression established. These are synthetic timings, not a WebKit benchmark. Memoization avoids re-sorting on unrelated state changes; search changes still trigger sorting. Existing display-map owner scans occur when keys/owners change, outside comparator.
- **CSS support:** actual stylesheet compilation also emitted sr-only's absolute positioning, 1px dimensions, clipping, overflow hiding and nowrap. It hides the raw key visually without display:none/visibility:hidden. Both new utilities are supported by installed setup; no theme configuration change needed.
- **Menus/remaining scope:** all right-click targets, Copy name, menu button and Shift+F10 tests pass. Event handlers and raw identity arguments remain unchanged. Rechecked extracted summary against removed ConnectionCard helpers and reviewed section heading/navigation/icon changes; no further regression established. No new secret fetch, storage, logging or telemetry introduced by fixes.

## Review: credentials grouping, sort label, nav tips, draggable assistant

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-21 on `feat/connection-form-polish`, HEAD `c6cd12be02ca84f3e982f798a3ad4c504973c740`. Read AGENTS.md, referenced RTK instructions and .agent/WORKFLOW.md. Scope: complete uncommitted tracked diff and untracked `src/lib/ai-assistant/launcherPosition.ts`, `launcherPosition.test.ts`, and `src/components/ai-assistant/FloatingAssistantLauncher.test.tsx`. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Application code and staging unchanged.

### P0-P3 findings

- **P1 — `src/screens/main/KeychainSection.tsx:276-288`: bulk deletion can destroy credentials belonging to live owners.** Profile/provider arrays initially equal `[]`; failed list requests also become `[]` at lines 226-233. Once secretsList finishes, canSubmit can be true while either owner request is pending or has failed. All corresponding recognized credentials then enter Unused, and the new confirmation incorrectly guarantees that no owner uses them. Clicking Delete unused can delete active database passwords, SSH secrets or provider API keys. The captured unusedKeys array also survives asynchronous confirmation: if owner loading finishes while the dialog is open, confirming still deletes those now-known-used keys. Track successful completion/error states separately from empty results, fail closed for destructive cleanup, and refresh/revalidate owner references after confirmation before deleting. Add pending-load, rejected-load and ownership-change-during-confirm regression tests.
- **P2 — `src/screens/main/KeychainSection.tsx:288`: batch failures are swallowed and invisible.** handleDeleteByKey catches errors (lines 163-164), returns no result, and clears error/message at every subsequent iteration (149-150). If first deletion fails and second succeeds, the batch ends with a success message and no failure. All feedback renders only inside the normally closed editor (431-440), so even a final failure has no visible report on the page. Return per-key outcomes, retain failed keys, aggregate deleted/failed counts, and show completion/errors outside the editor. Test first/middle/final failures and all-failed batches.
- **P2 — `src/components/ai-assistant/FloatingAssistantLauncher.tsx:128-134`: cancellation suppresses an unrelated activation.** onPointerCancel calls endDrag and sets suppressClick after movement, but a cancelled pointer does not produce the terminating click. The flag persists until the next onClick (282-285), so the next deliberate click or keyboard-generated activation silently does nothing. Scope suppression to the completed drag's click; cancel must clear drag state without leaving suppression armed. Test drag -> pointercancel -> click/keyboard activation, and pointerup without a subsequent click.
- **P2 — `src/components/ai-assistant/FloatingAssistantLauncher.tsx:106-118`: pointer capture failure has no recovery.** Capture is attempted only after a move already reaches the button; when capture throws, moves/up outside the button are not observed because handlers exist only on that button. Leaving it strands drag.current, skips final persistence, and re-entering later with the same mouse pointer ID can move it despite no pressed button. A fast move leaving the button before the threshold event reaches it has the same problem. Capture on down or install window-level move/up/cancel fallbacks, clean up lost capture, and require an active initiating pointer. Test thrown capture plus outside release and re-entry.

No P0 established. P1 blocks verification; P2 findings also need fixes.

### Additional behavior and edge-case review

- Grouping uses already sorted, search-filtered keys; preserves order within groups; omits empty groups; puts unused first in classification and last in rendering. AI/database/SSH/custom classification matches display icon and existing owner-existence semantics. The two added grouping tests cover all groups, order and empty omission. Existing display tests cover owner identity/fallback cases. Owner existence does not establish actual use of every old secret; this semantic predates the change.
- Confirmation uses native confirmDialog wrapper, warning kind, explicit Delete/Cancel and correct singular/plural count. Count and delete targets agree with the visible filtered unused group. Actual button text is `Delete unused`, not the requested description's `Delete all`; this avoids implying deletion of hidden search results. No extra per-key confirmation occurs inside the batch.
- Successful deletes functionally remove only their key from local state; failed deletes retain their card. No stale successful card established for ordinary sequential deletion. Batch uses a captured snapshot, with no owner revalidation (P1 above).
- Deletes are awaited sequentially. Each child toggles the same busy flag; there is no batch-owned lock spanning confirmation through completion. Do not infer an interactive render gap merely from adjacent false/true writes, which can batch. Existing card double-click directly invokes onOpen even when disabled, allowing a concurrent read to clear shared busy state during cleanup; batch-level guarding should cover handlers, not only button styling. No native concurrency test performed.
- Card dimensions and selection handlers match HEAD; only unused icon styling changes. Grid now has 1/2/3 columns. Sort labels share SORT_LABELS between trigger/menu; connection date modes remain available, credentials retain their existing name-only sort modes. Search/create wording matches requested copy. Status-bar tips removed; per-page nav tips added. Blue active ring has an explicit dark variant, and global ring overrides do not target ring-blue-300 or dark:ring-blue-500/50. No source-level nav ring defect established.
- Launcher remains a native named button; ordinary keyboard activation is preserved except the suppression finding. Alt+arrows prevent default and move/save 24px with aria-keyshortcuts supplied. Panel still uses default corner. No new focus management introduced; native screen-reader/focus behavior untested.
- Right mouse button is rejected. touch-none prevents browser pan on the launcher. onPointerDown does not reject a second pointer or nonprimary touch: another finger can replace drag.current mid-gesture. Add an active-pointer guard and multi-touch coverage alongside capture lifecycle fixes. Existing synthetic tests do not exercise native capture or multi-touch.
- Clamping handles finite oversized/negative offsets and resize, using measured closed-root width or 40px fallback. A viewport smaller than the button cannot contain the whole button; current clamp returns 8px offsets, potentially leaving it partially outside. Test explicitly accepts that fallback; no practical desktop minimum-window regression established. Resize correction is in memory, not persisted until a later move; next mount reclamps the saved value.
- Storage catches denied access, malformed JSON (including null via catch), invalid/missing/nonfinite fields, and write failures; returns defaults without leaking data. Finite out-of-range stored offsets are reclamped by mount effect. Persisted content contains offsets only. No new SQL/secret logging or telemetry introduced.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx vitest run src/lib src/screens src/components/ai-assistant` via `rtk proxy`: **99 files, 614 tests passed**.
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` via `rtk proxy`: **passed**.
- `git diff --check` before append: **passed**.
- New launcher tests cover defaults, synthetic successful drag + explicit click, small-motion click, saved restoration and Alt+arrows. Helper tests cover bounds, undersized viewport, persistence, invalid storage and denied storage. They do not cover cancellation/no-click suppression, capture failure/outside release, resize integration, multi-touch or destructive batch failure/load races. Findings above use source tracing, not live keychain deletion or newly written tests.
- No production build, native/browser visual session, screen reader, actual touch/pointer capture session, live keychain operation or Rust checks performed. Tests/type-check do not prove native dialog, dark-mode pixels, focus announcements or OS pointer behavior. Keychain workflow requires a separate Claude security-review pass; not performed here.

## Re-review: credentials grouping, sort label, nav tips, draggable assistant round 2

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-21 on `feat/connection-form-polish`. Read AGENTS.md, referenced RTK instructions, .agent/WORKFLOW.md, and the preceding review. Reviewed tracked application diff and all five requested untracked implementation/test files. Ignored `.claude/launch.json` and the existing `.agent/REVIEW.md` diff. Findings below are source-traced; no application files, staging, or commits changed.

### Previous finding disposition

- **P1, unsafe owner classification/confirmation snapshot: CLOSED for the reported scenarios.** Missing owner lists remain unknown and never classify corresponding keys as unused. Bulk cleanup reloads both lists after confirmation, aborts before deletion on rejection, and reclassifies every candidate against that successful snapshot. A newly owned key is skipped. Helper tests cover unknown sources, fresh ownership, and rejected reload. This is snapshot validation, not an atomic owner-store/keychain transaction.
- **P2, swallowed batch failures: CLOSED.** Per-key deleted/failed/skipped results survive subsequent operations; failed keys remain locally; one aggregate notice renders outside the editor with role=status. `cleaning` spans owner reload and deletion. Remaining interaction and reconciliation issues are separate findings below.
- **P2, cancellation suppressing next activation: CLOSED at source/synthetic-test level.** Cancellation never arms suppression; pointerup suppression expires on a zero-delay timer. Tests cover cancellation followed by click and pointerup without a terminating click followed by a later click. Native WebKit timing remains a verification limitation.
- **P2, capture failure/outside release: CLOSED at source/synthetic-test level.** Window move/up/cancel listeners start on pointerdown; capture is no longer required. Matching release/cancel removes listeners; unmount removes an active set; mouse moves with buttons===0 finish without moving further. An outside-release test verifies subsequent moves do not drag.

### New P0-P3 findings

- **P2 — `src/screens/main/KeychainSection.tsx:442` (also `src/screens/main/KeychainCard.tsx:114`): disabled cards still start reads during cleanup or single-key deletion.** `disabled={!canSubmit}` disables menu items, but card `onDblClick={onOpen}` unconditionally reaches `handleUseImportedKey`, which has no busy/cleaning guard. During a delayed cleanup, double-click a candidate: its read can complete and open the editor with a secret that cleanup subsequently deletes; the cleanup only clears selection and leaves editor key/resolved value intact. During a delayed single-key delete, another card read can finish first and set the shared busy flag false, enabling cleanup while that delete still runs; owner reload/regrouping does not serialize those operations. Guard operation entry points and the card double-click path, and ensure one operation cannot clear another operation's busy state. Add deferred read/delete/owner-load integration tests. This carries forward the prior review's additional disabled-card warning; the new cleaning flag alone does not close it.
- **P2 — `src/screens/main/KeychainSection.tsx:333`: cleanup assumes failed deletions leave their keys present.** Native `CredentialStore::delete` deletes branded entries first, then checks/deletes other service aliases and returns any error (`src-tauri/src/security/credential_store.rs:146-176`). Concrete case: an unused key exists only in the current branded service; that deletion succeeds, but legacy alias enumeration transiently rejects. Cleanup records failure and retains its card, although a subsequent successful `secretsList()` omits it. With no page refresh action, the list remains wrong until remount. Reconcile keys using `secretsList()` after cleanup, preserving the aggregate deletion outcomes and reporting refresh failure separately; add a partial-native-delete/relist regression test. Do not equate a rejected multi-store delete with no mutation.
- **P3 — `src/screens/main/KeychainSection.tsx:240-246`: successful owner retry leaves a false warning.** Initial AI-provider loading rejects and sets ownersFailed=true; a later providers-changed event reload succeeds via `.then(setAiProviders)` but never resets that flag. With profiles already loaded, unused classification works and the Unused group can appear beside “unused keys cannot be identified.” Track failure separately per owner source and derive the banner from current states; clear the recovered source's failure on success. The bulk notice also has no dismiss action and clears only on the next confirmed cleanup, so an old error remains after manual correction; provide dismissal or clear it when a subsequent relevant operation starts.

No new P0/P1 established. Previous four findings are closed as scoped above; remaining P2 findings prevent verification.

### Additional checks

- Ordinary successful cleanup uses functional filtering: successful keys disappear, failed/skipped keys remain, and unrelated local keys are preserved. No concurrent secretsList overwrite is introduced by the cleanup path itself. Partial native failure is the reconciliation finding above.
- Skipped candidates were selected from recognized unused keys and are rechecked against complete owner lists; “in use now” accurately describes owner existence under the existing classification contract. This does not prove every existing owner actually consumes that secret. Custom keys cannot enter the normal unused candidate list.
- AI-provider owner reload can move cards between groups while a single delete awaits; its functional key removal still removes the target from the current list. Regrouping alone does not resurrect deleted keys. The shared busy/read overlap is documented separately above.
- Listener references match on add/remove; unrelated pointer IDs are ignored. Non-left buttons and nonprimary touches cannot replace the drag. Completion saves the last computed position rather than waiting for a render. No persistent listener leak established. The short suppression timer is not cleared on unmount but only mutates its old ref; no state/storage side effect follows.
- `touch-none` remains on the native button: gestures starting there intentionally cannot scroll/pan; surrounding page scrolling is unaffected by this class. Tests do not establish actual primary-touch dragging or browser-generated clicks. Zero-delay expiry fixes stale suppression but synthetic immediate click tests cannot establish WebKit pointerup/click task ordering or delayed touch compatibility clicks; no WebKit defect asserted without native reproduction.
- Alt+Arrow uses the clamped current position, updates state, and calls saveLauncherPosition for each move. Storage helper round-trip tests pass; launcher test asserts resulting position but does not assert keyboard-written storage/remount. Ordinary arrows are ignored. Resize correction remains in memory until a later move.
- Sort trigger/menu use the same labels; grouping preserves filtered/sorted order. Nav tips, active-ring classes, user's status-bar tip removal, and user's history h3-to-h2 change show no additional source-level regression.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` via `rtk proxy`: **passed**.
- `git diff --check` before append: **passed**.

Batch tests cover middle failure and all-failed description; first/final deletion failures are not separately exercised.

Native dialog timing, platform click ordering, and actual store reconciliation remain unverified. Workflow's separate Claude security-review pass remains required for keychain scope; not performed here.

## Re-review: credentials grouping, sort label, nav tips, draggable assistant round 3

Status: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS

Reviewed 2026-10-21 on `feat/connection-form-polish`. Read AGENTS.md, referenced RTK instructions, .agent/WORKFLOW.md, and round-2 findings. Reviewed the uncommitted tracked application diff and all six requested untracked implementation/test files. Ignored `.claude/launch.json` and the pre-existing `.agent/REVIEW.md` diff. The user's `Delete unused` label and per-group text are intended changes.

### Previous finding disposition

- **P2, operation serialization: CLOSED.** `src/screens/main/KeychainSection.tsx:111-121` provides a synchronous ref lock, shared by set/get/delete/import/card-read and confirmed cleanup. Each successful acquisition reaches a `finally` release; validation returns precede acquisition, and cancelled confirmation never acquires. A refused operation never releases another operation's lock. Cleanup checks the ref before showing confirmation and acquires again after confirmation (`:341-351`), holding it through owner reload, sequential deletion, and list reconciliation. Card double-click is disabled (`src/screens/main/KeychainCard.tsx:98`); the section handler independently guards stale callbacks (`KeychainSection.tsx:235`). Successful cleanup clears a deleted editor key and resolved value (`:372-376`). The deferred-cleanup integration test establishes no double-click read during cleanup; the card regression test independently covers disabled double-click.
- **P2, reconciliation after partial native deletion: CLOSED.** `src/screens/main/KeychainSection.tsx:381-385` re-reads `secretsList()` after every completed cleanup, including rejected per-key deletions, while retaining aggregate outcomes. On relist failure it filters only confirmed successful deletions and explicitly reports that refresh failed. The integration test simulates a rejected delete whose key is absent on relist, verifies its card disappears while the failure notice remains, and exercises dismissal. This verifies frontend handling of a mocked partial deletion, not an actual native alias-store failure.
- **P3, stale owner warning and persistent notice: CLOSED.** Provider/profile failures are separate (`src/screens/main/KeychainSection.tsx:107-108`); each initial/event load clears its own failure on success (`:272-289`), and successful cleanup owner reload clears both (`:364-367`). The provider-change recovery test verifies warning removal. Notice has an accessible Dismiss button (`:430-434`) with integration coverage.

### New P0-P3 findings

None established in this re-review. No remaining round-2 blocker identified.

### Regression checks

- **Lock/state consistency:** only acquire/release write `busy`; only a successful acquirer releases. `lockRef` closes the pre-render interaction window even before disabled props update. `cleaning` adds presentation state without owning a competing unlock path. No early-return lock leak or read completion that clears another operation's busy state found.
- **Keyboard/editor actions:** Enter/Space on a card only select it (`src/screens/main/KeychainCard.tsx:101-104`), preserving existing behavior without reading secrets. Edit/Delete menu actions receive disabled state. Editor Load/Save/Delete pass both `disabled={!canSubmit}` and `loading={busy}` (`KeychainSection.tsx:589-615`); Button renders native disabled when either applies. Handler ref guards provide a second barrier. Selection can still change while busy; it does not initiate keychain I/O.
- **Initial import/owners:** initial secretsList owns the same operation lock. Owner loads only update names/classification, never the key array or busy flag. Unknown owner sources remain null and their keys cannot classify as unused. If one source resolves before the other, cleanup still requires fresh successful loads of both after confirmation. An initial import cannot later overwrite cleanup's relist because cleanup cannot acquire until import releases.
- **Cleanup error paths:** owner reload rejection prevents deletion and releases in finally; per-key failures preserve aggregate outcomes; relist rejection uses functional filtering of current keys and also releases. Dismissing feedback does not touch the lock. Owner revalidation remains a snapshot, not an atomic transaction across owner stores and keychain.
- **Remaining scope:** grouping retains sorted/search-filtered order and raw identities; sort trigger/menu share labels; navigation tips and status-bar tip removal remain consistent. Launcher cancellation, outside-release listeners, storage validation, clamping and keyboard movement retain the previously reviewed fixes. No additional source-level regression established.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` via `rtk proxy`: **passed**.
- `git diff --check` before append: **passed**.
- New component tests cover owner failure, provider-event recovery, rejected-delete reconciliation plus dismiss, and no double-click read during deferred cleanup. They do not directly exercise every handler rejection/release path, editor button interaction under lock, pending initial import/owner ordering, failed relist fallback, or editor clearing; those paths were source-traced here. No additional test/application files created.
- No emitting production build, native desktop/browser visual session, screen reader, live keychain deletion, Rust checks, or real WebKit/touch pointer run. Native confirmation timing, platform pointerup/click ordering, pixel layout and real multi-store reconciliation remain unverified. Production build omitted to keep this review non-emitting and confined to the requested checks. Workflow's separate Claude security-review pass for keychain scope was not performed here.

## Review: History page redesign

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-21 on `feat/connection-form-polish`. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK instructions, and `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`. Reviewed all six tracked changed files plus untracked `src/utils/connectionLog.test.ts` and `src/stores/connectionLog.test.ts`. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. No application/test edits or staging changes.

### P0-P3 findings

- **P2 — `src/screens/main/ConnectionLogsSection.tsx:338-341`: row Enter handling intercepts descendant buttons.** Tab to a saved session's “Open menu” button and press Enter: its keydown bubbles to the row, which prevents the native button activation and opens a new connection tab instead. Restrict the row's Enter shortcut to `e.target === e.currentTarget`; preserve descendant button behavior. Add a regression asserting Enter on the kebab opens its menu without calling `onOpenConnection`, while Enter on the row still opens once.
- **P2 — `src/screens/main/ConnectionLogsSection.tsx:337` (also `:414-416`): double-clicking Retry opens three tabs.** Each of the two Retry clicks calls `onOpen`; the subsequent bubbling `dblclick` reaches the row and calls it again. `MainScreen.tsx:220-234` always creates a fresh UUID/tab, so this produces three independent connection attempts. Stopping `click` propagation does not stop `dblclick`. Ignore interactive descendants in the row double-click handler, and suppress repeated Retry activation within the same double-click gesture. Test the complete click/click/dblclick sequence, including kebab double-click, rather than dispatching only `dblClick` on the row.
- **P2 — `src/screens/main/ConnectionLogsSection.tsx:245` (also `:335`): new day-heading and keyboard-focus backgrounds remain light in dark mode.** `bg-slate-50/60` and `focus-visible:bg-blue-50/60` have no mappings in `src/styles.css`; the existing mappings cover other opacity variants, not these selectors. Dark mode remaps `text-slate-500` to light gray, leaving day headings low contrast on a pale surface; a focused session row similarly retains light background with remapped light text. Add explicit dark backgrounds for these new states or extend the existing theme mappings. Verify day headings and focused rows visually in dark mode.
- **P3 — `src/screens/main/ConnectionLogsSection.tsx:179-190`: ARIA radios lack radio keyboard behavior.** All three buttons remain Tab stops and Left/Right/Up/Down never move selection. A screen-reader user encountering “Filter by status” as a radiogroup cannot use expected radio navigation. Use native radio inputs or implement roving tabindex and arrow-key selection/focus; alternatively expose independent filter buttons with `aria-pressed`. Add keyboard coverage.

No P0/P1 finding established.

### Correctness and regression assessment

- **Pagination/grouping:** collapse runs over the entire filtered list before `useInfiniteScroll` slices rows (`ConnectionLogsSection.tsx:106-121`). A folded run cannot split across pages. Grouping visible rows is safe: each subsequent render regroups the whole visible prefix, extending the existing day rather than duplicating its heading. Page size counts folded rows, while header/filter counts count sessions; this is consistent with their labels. Reset-on-row-count-change can return pagination to page one when new sessions arrive, matching the prior length-based reset approach.
- **Fold identity:** exact stored error, profile ID, outcome and local day are compared; active sessions are explicitly excluded. Distinct stored errors and open sessions do not fold. Folding is adjacency in the filtered list, not a maximum interval between sessions; two short sessions hours apart can fold if no intervening visible session exists. This matches the stated same-day/short-duration rule, but should be confirmed if “back-to-back” is intended to constrain elapsed gaps. Host/user/device snapshots are not part of fold identity, so only the newest snapshot survives when a saved profile changes; no new cross-device persistence path was identified here.
- **Timezone/DST:** local midnight plus calendar `setDate(-1)` avoids the fixed-24-hour Yesterday bug over DST. Group keys and labels use the same local-day calculation. Existing tests use local calendar constructors, but do not exercise DST transition dates or timezone changes. With no active session, `now` stops refreshing, so a page left open across midnight retains yesterday's relative headings until entries change or remount; this is a minor remaining date-refresh limitation.
- **Clear/persistence:** `clearLog` retains entries referenced by `activeByTabId`, preserves that map, and schedules the existing persistent save. `endSession` can still close retained entries and remove mappings. Persistent restore loads profiles first, hydrates entries/map together, then closes prior-run active sessions at `snap.savedAt` (`persistentStore.ts:271-290`); clear does not introduce a competing representation. Existing 300-entry clamping can evict active entries while retaining their mappings, but predates this patch. No new clear/hydrate corruption established.
- **Loaded/deleted profiles:** empty `knownProfileIds` temporarily disables Retry/open/show. Once profiles load, the memoized set and row props update; there is no cached disabled state. Persistent restore itself waits for profile loading before hydrating history. Deleted profiles remain non-openable. A profile-load failure leaves open actions unavailable, safely, without recovery feedback in this page.
- **Privacy:** changed presentation paths render stored hosts/errors and use native title tooltips. Clipboard write occurs only from the user-selected Copy error message action. No added console logging or analytics emission found. Existing stored error truncation to 500 characters is unchanged; “full text” tooltip means full stored error, not necessarily full original exception.
- **Menus/performance:** every visible row mounts a closed ContextMenu component and allocates items, but closed menus return null and install no global listeners; bounded at 300 stored sessions. No performance blocker established without profiling. Open menus are rendered as `div` siblings of `tr` inside `tbody`, which is invalid table structure; actual browser/accessibility-tree behavior remains unverified. The shared ContextMenu does not focus its first item or implement arrow navigation; Shift+F10 creates the menu but keeps focus on the row, requiring Tab navigation through intervening controls. A focused menu/portal integration would improve this path.
- **Other requested behavior:** column order and optional Device column match scope; address truncation retains 40% head/60% tail, with database in a nonshrinking span; onInput updates the connection select; failed error tooltip, status counts, exported limit, native confirmation, sidebar copy and tips are present. Long database/error layout still requires real viewport verification.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx vitest run src/utils src/stores src/screens` via `rtk proxy`: **34 files, 192 tests passed**.
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` via `rtk proxy`: **passed**.
- `git diff --check` before review append: **passed**.
- Component tests now exercise real rendering, Retry/copy, filters, deleted-profile guard, collapse and confirmation. Utility tests cover basic local day boundaries, formatting and fold exclusion. Store test proves retained active entry can close after clear.
- Missing focused coverage: 30-row pagination boundary/observer loading; fake-timer advancement of the 30-second refresh (the “keeps counting” test checks only initial text); full pointer event sequences and descendant key handling; Shift+F10/menu focus; radio arrows; rejected confirmation; profile loading transition; persistence save assertion and hydrate/close-orphan round-trip. Store test mocks scheduleSave with a disposable spy and never asserts persistence.
- Findings are source-traced, not reproduced in a running desktop/browser. No native Tauri confirmation/clipboard execution, screen-reader session, visual viewport/dark-mode inspection, runtime timezone-change test, or performance profile performed. No production build run: review used the requested non-emitting type check and tests, avoiding generated output. No Rust change, so no Rust checks run.
- Only this section appended to `.agent/REVIEW.md`; no staging or commits.
