- **Native keyboard/focus — `src/components/settings/AppearanceSettings.tsx:53-75`:** enabled native radio inputs share a name, retain browser Tab/arrow/Space behavior, and have no key handler overriding it. `sr-only` visually hides rather than disables/removes focus. The preview span follows its `peer` input under the same label, so `peer-focus-visible` targets the visible tile. Checked and focused rings have separate classes; focus adds a two-pixel blue-300 ring. This is source-level verification, not a real-browser keyboard or compiled-CSS check.
- **Accessible names/grouping — `AppearanceSettings.tsx:53-99`:** each wrapping label contains exactly one radio and its visible System/Light/Dark text; preview shapes contain no text or interactive descendants. Implicit label association supplies the radio name without IDs. The named `radiogroup` supplies a group label; it does not replace native radio semantics or require custom key handlers. It is compatible with these children, not a demonstrated harmful redundant role. The font and density selects now have explicit names. Descriptions are visible but not programmatically linked via `aria-describedby`; this pre-existing FieldRow limitation is not introduced here.
- **Multiple instances — `AppearanceSettings.tsx:63`:** simultaneous pickers without separate form owners would share the native radio group across component boundaries; a `radiogroup` div does not scope HTML names. Current source has one SettingsDialog call site (`src/screens/main/LeftNav.tsx:290`), and closed Dialog returns null, so no current collision scenario established. Use an instance-specific name before allowing concurrent instances; add a two-instance test then. Separate SettingsDialog local settings states are also not synchronized today.
- **Persistence — `AppearanceSettings.tsx:119-121`, `src/components/modal/SettingsDialog.tsx:65-87`:** the new handler still calls `update("theme", theme)`. That merges the authoritative local settings, applies the theme immediately, and the existing effect saves the new object through `saveAppSettings`. Radio DOM state is derived from settings; no second application state added. System preference resolution/listening remains unchanged. Font selection similarly retains update/apply/save behavior; the preview derives directly from the same `settings.uiFont`.
- **Dark mode — `AppearanceSettings.tsx:21-23,71-95`:** preview surfaces use literal inline backgrounds without remapped background utility classes, so Light remains light. Labels sit outside the previews on the dialog surface: existing CSS maps selected text to #e8e8e8 and other labels to #a3a3a3 over #242424. Unselected borders map to #353535, subtle against the dark surface; selected border remains blue-500 and focus ring blue-300. No pale-background/light-text mismatch established. Pixel contrast, selected/hover/focus combinations, and native forced-colors behavior remain unverified visually.
- **Fonts/WebKit — `AppearanceSettings.tsx:127-158`:** real optgroups preserve every prior option/value/order and remove the fake disabled option. Per-option font-family is a platform-dependent enhancement; native WebKit popup rendering may ignore it. The independent preview uses `getUiFontStack` on a normal DOM element and does not depend on popup styling. The Default option lacks an explicit own-font style, so its popup face may inherit the current application font; its value and preview still resolve to the system stack. No selection/persistence regression found. SQL editor wording matches the fixed monospace stack at `src/components/editor/SqlEditorPane.tsx:560-561`.
- **FieldRow/title/history — `src/components/settings/FieldRow.tsx:7-8,26`:** optional extra renders only where supplied and leaves existing children/description/class paths intact. Other callers need no changes. Dialog title text changes only. The History assertion matches the committed `Last 300 sessions (2)` copy and checks the fixture count without changing runtime history behavior.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx vitest run src/components src/screens` via `rtk proxy`: **77 files, 508 tests passed**, including all three new AppearanceSettings tests.
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` via `rtk proxy`: **passed**.
- `git diff --check` before append: **passed**.
- New tests verify radio order/initial checked value, accessible Light lookup and click callback, optgroup labels, separator removal, initial Georgia preview, and explanatory copy. They do not simulate native arrow/Tab/Space behavior, click the tile label itself, rerender after an update, assert font/density change callbacks, verify all radio names, test reset/reopen/persisted reload, or test multiple instances. The mock update does not change props, so the click test alone does not prove controlled selection or persistence. Recommend focused stateful interaction coverage when extending this component.
- No running desktop/browser, screen reader, or real WebKit inspection performed. Native radio keyboard/focus and popup font behavior remain source-assessed; jsdom event dispatch does not prove browser default actions. No emitting build or generated CSS inspection: review used the requested non-emitting type check and tests, keeping edits confined to this report. No Rust changes; no Rust checks run.

## Review: Settings license, privacy and shortcuts

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-21 on `feat/connection-form-polish`, against HEAD. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK instructions, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, and the preceding Settings appearance review. Reviewed tracked working-tree changes and all seven untracked source/test files, including `src/lib/license/keyDisplay.ts`; ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Appearance was already reviewed; checked the removed font preview and updated test without reopening that review. Only this section appended; no application/test edits, staging, or commits.

### P0-P3 findings

1. **P2 — Do not promise that query content is never sent — `src/components/settings/AnalyticsSettings.tsx:85-87`.** With detailed analytics enabled, running `--CUSTOMER_SECRET` followed by a newline and `SELECT 1` puts `--CUSTOMER_SECRET` into `sql_kind`: `detectSqlKind` returns the first whitespace-delimited token, not an allowlisted SQL category (`src/lib/analytics.ts:410-416`; `src/lib/tauri/query.ts:235,352-357,456,487-491`). The sanitizer preserves this token. Error events also forward free-form database errors through regex masking (`query.ts:379-383,497-501`; connection/runtime call sites listed below); unquoted identifiers such as `no such table: private_customer_orders` survive unchanged. The telemetry behavior predates this diff, and the older card also made a broad claim; this is not a newly introduced leak. However, the replacement categorical “Never sent” disclosure is still unsupported. Before shipping that guarantee, allowlist SQL categories and send structured error codes/categories instead of free-form errors, with regression tests for comments and unquoted identifiers. Otherwise replace the absolute guarantee with wording that accurately describes current collection. Merely changing copy does not resolve the existing AGENTS.md privacy gap.

2. **P2 — Missing display name must not identify an active paid license as Free — `src/components/settings/LicenseKeySettings.tsx:76,93-94,153`.** A supported state such as `{status: "active", plan: "pro", paid_license: true, plan_name: null}` now renders the green banner “Free plan is active on this device.” and a Free Plan row. Backend tier and display name are independent: `src-tauri/src/license.rs:338-346,374-379` accepts the explicit Pro tier, while `:823` permits a missing display name. `accountPlanLabel` ignores `plan` and delegates an absent name to `formatLicensePlanLabel`, whose fallback is Free (`src/lib/license/planLabel.ts:8-23`; `src/lib/tauri/license.ts:97-100`). Resolve known tiers before the Free fallback; when both tier/name are unknown, use an unknown-plan label and a generic active-license banner. Add component coverage for paid Pro without a name and active status with no plan metadata.

No P0/P1 finding established. No additional P3 change request.

### Security, privacy and regression assessment

- **License key visibility/copy:** acceptable as an explicit local account-management action within the requested scope. The full key already exists in the authoritative license store/DTO; the change does not introduce another persistence path, transmit it to telemetry, or put it into DOM text, title, aria-label, or an attribute. Copy reads the current rendered license state, trims it consistently with activation, and writes it only on user action. The clipboard intentionally receives the full reusable credential and may be observable by the OS/clipboard tools; masking protects casual screenshots, not clipboard access or a compromised webview. `maskLicenseKey` trims blanks, shows first eight/last four only above length 12, otherwise last four with an ellipsis, and hides the row for missing keys. Four-character-or-shorter inputs necessarily reveal all their characters; the helper is a display convention, not a cryptographic secrecy boundary. No evidence that production PLD keys have that short format. Clipboard rejection/absence is not handled and success is not announced; the mocked test verifies arguments, not successful native copying. Native failure UX remains a limitation rather than a demonstrated platform failure.
- **Trials versus paid:** `accountPlanLabel` prioritizes an unexpired server trial only when `paid_license !== true` and the distribution supports server trials; paid licenses retain their paid plan label. Existing `planLabel.test.ts` covers running/ended trials, paid plus trial, and unsupported distributions. The new component tests cover only named paid plans, missing key and inactive status, not trial rendering or unknown plan metadata. The existing `isActive` branch remains status-based; the label does not grant access or change Rust entitlement checks. Trial-only inactive-license states still follow the existing activation/trial-note path.
- **Analytics inventory:** traced every application caller of `trackEvent`, `trackEssentialEvent`, `trackAppLifecycle` and `trackScreenView`, and searched for direct PostHog/capture/identify use. Essential lifecycle events originate at `src/main.tsx:79-82` and `analytics.ts:379-405`; updater availability/install-start events at `src/hooks/useAppUpdater.ts:18-21,103-106`. Their explicit properties contain dates, runtime/mode, versions and generated identifiers, not account email or license state. Detailed connection events live in `ConnectionFormDialog.tsx:112-251`, `MainScreen.tsx:401-437`, and `useEnsureRuntimeConnection.ts:70-135`; they carry engine/mode/source/timings and free-form errors. Query events in `src/lib/tauri/query.ts:352-501` carry kind/length/timing/count/error. Navigation comes from `src/layout/index.tsx:45`, with synthetic screen URLs and scroll metrics in `analytics.ts`. No application caller intentionally passes `customer_email`, `license_key`, raw SQL as a complete query, or database names as dedicated properties. This is not proof that arbitrary error strings or SQL tokens cannot contain sensitive content; see finding 1.
- **Random ID and consent:** `readOrCreate(SESSION_ID_KEY)` generates a UUID with a timestamp/Math.random fallback and persists it in localStorage; `identify` uses that stable install identifier without email/key properties (`analytics.ts:38-45,63-69,339-341`). It is not the license device ID. Essential capture bypasses detailed consent, while detailed actions/navigation check it (`:253,261,362-374`). The switch does not turn off essential events, matching the new wording. “Always sent” still means attempted when analytics build configuration/key are enabled, storage/date conditions allow it, and delivery succeeds; installs/daily usage are not resent on every action. No new telemetry or consent-policy change in this diff.
- **PostHog IP/geo qualification:** a random identifier is pseudonymous, not a guarantee of anonymity or an exhaustive payload description. SDK-added device/browser/URL metadata and request IP handling occur beyond `sanitizeProps`; initialization has no global `before_send`/property allowlist or GeoIP suppression. Current official documentation says PostHog uses the request IP when no `$ip` is supplied, and project-level IP discard can still permit GeoIP processing before discard. Thus “never your email or license key” is supported for the explicit essential-event properties, not a promise that no personal/network data reaches PostHog. Recommend disclosing network/device metadata and possible approximate location, or verifying and documenting enforced project settings. The current text does not explicitly promise “only a random ID,” no IP, or no location, so IP omission alone is not a separate proven code regression. Sources checked through Context7 and official pages: [IP handling](https://posthog.com/tutorials/web-redact-properties#hiding-customer-ip-address), [data storage and GeoIP](https://posthog.com/docs/privacy/data-storage#discarding-ip-addresses-at-project-level). Actual hosted project settings were not accessible or verified; do not infer that IP/geo are disabled from SDK `ip: false` defaults.
- **Shortcut formatting/callers:** normalization, storage and `matchesShortcut` still use canonical bindings, never display labels. All `formatShortcutLabel` consumers are presentation/error messages: KeyboardSettings, AppHeader/NavigationTabs context menus, SnippetLibraryPanel, SnippetEditorDialog and SnippetPickerDialog. ContextMenu renders shortcut strings directly; no label splitting, parsing or comparison found. New macOS modifier sorting produces `⌃⌥⇧⌘` immediately before the key; Windows/Linux form is unchanged. Named keys remain literal (`⌘Enter`, `⌘ArrowLeft`, `⌘Escape`, `⌘F1`), retaining the existing normalization behavior rather than converting to glyphs. No new functional key-matching regression established. New formatter tests cover common letters/modifier order and non-Mac formatting, but not named keys or every caller.
- **Reset/focus/confirmation:** change detection normalizes both bindings against the existing defaults; reset actions still write the same authoritative store and localStorage. Button forwards `disabled`, `aria-hidden` and `tabIndex`; unchanged placeholders are disabled, invisible and `tabIndex=-1`, with no focusable descendants. The containing FieldRow/div is not focusable, and the sibling record button remains outside the hidden subtree, so no aria-hidden focusable-descendant defect established. A changed reset becomes hidden/disabled after activation; real focus restoration remains untested. Reset all stays outline/disabled at zero, reports singular/plural changed count, uses the native wrapper with explicit Reset/Cancel labels, cancels without resetting, and clears recording/error on acceptance. Resetting already-default entries has no semantic change, so “changed shortcuts” matches the operation.
- **Appearance follow-up:** independent font preview is removed; option font styles and optgroups remain. Updated test now checks Georgia's option style instead of a preview. Prior review statements about an independent preview no longer apply. No other new Appearance regression established. History assertion remains aligned with the existing “Last 300 sessions (2)” copy.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx vitest run src/components src/stores src/lib src/screens` via `rtk proxy`: **152 files, 955 tests passed**.
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` via `rtk proxy`: **passed**.
- `git diff --check` before report append: **passed**.
- Read-only Node probe transpiled the actual sanitizer helpers from `src/lib/analytics.ts` in memory: unquoted database/table identifiers, `--CUSTOMER_SECRET`, and a synthetic unquoted email error survive unchanged. No actual customer data, network capture, or database write used. SQL comment-to-kind path was source-traced, not executed against a database.
- New UI tests mock license/shortcut state, clipboard and confirmation. They do not prove store persistence/rerender after per-row reset, reset-all plural counts, real focus after controls become disabled, native clipboard failures, actual Tauri dialog behavior, or PostHog wire payloads. Analytics test checks ordering/copy, not collection guarantees or switch persistence.
- No desktop/browser, screen-reader or real WebKit inspection performed. No emitting frontend build run, to keep this review confined to the requested tests/type check and report-only edits; generated CSS/visual behavior remain unverified. No Rust changes/checks. The workflow's subsequent Claude security-review pass for license-sensitive changes remains outstanding; this report does not replace it.

## Re-review: Settings license, privacy and shortcuts round 2

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-21 on `feat/connection-form-polish`. Read `AGENTS.md`, its referenced RTK instructions, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, and the preceding Settings license/privacy/shortcuts review. Reviewed the uncommitted application diff against HEAD and all eight untracked source/test files, including `src/lib/license/keyDisplay.ts` and `src/lib/analytics.sqlKind.test.ts`. Ignored `.claude/launch.json` and the pre-existing `.agent/REVIEW.md` diff. Only this section appended; no application/test edits or git staging.

### Previous finding disposition

- **P2, plan label: CLOSED.** `src/lib/license/planLabel.ts:25` now resolves a nonblank display name, then the backend tier, before the existing Free fallback. `knownPlanLabel` returns null without a name, tier or eligible running trial. `src/components/settings/LicenseKeySettings.tsx:93-96,153-155` consequently renders the generic active-license banner and Plan `Unknown` for active status with no plan metadata. Added helper/component tests cover paid Pro without a name and active status with no metadata. Existing paid/trial, expired-trial and unsupported-distribution tests pass. A read-only probe of the actual helpers confirms `{plan: "free", plan_name: null, paid_license: false, trial: null}` produces `Free plan`; LeftNav still uses `accountPlanLabel`, preserving the account-menu fallback.
- **P2, privacy disclosure: PARTIALLY FIXED / OPEN.** The comment-to-`sql_kind` leak is closed: `detectSqlKind` now returns only allowlisted keywords, OTHER or UNKNOWN, including for mixed leading line/block comments. The new tests exercise the original secret-comment case. Acknowledging unquoted table/column names improves the disclosure, but the remaining absolute guarantees still exceed what the unchanged sanitizer enforces; see the carried-forward P2 below. The requested deferral of free-form error telemetry redesign is respected: the required change here can be confined to accurate disclosure.

### P0-P3 findings

1. **P2 — Qualify the remaining “Never sent” and complete-masking claims — `src/components/settings/AnalyticsSettings.tsx:85-90`.** With detailed analytics enabled, connection and query errors still reach `trackEvent` as free-form strings (`src/components/connection/ConnectionFormDialog.tsx:119-124,160-165,246-251`; `src/hooks/useEnsureRuntimeConnection.ts:81-85,131-135`; `src/lib/tauri/query.ts:379-383,497-501`). `sanitizeErrorMessage` only applies three regexes, takes the first line and truncates to 160 characters (`src/lib/analytics.ts:91-104`). It has no general email, credential, host, username or row-value redaction. For a concrete error-input scenario, `Connection refused at db.internal:5432 for user alice@example.invalid` survives byte-for-byte; so does `Failing row contains (alice@example.invalid, confidential_value).`. If a driver/server supplies those strings, the existing call sites send them despite “Never sent ... results ... your email.” Similarly, `Server error: SHOW private_customer_orders` survives: the SQL regex recognizes only select/insert/update/delete/alter/create/drop/truncate, not every SQL statement. These are synthetic probes of the actual helpers, not claims of a reproduced live-driver response or observed production leak. Ordinary MySQL-style quoted user/host strings _are_ masked; the issue is the guarantee across arbitrary error formats. A warning limited to table/column names does not explain this broader residual content. Replace the categorical claim with the actual boundary: query text/results and account credentials are not deliberately attached as event fields; error text is truncated and heuristically masked and can retain connection details, identifiers or other sensitive text. Describe SQL/path/name masking as best effort. This closes the disclosure issue without changing the deferred error-telemetry policy.

No new P0/P1/P3 finding established. The P2 above is the unresolved portion of the previous privacy finding, not a newly introduced telemetry leak.

### Regression and collection assessment

- Query telemetry continues to include bounded statement kind, SQL character length, duration and, on success, row count. Connection telemetry includes engine, duration and operation/source metadata. These are query/connection metadata, distinct from attaching complete SQL or result rows. The current “connection and query actions” description is broad but compatible with those fields; enumerating them would make the disclosure clearer.
- All production `detectSqlKind` references are in `src/lib/tauri/query.ts`; the computed kind is used only in event properties. Execution, transaction selection, retries, timeout/cancellation and SQL history do not branch on it. New classification cannot change query execution. This is an analytics classifier, not a dialect parser: nested block comments, MySQL `#` comments, CR-only line endings and identifiers beginning with allowlisted words may yield approximate categories, but output stays bounded. No extra privacy finding or execution regression from those cases.
- `knownPlanLabel` affects only the Settings display. Account menu retains its Free fallback, trial precedence still excludes paid licenses, and no entitlement/store/backend state changes. New tests do not explicitly cover every combination of unknown metadata and trial support; source inspection and existing account-plan tests support the unchanged precedence.
- License-key masking/copy and shortcut changes retain the previous review's assessment. Copy remains explicit, local and full-key; no new persistence or telemetry path. Canonical shortcut bindings, matching and storage remain unchanged; modifier glyph/order changes affect presentation only. Reset confirmation and hidden/disabled default-row controls remain intact. Appearance follow-up still removes the standalone font preview while retaining option font styles. No additional source-level regression established.
- Essential events remain independent of detailed consent and use the generated install identifier. No added email/key event property found. “Always sent” remains subject to analytics configuration and delivery, as previously documented. Hosted PostHog IP/GeoIP/project settings were not inspected in this round; this review makes no new claim about SDK/network metadata.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx vitest run src/components src/stores src/lib src/screens` via `rtk proxy`: **153 files, 962 tests passed**.
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` via `rtk proxy`: **passed**.
- `git diff --check` before append: **passed**.
- Read-only Node probes transpiled actual sanitizer and plan-label helpers in memory. Confirmed unquoted email/host/row-value text and a SHOW statement survive, quoted names and a simple file path are masked, and explicit free tier labels as `Free plan`. Used synthetic data only; no source/test file written, database operation or network capture performed.
- SQL-kind tests verify category bounding and leading comments, not complete SQL parsing. AnalyticsSettings test asserts copy/order, not the privacy boundary or wire payloads. License component tests mock store and clipboard; they do not prove native clipboard success, persistence or desktop behavior. Existing plan-label tests and broader suite pass, but no dedicated LeftNav free-tier rendering test was added in this report-only task.
- No emitting frontend build, running desktop/browser, screen reader, native dialog/WebKit inspection, live driver-error reproduction, actual telemetry capture or Rust checks performed. Review used the requested tests/non-emitting type check and source tracing. The workflow's separate Claude security-review pass for license-sensitive scope remains outstanding; this report does not replace it.

## Review: Settings follow-ups (deactivate confirm, Cmd+comma, error categories, polish)

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-21 on `feat/connection-form-polish`. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK instructions, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, and the preceding Settings review rounds through “Re-review: Settings license, privacy and shortcuts round 3”. Scope: full `git diff HEAD` for tracked source/tests (staged and unstaged together), plus untracked `src/lib/analytics.errors.test.ts`, `src/lib/license/confirmDeactivate.ts`, and `src/lib/settings/`. Ignored `.claude/launch.json` and the pre-existing `.agent/REVIEW.md` diff. Earlier appearance, plan-label, masked-key and privacy-wording work received regression assessment. Only this section appended; no application edits, staging or commits.

### P0-P3 findings

1. **P2 — Reserve the Settings binding before accepting custom shortcuts — `src/components/settings/KeyboardSettings.tsx:58-69`, `src/layout/index.tsx:61-67`.** Concrete scenario: record Cmd+, (Ctrl+, outside macOS) for “Close current tab” or “Save changes”. `keyboardEventToShortcut` returns `Mod+,`; `usedBindings` contains only the six configurable actions, so recording accepts and persists it without conflict. After returning to a connection, the new unconditional Settings listener and the existing connection/editor listeners all match that same key. Settings navigates home while the old action can also close a tab or start saving. `preventDefault` and the other listeners' `stopPropagation` do not arbitrate listeners on the same window. Reserve this chord in recording and stored-binding validation (including already stored bindings), or register Settings in a common conflict-aware dispatcher with exclusive handling. Add a regression covering an attempted assignment and a restored conflicting binding. Recording itself correctly stops the window bubble listener for that keystroke; the defect is accepting a binding that conflicts on subsequent use.

2. **P2 — Coordinate existing modals before opening Settings — `src/layout/index.tsx:62-65`, `src/screens/main/LeftNav.tsx:93-97`.** Concrete scenario: open New Group, type a draft name, then press Cmd+, / Ctrl+,. The listener opens Settings without checking or coordinating the existing dialog. `MainScreen.tsx:577-580` renders New Group after LeftNav's Settings dialog (`LeftNav.tsx:318-322`); both use the shared `Dialog` overlay with `z-1000` (`Dialog.tsx:94`), so New Group remains on top of the newly opened Settings. Both retain `aria-modal=true` and independent document Escape listeners (`Dialog.tsx:69-79`): Escape dismisses both and the group dialog resets its draft. The shortcut therefore fails to visibly open Settings in this reachable workflow and creates conflicting modal/keyboard state. Introduce an explicit modal policy: defer/decline the Settings request while another blocking modal is active, or use a managed stack that preserves the underlying draft and routes focus/Escape only to the top dialog. Test opening from an existing modal and closing only the intended dialog. Do not simply close unsaved forms to make room.

No P0/P1 finding established. No separate P3 blocker. Both P2s concern the newly global shortcut, not the previously verified presentation changes.

### Privacy/security assessment

- **Error text boundary:** string properties whose keys contain `error` now return a fixed `ERROR_KINDS` category or `other`, never any matched substring. Ordered regexes can misclassify a message containing several indicators, but cannot copy its text into the category. Keys caught by the earlier sensitive-key branch instead produce the fixed `[masked]` token, also without free text. Synthetic probes confirmed unquoted host/email input becomes `other` and SQL-containing error text becomes `other`. Numeric `error_count: 3` remains `3`; numbers/booleans/null are not error messages and should not be converted to a category. No production `error_count` caller found.
- **Other strings remain a best-effort boundary, not a universal privacy guarantee.** `sanitizeProps({ message: "alice@example.invalid at db.internal", reason: ..., source: ... })` preserves that synthetic content. The generic string branch still uses the previous regex masking/truncation. Current production event callers were traced: error payloads use `error`; source values are fixed `auto_restore`, `manual_reload` or `privacy_dialog`; engine/mode/runtime and update/lifecycle fields are controlled metadata. No current caller attaching user text as `message`, `reason` or `source` was found, so no reachable new leak is asserted. Any future free-form field needs explicit allowlisting/classification; the exported sanitizer alone does not guarantee that arbitrary user content is removed.
- **SQL kinds:** `sql_kind` now passes only exact `SQL_KINDS` entries or `UNKNOWN`; other strings become `OTHER`. Query text, comments and identifiers cannot pass through that field. `detectSqlKind` remains analytics-only; execution semantics are unchanged. Its approximate comment/token parsing can affect category accuracy, not disclose an arbitrary token.
- **Screen paths bypass `sanitizeProps`** in `$pageview`/`$pageleave`; current layout supplies `main` or locally generated `tab-<uuid>` IDs, not profile/database names. Traced tab creation in ConnectionFormDialog and MainScreen. No current user-content path established. `trackScreenView` is not intrinsically safe for an arbitrary string ID; future callers must retain this invariant.
- Detailed telemetry consent remains enforced; essential events retain their existing independent behavior. Updated privacy wording matches the current explicit caller payloads and categorical errors. This does not establish anything about hosted PostHog settings or SDK/network metadata. No new SQL, credentials, license key or database-name event attachment found.

### Regression, UX and compatibility assessment

- **Deactivate:** both Settings and LicenseDialog await the same `confirmDeactivateLicense`; Cancel skips deactivation and confirmation precedes the store action. Shared helper supplies warning, consequence, Deactivate and Cancel labels without key/email content. Settings component test covers false/true responses. No entitlement, keychain or backend deactivation logic changed. Native focus/default-button behavior, dialog rejection and repeated activation while confirmation is pending were not exercised; LicenseDialog has no equivalent new cancel/confirm interaction test. Browser fallback uses `window.confirm` and ignores object-option titles/button labels, as before.
- **Shortcut routing:** ordinary inputs do not suppress the global chord. Repeated opening while Settings is already open does not toggle it closed. Account menu is closed by the request consumer. Keyboard recording uses a capture listener with `stopPropagation`, which a jsdom propagation probe confirmed prevents the window bubble Settings listener. Monaco may consume key events before window bubbling; no real Monaco/WebKit test was performed, so “any screen” is not runtime-proven inside the editor. Configurable actions/snippet bindings need the conflict policy in finding 1.
- **Pending request:** a boolean coalesces repeated requests and is consumed once on LeftNav mount/event. MainLayout switches synchronously to `main`; MainScreen renders LeftNav unconditionally. No normal successful render path where LeftNav never mounts was found. If rendering fails before mount, the flag remains until a later mount in the same module lifetime, potentially opening Settings late; it does not persist across reload or grow without bound. No separate production failure established.
- **Distribution:** shortcut/Settings availability is not purchase-gated and remains available in App Store builds. `websiteLinks` is true for direct and false for appstore; privacy URL rendering follows it, compatible with the existing native support-only App Store external-URL policy. Feature-map tests cover both flavors; no actual App Store binary was exercised.
- **Dates:** actual helper probes returned empty for null/undefined/empty/invalid values; ISO UTC and equivalent millisecond input produced the same local en-US medium date/short time. Local timezone remains in effect and time is now 12-hour with AM/PM on en-US. Epoch `0` still returns empty because of the pre-existing falsy guard; numeric seconds/numeric strings were not newly normalized. No changed caller requires epoch-zero or second-based input. Renewal/trial tests passed; no dedicated formatter locale/DST suite added.
- **Earlier UI:** appearance radio/font/density updates, known/unknown plan fallback, shortened key display, trial/renewal precedence and colon-free labels show no source/test regression. ConnectionLogsSection test change updates its existing text expectation only. Active Settings navigation now exposes `aria-current`; shortcut edit controls have accessible names, hover/focus icon and recording help; error banner uses `role=alert`. Fixed dialog height retains independently scrollable nav/content.
- **Accessibility limits:** the existing shared Dialog has no focus transfer/trap/restore or accessible title association. New global opening does not add those: from a main-screen input, focus can remain behind Settings. This is a carried/shared dialog limitation amplified by keyboard entry; modal stacking is the concrete new finding above. Contrast, small-window scrolling, zoom, focus order and screen-reader announcements were not visually/runtime assessed.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx vitest run src` via `rtk proxy`: **168 files, 1,076 tests passed**.
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` via `rtk proxy`: **passed**.
- `git diff --check` before append: **passed**.
- Read-only Node probes transpiled the actual analytics, shortcut and date helpers in memory, using synthetic input only; jsdom probe checked capture/bubble propagation. No source/test files written for probes, no database actions or telemetry network requests.
- Existing tests do not integrate MainLayout + LeftNav + Monaco, conflicting stored bindings, multiple simultaneous dialogs, real native confirm behavior, or privacy-link rendering in both build flavors. Findings derive from source paths plus focused helper/DOM propagation probes, not a launched desktop reproduction.
- No emitting `npm run build`, dev-server/desktop launch, screenshots, screen-reader/native WebKit inspection, live telemetry capture or Rust checks. Kept this report-only task non-emitting and limited writes to REVIEW.md as requested; requested full frontend suite and non-emitting project type check passed. Visual/native behavior remains unverified. The workflow's separate Claude security-review pass for license-sensitive changes remains required; this review does not replace it.

Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK instructions, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, and “Review: Settings follow-ups (deactivate confirm, Cmd+comma, error categories, polish)”. Ignored `.claude/launch.json` and the existing `.agent/REVIEW.md` diff. Only this report appended; no application edits, staging or commits.

### Previous finding disposition

- **P2 #1, reserve Settings binding: PARTIALLY CLOSED.** `reservedShortcutOwner` normalizes bindings, `setShortcut` refuses reserved assignments, and `loadShortcuts` replaces stored reserved action bindings with their defaults (`src/stores/keyboardShortcuts.ts:69-75,175-185,273-284`). KeyboardSettings refuses the chord with an alert before assigning it (`src/components/settings/KeyboardSettings.tsx:59-66`). Added tests cover assignment, stored action restoration and recording. This closes the six configurable-action collision, including close/save. It does not close the separate snippet-hotkey dispatcher; finding 1 below carries forward the remaining conflict.
- **P2 #2, existing blocking modals: CLOSED for the reported New Group/form case.** MainLayout checks `isModalDialogOpen()` before navigating or requesting Settings (`src/layout/index.tsx:63-69`); shared Dialog already renders `aria-modal=true`, and OverlayModal now does too. New Group, existing Settings, connection picker/forms and diagram overlays therefore decline the shortcut without closing drafts or creating a pending request. Settings itself is also declined rather than toggled or reopened. The helper test covers presence/removal of an aria-modal node, but does not mount MainLayout with New Group. Nonmodal menus remain a distinct reachable overlap, finding 2 below.

### New P0-P3 findings / carried-forward remainder

1. **P2 — Exclude reserved chords from snippet dispatch too — `src/layout/index.tsx:63-69` (remaining consumer: `src/screens/connection/hooks/useConnectionShortcuts.ts:78-82`).** Create or retain a visible snippet with insert hotkey `Mod+,`, open a connection/editor, then press Cmd+, on macOS or Ctrl+, elsewhere. SnippetEditorDialog still records that chord (`src/components/modal/SnippetEditorDialog.tsx:64-88`), the snippets store accepts it (`src/stores/snippets.ts:21-24`), and the connection window listener invokes `insertSnippetByHotkey` before its editable-target guard. `ConnectionScreen.tsx:666-674` resolves the snippet and starts inserting its SQL. MainLayout also opens Settings on the same event: neither handler checks `defaultPrevented`, and `stopPropagation` does not stop another listener on the same window. A read-only jsdom probe executing the actual hook with mocked hooks/actions produced one snippet insertion and one Settings request for one Ctrl+, event. SQL insertion is established; SQL execution is not asserted. Reject reserved chords in snippet recording/validation and skip reserved bindings in snippet dispatch so existing persisted snippets cannot collide. Add a regression for an already saved conflicting snippet and both platform chords.

2. **P2 — Dismiss or decline open context menus before opening Settings — `src/layout/index.tsx:67-69`, `src/lib/settings/openSettings.ts:31-32`.** On the main Connections screen, right-click a connection card, then press Cmd/Ctrl+,. ContextMenu has `role=menu`, no `aria-modal`, and closes only on Escape, outside mousedown or item selection (`src/components/common/ContextMenu.tsx:35-54,92-105`). Opening Settings causes none of those; MainScreen remains mounted, so the card's menu state survives. Its `z-9999` keeps the actionable menu above Settings' shared `z-1000` dialog. Selecting “Move to Group” can then open AssignConnectionGroupDialog alongside Settings (`src/screens/main/ConnectionCard.tsx:370-376,403-414`), recreating simultaneous modal/Escape state; “Edit Connection” similarly opens an overlay behind Settings. LeftNav explicitly closes only its own account menu. Coordinate transient menu dismissal before opening Settings, or decline the chord while an app menu is open; do not mark every nonmodal popover as modal. Add a main-screen context-menu + Settings integration regression. This scenario is source-derived, not desktop-reproduced.

No P0/P1 established. No additional P3 finding established.

### Regression and compatibility assessment

- **Platform comparison:** recording and matching share `keyboardEventToShortcut`: macOS Meta and other-platform Ctrl become `Mod`; macOS physical Ctrl remains `Ctrl`. Therefore `Mod+,` reservation works on both platforms and follows the primary modifier when stored settings move platforms. Literal stored `Ctrl+,` is not reserved, but also does not match Ctrl+, on Windows/Linux because the event becomes `Mod+,`; on macOS it represents physical Ctrl rather than the Settings Cmd chord. This pre-existing representation issue does not bypass the new reservation. Cmd/Command aliases normalize to Mod and are reserved. Opposite primary, Alt/Shift and combined primary modifiers do not match the Settings helper. No platform-specific reservation bypass established for bindings generated by the recorder.
- **OverlayModal structure:** audited every direct call site: ConnectionModal's picker/form/unavailable branch, MainScreen/ConnectionScreen profile-edit forms, and DiagramGeneratorDialog. Their immediate panels are ordinary divs/ConnectionFormDialog, not shared Dialog wrappers; the added role does not unconditionally introduce a nested Dialog at those entry points. Secondary child dialogs can still be opened by features inside an overlay; those are separate interactions, not a duplicated wrapper introduced here. New outer dialog has no accessible name, and adding aria-modal does not implement focus transfer/trapping/restoration or inert background. Shared Dialog already has analogous naming/focus limitations. No screen-reader behavior claimed from DOM annotations alone; real accessibility assessment remains outstanding. Existing full-suite role queries pass with the added wrapper.
- **Other surfaces:** ConfirmPopover already uses aria-modal and blocks Settings. Generic Popover/MenuPopover lack aria-modal and intentionally remain nonmodal; portal content on a main-screen surface can remain mounted until its own close policy fires. Connection-screen surfaces normally unmount when navigating to main; this does not solve the same-screen ContextMenu case above. FloatingAssistantLauncher has a named nonmodal dialog (`FloatingAssistantLauncher.tsx:236-252`), stays mounted at layout level, and sits at z-50 beneath Settings; no blocked-Settings scenario established. Its document Escape handler can also close the assistant when dismissing Settings, a shared independent-listener limitation rather than evidence that every role=dialog should block Settings.
- **Native dialogs:** `src/lib/system-dialog/index.ts` uses native Tauri dialogs or blocking browser confirm/alert, not DOM aria-modal nodes. The selector cannot observe them. Actual native keyboard delivery while a sheet/picker is open was not exercised, so no native-overlap defect is asserted solely from absent markup. Deactivation/reset confirmations are normally launched while Settings/LicenseDialog already supplies a DOM modal guard.
- **Existing Settings follow-ups:** categorical error/SQL-kind allowlists still bound those event values, and detailed consent behavior is unchanged. Deactivation still awaits the shared confirmation before the store action. No new source/test regression established in appearance, plan labels, masked key display, date presentation, distribution-gated privacy link or categorical error copy. Existing privacy limitations for arbitrary future free-form non-error fields, hosted analytics configuration and native dialog behavior remain as documented in the previous review.

## Re-review: Settings follow-ups round 3

Status: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS

Reviewed 2026-10-21 on `feat/connection-form-polish`. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK instructions and `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, then the round 2 findings. Reviewed working-tree changes against HEAD (staged and unstaged together) and relevant untracked files, including `src/screens/connection/hooks/useConnectionShortcuts.test.ts`, `src/lib/settings/`, `src/lib/license/confirmDeactivate.ts` and `src/lib/analytics.errors.test.ts`. Ignored `.claude/launch.json` and the existing REVIEW.md diff. Only this section appended; no application edits, staging or commits.

### Previous finding disposition

- **P2 #1 — reserved snippet chord: CLOSED for the reported dispatch collision.** `SnippetEditorDialog.tsx:79-85` refuses recording a reserved binding with a Settings hint, retaining recording so another chord can be chosen. `useConnectionShortcuts.ts:78-86` checks the normalized event binding before calling `insertSnippetByHotkey`; saved conflicting snippets therefore cannot insert SQL when the Settings chord is pressed. This guard applies independently of which window listener runs first and whether a dialog/menu prevents Settings from opening. Configurable action assignments and restored bindings retain the earlier reservation checks.
- **P2 #2 — Settings over an open menu: CLOSED.** `src/lib/settings/openSettings.ts:35-36` detects `[role="menu"]` as well as existing modal markers. MainLayout checks it before navigation/request (`src/layout/index.tsx:63-69`), so a connection-card context menu, MenuDropdown or assistant mode picker declines the chord. No pending request is created for later unexpected opening. LeftNav's account Dropdown has no menu role; its existing request handler closes the account menu while opening Settings (`src/screens/main/LeftNav.tsx:92-101`). This preserves that separate behavior.

### New P0-P3 findings

None established. No remaining actionable P0, P1, P2 or P3 regression found in the reviewed fixes.

### Regression and compatibility assessment

- **Persistence/import/sync boundary:** reservation is not a snippet-storage invariant. `normalizeSnippetHotkey` only normalizes (`src/stores/snippets.ts:21-24`); create/update can still save a reserved binding (`:143-181`), including saving an older snippet without changing its hotkey. `parseSnippetLibrary` retains string hotkeys (`src/lib/snippets/library.ts:225`), and native load/save stores the DTO without reservation validation (`src-tauri/src/commands/snippets.rs:47,55-63`). Store reload and an externally restored/imported library can therefore retain `Mod+,`. No separate snippet import/cloud-sync dispatcher was found in the current source. Crucially, the only production hotkey lookup caller is ConnectionScreen's `insertSnippetByHotkey` callback (`src/screens/connection/ConnectionScreen.tsx:666-674`), reached through the new guarded hook. Retaining an inert stored chord does not recreate the reported double action; dispatch protection suffices for that finding. Existing library data is not migrated or cleaned, and picker/editor labels may still display the inert chord. Manual snippet insertion remains available.
- **Platform behavior:** actual helper code, transpiled in memory with synthetic MacIntel and Win32 navigator platforms, mapped Cmd+, and Ctrl+, respectively to `Mod+,` and reserved Settings. Literal stored `Ctrl+,` cannot bypass the primary Settings chord: macOS treats it as physical Ctrl; elsewhere primary Ctrl events normalize to Mod. The new hook test dispatches both modifier events but does not change navigator.platform; its Meta assertion on a non-Mac test platform alone does not exercise macOS conversion. The separate read-only helper probe supplies that conversion evidence, not a native macOS integration test.
- **Menu lifetime / hidden DOM:** all three production role=menu renderers were traced. ContextMenu returns null when closed (`src/components/common/ContextMenu.tsx:76`); MenuDropdown's child menu is behind MenuPopover's closed return-null boundary (`src/components/common/MenuPopover.tsx:158`). Top-aligned MenuPopover may be temporarily visibility:hidden while measuring an explicitly open menu; that is not an always-rendered closed menu. AssistantModePicker uses shared Popover, whose close transition keeps content mounted for 140 ms (`src/components/common/Popover.tsx:24,56-59`), after which TinyPopover receives isOpen=false and omits its portal content (confirmed in installed package source). Settings can therefore be briefly declined during the closing transition, but no permanent hidden-menu blocker was found. The selector deliberately does not test CSS visibility; future permanently mounted menu markup would require reconsidering that assumption.
- **Connection dialogs/menus:** existing search, snippet picker/editor, save/truncate, health inspector and diagram dialogs resolve through shared Dialog or OverlayModal. Closed wrappers unmount their modal markup; open wrappers block Settings without navigating away from drafts. ContextMenu/MenuDropdown instances in connection workspace surfaces use the same audited boundaries, and assistant menu portals are found through the document-wide selector. The snippet guard still declines the reserved chord while those blockers are present. No change to other snippet hotkeys or explicit picker insertion paths found.
- **Adjacent Settings changes:** reviewed HEAD diff for deactivation confirmation, categorical analytics/error and SQL-kind handling, appearance/keyboard UI, distribution-gated privacy link, plan/key presentation and dates. No additional regression established in this pass. Earlier documented native-dialog, shared focus/accessibility and analytics-host limitations remain; this pass does not claim to resolve them.

## Review: Keep home screen view state

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-21 on `feat/connection-form-polish`. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK instructions and `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`. Reviewed the complete tracked working-tree diff against HEAD plus untracked `src/stores/mainView.ts` and `src/stores/mainView.test.ts`; ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Traced layout, Settings requests, profile selection, groups, credentials, History pagination and scroll containers. Only this report appended; no application edits, staging or commits.

### P0-P3 findings

1. **P2 — Save outgoing scroll before replacing its content — `src/screens/main/MainScreen.tsx:139-145`.** Scroll a long Connections page, then switch to a short Credentials page. Both pages reuse the same outer OverlayScrollArea and DOM scroller. Passive effect cleanup runs after the new page has replaced the content; the browser can already have clamped scrollTop to the short page's maximum. Cleanup then overwrites the Connections offset with that clamped value, even if its scroll listener previously saved the correct offset. Returning to Connections restores the wrong position. Capture the outgoing offset before the content swap, or give pages distinct scroller lifetimes and save before their DOM is removed; do not read outgoing geometry from the reused node after commit. Add a long-page → short-page → long-page regression with scroll geometry.

2. **P2 — Coordinate restoration with loading/pagination and user input — `src/screens/main/MainScreen.tsx:119-135`.** Credentials reloads its keys asynchronously on mount; if that takes longer than 30 animation frames (roughly half a second at 60 Hz), restoration exhausts its retries while the list is empty. The effect depends only on activeNav, so loaded keys never restart it, and scroll events/cleanup can replace the saved target with the temporary clamped offset. History also remounts with only 30 visible rows (`ConnectionLogsSection.tsx:118-121`, `useInfiniteScroll.ts:14-18`): a deep target requires pagination through IntersectionObserver and successive renders, which this frame budget does not await. During any incomplete restore, scrolling upward is overwritten by the next frame because no user-intent cancellation exists. Restore against explicit readiness/content growth, coordinate History's visible range with the target, stop on wheel/touch/keyboard/scrollbar interaction, and avoid committing transient programmatic/clamped offsets as the desired position. Test delayed loading beyond the retry budget, deep History return and user scroll during restoration. Exact native timing remains unverified; the loading and cancellation gaps are directly visible in the control flow.

3. **P2 — Reconcile retained History profile filters with available options — `src/screens/main/ConnectionLogsSection.tsx:69-70` (consumers `:87-101`, `:184-196`).** Filter History to profile A, leave for a connection tab, and let A's last session be evicted from the bounded 300-entry log while other profiles accumulate sessions. Returning now retains A in the store although the select contains only All connections and other profiles. Filtering still compares every row with A, yielding “No matching sessions” with no option naming the active restriction; the native select can appear blank or show a fallback that does not match the store. Clearing history and later opening another profile also leaves this stale restriction, and leaving/re-entering History no longer clears it as before. Reset to `all` when the selected profile no longer occurs in the loaded log, or retain a clearly labeled unavailable option plus a visible reset action. Reconcile against log entries, not saved profiles: sessions for deleted connections intentionally remain readable. Add an unmount/remount test with A removed and B present. The pre-existing mounted-component case also lacked reconciliation; extending filter lifetime now carries it across navigation for the rest of the session.

No P0/P1 established. No separate P3 finding.

### Behavioral compatibility and scope

- **Authoritative state:** all migrated values have one authoritative store representation. MainScreen and ConnectionLogsSection no longer keep parallel local copies. Store selectors return primitives/stable actions; scroll updates do not subscribe MainScreen to the whole scroll map. Selected profile remains authoritative in useProfileStore, distinct from group selection.
- **Group lifecycle:** handleDeleteGroup clears the selected group, and the existing groupedProfiles effect clears IDs absent from groups (`MainScreen.tsx:237-244`). Groups are synchronously loaded and retained in their store, so normal return from a connection does not recreate an empty initial group snapshot. No additional deleted-group regression established.
- **Navigation/Settings:** layout still mounts MainScreen only for `main`. Cmd/Ctrl+, switches the workspace to main and LeftNav consumes its pending Settings request; it does not assign activeNav, so the last home page remains behind Settings as intended. History's onShowConnection explicitly writes `connections` and selects the profile. Its pre-existing behavior does not clear shared search or selected group, so a selected connection can remain hidden by those filters; this is not a new regression from moving state. Ordinary openProfileInTab does not modify the stored page.
- **Shared search/layout:** searchQuery and viewMode were already shared across home pages; retaining that model preserves existing semantics. Search remains visible in TopBar. No unrequested per-page search split or automatic clearing is warranted by this change.
- **Credentials editor/newSignal:** editorOpen, newSignal, drafts and selectedKey remain transient. A genuine MainScreen remount initializes editorOpen=false and newSignal=0; KeychainSection clears its editor fields when newSignal changes. Returning to Credentials therefore does not reopen an old secret editor. Existing within-home editor-state lifetime was not redesigned here.
- **Scroll element:** scoped query selects the main column's outer vertical data-scroll-root, not History's nested horizontal table scroller. The root persists between home pages, causing finding 1. Listener and both animation-frame handles are removed/cancelled on cleanup; retries are bounded, not an infinite loop. Three NavId entries keep memory bounded. Each scroll frame allocates a small map and notifies store subscribers; no unbounded memory/performance issue established.
- **Restart behavior:** mainView has no persist middleware and is absent from persistentStore. Defaults on a real app restart are intentional; no requirement to persist these values or secret editor state across restarts.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx vitest run src/screens src/stores` via `rtk proxy`: **31 files, 149 tests passed** (4.38s).
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` via `rtk proxy`: **passed**.
- `git diff --check` before report append: **passed**.
- New store tests cover selected defaults and independent scroll-map writes only. Existing History interaction tests now use a reactive vanilla-store mock, but do not mount the production zustand hook or verify filter retention across unmount/remount, stale options, or MainScreen integration. No scroll restoration regression test was added. Passing tests therefore do not cover the findings above.
- Findings are source/lifecycle analysis, not launched desktop reproductions. No desktop/dev-server launch, real layout/scroll/IntersectionObserver timing, screenshot, native Settings interaction, emitting build or Rust check. Kept writes restricted to REVIEW.md and ran the requested non-emitting checks. Browser-specific clamping/selection presentation and deep-History pagination timing need integrated UI verification after fixes.

## Re-review: Keep home screen view state round 2

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed 2026-10-21 on `feat/connection-form-polish`. Read `AGENTS.md`, referenced RTK instructions and `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`. Reviewed the tracked application diff against HEAD and untracked `src/stores/mainView.ts`, `src/stores/mainView.test.ts`, `src/hooks/useScrollMemory.ts` and `src/hooks/useScrollMemory.test.ts`. Ignored `.claude/launch.json` and the existing REVIEW.md diff. Only this section appended; no application changes, staging or commits.

### Previous finding disposition

- **Previous P2 #1 — CLOSED for the reported outgoing-offset overwrite.** `useScrollMemory.ts:56-58` checks the live active page before saving scroll events, and cleanup no longer reads/writes geometry. Browser clamping after activeNav changes cannot overwrite the outgoing page through its old listener. The new zero-target issue below is a separate incoming-page regression.
- **Previous P2 #2 — PARTIALLY FIXED; remains open for custom scrollbar interaction.** Restoration observes content growth rather than spending 30 frames, preserves the target while incomplete, and cancels on wheel/touch/keyboard/pointer events delivered to the scroller. The 5-second deadline bounds delayed/empty content. Custom scrollbar dragging bypasses cancellation; see finding 2. Deep History restoration is supported by source flow but not verified in real layout.
- **Previous P2 #3 — CLOSED.** ConnectionLogsSection derives availability from all log entries, uses activeProfileFilter for rows, select, empty-state filtering and pagination resetKey, then resets stale stored values to `all`. Deleted saved profiles with remaining sessions stay available. The added stale-filter test passes; it seeds stale state before mount rather than executing a full leave/evict/return cycle.
- The group reconciliation effect now includes setSelectedGroupId in its dependencies.

### P0-P3 findings

1. **P2 — Restore zero offsets on page switches — `src/hooks/useScrollMemory.ts:32-33`, `:61-67`.** Scroll Connections to 600, then switch to a sufficiently long History page whose saved offset is absent (defaults to 0) or explicitly 0. Both pages reuse the same scroller; if the incoming content can accommodate 600, its scrollTop remains 600. With target 0, restoring is false and apply is never called, so History opens partway down instead of at its own saved top. The same failure occurs returning to any page previously left at the top. Always apply the incoming target, including zero, while preserving the outgoing-page save guard. Add a key-change test using one shared nonzero scroller and an incoming target of zero; the existing setup(0) test starts with scrollTop already zero and misses this case.

2. **P2 — Cancel restoration when dragging the overlay thumb — `src/hooks/useScrollMemory.ts:61-64`.** Return to a deep History target while only the first rows have loaded, then drag the visible vertical thumb upward before restoration finishes. The pointerdown listeners are attached only to the scroller. OverlayScrollArea renders its thumbs as siblings of that scroller (`src/components/common/OverlayScrollArea.tsx:239-258`), and their handler also stops propagation (`:149-163`), so thumb pointerdown never reaches those listeners. The drag changes scrollTop, but onScroll discards it while restoring; the next content ResizeObserver notification writes the old deep target again and overrides the user's choice. Even without further growth, the drag offset is not remembered before timeout/navigation. Wire thumb interaction explicitly into cancellation, or listen on the shared wrapper in capture phase with suitable scoping; a bubbling wrapper listener alone is blocked by stopPropagation. Add coverage using the actual overlay DOM relationship and delayed content growth during/after a drag.

No P0/P1 or separate P3 finding established.

### Regression checks and lifecycle assessment

- **Keyboard inside content:** ordinary keydown from focused descendants bubbles to the scroller and cancels restoration; the History row handler prevents default for its actions but does not stop propagation. No keyboard cancellation regression established for these paths. Portaled menus and TopBar are outside the scroller; their events do not reach it.
- **Observed content:** the outer OverlayScrollArea always provides a stable, auto-height first-child div wrapping the page. The hook observes that wrapper, not a thumb or the nested History horizontal scroller. Normal list/pagination growth changes its size. Page key changes clean up the old observer and install a new effect on the shared scroller; no first-child replacement issue established in this integration.
- **History loading:** clamping to the currently available bottom can expose the sentinel; useInfiniteScroll recreates IntersectionObserver when visibleCount changes, and subsequent wrapper growth retries the target. This can advance beyond the initial 30 rows until the target is reached or the deadline expires. Real intersection/clipping and resize scheduling were not exercised by the hook's manual observer mock.
- **Empty/short content:** an unreachable positive target waits at most 5 seconds, then disconnects observation and input listeners. With no later scroll event it retains the old target for another visit; bounded waiting is intentional. No permanent timer/observer loop found.
- **Cleanup/key changes:** cleanup calls stop(), disconnects ResizeObserver, clears the timeout, removes all four cancellation listeners and removes the scroll listener. A late resize callback cannot apply after stop because restoring is false. Same-key rerenders intentionally keep the effect; production callbacks read live store state and the queried scroller remains stable. The added tests do not directly assert unmount cleanup or effect key transitions.

### Verification and limitations

- `npx vitest run src/hooks src/screens src/stores` via `rtk proxy`: **38 files, 180 tests passed** (5.27s).
- `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` via `rtk proxy`: **passed**.
- `git diff --check` before report append: **passed**.
- New hook tests cover clamped delayed growth, wheel cancellation, deadline expiry and ignoring an inactive page's clamping. The mock emits scroll synchronously from its scrollTop setter; browser scroll events are asynchronous. It neither renders OverlayScrollArea nor drives real History pagination, keyboard bubbling, sibling thumb dragging, unmount cleanup or key-change restoration. Passing checks do not close the two findings above.
- Findings are source/control-flow analysis, not native desktop reproductions. No desktop/dev-server launch, real browser geometry, screenshot, emitting build or Rust check. Current scope is frontend state/scroll behavior; native Settings and database operations were not changed or exercised. Writes kept to this report as requested.

### Previous finding disposition

The same effect body runs on key changes; the test covers entry with reused geometry rather than an actual hook rerender.
- **Round 2 P2 #2 — CLOSED.** `src/hooks/useScrollMemory.ts:37,65-69` installs cancellation on the immediate wrapper in capture phase. OverlayScrollArea renders its scroller and overlay thumbs within that wrapper (`src/components/common/OverlayScrollArea.tsx:239-258`); capture runs before the thumb's target handler stops propagation (`:149-163`). Cancellation happens before pointer movement changes scrollTop, so subsequent drag scrolling is saved and content growth cannot reapply the old target. The sibling-thumb regression at `src/hooks/useScrollMemory.test.ts:121-130` checks stopPropagation, ignored later growth, and saving the drag offset of 90.
- Earlier outgoing-offset and stale History-filter findings remain closed: saving checks live activeNav, cleanup never saves outgoing geometry, and History reconciles missing profile filters against retained log entries.

No new P0, P1, P2 or P3 finding established in the reviewed scope.

### Regression checks and lifecycle assessment

- **Wrapper scope / TopBar search:** MainScreen queries the first data-scroll-root within its main column (`src/screens/main/MainScreen.tsx:113-123`). That is the outer content scroller, whose immediate parent is OverlayScrollArea's own root, not the main column. TopBar is in a separate sibling preceding OverlayScrollArea (`:488-510`). Clicking its search field or typing while that field has focus does not traverse the capture surface and therefore does not cancel restoration. No broadened whole-column listener exists. The generic parentElement fallback assumes this DOM relationship; current production integration satisfies it.
- **Keyboard within content:** keydown from descendants of the content surface cancels, including typing into an input inside that surface. This preserves the previous scroller-keydown policy and lets direct interaction take precedence over a pending restore. TopBar search is outside that surface. Search changes can still resize/filter content and trigger an existing pending restore; this behavior predates these two fixes and is not evidence of accidental search-key cancellation.
- **Zero target / save behavior:** zero assignment occurs before the new scroll listener is added (`src/hooks/useScrollMemory.ts:74-76`). The test's synchronous setter therefore does not save that initialization. A browser's later asynchronous scroll event can save the resulting zero through `:60-62`, but only for the current page. That value agrees with the requested saved target; it cannot replace a positive target in this branch. An old page listener, if still present before cleanup, rejects the event through isCurrent. Cleanup never reads geometry. No outgoing-offset overwrite reintroduced.
- **Cleanup symmetry:** all four input listeners use the same surface, event type, stop callback and capture=true for addition/removal (`src/hooks/useScrollMemory.ts:46-51,65-67`). Passive need not match for removal. stop also disconnects ResizeObserver and clears the timeout. It is safe when repeated on target completion, cancellation, timeout or cleanup; late resize callbacks return because restoring=false. Cleanup removes the non-capture scroll listener with the same callback (`:78-80`). The zero branch stops the timer/observer immediately and installs no input listeners.
- **Adjacent state:** one authoritative mainView store remains responsible for retained home state; scroll-map writes do not change activeNav or search/filter values. History's stale-filter fallback, selected-group reconciliation and nonpersistent session lifetime remain consistent with earlier review. No additional regression established in the complete application diff.
