## Review: PostgreSQL DB Health Inspector progressive collection and UX — 2026-10-21

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed the uncommitted tracked diff against HEAD plus untracked `src/components/health-inspector/InfoHint.tsx`. Ignored `.claude/launch.json`. Read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` and referenced RTK instructions first. Scope includes the paid Pro gate, progressive collector/hook, shared query payload/native execution, Tables/Indexes SQL, Overview/dialog UX and associated tests. Only this section appended; no source edits, staging or commits.

### Findings

1. **P2 — Apply read-only to the current transaction — `src/lib/tauri/health-inspector.ts:123`, `src/lib/tauri/query.ts:314`; backend dependency: `src-tauri/src/engines/postgres/operation.rs:153-169`.** The new Inspector call requests `readOnly: true`, but the existing native branch starts a transaction and then sets `default_transaction_read_only`. That changes the default for subsequent transactions, not the already-open transaction; SET LOCAL then reverts it when this transaction ends. The Inspector's fixed catalog SELECTs do not themselves introduce a write, but the newly exposed per-query read-only option does not enforce its contract. Fix with a read-only transaction builder or `SET TRANSACTION READ ONLY` / `SET LOCAL transaction_read_only = on` before preparing/executing SQL. Add a PostgreSQL regression asserting `current_setting('transaction_read_only') = 'on'` for opt-in queries, a rejected non-temporary write, and an unaffected later ordinary query on the same pooled connection. PostgreSQL distinguishes the [next-transaction default from the current transaction setting](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/runtime-config-client.html#GUC-TRANSACTION-READ-ONLY).

2. **P2 — Preserve server timeout as failure — `src/lib/tauri/health-inspector.ts:120-121`; backend dependency: `src-tauri/src/engines/postgres/operation.rs:17-20,325-340,362-375`.** The new server-side statement limit relies on an existing runner that classifies every SQLSTATE `57014` as cancellation and emits successful `op:done`, including `statement_timeout`. If that server result reaches a still-live frontend helper (for example when its JavaScript deadline timer is delayed), `runSqlQuery` resolves with empty/partial rows instead of rejecting. The collector marks list sections empty/ready; an empty Overview is decoded as unrestricted unknown metrics and the new strip can say “All clear”. Normally the frontend deadline starts earlier and wins; that timing does not make the native success result correct. Distinguish an explicit operation cancel from a server timeout and emit an error for the latter; do not return partial data as complete. Add native coverage for server timeout before/after a chunk and a frontend test that it degrades the section. This is a pre-existing runner defect now depended on by the new per-query limit, not a claim that the lock-timeout branch swallows errors (`lock_timeout` uses a different SQLSTATE).

3. **P2 — Reset section state when Refresh begins — `src/components/health-inspector/DbHealthInspectorOverview.tsx:295-300`, `src/components/health-inspector/DbHealthInspectorDialog.tsx:400-403`; orchestration: `src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector.ts:74-85`.** After a completed snapshot, press Refresh while the next Overview query is pending. The hook sets `loading` but retains the entire old snapshot; the collector emits nothing until Overview finishes. The new per-section conditions therefore render the old ready Overview and lists, including its green “All clear” strip, under “Collecting…” with the old snapshot age removed. This can last the full 10-second Overview budget. The first progress event then replaces those old ready lists with loading sections, making them disappear belatedly. Emit the new all-loading snapshot at collection start, or reset the hook snapshot before awaiting collection. If intentionally retaining old data, explicitly label its collection time/staleness until replacement. Add a second-collection test with deferred Overview that distinguishes old completed sections from sections completed in the current generation.

4. **P2 — Disclose candidate-limited size rankings — `src/lib/queries/sql/health-inspector.ts:346-353,416-420`; UI: `src/components/health-inspector/DbHealthInspectorDialog.tsx:249-256,576-579,639-642`.** On databases with more than 500 tables/indexes, the new query can omit a truly large relation whose catalog estimate is stale or zero, then sort only the retained candidates by actual bytes. For example, 500 analyzed small relations can exclude a newly bulk-loaded large relation before its first ANALYZE. Users see only “Showing first 100 rows” and tuple/partition or scan caveats; nothing discloses that size ordering is approximate and restricted to 500 estimated candidates. Table scoring also omits the TOAST index pages included in the final total, introducing another ranking difference even with otherwise fresh estimates. Keep bounded sizing, but return candidate-limit metadata and show a clear sampling/stale-statistics caveat; include TOAST index pages if the score is intended to approximate total relation size. Add a >500-relation fixture with stale estimates and a TOAST-heavy case. [PostgreSQL documents relpages as a maintenance-updated estimate](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/12/catalog-pg-class.html), while [total size includes indexes and TOAST](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/functions-admin.html#FUNCTIONS-ADMIN-DBSIZE).

5. **P2 — Make InfoHint text visible on keyboard focus — `src/components/health-inspector/InfoHint.tsx:9-15`.** Important text previously visible in Overview (including attention evidence, unresolved blocker details and cumulative-counter caveats) is now available visually only through native `title`. `tabIndex=0` provides a focus stop, but there is no focus-triggered tooltip or activation handler; native title is not a reliable keyboard-visible disclosure. `aria-label` supplies a screen-reader name but does not solve access for sighted keyboard users. Use the existing accessible tooltip/popover pattern with visible hover/focus content, Escape dismissal and an appropriate description relationship, or keep essential evidence visible. Verify Tab/focus, Escape, pointer hover and scrolling/clipping in the desktop webview; existing tests only assert the accessible name.

No P0/P1 finding established. Findings 1–2 identify existing native behavior newly relied upon by this diff; findings 3–5 are regressions in the changed collection/presentation paths.

### Collection, cancellation and shared-call assessment

- Overview runs alone; the four remaining section tasks share two workers. A statements task executes its capability/info/data queries serially. No path through this collector schedules more than two simultaneous `runSqlQuery` calls. Full optional-statements path uses seven Inspector query operations, under the ten-operation guard; this count excludes native BEGIN/SET/prepare/backend-PID/COMMIT protocol work.
- `runSectionQuery` checks abort before dispatch and computes each timeout as min(10 seconds, remaining 30-second budget). The shared helper's existing local deadline covers initialization, dispatch, pool waiting and result subscription; late-created operation IDs are cancelled. Backend cancellation aborts the operation task even before a pool client/cancel token exists (`operations/dispatcher.rs:32-43`), so pool waiting is not dependent solely on SET LOCAL. Deadline behavior was traced, not live stress-tested.
- Generation checks guard both progress and final results; clear/context changes and loss of Pro access increment the generation and abort pending collection. Refresh cancels the previous controller before advancing the generation. No stale progress-after-return path established with the production helper: `settled` suppresses sibling emits, sections are replaced rather than mutated, and shallow returned snapshots are not overwritten by later sibling assignments. Current tests do not directly cover a delayed sibling completing after cancellation or stale progress from a superseded generation.
- Cancel affects the Inspector's own operation IDs. Direct-mode calls bypass the stream SQL-busy registry, so these two workers do not use the busy-retry path to cancel a user's stream. Default PostgreSQL pool size is five, configurable down to one: concurrency two does not guarantee spare capacity on every profile. A one-slot pool serializes these requests and can queue user queries; frontend timeouts/task cancellation bound the Inspector's occupancy/wait. No pool-exhaustion leak established by source tracing; no live pool saturation test available.
- New options are optional; ordinary `runSqlQuery` callers provide no `readOnly`, `statementTimeoutMs` or `lockTimeoutMs`. Omitted values preserve the existing backend 60-second statement default and existing lock/read-only behavior. SET LOCAL remains scoped to the query transaction. Metadata, schema/table/object mutations, history, backup/restore, diagram and health-check callers were inspected; no new read-only/one-second lock limit applied to those callers. `startSqlQueryStream` does not forward these new options; no changed caller relies on it doing so.
- Pro access is checked at refresh time, subscribed for revocation, and rechecked by the hook independently of dialog presentation. Existing Pro gating tests pass. No entitlement-bypass finding established in this diff.

### SQL compatibility and UI assessment

- Candidate subqueries limit rows before size functions are applied; outer lists still fetch 101 rows and decoder retains 100 with truncation. Table score uses bigint heap/TOAST/ordinary-index page estimates; final table bytes include TOAST/FSM/VM, index bytes are separate, total bytes are their sum. The estimate is not an exact total-size ranking (finding 4).
- Partition parent/leaf markers remain; no descendant aggregation was added, so leaf storage is not double-counted as parent storage. Catalog estimates naturally push storage-free parents behind large leaves; the result is a capped list, not a complete partition inventory. All changed catalog fields and syntax are available by PG12, with no PG13–17-only construct introduced. This is source/documentation compatibility assessment, not an executed PG12–17 matrix.
- Added catalog joins do not require application-table SELECT privileges. Existing permission/missing-relation failures degrade the relevant section. Actual restricted-role grants, concurrent DDL/drop races and lock-timeout errors were not exercised against PostgreSQL.
- All four list `useMemo` calls now precede loading returns, fixing the prior conditional-hook pattern. Overview adds no conditional hooks; dialog `useNow` is unconditional and its interval cleans up. Tabs remain switchable during collection; per-section skeleton and footer transitions are covered by tests.
- New overview tones have explicit dark variants; remaining neutral/table/dialog classes also receive existing global dark-theme remaps in `src/styles.css`. No source-confirmed dark-mode regression established. This is not visual contrast certification. Long transactions over five minutes produce an attention item; idle-in-transaction duplicates are suppressed. Historical deadlocks remain informational, and restricted visibility prevents the clean-summary path. Relative time and collection duration have focused formatting tests.
- InfoHint's accessible name is present and SVG is hidden from assistive technology, but keyboard-visible disclosure is missing (finding 5). Shared Tabs remain buttons without tablist/tab/tabpanel semantics; that is unchanged shared behavior, not a new finding in this diff.

### Verification and limitations

- Requested command via `rtk proxy`: `npx vitest run src/components/health-inspector src/lib/health-inspector src/lib/tauri/health-inspector src/lib/queries/sql/health-inspector src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector` — **8 files, 70 tests passed**.
- Additional shared-query regression command: `npx vitest run src/lib/tauri/query.test.ts src/lib/tauri/query.review.test.ts` — **2 files, 26 tests passed**.
- `npm run build` — **passed** (TypeScript and Vite). Warnings: browser externalization of `node:zlib` in existing dump code, mixed static/dynamic imports, and large chunks; no compile failure.
- `cargo check` in `src-tauri/` — **passed**, dev profile, 1.56 seconds. Native timeout/read-only behavior was not integration-tested by this compiler check.
- `git diff --check` before report append — **passed**.
- Live PostgreSQL unavailable: `pg_isready -h localhost -p 5432` reported no response; Docker inspection failed because the environment denies access to its Unix socket. No customer database was accessed or altered. SQL tests assert builder text, not actual query planning, candidate accuracy, permissions or PG-version behavior.
- Attempted a temporary, fixture-only Overview Vite harness outside source files; server startup failed with `listen EPERM: operation not permitted ::1:1439`. No browser/native webview render, screenshots, tooltip keyboard interaction or measured dark-mode contrast obtained. No approval was requested or bypassed. Temporary harness does not modify application source.
- Passing tests/build/check do not close the findings above. Follow-up tests should cover a full Refresh with deferred Overview, superseded onProgress, a delayed sibling after cancel, the seven-query capabilities/info path near deadline, native scoped settings/timeout error semantics and the PostgreSQL size/partition/permission matrix.

### Previous findings 1–5

1. **CLOSED, with native integration limitations.** `src-tauri/src/engines/postgres/operation.rs:179-184` now executes `SET TRANSACTION READ ONLY` immediately after transaction creation, before scoped settings, prepare and execution. This targets the current transaction and cannot persist as the default of the next pooled transaction. Omitted `read_only` still preserves ordinary write behavior. The implementer's reported PG16 checks (setting on, CREATE TABLE rejected, next transaction unaffected) are consistent with the source, but were not independently repeated here. No committed native integration regression covers these three assertions. The existing nontransactional database-DDL branch precedes transaction settings; this closure concerns the Inspector catalog-query path and the reported current-transaction defect, not a general authorization boundary for arbitrary SQL.

2. **CLOSED for server timeout misclassification; race/integration coverage remains limited.** `operation.rs:28-36,124,340,374` requires both a cancellation error and a recorded app request before quiet `op:done`. A server `statement_timeout` or external `pg_cancel_backend` without an app cancel now follows `op:error`, including after emitted chunks; the error is queued after preceding chunks and no success terminal is emitted on that path. Keeping the pre-token request until guard cleanup makes the new predicate usable rather than consuming its evidence. `ActiveGuard` and outer `OpCleanup` remove the request on return/abort. This is a shared-runner correction: SQL editor streams with the unchanged default 60,000 ms server timeout now become errors instead of successful partial results. `useSqlStreamResult.ts:265-272` records that error and clears its idle timer; direct-query subscribers reject and clean up. Validation and nontransactional DDL error branches use the new predicate too; setup/prepare/commit errors already emitted errors. Concurrent app cancellation can still win a coincident server timeout, intentionally treating the operation as cancelled. Existing dispatcher task-abort and asynchronous CancelRequest timing were not redesigned, and no new race was established from retaining the marker. The new native test checks only the Boolean truth table, not dispatch/token/guard races or actual PostgreSQL events before/after a chunk.

3. **CLOSED.** `src/lib/tauri/health-inspector.ts:437-443` synchronously emits a fresh all-loading snapshot before awaiting Overview. The production hook's generation-checked progress callback replaces the old snapshot, so Refresh cannot keep old ready sections or old “All clear” displayed during the pending Overview. Section objects are replaced rather than mutated; the initial shallow snapshot is not subsequently rewritten. The collector test asserts the first all-loading event and subsequent ready-Overview event. It does not exercise a completed first collection followed by a deferred second Overview through the real hook.

4. **CLOSED, with live SQL limitations.** `src/lib/queries/sql/health-inspector.ts:349-359` includes ordinary and TOAST-table index pages in the candidate score. Both list queries return total `relation_count`; decoder → collector → `sizeRankingEstimated` reaches the amber note at `DbHealthInspectorDialog.tsx:587,651` when the count exceeds 500. Exactly 500 stays unflagged because every candidate is sized. The note explicitly explains estimate-based selection and missing recently grown relations. Builder tests check the TOAST-index predicate, metadata and bounded sizing; the mocked collector covers 600 tables versus 500 indexes, and dialog coverage checks the note. These are not an executed >500-relation/stale-estimate or TOAST-heavy database fixture, nor proof of query-plan performance on large catalogs.

5. **PARTIALLY FIXED; new P2 below remains.** Focus now visibly opens a portaled tooltip, `aria-describedby` resolves even while hidden, blur hides it, and focused Escape stops the bubbling document Dialog handler only while open. A second Escape reaches Dialog. However, the hover-only path does not receive Escape unless the marker also owns focus. The existing test sends keydown directly to the hovered marker, which conceals that real event-target difference.

### New findings

6. **P2 — Dismiss a hover-opened hint before closing the Inspector — `src/components/health-inspector/InfoHint.tsx:64-74`; interacting listener: `src/components/common/Dialog.tsx:69-77`.** Leave focus on another dialog control, hover an InfoHint, then press Escape. Keyboard events target the focused control, so the marker's `onKeyDown` never runs; Dialog's document listener closes the entire Inspector instead of dismissing the open hint. Reproduced with a temporary Vitest probe importing the actual InfoHint and Dialog: hover made the portal visible, Escape on the other focused button called `onClose`, and the hint stayed visible when the spy did not unmount the dialog. Install a scoped Escape listener while the hint is open that runs before Dialog's bubbling document listener (for example, document capture), consumes only Escape while open, dismisses the hint, and cleans up on hide/unmount. Preserve the second-Escape-to-close behavior and add a regression that focuses a different control before hover; do not dispatch the keyboard event to an unfocused marker.

No new P0/P1 established. Findings 1–4 are closed at source-review level; finding 5's original keyboard-focus disclosure is fixed, but the new hover/Escape interaction prevents verification of the complete replacement.

### Regression and lifecycle assessment

- Collector still runs Overview alone followed by two workers. Initial progress adds no query or timer. Generation checks reject superseded progress/results; settled prevents late sibling progress or new work after collector return. Shared query deadlines still include IPC/pool wait, cancel late-created IDs without awaiting cancellation, and clear timer/listeners on settlement. No unbounded timer or new shared-operation cancellation path established.

Temporary probe confirmed unmount removes the portal; focused first/second Escape behavior also passed against real Dialog. Committed tests cover focus/blur, hover and marker-targeted Escape only, not scroll/resize listener removal, viewport geometry, hover-only Escape or integrated Dialog behavior. Tooltip has explicit dark classes; the app's dark selector is rooted on html, so body portals retain theme scope. No source-confirmed dark-theme regression found; native contrast/layout unverified.
- Existing asynchronous server-cancel delivery/task-abort behavior and transaction rollback on drop were traced, not stress-tested. Neither a one-slot pool/cancel storm nor a real external-cancel/timeout-after-chunk scenario was executed. Passing Boolean-helper tests do not establish these race guarantees.

## Re-review: PostgreSQL DB Health Inspector hint Escape capture and cleanup — 2026-10-22

Re-reviewed the full uncommitted task diff against HEAD, including untracked `src/components/health-inspector/InfoHint.tsx` and `InfoHint.test.tsx`, against the preceding **Re-review: PostgreSQL DB Health Inspector progressive collection and UX — 2026-10-21**. Ordinary callers omit the optional setting. No change reintroduces a pooled-session default or affects subsequent transactions. 2. **CLOSED; prior cancellation-race limitations remain.** `operation.rs:28-36,124,136,261,343,377` still requires both a cancellation error and the retained app-request marker before quiet completion. Server timeout/external cancellation without that marker takes the error path, including streaming failures after chunks. Cleanup remains scoped to the operation. Shared `runSqlQuery` options remain optional (`src/lib/tauri/query.ts:312-314`); ordinary callers retain existing defaults. No live timeout-after-chunk or cancel-race test in this pass.

Refresh therefore replaces the previous completed sections immediately. Overview-first/two-worker collection and settled-result protection remain intact.

Decoder and collector preserve `sizeRankingEstimated`; `src/components/health-inspector/DbHealthInspectorDialog.tsx:587,651` still displays the estimate/stale-statistics caveat above 500 relations. Requested tests cover the metadata boundary and presentation; no live >500-relation/TOAST-heavy or PostgreSQL-version fixture was executed.

Focus/blur test passes. Additional real-Dialog probe verifies focused Escape hides the hint without moving focus or closing the Inspector, then a second Escape closes it. Native webview clipping, visual contrast and screen-reader behavior remain unverified.
6. **CLOSED.** `InfoHint.tsx:45-60` registers a document capture listener only while open. It checks `event.key`, calls `stopPropagation()` only for Escape, and hides the hint. It no longer relies on marker-targeted keydown. `InfoHint.test.tsx:24-46` uses the actual Dialog, keeps focus on Refresh, opens by hover, and verifies first Escape dismisses only the hint and second Escape calls Dialog close. `InfoHint.test.tsx:49-58` verifies portal/listener cleanup on unmount. Both pass. Effect cleanup removes the exact callback and capture flag on hide/replacement/unmount.

### Regression assessment

- **Capture ordering:** Dialog listens at document bubble (`src/components/common/Dialog.tsx:69-80`), so document capture prevents its first-Escape close regardless of listener registration order. `stopPropagation` deliberately does not suppress other listeners on the same document capture target. A temporary probe registered capture listeners before and after the hint; both ran, both hints can still dismiss, and Dialog stayed open. No application document-capture Escape closer was found: `src/screens/connection/hooks/useTableRowSelection.ts:36-85` handles Cmd/Ctrl+A and Backspace, not Escape. Window-capture hotkey recorders in KeyboardSettings and SnippetEditorDialog precede document capture and retain their own propagation behavior; neither is part of the Inspector's child tree. No demonstrated conflicting capture path warrants a new finding.
- **Other Escape consumers:** MenuPopover and ContextMenu listen on window bubble; ConfirmPopover and FloatingAssistantLauncher listen on document bubble; TagInput and AI combobox/picker handle keys at their controls. While a hint is open, its first Escape also prevents these downstream consumers from seeing that key. Once hidden, the next Escape reaches the existing handlers. The current Inspector Overview contains no nested menu/popover/input consumer, and its Dialog prevents context-menu opening. This is a tooltip-first policy, not general topmost-overlay arbitration. If interactive nested overlays are later added alongside persistent hints, coordinate Escape ownership then; do not claim the new listener preserves simultaneous downstream Escape delivery. No source-confirmed current Inspector regression established.
- **Multiple hints:** Focusing one marker while hovering another can open both. Since listeners share document capture and use `stopPropagation`, not `stopImmediatePropagation`, one Escape dismisses both without closing Dialog. A temporary real-Dialog probe confirms this and second-Escape close. No orphaned interception established after either hint hides.
- **Focus and lifecycle:** Non-Escape keys continue to the focused target and window. Temporary probes confirm blur, mouseleave, resize, and captured descendant scroll hide the hint and restore subsequent Escape delivery. The committed unmount test passes. Existing independent pointer/focus behavior remains: mouseleave hides even a focused marker, and blur hides even a hovered marker; it reopens on a subsequent enter/focus. This is unchanged from the preceding replacement, not introduced by capture handling. DOM tests do not simulate native Tab navigation or real pointer geometry.
- **Collection/shared state:** Reviewed the tracked collection, decoder, SQL, native-runner, signals, format, hook and presentation changes. No new query, shared cancellation, generation, Pro-gate, timer, or size-ranking regression established. Prior live-database/race coverage gaps remain limitations rather than evidence of failures.

## Review: PostgreSQL DB Health Inspector batches 3 and 4 — 2026-10-22

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewed the uncommitted working-tree diff against HEAD, including untracked `SectionToolbar.tsx`, `autoRefresh.ts`, and `server.test.ts`. Read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, and referenced RTK instructions first. Excluded `.claude/launch.json` and `.agent/plans/health-inspector-session-actions.md`; the separate DRAFT session-actions plan was not reviewed or implemented. No source edits, staging, or commits.

### Findings

1. **P2 — Restricted roles silently undercount server connections.** `src/lib/queries/sql/health-inspector.ts:240`: `WHERE a.backend_type = 'client backend'` excludes other roles' sessions because PostgreSQL masks their backend_type to NULL without statistics privileges. A nearly full server can therefore show only this role's handful of connections with a green Connections card. PostgreSQL 12 and 17 source both assign backend type at result slot 17 inside the privilege branch and explicitly null it otherwise. **Fix:** obtain a privilege-independent client count (for example aggregate the database backend counters where appropriate), or propagate restricted/unknown count status and suppress the healthy percentage. Add mixed-role fixtures and privileged/restricted-role SQL integration coverage; do not present the visible subset as server-wide occupancy.

2. **P2 — Failed or cancelled server checks become an all-clear summary.** `src/components/health-inspector/DbHealthInspectorOverview.tsx:432-438`: serverPending only covers active loading, and non-ready server sections contribute no attention item. With a healthy Overview and server status `error`/`unavailable`, AttentionSummary falls through to green “All clear in this snapshot”; cancelling after Overview but before server completion can do the same once loading becomes false. Individual cards show unavailable details, but the headline still asserts successful health checks. **Fix:** represent failed/unavailable/cancelled-unfinished server checks as incomplete, distinguish them from pending, and prevent all-clear until required checks completed with sufficient data. Test server error, permission failure, cancellation before server completion, and ready-but-unknown metrics, alongside the existing pending test.

3. **P2 — Successful auto-refresh does not clear an earlier collection error.** `src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector.ts:129-134` (also 93-98): only a manual refresh clears error on start; completion sets an error for a failed result but never clears it for a successful background result. After an initial collection-level failure (for example a transient capability-query failure), auto-refresh remains armed on the finished snapshot; its successful replacement leaves the old red error banner above healthy results indefinitely until manual refresh. **Fix:** update error from every accepted completed result, clearing it when that result succeeds; preserve old error only while retaining the previous snapshot during an in-flight or discarded cancelled background run. Add a fake-timer failure -> successful background recovery regression.

4. **P2 — Exclusion-constraint indexes are offered as unused DROP candidates.** `src/lib/queries/sql/health-inspector.ts:463` and `src/lib/health-inspector/signals.ts:36-37`: excluding only indisunique/indisprimary misses non-unique GiST indexes backing EXCLUDE constraints (`indisexclusion = true`). With zero scans they receive the Unused badge/filter and Copy DROP even though they enforce a constraint; PostgreSQL rejects dropping the supporting index directly. **Fix:** decode a separate constraint-backed/drop-eligible flag including indisexclusion (and preserve actual unique labeling), suppress maintenance suggestions for those indexes, and test an exclusion-constraint index with zero scans. Unknown eligibility should also fail closed rather than becoming eligible through the boolean decoder's false fallback.

### Other scope assessment

- New server query uses catalog/functions available in PG12–17; no version-specific SQL syntax problem established. XID uses age(datfrozenxid) across databases and the 2^31 wraparound scale; 2x freeze-age / 50% warning and 75% critical are conservative heuristics. Connection thresholds correctly use max minus superuser-reserved for known inputs; PG16+ additional reserved_connections is outside the explicitly requested denominator. Cache ratio is database-local, cumulative, and withheld below 1,000 requests; its hint explains OS-cache misses. Standby wall-clock age is capped at warning with the idle-primary caveat (implementation uses >300 seconds, whereas the general threshold comment describes >60 seconds). These heuristics are not evidence of an outage.
- PostgreSQL replay_lag can legitimately become NULL when a replica is caught up and idle, even for privileged users. `DbHealthInspectorOverview.tsx:289-290` labels every such NULL “Lag not visible to this role”. Improve this to an ambiguous “Lag unavailable (idle or restricted visibility)” rather than diagnosing privileges from NULL alone. Replication remains neutral for unknown lag. Unknown reservedConnections currently defaults to zero, and unknown replicaCount renders None; preserve unknown presentation if malformed/partial result compatibility is intended. These are additional accuracy notes, not additional blocking findings.
- ListSection hooks precede early returns. Search requires every case-insensitive term and omits hidden query text. Flags, filtered counts, table/index derived sort fields, shared Tabs badge prop, and blocked/table row highlighting were traced through the existing Table/useIndexedSort implementation. Production PostgreSQL timestamp decoding emits UTC RFC3339 strings; derived dates use the existing latestHealthTimestamp helper. No duplicate authoritative row state introduced.
- Maintenance actions only copy SQL through clipboard; no execution path added. Identifier quoting doubles embedded double quotes; VACUUM (ANALYZE) and DROP INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF EXISTS syntax is valid on target versions, subject to the eligibility finding above and PostgreSQL ownership/transaction restrictions. Existing zero-scan caveat remains visible.
- Auto-refresh retains displayed snapshot, omits progress callbacks, discards cancelled background results, gates timers on open/Pro/completed/non-cancelled state, checks document.hidden before starting, and clears timer on effect cleanup. Manual refresh increments generation and invalidates background completion; clear/context/license changes invalidate old results. A cancelled background run retains the previous complete snapshot and can re-arm the timer; a cancelled displayed snapshot pauses it. No confirmed new overlap/generation defect established by source inspection.
- No new telemetry or logging found. Search has an accessible label; flag buttons use aria-pressed; count uses aria-live; footer select has a wrapping label. Search Escape stops propagation when non-empty, so the existing document-bubble Dialog handler does not close it. Added toolbar/cards/badges/row states have dark variants; the shared Tabs component's pre-existing tab-role/keyboard behavior and light base styles were not redesigned by the badge change. Native contrast, layout with all badges, real keyboard defaults and screen-reader output remain unverified.

### Verification and coverage limits

- Requested `npx vitest run src/components/health-inspector src/components/common src/lib/health-inspector src/lib/tauri/health-inspector src/lib/queries/sql/health-inspector src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector` through `rtk proxy`: **15 files, 121 tests passed**, 3.92 s. Includes all three requested untracked files where applicable.
- `npm run build`: **passed**, TypeScript + Vite. Warnings: node:zlib browser externalization, mixed static/dynamic imports, large chunks.
- `git diff --check`: **passed** before report append.
- Existing tests cover healthy/server warning signals, basic decode, low-traffic cache, filtering, one numeric table sort, unique index exclusion, quoting, search Escape, pending strip, footer choice, background snapshot retention/no progress, cancelled background discard, paused cancelled snapshot and hidden/off timers. They do not cover findings 1–4, nor a background-specific manual-refresh/clear/context/unmount race and timer-cleanup matrix; add focused regressions rather than relying on passing happy paths. Index-derived sorting and all derived table columns are not each exercised by the new UI test.
- No live PostgreSQL 12–17 matrix, restricted-role SQL execution, real standby/replica, exclusion-constraint fixture, native webview, visual dark-mode or assistive-technology validation performed. SQL compatibility/privileges assessment uses source and official documentation, not independent live integration evidence. No claim that native runtime behavior is certified.

References: [PostgreSQL 17 statistics/visibility and lag semantics](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/monitoring-stats.html); [PG12 pgstatfuncs.c](https://github.com/postgres/postgres/blob/REL_12_STABLE/src/backend/utils/adt/pgstatfuncs.c); [PG17 pgstatfuncs.c](https://github.com/postgres/postgres/blob/REL_17_STABLE/src/backend/utils/adt/pgstatfuncs.c); [pg_index flags](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/catalog-pg-index.html); [DROP INDEX restrictions](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/sql-dropindex.html). Documentation consulted through Context7; masked backend_type confirmed against upstream source.

## Re-review: PostgreSQL DB Health Inspector batches 3 and 4, findings 1–4 — 2026-10-22

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, and referenced RTK instructions first. Re-reviewed the uncommitted task diff against HEAD and the preceding batches 3–4 CHANGES REQUIRED section, including untracked `SectionToolbar.tsx`, `src/lib/health-inspector/autoRefresh.ts`, and `server.test.ts`. Ignored `.claude/launch.json` and the separate DRAFT `.agent/plans/health-inspector-session-actions.md`. No application source edits, staging, or commits; only this report appended.

### Status of findings 1–4

1. **PARTIALLY FIXED; P2 remains open with replacement accuracy defect.** `src/lib/queries/sql/health-inspector.ts:240-244` now sums `pg_stat_database.numbackends` for named databases, with no backend_type filter. This removes privilege-dependent undercount: upstream PG16 `pg_stat_get_db_numbackends` counts matching database IDs without a privilege check. However, it also counts database-attached autovacuum, parallel, and other background workers. Those workers use separate limits, not max_connections slots. `src/lib/health-inspector/signals.ts:204-221,311-320` still grades this total against max_connections minus superuser-reserved slots and claims new connections fail at that limit. Example: max_connections=20, reserved=3, 14 clients and 3 database-attached workers yields 17/17 and a critical connection alert, although client occupancy is 14/17 (warning). More workers can produce an apparent occupancy above 100%. The implementer's one-session PG16 comparison supports privilege independence for that sample; it cannot establish client-only semantics or busy-server accuracy. **Fix:** obtain a client-slot count with sufficient visibility and withhold occupancy/healthy claims when visibility is insufficient; alternatively expose numbackends explicitly as a broader backend count without grading it as client-slot exhaustion. Add mixed-role and active-worker integration fixtures, including a small max_connections boundary. Do not revert to a silently restricted backend_type subset.
2. **CLOSED within source/unit/DOM scope.** `src/lib/health-inspector/signals.ts:258-283` distinguishes pending, complete and incomplete. Error/unavailable, loading after cancellation, ready/null data, and missing XID age/client count/max_connections cannot reach all-clear. `DbHealthInspectorOverview.tsx:149-198,438-464` wires this into the strip: current issues retain warning/critical priority, pending without issues says Checking, incomplete without issues uses the requested text, and healthy completed checks permit All clear. Historical notes do not override incomplete status. Failed Overview does not render the strip. A cancelled snapshot whose server checks already completed can still report their health, with cancellation retained in the snapshot header; this is consistent with cancelled-before-finish scope. Missing server section returns pending even when not loading; current collector always initializes it, so no current production path established. Invalid/nonpositive max_connections and unknown reserved count are not validated here; SQL settings supply valid values in supported execution.
3. **CLOSED.** `src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector.ts:117-140` checks generation before accepting snapshot/error updates, discards cancelled background results before changing either, and assigns error or null from every accepted result. Successful auto-refresh clears an earlier error. Manual refresh still clears on start; background refresh preserves previous snapshot/error while in flight. Clear/context changes/license loss invalidate generations; superseding refresh cancels previous controller and advances generation. Background-specific race matrix remains incompletely tested. Unmount cleanup aborts without advancing generation, inherited from the prior hook: a late completion can attempt state updates after unmount, but no mounted-state leak or new error regression was established. Collector returns failed/cancelled snapshots through its catch path; no demonstrated escaping rejection introduced by this patch.
4. **CLOSED within source/unit scope.** `src/lib/queries/sql/health-inspector.ts:465-468,491-492` selects and returns `(indisunique OR indisprimary OR indisexclusion) AS enforces_constraint` while preserving unique labeling separately. `src/lib/health-inspector/decode.ts:64-76,475-477` permits only recognized explicit false representations to make eligibility false; missing/null/unrecognized values become protected. `signals.ts:36-37` uses that authoritative flag, and Dialog Unused badge/filter/Copy DROP all share `isUnusedIndex`. Exclusion indexes are therefore suppressed without falsely labeling them unique. The attributed PG16 EXCLUDE result is consistent with the catalog contract; not independently reproduced live.

### Regression assessment and coverage

- **Connection-count regression above is the only new blocking issue established**, tracked under original finding 1 rather than duplicated. Upstream PG16 source counts database IDs across backend status entries, whose storage explicitly includes autovacuum/background workers; datname IS NOT NULL removes unassociated processes, not workers attached to a database.
- Removed line SQL comment is absent from the server template; whitespace collapse no longer comments out the remaining SELECT. Current SQL contains the expected aliases and catalog-only metric expressions. SQL string tests are not execution/privilege/worker semantics tests.
- `server.test.ts:166-187` covers pending, cancelled-unfinished, error, unavailable, missing XID, and complete state. Dialog test at `DbHealthInspectorDialog.test.tsx:961-974` verifies incomplete text/no All clear for server error; existing pending/healthy DOM test remains. Missing connections/max_connections and ready/null are source-guarded but not individually asserted; unavailable/cancelled states are helper-tested rather than full Dialog integration-tested. Warning/critical with incomplete checks and history-only combinations lack dedicated DOM regressions.
- `useDbHealthInspector.test.ts:319-346` adds the requested fake-timer failure → successful background recovery regression. Existing tests cover background retention/no progress, cancelled background discard, paused cancelled displayed snapshot, hidden/off timers, manual double refresh, context replacement and downgrade invalidation. No dedicated background-vs-manual/clear/context/unmount late-resolution and timer-cleanup matrix; error preservation on discarded cancelled background results is source-confirmed rather than separately asserted.
- `server.test.ts:144-164,224-233` covers explicit false/true/null/missing constraint flag, zero-scan exclusion suppression, unique suppression and unknown scans. SQL test checks indisexclusion/alias. Current Dialog Copy DROP test exercises unique vs ordinary indexes, not an EXCLUDE fixture through SQL → decoder → UI. Numeric/string false representations and malformed values have broader implementation handling than individually tested cases.
- Rechecked list filtering/sort view rows, Tabs badges, toolbar Escape, copy-only SQL, auto-refresh option plumbing, collector server queue/decode and shared snapshot state. No additional confirmed regression established. Previous replication NULL wording/unknown-value presentation notes remain; this pass does not certify every table sort, native visual state or screen-reader behavior.

## Re-review: PostgreSQL DB Health Inspector batches 3 and 4, client visibility and reserved slots — 2026-10-22

Status: CHANGES REQUIRED

Reviewer: Codex. Baseline: HEAD plus all uncommitted task changes. Read AGENTS.md, .agent/WORKFLOW.md and referenced RTK instructions first; compared with the latest batches 3–4 re-review (finding 1 open: numbackends counts workers). Included untracked SectionToolbar.tsx, src/lib/health-inspector/autoRefresh.ts and server.test.ts. Excluded .claude/launch.json and the DRAFT .agent/plans/health-inspector-session-actions.md. Only this dated report section was appended; no source changes, staging or commits.

### Findings 1–4

1. **P2 — OPEN, partially fixed: membership is not effective statistics privilege.** `src/lib/queries/sql/health-inspector.ts:244` uses `pg_has_role('pg_read_all_stats', 'MEMBER')`. On PG15–17, pg_stat_activity instead checks `has_privs_of_role`, which honors inheritance. A non-superuser with non-inheriting membership in pg_read_all_stats (directly or through pg_monitor) can therefore return MEMBER=true while other roles' backend_type remains NULL. COUNT then returns only the visible subset; `decode.ts:336` accepts it, Connections becomes green, and `signals.ts:274` permits a complete/all-clear strip. For example, on PG16 create a LOGIN NOINHERIT role, grant it pg_read_all_stats with the default non-inherited membership, connect as that role without SET ROLE, and keep sessions owned by unrelated roles open: MEMBER is true, USAGE is false, and those other sessions remain uncounted. This is a source-confirmed counterexample, not an independently executed SQL fixture. **Fix:** test effective privilege with `pg_has_role('pg_read_all_stats', 'USAGE')`; keep null/neutral/incomplete handling when false. This is conservative and safe on PG12–14, whose statistics implementation used membership rather than inheritance-aware privilege checking. Update the SQL assertion currently requiring MEMBER and add direct/indirect NOINHERIT (PG16+ INHERIT FALSE), inherited pg_monitor, superuser and ordinary-role coverage, with unrelated client sessions. The earlier worker overcount itself is resolved by client-backend filtering; this replacement privilege defect remains under original finding 1.
2. **CLOSED within source/unit/DOM scope.** `src/lib/health-inspector/signals.ts:265-279` treats ready-but-null clientConnections as incomplete, alongside failed/unavailable/cancelled-unfinished checks and missing required metrics. `DbHealthInspectorOverview.tsx:149-198,438-464` prevents all-clear for incomplete checks without current issues; warning/critical issues retain priority. The added Dialog test at `DbHealthInspectorDialog.test.tsx:976-986` exercises canReadAllStats=false/clientConnections=null and verifies both “Needs pg_read_all_stats” and incomplete text. Finding 1 can feed falsely trusted data into this otherwise correct state handling; that is not a second independent strip defect.
3. **CLOSED.** `src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector.ts:114-135` rejects stale generations and discards cancelled background results before accepting snapshot/error updates; every accepted result assigns its error or null. The failure → successful auto-refresh regression at `useDbHealthInspector.test.ts:319-346` passes. Background retention, cancellation, timer gates and context/license invalidation remain intact; the prior unmount/race coverage limitations remain.
4. **CLOSED within source/unit scope.** `src/lib/queries/sql/health-inspector.ts:471-473,497-498` preserves unique labeling and returns enforces_constraint for unique, primary and exclusion indexes. `decode.ts:482-485` protects missing/null/unrecognized eligibility. `signals.ts:36-37` remains the shared gate for badge/filter/Copy DROP, so zero-scan exclusion indexes stay protected. Existing decoder/signal/SQL and ordinary-vs-unique UI tests pass; no new live EXCLUDE integration was run.

### PostgreSQL semantics and compatibility

- **Superuser / pg_monitor:** both MEMBER and USAGE succeed for the current superuser through PostgreSQL's superuser shortcut; no explicit grant is required. Inherited direct/indirect pg_monitor membership supplies pg_read_all_stats and succeeds. Non-inheriting membership is the counterexample above; logging in as a superuser and then SET ROLE to a restricted current_user does not retain that shortcut. The claimed PG16 restricted-role 0/false → decoded null and superuser 2/true observations are consistent, but do not test non-inheriting membership.
- **PG12–17 API availability:** pg_read_all_stats and pg_monitor already exist in PG12, as do the two-argument pg_has_role overload and MEMBER/USAGE privilege names. `current_setting(name, true)` also exists in PG12 and returns NULL for an absent setting. COALESCE to '0' is therefore compatible with PG12–15, where reserved_connections is absent; PG16–17 read the real value. No unsupported API syntax found. Source inspection shows the statistics privilege macro uses is_member_of_role in PG12–14 and has_privs_of_role in PG15–17; the distinction is behavioral, not function availability.
- **WAL senders: exclude them for the supported versions.** The premise that replication connections consume max_connections slots applies to older PostgreSQL, not PG12–17: PG12 explicitly separated max_wal_senders from max_connections. PG16 proc.c allocates separate normal, autovacuum, background-worker and WAL-sender free lists and chooses the WAL-sender list for am_walsender. Thus client backend is the appropriate registered-session category for this denominator; do not add walsender, parallel worker, autovacuum worker or logical replication worker. A replication-capable role using an ordinary SQL connection still has a client backend and is counted. pg_stat_activity remains a sampled view of registered sessions, not an exact admission-control/free-slot counter during connection startup/teardown.
- **Reserved arithmetic:** `decode.ts:327-334` correctly sums superuser_reserved_connections and PG16+ reserved_connections. Signals subtract the sum from maxConnections, so 100 maximum, 3 superuser and 2 role-reserved gives 95 ordinary slots. This is the ordinary-role admission threshold; superusers and roles with effective pg_use_reserved_connections privileges have additional headroom. Existing “New connections fail” text at `signals.ts:307` is broader than that ordinary-role scope, and the title at line 305 calls a usable-slot percentage a percentage of max_connections. Wording accuracy note: name usable/ordinary slots and ordinary new connections; no additional blocking finding raised.
- **Unknown UI:** false/missing can_read_all_stats decodes to false and clientConnections=null. Connections shows an em dash and permission detail with neutral tone; connectionRatio is null, no connection attention item is generated, and the strip is incomplete unless another current issue takes warning/critical priority. All-clear is withheld. The remaining failure occurs when MEMBER incorrectly supplies true.

### Coverage, verification and limitations

- Requested `npx vitest run src/components/health-inspector src/components/common src/lib/health-inspector src/lib/tauri/health-inspector src/lib/queries/sql/health-inspector src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector` through rtk proxy: **15 files, 126 tests passed**, 3.07 s in the captured run.
- `npm run build`: **passed**, TypeScript + Vite. Existing warning categories: node:zlib browser externalization, mixed static/dynamic imports, large chunks. Logs: /tmp/health-review-vitest.log and /tmp/health-review-build.log.
- `git diff --check`: **passed** before append. Only REVIEW.md edited by this review.
- server.test.ts now verifies false visibility discards a supplied numeric subset and true visibility preserves it; the same fixture verifies 3+2 reserved slots. The new DOM restricted-state test passes. Tests still mock the SQL privilege boolean rather than establish its semantics; the SQL string test currently locks in the incorrect MEMBER check. No live mixed-role/NOINHERIT/worker/WAL-sender fixture or PG12–17 execution matrix was run. No native webview, visual dark-mode, assistive-technology or cancellation-stress certification claimed.
- Rechecked shared Tabs badges, list filtering/sort view rows, toolbar Escape, copy-only maintenance SQL, collector server integration and auto-refresh state flow. No additional confirmed regression established. Earlier replication NULL wording and broader race/derived-sort coverage limitations remain.

**New independent findings: none.** Finding 1 remains open for the privilege counterexample; findings 2–4 remain closed within the stated verification scope.

Client-backend filtering still excludes workers and WAL senders. `src/lib/health-inspector/decode.ts:327-338` combines both reserved-slot settings and discards the numeric client subset when effective visibility is false. `signals.ts:211-220,265-279` then withholds connection grading and complete status, and `DbHealthInspectorOverview.tsx:303-317` presents neutral unknown Connections with the permission detail.
