## Review: health-inspector-session-actions — Revision 4.1 implementation

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-22.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, RTK instructions and the complete task plan, including Decisions, Design, Verification, human approvals, implementation evidence and prior review rounds. Reviewed the uncommitted tracked diff and task-owned untracked Rust/frontend files and tests against HEAD. Excluded `.claude/launch.json` and the human's one-line `contentClassName` `p-1` edit. No application source edits, staging, commits or live database signals; only this review appended. Temporary regression harness lives outside the repository at `/private/tmp/politedb-session-review/`.

### Required findings

1. **P1 — Unmount does not invalidate a destructive attempt.** `src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector.ts:230` and `src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector.ts:321`: `current()` checks only epoch/lock, while effect cleanup only calls `cancel()`. Unmounting the owning Inspector during a pending confirmation leaves its epoch, lock and `ctxRef.current.open` intact. When confirmation subsequently resolves true, the removed Inspector still invokes `health_inspector_signal_backend`; if unmounted after dispatch, its completion can also start reconciliation. This violates the unsent-action/context lifetime contract. **Reproduced:** an external Vitest harness opens the real hook, starts a deferred terminate confirmation, unmounts, then resolves true; the assertion that the signal wrapper was never called fails with exactly one terminate call for the old runtime/PID/token. **Fix:** invalidate action epoch/ownership and collection generation on unmount, with a mounted/lifetime guard checked after confirmation and after dispatch. Do not cancel or retry an already issued database signal; preserve exactly-once analytics for that issued attempt while dropping UI writes/reconciliation. Add regressions for unmount before confirmation resolves and unmount during dispatch, alongside close/reopen with a newer attempt whose lock must remain owned by that newer attempt.

2. **P2 — Required cancellation/deadline cleanup tests are absent.** `src-tauri/src/engines/postgres/session_signal.rs:268` and `src-tauri/src/engines/postgres/session_signal.rs:306`: current tests exercise validators, SQL substrings, row mappings and some pool-error constructors; the ignored live smoke covers successful operations and permission failures. None drives a delayed BEGIN/SELECT/COMMIT, the aggregate deadline, task abort, transport loss or commit failure, and none observes an abandoned client being detached rather than recycled. The guard structure looks conservative, but code inspection and a later successful pool borrow do not establish these explicitly required safety properties. **Fix:** add a deterministic fault-injection/protocol fixture around the actual runner; abort/drop the action at each phase, assert pre-dispatch `setup_failed` versus post-dispatch `unknown`, exactly one signal attempt, total deadline bounded across phases, `Object::take` detachment on every non-clean exit and recovery via a fresh usable client with no leaked transaction/settings. Retain clean-COMMIT reuse as a control. Cover acquisition exhaustion/closed/create/recycle failures and denied SELECT as applicable. These required automated checks are missing implementation work, distinct from unavailable PG versions or native accessibility checks.

3. **P2 — Analytics privacy/consent acceptance is tested only at the props helper.** `src/lib/analytics.sessionAction.test.ts:5`: the file never calls `trackHealthInspectorSessionAction` or captures actual PostHog output, never varies granted/denied/unknown consent, and uses `arrayContaining` where it claims exactly two keys. Hook tests mock the analytics boundary completely. The implementation does construct an enum-validated two-property object and route through opt-in `trackEvent`; no new identifier leak was found, but the approved privacy verification remains missing. **Fix:** spy on actual capture through the event helper with analytics initialized and each consent state; assert the exact two custom properties for valid kinds/outcomes, zero capture without consent and for malformed values, and no caller/IPC identifiers or extra properties. Exercise success, denied, timeout/unknown and IPC failure outcomes; retain hook assertions for exactly-once reporting after stale completion and no event on dismissal.

### Safety and compatibility assessment

- **Rust action boundary:** UUID/PID/int4/token/kind validation precedes pool I/O; invalid kinds are parsed into a closed enum (the command accepts a string rather than serde-deserializing that enum). Missing/wrong-engine runtime returns `unavailable`. The handler clones the existing PostgreSQL runtime pool, preserving SSH routing. Both fixed typed statements retain PID, UTC microsecond backend-start token, current database, client-backend type, executing-PID exclusion, nonempty application name and this-instance tag exclusion in the same statement. No arbitrary SQL interpolation, generic busy-retry path, signal retry or identifier/error-text logging found.
- **Cleanup/outcomes:** `DiscardGuard` owns the pooled Object outside the timed transaction future, armed before BEGIN; non-clean returns and cancellation unwind through detach/drop. Only successful COMMIT disarms it. Acquisition has a five-second cap and its elapsed time is deducted from the eight-second operation budget. Dispatch is marked immediately before polling SELECT; known true/false/zero/42501 results retain their mappings, while uncertain SELECT/COMMIT and malformed rows map conservatively. Required dynamic proof remains finding 2. The timer starts inside the service immediately after synchronous handler lookup/tag resolution.
- **Tagging:** process-global OnceLock value, lowercase eight-hex random tag, no fixed/empty fallback on SysRng failure. Shared `build_pg_config` sets startup application_name, and both real `connect_pg` and `test_pg_direct` call it; removing the old runtime override avoids overwriting the tag. Randomness failure is not injected by the tests. The concurrency test spawns and joins one thread at a time, so it does not actually exercise simultaneous initialization; add a barrier-based first-call test when expanding this coverage.
- **Hook:** one attempt lock is acquired before confirmation; call-time Pro/open/engine/runtime checks precede dispatch, pre-action collection is invalidated, manual refresh is gated and auto-refresh paused. Outcome analytics occurs before stale-epoch acceptance; close/context/Pro clearing prevents stale attempts from releasing a newer lock. Unknown rows are stored independently by PID/token and only cleared by a later accepted ready Sessions section. Reconciliation releases its lock in finally. Unmount is the exception in finding 1. The rejected-collector test checks lock release but not `loading`: a rejection leaves `loading=true` and therefore the dialog showing Cancel instead of manual Refresh. The production collector currently catches ordinary collection failures and returns a snapshot, so this is a defensive recovery gap rather than a demonstrated ordinary database-error path; clear loading/abort ownership in a generation-safe failure finalizer if rejection is part of the promised contract.
- **UI:** eligibility fails closed when tag/identity/type/name is unavailable, owns no duplicate session state, and disables Cancel for nonactive snapshot rows. Confirmation strips C0/C1, line breaks and Cf/bidi characters, bounds labels/preview, hides hidden query text, and names the action in the body for browser fallback. Menu naming, first enabled focus, arrow/Home/End handling, capture Escape, focus fallback and polite status region are present. Enter/Space rely on native button activation. Existing Tab test only checks menu removal, not the resulting focus order; the menu is portalled to body, so real Tab/Shift+Tab sequencing relative to the trigger still needs browser verification. Outcome text is temporarily replaced by pending text during reconciliation. No independent browser/native/VoiceOver smoke run in this review.
- **Accepted boundaries:** renamed-own-session eligibility, unnamed refusal, public/colliding tags, transaction/statement-pooling and name-rewriting proxies, PID-reuse race and cancel-whatever-is-running semantics are recorded as human-approved limitations, not new blockers. This review does not claim unconditional ownership protection beyond those boundaries.

### Independent verification

- Requested `npx vitest run src/components/health-inspector src/lib/health-inspector src/lib/tauri src/lib/queries/sql/health-inspector src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector src/lib/analytics`: **24 files / 205 tests passed**.
- `npm run build`: **passed**. Warnings for browser-externalized `node:zlib`, mixed static/dynamic imports and large chunks; no build/type error.
- In `src-tauri`, `cargo check`: **passed**.
- In `src-tauri`, `cargo test engines::postgres`: **13 passed / 1 ignored**, no failures. Ignored test is `session_signal_live`; no `HEALTH_PG_URL` live run performed for this review. Prior implementer PG16 results are plan evidence, not independently rerun results.
- External regression: **1 test failed as expected**, reproducing finding 1 against the current hook, with signal transport mocked (no database action). Initial harness import-resolution failure was corrected by linking the repository's node_modules into the temporary harness; the reported failure is the assertion reproduction, not setup failure.
- `git diff --check`: **passed** before report append.

### Remaining verification against the approved plan

Beyond findings 2 and 3, no independent evidence here for actual direct/SSH `connect_pg` initial/replacement clients, held `test_pg_direct`, surviving checked-out clients after runtime removal, concurrent editor/export/collection, wrong-engine direct-command execution, member-role/ordinary `pg_signal_backend` success, revoked function EXECUTE, cross-database/non-client/self live refusal, pool exhaustion, or the full fractional-token/TimeZone/DST/DateStyle matrix. The single live test uses synthetic Config clients and a smoke subset; the source guard is supplementary. PG12/PG17, real tagging/SSH, transport fault injection and native confirmation safe-default/Enter/Escape/VoiceOver were explicitly recorded as unavailable or not done by the implementer. Keep these gaps visible; source assertions do not replace connection-level evidence. Browser fallback has body-copy unit coverage but no actual window.confirm integration test.

Next: Claude fixes finding 1, completes the required automated safety/privacy tests, records verification and requests Codex re-review. The workflow's separate Claude `/security-review` remains required after this review; no commit/push/merge authorized.

## Re-review: health-inspector-session-actions — Revision 4.1 implementation fixes

**Verdict: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS.** Reviewer: Codex. Date: 2026-10-22.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, RTK instructions, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, the task plan and its “Fixes after Codex implementation review” evidence, and the preceding Revision 4.1 implementation review. Re-reviewed tracked implementation changes and task-owned untracked files, including the fault fixture and analytics tests. Excluded `.claude/launch.json` and the human's `contentClassName` `p-1` edit. No application source edits, staging, commits, database connections or signals; only this report appended.

### Prior required findings

| Prior finding                                            | Status                                                                   | Evidence and limits                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| -------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| P1 — Unmount does not invalidate a destructive attempt   | **Resolved**                                                             | `src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector.ts:315` cleanup advances epoch and collection generation, clears action ownership and aborts collection. Pending confirmation then fails `current()`; an issued attempt still reports analytics once but cannot write results, release a newer lock or reconcile. Passing regressions at `src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector.test.ts:636`, `:655`, `:673` exercise confirmation unmount, dispatch unmount and late older completion while a newer confirmation owns the lock. Existing context/Pro/collection-generation guards remain intact.                                                                                                                                                             |
| P2 — Required cancellation/deadline cleanup tests absent | **Resolved for the reported missing fixture; live verification limited** | `session_signal_faults` now drives the actual `signal_backend_with` runner through a wire-protocol proxy: BEGIN/Bind/COMMIT stalls, COMMIT transport drop after a real cancel, task abort, acquisition exhaustion, elapsed acquisition deducted from the overall deadline and closed pool. Clean COMMIT retains pool size; principal non-clean transaction cases assert size zero before recovery and a usable fresh client with `transaction_read_only = off`. Production still uses the original 8 s / 5 s timing and armed detach guard. Both live suites compile but were ignored in this review; implementer's PG16 execution remains supplied evidence, not independently reproduced. See nonblocking fixture limitations below. |
| P2 — Analytics tested only at props helper               | **Resolved**                                                             | `src/lib/analytics.sessionAction.test.ts` calls the real `trackHealthInspectorSessionAction` → opt-in `trackEvent` → sanitization/capture path, mocking PostHog itself. Exact payload equality and exact custom-key set cover signalled, denied, unknown and busy; denied/unknown/absent consent and malformed values produce no action capture. Wrapper tests independently establish IPC rejection → unknown; hook tests establish dismissal → no event and stale/unmounted issued completion → one event. These layers cover the requested path without leaking caller/IPC objects.                                                                                                                                                 |

No new P0/P1/P2 findings identified in the reviewed scope.

### Nonblocking findings

1. **P3 — Abort fixture does not establish which phase was interrupted.** `src-tauri/src/engines/postgres/session_signal.rs:907`: the loop exits as soon as the pool client is checked out, while a stalled Bind is deliberately not added to `binds`; the subsequent 200 ms sleep does not prove the proxy reached Bind. Under slow setup the test can pass after aborting BEGIN/setup instead of the claimed mid-statement phase. **Fix:** notify the test when the selected trigger is intercepted, await that notification with a bound, then abort and assert the JoinError is cancellation; use this handshake for phase-specific abort cases. This does not invalidate the separate Bind deadline test or reveal a production cleanup defect.

2. **P3 — Barrier covers concurrent reads but cannot guarantee first initialization.** `src-tauri/src/engines/postgres/app_name.rs:68`: other tests use the same process-global OnceLock and may initialize it before the barrier releases. **Fix:** exercise initialization in an isolated test process or extract an initializer accepting a fresh local OnceLock; release simultaneous callers against that empty cell. Current barrier improves concurrent access coverage, but the comment/plan claim of a guaranteed first-call race is stronger than the test.

### Additional fixes and regression assessment

- Rejected reconciliation collection now clears loading/refreshing and its abort ownership only for the current generation (`src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector.ts:165`); regression asserts `loading=false`, retained outcome, released lock and usable manual refresh. Older failures cannot clear a newer collection's indicators.
- Sessions status now retains the outcome throughout reconciliation; only the sending phase replaces it with progress. No duplicate session snapshot/state owner introduced.
- Tab handling focuses the row trigger before closing the portalled menu and leaves default traversal unprevented. Passing component test establishes trigger focus after synthetic keydown; actual browser Tab/Shift+Tab traversal and native accessibility remain unverified here.
- Fixed SQL identity/database/type/name/self predicates, typed parameters, no signal retry, conservative uncertain outcomes, runtime-pool reuse and opt-in enum-only analytics remain unchanged. No new identifier/error logging found.
- Fault recovery helper checks read-only state, not all local timeout settings; aggregate-deadline case does not repeat detachment/recovery assertions. Create/recycle faults, setup/prepare faults, explicit server COMMIT rejection and deterministic abort at each phase remain coverage extensions. Existing armed-guard structure supports those paths, but this review does not claim they were dynamically exercised.

### Independent verification

- Requested `npx vitest run src/components/health-inspector src/lib/health-inspector src/lib/tauri src/lib/queries/sql/health-inspector src/hooks/useDbHealthInspector src/lib/analytics`: **24 files / 216 tests passed**.
- `npm run build`: **passed** (TypeScript and production bundle). Existing browser-externalized `node:zlib`, mixed static/dynamic import and large-chunk warnings remain.
- In `src-tauri`, `cargo check`: **passed**.
- In `src-tauri`, `cargo test engines::postgres`: **13 passed / 2 ignored**, no failures. Ignored: `session_signal_live`, `session_signal_faults`. No `--ignored` run and no database URL used; no disposable database was independently provisioned or verified for this review.
- `git diff --check`: **passed** before report append.

Limitations from the preceding review remain: PG12/PG17, real direct/SSH startup/replacement/test-connection observations, surviving checked-out clients after runtime removal, the remaining live privilege/identity/token matrix, concurrent editor/export/collection and native confirmation/VoiceOver checks were not independently exercised. The plan records only part of that matrix as implementer evidence. Accepted tag-renaming/proxy/PID-reuse/cancel-current-work limits remain accepted boundaries. Workflow still requires the separate Claude `/security-review`; this verdict does not mark that pass complete or authorize commit/push/merge.

## Review: Pro Query Analyzer polish — 2026-10-22

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Branch: `feat/query-analyzer-polish`.

Scope: read `AGENTS.md`, `/Users/lamtung/.codex/RTK.md` and `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` first. Reviewed the uncommitted tracked/staged diff against HEAD and the untracked `PlanTree.tsx`, `NodeDetails.tsx`, `CompareView.tsx`, `details.ts` and `details.test.ts`, including the common `InfoHint` move. Excluded `.claude/launch.json` and the separately reviewed DRAFT `.agent/plans/query-analyzer-explain-analyze.md`; no implementation of that plan reviewed or authorized. No application source changes, staging or commits; only this review appended. Temporary regression harness lives outside the repository.

### Findings

1. **P2 — CTE producer time is counted as CTE Scan's own work.** `src/lib/query-analyzer/plan.ts:182-188`: displayed pre-order parents are not always runtime timing parents. A materialized CTE producer is displayed as an InitPlan under an ancestor, while `CTE Scan` executes that producer internally. For a 100 ms join with sibling children consisting of an 80 ms CTE producer, an 81 ms CTE Scan and a 5 ms other input, current heat assigns 81 ms own time to CTE Scan, separately assigns 80 ms to the producer, and clamps the join to zero. CTE Scan includes producer work; it is not an 81 ms exclusive bottleneck. This can pick the wrong hottest node even without parallelism. **Fix:** separate display ancestry from timing attribution; account for CTE producer/consumer relationships without double subtraction, or mark exclusive shares unavailable where attribution cannot be established. Cover materialized/reused CTEs and InitPlan/SubPlan relationships with real-plan fixtures. PostgreSQL's executor explicitly invokes the producer inside CTE Scan: [nodeCtescan.c](https://github.com/postgres/postgres/blob/REL_17_STABLE/src/backend/executor/nodeCtescan.c#L80-L95). The external synthetic regression demonstrates current arithmetic; no live PG timing experiment was run.

2. **P2 — PostgreSQL parameter scanner rejects valid literals/comments.** `src/lib/queries/sql/explain.ts:34-54`: dollar tags exclude digits after their first character, E-string scanning does not honor escaped quotes, and block comments stop at the first closing delimiter instead of tracking nesting. Independently reproduced false parameter errors for `select $tag1$ $1 $tag1$`, `select E'it\'s $1'` and `select 1 /* outer /* inner */ $1 */`. Escaped quote desynchronization can also hide a real parameter after a string. `leadingSqlKeyword` has the same nested-comment issue, so a valid typed EXPLAIN following a nested leading comment is not recognized. **Fix:** use a consistent PostgreSQL-aware lexer for these checks, supporting E escapes, nested comments, valid dollar tags (including digit continuations) and identifier boundaries; add positive and negative regressions. See PostgreSQL [lexical structure](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/17/sql-syntax-lexical.html).

3. **P2 — Trailing comments are mistaken for a second statement.** `src/lib/queries/sql/explain.ts:78`: `splitSqlStatements` returns comment-only fragments, so `buildExplainSql("postgres", "select 1; -- note")` now returns the multiple-statement error. The same happens with a trailing block comment; this is one executable statement. **Fix:** count executable fragments, discard comment-only tails and wrap the selected executable statement while preserving required comments. Keep refusal of two actual statements. External regression reproduces the false rejection.

4. **P2 — Recommendations after the first 100 are unreachable.** `src/components/editor/query-analyzer/QueryAnalyzer.tsx:424-445`: findings are still sliced by page, but the previous Recommendations pagination controls were removed. Every tab click resets page to zero, so there is no path to later findings. **Fix:** restore a pager for `findings.length` (with recommendation-specific page state if necessary), and test a plan with more than 100 findings. External 120-scan regression reproduces the missing Next page control.

5. **P2 — Listbox can lose every keyboard entry point.** `src/components/editor/query-analyzer/PlanTree.tsx:258-261` (also `QueryAnalyzer.tsx:91-95`): only the selected node gets `tabIndex=0`, while the listbox itself is not focusable. Initial selection uses the hottest node but initial page is always zero; when hottest is beyond node 100, all mounted options have `tabIndex=-1`. Paging, filtering, changing order or collapsing the selected branch can create the same state. Keyboard users cannot enter the list to use its arrow/Home/End handlers. **Fix:** maintain a visible active/focus target independently of selection or use a focusable listbox with a valid active descendant; reconcile page/filter/collapse changes and ensure actual focus moves after rendering. Test Tab entry, focused element and page-boundary navigation, not only `aria-selected`. External off-page hottest regression confirms zero tabbable options.

6. **P2 — Node shortcuts always reveal page one.** `src/components/editor/query-analyzer/QueryAnalyzer.tsx:132-134`: `revealInPlan` selects the target and expands ancestors but unconditionally calls `setPage(0)`. Clicking Most time/Most cost or a recommendation for a node beyond the first 100 leaves the selected node absent from the tree. Previous recommendation navigation calculated the target page. **Fix:** calculate target index in the resulting plan-order rows after clearing search and expanding ancestors, set its page, then reveal/focus that row. External node-120 shortcut regression reproduces the missing target option.

7. **P2 — Earlier streamed EXPLAIN runs are excluded from Compare.** `src/components/editor/SqlResultsPane.tsx:474`: baseline enumeration requires `candidate.result`, but completed stream slots only receive status/finishedAt; their rows remain in the stream registry (`useSqlRunner.ts:317-341`, `useSqlStreamResult.ts`). Normal non-DDL execution uses streaming (`useSqlHistoryRunner.ts:59-63`), so ordinary user-typed EXPLAIN runs can open the analyzer yet disappear as baseline choices for the next run. Current baseline test supplies direct results and misses this path. **Fix:** enumerate completed stream candidates with an available opId/snapshot, and load/parse a bounded snapshot on demand through the existing stream access boundary. Preserve one authoritative row store. Test earlier typed and Explain-action runs in stream mode, unavailable snapshots, and Pro revocation.

8. **P2 — Switching views discards the comparison baseline.** `src/components/editor/query-analyzer/CompareView.tsx:36-39`, mounted conditionally at `QueryAnalyzer.tsx:448-454`: baseline state moved from QueryAnalyzer into CompareView, which unmounts whenever Plan/Recommendations/JSON is opened. Returning to Compare loses the earlier-run choice or imported plan and requires selecting/importing again. Previously baseline state survived view changes. **Fix:** keep comparison state in the persistent parent (or retain the mounted comparison view) and invalidate pending imports on actual plan disposal; add select/import → other view → Compare regressions. External earlier-run regression reproduces disappearance of the comparison table.

### Verification

- Requested `npx vitest run src/components/editor src/lib/query-analyzer src/lib/queries/sql/explain src/components/common src/components/health-inspector`: **17 files / 163 tests passed**.
- Requested `npm run build`: **passed**, including TypeScript. Existing warnings: browser-externalized `node:zlib`, ineffective mixed static/dynamic imports, and large chunks.
- Independent temporary Vitest harness under `/private/tmp/politedb-analyzer-review`: **9 expected regression failures / 1 pass**. Failures cover three lexer cases, trailing-comment statement count, off-page keyboard entry, Recommendations pagination, hottest shortcut pagination, CTE attribution and comparison persistence. Passing test verifies non-contiguous depth parent inference. Initial harness setup lacked Vitest globals; corrected before the reported execution. These are additional review probes, not failures of the requested existing suite.
- `git diff --check`: **passed** before report append.

### Other scope observations and limits

- `planParents` correctly uses a monotonic depth stack rather than assuming depth increments of one; synthetic arbitrary-depth coverage passed. Ordinary tree subtraction, time × Actual Loops, estimated-cost fallback and parallel share/nonnegative clamping are present. Existing tests cover nested loops and a Gather overlap case. This does not prove exclusive attribution for every engine: MySQL normalized costs can be absent/noninclusive, and multi-root tabular plans use only the first root as denominator. Do not claim a complete cost decomposition for those inputs without a defined metric contract. No independent live plan matrix for PostgreSQL InitPlan/SubPlan/CTE/Append/Gather/Memoize or MySQL/SQLite/SQL Server/Snowflake/Oracle was run.
- SqlResultsPane hooks remain before its conditional return; active-plan memo dependencies include explain kind, engine, Pro, slot and stream inputs. Typed EXPLAIN disables generic fallback, and current tests verify raw-table retention for non-plan results and the Pro upgrade banner. Direct baseline parsing is deferred until selection. Earlier-versus-later ordering is not enforced: every other completed run is offered, even while viewing an older active run; labels should reflect this or enumeration should restrict chronology.
- Export still checks Pro before confirmation/dialog/I/O and retains private-identifiers/literals consent. Existing denial/cancel tests pass. Baseline import retains 5 MB and parser node/metadata limits plus replacement-version checks. MySQL scalar fields remain available through grouped/Other details and existing regression passes. Single-node UI hides shares/filter/order controls. JSON stringify is memoized by plan; copy handles unavailable clipboard.
- `InfoHint.tsx` is an unchanged move; health-inspector import updated and tooltip regressions pass. Dark-mode classes are present in the rewritten views. No independent rendered browser/native/VoiceOver, contrast, responsive-layout or clipboard smoke run in this review; DOM tests do not establish visual/accessibility completion. Tabs also need a complete keyboard/tabpanel accessibility pass after the required focus fixes.

Next: Claude fixes the findings, adds focused regressions, runs the requested checks and requests Codex re-review. No source implementation, commit, push or merge performed by this review.

## Implementation review: Explain Analyze Revision 5 + focus replay — 2026-10-23

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Branch: `feat/query-analyzer-polish`. Scope: uncommitted implementation (staged, unstaged and task-owned untracked files) against APPROVED `.agent/plans/query-analyzer-explain-analyze.md`, Revision 5, its Claude implementation handoff and round-5 constraints. Read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` and RTK guidance first. No application source/test changes, staging or commits. Prior report content preserved; this section appended. No live database accessed.

### Required findings

1. **P1 — Server notices bypass the fixed-category privacy boundary through the connection driver.** `src-tauri/src/engines/postgres/explain_analyze.rs:534` creates a connection using the existing manager; `src-tauri/src/engines/postgres/driver.rs:155` constructs that manager with the default `Manager::from_config` connector. Installed locked dependency source establishes the path: `deadpool-postgres-0.14.1/src/lib.rs:221-225` spawns `connection.await` and logs connection errors verbatim; `tokio-postgres-0.7.18/src/connection.rs:349-351` logs every server NOTICE's severity and primary message at info level. `src-tauri/src/main.rs:86-88` installs the environment-controlled tracing subscriber. Thus a permitted SELECT function emitting a NOTICE containing customer data reaches logs whenever that target/level is enabled (including diagnostic logging), despite `error_category` sanitizing returned errors. D6 explicitly includes notices and logs; logger defaults do not satisfy that contract. **Fix:** use an analysis connection driver that consumes asynchronous messages without logging server text and sanitizes connection-task failures; preserve config/SSH and disposable teardown. Add hostile NOTICE/connection-error logger-capture tests, alongside primary/DETAIL/HINT tests. Static dependency-path evidence; no hostile SQL executed during review.

2. **P2 — Pre-dispatch checks omit statement/profile identity and compare only effective safety mode.** `src/screens/connection/hooks/useSqlRunner.ts:701-718` snapshots engine/effective mode/lock and compares connection/engine/lock/Pro; it has no current statement or originating-profile identity input. `src/components/editor/SqlEditorPane.tsx:483-490` passes only a one-time string, so edits during async authentication/listener initialization cannot invalidate the click. A locked profile changing raw mode from safe to production also passes because both normalize to lock. Revision-5 step 6 explicitly requires refusal on statement, profile and safety-mode changes. **Fix:** bind the attempt to source identity/revision (editor statement for toolbar; immutable run/slot for plan remeasure), originating profile tab/assignment and raw safety mode; recheck live values immediately before execution IPC. Add zero-execution/history tests for those changes during Touch ID, confirmation and listener initialization. Independent temporary hook probe reproduced the locked safe→production case: dispatch occurred once where zero was required.

3. **P2 — Confirmation failure leaks the per-window attempt guard.** `src/screens/connection/hooks/useSqlRunner.ts:684` reserves ownership; `:764-773` awaits `confirmDialog` without catch/finally. The native wrapper at `src/lib/system-dialog/index.ts:94` propagates rejection. If dialog IPC rejects, execution exits without `release()`, and every later click returns at runner `:677` until window teardown/reset. **Fix:** handle confirmation/start exceptions with fixed failure text and attempt-owned cleanup, preserving ownership after successful adoption only until a terminal event. Add rejection→second-click regression; do not print raw IPC errors. Independent temporary probe reproduced this: the second click never reopened confirmation (one call instead of two).

4. **P2 — Cancel is labelled but is not the default confirmation action.** `src/screens/connection/hooks/useSqlRunner.ts:764-771` uses ordinary `confirmDialog` with positive `okLabel`; `src/lib/system-dialog/index.ts:94` forwards it to Tauri confirm. Locked `tauri-plugin-dialog` 2.6.0 `src/commands.rs:357-364` builds `OkCancelCustom(positive, cancel)`, and locked `rfd` 0.16.0 `src/backend/macos/message_dialog.rs:50-66` adds the positive button first without changing the default key equivalent. On macOS this leaves the first NSAlert button as the Return/default action, contrary to the approved “Cancel is the default button” contract. **Fix:** use a dialog mechanism that explicitly defaults focus/Return to cancellation, with native validation of Enter/Escape and separate explicit approval. Existing mocked dialog tests verify labels/boolean results, not the native default. Native dialog not opened in this review.

5. **P2 — Confirmation omits the approved lost-response/history and isolated-session disclosures.** `src/screens/connection/hooks/useSqlRunner.ts:109-110,763-765` says history is added “once it starts”; actual insertion is only on live ID adoption at `:827-828`. D6 specifically requires disclosure that an attempt may have executed without a history entry if cancelled/closed before ID delivery or if the start response is lost. The displayed confirmation also omits that this is a fresh session without editor temp tables/SET state and that setup/cleanup are outside the 60-second statement limit. **Fix:** replace the history claim with the approved adoption/late-response limitation; disclose isolated session and phase timing in confirmation/help before execution, retaining function/remote-effect and instrumentation warnings. Assert the complete required disclosures, not just inclusion of the main confirmation constant.

6. **P2 — Setup receives two separate ten-second budgets.** `src-tauri/src/engines/postgres/explain_analyze.rs:534,566` applies `within(SETUP_LIMIT)` once to connect and again to BEGIN/settings/prepare. `within` subtracts only the 80-second total (`:530-531`), so a nine-second connect permits another ten seconds of setup. D5 and backend phase 1 require ten seconds combined for connect + BEGIN + settings + prepare. **Fix:** establish one absolute setup deadline before connect and consume its remaining duration for all setup work (or timeout one complete setup future). Preserve total/query/cleanup bounds. Add paused-time fake-I/O tests for slow connect followed by slow setup and for cancellation/cleanup; current live smoke and category tests do not establish this timing contract.

7. **P2 — Plan remeasure uses the whole Explain run instead of the displayed statement.** `src/components/editor/SqlResultsPane.tsx:580-585` passes `activeRun.sql`; `src/screens/connection/hooks/useSqlRunner.ts:361-375` stores all original source SQL there while slots contain separate wrapped statements. Explain of `SELECT 1; SELECT 2` produces separate plans, but “Run with ANALYZE” on either passes both statements and is refused by the single-statement classifier. The approved entry point must remeasure exactly the statement that produced the selected plan. **Fix:** retain each slot's original statement separately from its EXPLAIN wrapper and pass that immutable statement plus the original connection identity. Add multi-statement Explain→select each slot→remeasure argument assertions, plus connection-change refusal; keep ordinary Run/Explain SQL/history unchanged.

### Contracts assessed / focus re-review

- **Read-only/rollback:** successful path prepares and executes only after READ ONLY and standard_conforming_strings setup; buffers rows; awaits explicit rollback before returning to emit chunk/done (`explain_analyze.rs:550-594`, `:484-505`). Early error/abort drops the disposable client; installed deadpool `ClientWrapper::drop` aborts its connection task. No shared-pool checkout or commit in the new path. No live assertion of rollback/termination performed here.
- **Classification:** shared fixture tests pass in both languages; conservative keyword refusal, row-lock/parameter/multiple-statement/unterminated checks remain. Removing END in both languages is a justified CASE compatibility deviation; leading allowlist and prepared single-statement parsing remain. No additional actionable classifier parity defect established by this review.
- **Permit/registration:** atomic DashMap entry acquisition; ownership moved into spawned future by value; oneshot gate prevents completion before handle registration. Four focused Rust tests pass, including abort before first poll. Default-path dependency wiring compiles. Disabled-Postgres feature build remains unverified and handoff documents pre-existing failures; new unguarded module/dispatcher references also require attention before claiming feature-off support.
- **Late IDs/streams:** dedicated wrapper initializes listeners before dispatch, never busy-retries, races pending init/start against abort/absolute deadline and detaches late-ID cancellation without awaiting cancel IPC. Attempt identity prevents an old finalizer releasing a newer owner. Terminal-only stream bypasses ordinary idle completion and 65-second unsubscribe; adopted watchdog uses remaining attempt budget. Hook/wrapper/stream tests pass for covered cases. Best-effort orphan-cache expiry remains the accepted limitation; no durable delivery claim.
- **History:** dedicated post-adoption insertion captures the original profile-tab key and checks that tab still exists with the same runtime; ordinary Run retains its earlier history route and legacy entries remain supported. Refused/late-abandoned starts do not insert in covered tests. Missing disclosure and context checking are findings above.
- **Privacy:** new error categories and frontend fixed-message mapping avoid ordinary PostgreSQL formatting/analytics; no new automatic plan transmission/telemetry found. This does not repair the dependency notice/connection logging in finding 1. Touch ID errors retain the existing authentication path; database errors must never use that raw-text path.
- **Earlier focus-replay P2: resolved for the reported interaction.** `src/components/editor/query-analyzer/PlanTree.tsx:170-180` acknowledges successful focus; `QueryAnalyzer.tsx:453-454` clears the parent-owned request. Returning to Plan no longer replays the consumed nonce; fresh-nonce repeat reveals remain supported. Repository regression at `QueryAnalyzer.test.tsx:452-470` passes actual-focus assertions and the next tab-navigation key. No new P3 finding; real keyboard/native/screen-reader validation remains a limitation.

### Independent verification and limits

- `npm run build`: **passed**, TypeScript and production bundle. Warnings: browser-externalized `node:zlib`, mixed static/dynamic imports, large chunks and plugin timing.
- `npx vitest run`: **184 files passed / 1 failed; 1325 tests passed / 1 failed**. Failure: untouched `src/screens/main/ConnectionLogsSection.test.tsx:119`, missing “Today” grouping, matching the handoff's reported date-sensitive failure. All Explain Analyze/classifier/history/stream/UI/focus tests in this run passed. No clean-full-suite claim.
- `cargo test` from `src-tauri`: **409 passed / 47 failed / 18 ignored** (plus empty additional suite). Failures include AI/model/license HTTP fixtures unable to bind loopback under sandbox (`Operation not permitted`) and consequent assertions. These are outside the reviewed diff; not attributed to this implementation. No ignored tests enabled, no database connection requested, no environment secrets printed.
- `cargo test explain_analyze` from `src-tauri`: **4 passed / 1 ignored / 469 filtered out**. Ignored live smoke not run.
- External probe `/private/tmp/politedb-ea-review/review.test.ts`, using a copy of the hook suite plus two independent assertions: **32 passed / 2 failed**. Failing assertions reproduce findings 2 (raw mode change still dispatches while locked) and 3 (dialog rejection retains guard). Config and copied test remain outside repository; no application source/tests edited.
- `git diff --check`: **passed** before append. No live DB, native dialog/webview, manual keyboard/screen-reader or PG 12–17 verification performed. Claude's PostgreSQL 16.15 smoke evidence was read, not independently reproduced. Hostile notice/error logger-capture, dispatcher cancellation/concurrency integration, rollback-failure and phase-budget fake-I/O coverage remain needed. The handoff's live evidence and acknowledged feature-off limitations do not replace these gates.

## Implementation re-review: Explain Analyze post-fix + modal keyboard — 2026-10-23

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Branch: `feat/query-analyzer-polish`. Scope: uncommitted implementation, including task-owned untracked files, and Claude's “Fixes after Codex implementation review (Claude, 2026-10-23)” in `.agent/plans/query-analyzer-explain-analyze.md:415`. Read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, RTK instructions and preceding Revision-5 review first. Only this report appended; no application source/test edits, staging or commits. No live database, ignored database tests, or secrets accessed/printed.

### Findings

- **P1:** No remaining P1 established in this pass. Analysis-session NOTICE and connection-task logging path is removed; live hostile-server verification remains a limitation below.
- **P2 — New confirmation does not isolate keyboard input from the workspace.** `src/components/editor/query-analyzer/ExplainAnalyzeConfirmHost.tsx:35-46` focuses Cancel but delegates modality to `Dialog`. `src/components/common/Dialog.tsx:73-80,117-118` supplies an Escape listener and `aria-modal` only: no key propagation boundary, focus trap, background inertness or focus restoration. Keys from either confirmation button bubble to `useConnectionShortcuts.ts:65-72,128-146`: Ctrl/Cmd+S invokes background `beforeSaveChanges`, Ctrl/Cmd+W closes the workspace target, and search/snippet shortcuts can stack another dialog. The Save path can open the unrelated save confirmation (`useConnectionActions.ts:729-747`) while Explain Analyze confirmation remains pending. Tab/Shift+Tab also have no boundary preventing navigation into underlying controls. This is a regression from replacing the native confirmation with an in-app modal, even though the shared Dialog weakness predates this change. **Fix:** give this confirmation proper focus containment/restoration and keyboard isolation; suspend background workspace shortcuts while it is open (including handlers registered on window), preserving explicit Run, default Cancel and Escape. Add integration coverage with the real shortcut hook for Save/Close/Search/Snippets and actual Tab/Shift+Tab/Enter behavior. **Independent reproduction:** external `/private/tmp/politedb-ea-rereview/dialog.test.tsx` renders the real host plus `useConnectionShortcuts`, opens confirmation, sends Ctrl+S from focused Cancel, and asserts zero Save callbacks; fails with **one callback**. No source files changed by the probe. Existing host test `ExplainAnalyzeConfirmHost.test.tsx:47-55` checks focus then clicks the active element; it does not exercise Enter or global shortcut isolation.
- **P3 — Test-only dependencies were also added to Windows production dependencies.** `src-tauri/Cargo.toml:144-149` repeats `log`, `tracing-log` and Tokio `test-util` under `[target.'cfg(target_os = "windows")'.dependencies]`, in addition to the intended `[dev-dependencies]` at `:157-162`. Windows release builds therefore enable test clock/runtime support and carry direct test-only dependencies. **Fix:** remove the accidental Windows copies; retain the dev-dependency declarations. No Windows release build performed.

### Previous finding disposition (code and test evidence)

1. **P1 notices/logs: resolved by static path and available unit tests; live check not repeated.** `explain_analyze.rs:535-545` uses `Config::connect` plus `poll_message`, drops asynchronous server messages and ends silently on connection errors. `SessionTask` aborts the disposable driver on drop (`:518-524`); no deadpool connection task in this path. `connection.rs:11` stores config; `driver.rs:150-161,181` builds it through the existing helper, clones it for the unchanged pool, and stores the same settings; dispatcher uses `pg.config.clone()` (`operations/dispatcher.rs:69`). Endpoint/credentials/application name and rewritten SSH endpoint are retained through that config. `config.rs:113-118` explicitly allowlists only this additional production opener; its architecture test passes. `log_filter.rs:18-41` intersects the environment filter with a hard target/level cap, used at `main.rs:87-89`; tracing and bridged-log tests (`log_filter.rs:92,114`) pass, including specific target directives. These test the budget helper, not live connect/rollback failure behavior.

### Routing, safety and compatibility evidence

- **One Explain button:** `src/components/editor/SqlEditorToolbar.tsx:83-106` renders mutually exclusive branches with the same Explain label. Pro callback takes precedence; Free keeps the estimate callback; both disable while executing. There is no separate Analyze button or estimated-plan menu. `SqlEditorToolbar.test.tsx:8-53` passes single-button, callback precedence and disabled-state assertions. `SqlEditorPane.tsx:479-506,814-815` still picks the selected/current SQL, flushes its draft, and supplies the live-source predicate for measured execution. Ordinary Run remains on its existing callback (`SqlWindowPane.tsx:136-140`).
- **Classifier routing:** `src/lib/sql/analyzeClassify.ts:28-39` restricts support to PostgreSQL and derives `explainModeFor` directly from `classifyForAnalyze`. `SqlWindowPane.tsx:142-155` calls the measured entry only for accepted SQL, forwards `sourceUnchanged`, and otherwise calls ordinary `startExplain`. No fallback calls ordinary Run. The measured hook classifies again (`useSqlRunner.ts:756-766`); backend classification wins independently (`src-tauri/src/operations/dispatcher.rs:54-70`). Shared fixtures pass in both languages. Refused DML/locking SQL therefore reaches estimated EXPLAIN, not Explain Analyze. Empty/invalid/multiple/parameterized/already-EXPLAIN input may still produce the existing fixed Explain error rather than a plan; the human's routing change does not remove builder validation (`src/lib/queries/sql/explain.ts:39-107`).
- **Confirmation and policies:** routed Analyze still calls the single `startExplainAnalyze` path. Pro and engine checks precede classification; read Touch ID applies through the shared effective-lock/read policy; confirmation is awaited, and cancellation exits (`useSqlRunner.ts:694-779`). `stillAllowed` compares current runtime, engine, raw safety mode, lock, Pro, originating tab/current assignment and source predicate (`:703-719`). It runs after authentication/confirmation and through the wrapper after listener initialization, immediately before IPC (`:773-809`; `src/lib/tauri/explainAnalyze.ts:106-132`). The UI callback never directly invokes the operation. Existing tests exercise cancellation, mode/lock/engine/tab/source changes, lost Pro and pre-IPC invalidation. Profile-lock precedence and accepted function/extension side-effect risk remain the approved D3 contract, not a new no-side-effects guarantee.
- **Pro/trial expiry while open:** UI visibility subscribes through `useProGate` (`SqlWindowPane.tsx:106-107,182-190`; `src/hooks/useProGate.ts:11-28`). Published access loss removes both measured callbacks; the next Explain uses estimated Explain. Action-time `hasProAccessNow` computes access against `Date.now()` (`src/stores/license.ts:241-243`; `src/lib/license/proAccess.ts:40-53`), so a stale render or expiry during authentication/confirmation/listener initialization cannot dispatch a new measurement. License expiry reload/focus machinery remains at `src/stores/license.ts:83-124`. UI switching depends on that async store publication; the hook itself has no clock subscription, so a delayed/failed reload can temporarily leave the measured-looking entry visible, but the action guard refuses execution. An already-dispatched operation is not cancelled solely because the license expires; no new measured dispatch is authorized by that fact. No automatic execution after upgrade.
- **Other engines:** `SqlWindowPane.tsx:107` withholds measured toolbar and result callbacks for every engine except PostgreSQL, regardless of tier. Their Explain builder/Run routes remain in place. Backend independently rejects non-PostgreSQL. Non-PostgreSQL Explain still has its existing per-engine support/error behavior; no claim that every engine supports every statement.
- **Analyzer remeasure:** `SqlResultsPane.tsx:582-599` requires an Explain-produced slot, its own `sourceSql`, an accepted classifier result and a supplied measured callback. It uses that slot's source and original connection. `QueryAnalyzer.tsx:330-337` additionally requires an estimated plan. Existing result tests (`SqlResultsPane.test.tsx:360-448`) distinguish slot/run text and cover refused UPDATE, legacy missing-source, typed EXPLAIN, measured plans and missing handlers. A user-typed EXPLAIN remains on the ordinary Run route; it does not acquire this guided action merely by returning a plan. Previous Free-generated Explain slots can acquire it after upgrading when they retain measurable source.
- **Whole-change reassessment:** isolated quiet session, server read-only transaction and settings before prepare/query, bounded setup/query/cleanup, explicit rollback before plan publication, RAII permit/session cleanup, fixed errors and driver logging cap remain intact by static inspection (`explain_analyze.rs:446-471,478-545,549-653`; `log_filter.rs:18-41`). ## Implementation review: Query Analyzer batch 3 — 2026-10-23

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Branch: `feat/query-analyzer-polish`. Scope: `git diff HEAD` plus both untracked test files; `.claude/launch.json` excluded. Read `AGENTS.md` and `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` first, then RTK guidance. No application source/test changes, staging or commits. Only this section appended. All probe inputs below are synthetic.

### Findings (P1/P2/P3)

1. **P1 — Literal contents leak into the supposedly literal-free suggested SQL.** `src/lib/query-analyzer/findings.ts:43-47,66-71` scans the entire Filter string without recognizing SQL string tokens. Reproduction: Filter `(note = '(secret_customer = 123)'::text)` produces `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY ON orders (note, secret_customer);`. `secret_customer` comes exclusively from the literal value, not a column reference. A quoted identifier-shaped substring inside that literal can carry spaces and other private text too. On a selective measured scan this reaches the new unconfirmed clipboard action (`src/components/editor/query-analyzer/QueryAnalyzer.tsx:78-81,560`) and Markdown suggestion (`src/lib/query-analyzer/report.ts:18-20`). Copy-only prevents automatic execution, but does not satisfy the explicit never-include-literal-values contract. Tokenize or parse the supported SQL subset, skip all literal tokens, and reject uncertain filters; add literal-with-predicate-text and escaped-quote regressions.

2. **P2 — Identifier quoting can produce invalid SQL and break identifier boundaries.** `src/lib/query-analyzer/findings.ts:51-53,75-79` treats raw PostgreSQL JSON relation/schema names as if they were already SQL-quoted, does not double embedded quotes, and leaves lowercase reserved words unquoted. Synthetic raw names `Order"Items`, `select`, and `"Odd Table` produce respectively `ON "Order"Items"`, `ON select`, and `ON "Odd Table`. Real quoted table/schema names can contain double quotes; interpreting those characters as syntax can also introduce additional statement text in a copied template. Encode raw metadata identifiers separately from parsed identifier tokens; always double internal quotes and wrap raw names in double quotes. Cover reserved words, embedded/leading quotes and schema names.

3. **P2 — Column extraction discards predicate context and relation ownership.** `src/lib/query-analyzer/findings.ts:45-46,66-79` accepts comparisons anywhere, discards the optional qualifier, and merges them into one multicolumn B-tree suggestion. `((o.id = 1) AND (outer_customer.secret_id = 2))`, on relation `orders` / alias `o`, emits `(id, secret_id)` even when `secret_id` belongs only to the outer relation. Valid correlated scan filters can reference outer Vars. `((a = 1) OR (b = 2))` emits `(a, b)` as though these were conjunctions; that index cannot directly provide the intended combined OR scan. Nested comparisons inside function/CASE arguments can similarly be mistaken for top-level searchable predicates. Restrict suggestions to a recognized conjunction of supported simple predicates on this scan's own columns, validating qualifiers and operand shapes; reject OR/NOT/expression/cast cases that cannot be proven safe. Direct `lower(name)` and casted-column examples currently yield no suggestion, which is safer than guessing, but those examples do not establish general expression safety. PostgreSQL describes the OR distinction in [Combining Multiple Indexes](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/indexes-bitmap-scans.html).

4. **P2 — Nested-loop rule reads the wrong child when InitPlans exist.** `src/lib/query-analyzer/findings.ts:186-196` selects `children[node.id][1]`. PostgreSQL emits InitPlans before Outer and Inner children; `[InitPlan loops=1, Outer loops=1, Inner loops=5000]` therefore produces no warning. Multiple InitPlans can also cause false positives based on unrelated children. Select `Parent Relationship === "Inner"` for PostgreSQL, using a positional fallback only for formats that genuinely lack relationship metadata. Add both offset-child and unrelated-child regressions. Confirmed against [PostgreSQL EXPLAIN implementation](https://github.com/postgres/postgres/blob/REL_18_STABLE/src/backend/commands/explain.c), `ExplainNode` child emission order.

5. **P2 — Rechecks/lossy blocks do not prove a work_mem overflow.** `src/lib/query-analyzer/findings.ts:227-233` reports any node with at least 1,000 index recheck removals as a lossy bitmap and promises that more work_mem keeps it exact. A GiST **Index Scan** with 5,000 recheck removals reproduces this bitmap warning without a bitmap at all. Bitmap scans over BRIN can have lossy blocks inherently, independent of work_mem. Restrict bitmap labeling to Bitmap Heap Scan and distinguish inherent access-method rechecks from possible memory-driven lossiness; make remedies conditional. Recheck removals are per-loop averages, so the displayed count must be labeled per loop or multiplied by Actual Loops. See [EXPLAIN ANALYZE](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/using-explain.html) and [BRIN](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/brin.html).

6. **P2 — Temporary-write warning incorrectly attributes inclusive buffers to each ancestor.** `src/lib/query-analyzer/findings.ts:177-182` suppresses only a node's own sort/hash warning. An Aggregate with `Temp Written Blocks=300` over a disk Sort with the same counter generates an Aggregate spill warning plus the actual Sort warning, claiming that the Aggregate's working data exceeded work_mem. The counter includes descendants; a deep pipeline can repeat the same writes at every ancestor and misdirect tuning. Describe this as subtree temporary I/O, or attribute only established own writes and suppress covered ancestor duplicates; avoid claiming the counter alone proves this operator exceeded work_mem. Add an ancestor-over-sort/hash test. [EXPLAIN BUFFERS](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/18/sql-explain.html) documents inclusive buffer counters. The hardcoded 8 kB is also a default build size, not a universal block-size guarantee.

7. **P2 — JIT share compares aggregate parallel work with wall-clock time.** `src/lib/query-analyzer/findings.ts:248-266` correctly reads document-level `JIT.Timing.Total`, but PostgreSQL aggregates leader and worker JIT instrumentation. Concurrent workers' total compilation time is not a fraction of statement wall time. A synthetic parallel document with `JIT.Timing.Total=800` and `Execution Time=500` emits “JIT took 800 ms of 500 ms”; even totals below wall time can incorrectly cross the 10% threshold. Distinguish aggregate compilation work from elapsed execution, and limit the wall-time-share heuristic to serial execution or derive defensible parallel attribution. Missing JIT Timing (e.g. TIMING OFF) correctly produces no finding. Source: [ExplainPrintJITSummary](https://github.com/postgres/postgres/blob/REL_18_STABLE/src/backend/commands/explain.c) merges leader and worker instrumentation before printing Timing.Total.

8. **P2 — Stream cache stays stale after same-count metadata/row updates.** `src/components/editor/SqlResultsPane.tsx:480-489,493-501,534-545` keys both paths only by opId, row count, engine and kind. Stream updates can replace columns or row slots without changing totalRows (`src/screens/connection/hooks/useSqlStreamResult.ts:270-276,285-296`), and `rowsVersion` is available but unused. External component regression: start done with totalRows=1, empty columns and a plan row; then publish done with QUERY PLAN columns, the same totalRows and a newer rowsVersion. Analyzer stays absent because cached null wins even though the column dependency reruns the memo. Row replacements have the same problem; with stable columns/getRowAt the memo also omits rowsVersion. Include a content/metadata revision in both cache keys and the memo dependencies, or otherwise invalidate when snapshot contents change. Keep the existing same-result tab-switch performance and late-row-count regression tests.

**P3:** no additional blocking defect established. New Copy controls have visible text, are native keyboard-focusable buttons, and severity uses text as well as color. Repeated generic Copy names and silent clipboard failures could be improved with node-specific accessible labels and a status announcement; no native screen-reader or actual clipboard session was available to validate those refinements.

### Rule and compatibility assessment

- Severity ordering is stable: high, medium, low, with insertion/plan order retained within each level. Existing selective-scan, estimate, sort and hash conditions are preserved apart from severity/attached SQL. Existing per-loop selectivity/estimate comparisons remain comparable; heap-fetch comparison correctly multiplies Actual Rows by Actual Loops rather than treating Heap Fetches as a per-loop average. Returned rows exclude filtered rows, so that ratio is a heuristic, not a physical visibility-map percentage. VACUUM cannot guarantee all-visible pages under ongoing writes/snapshots.
- SubPlan detection correctly uses Parent Relationship and excludes InitPlan; loops >=1,000 establishes repeated execution, not necessarily every outer row (conditional predicates can skip evaluations). Avoid treating the wording as a guarantee or recommending semantics-changing joins for scalar/NULL/cardinality-sensitive subqueries. Likewise many cheap nested-loop index probes, Memoize hits or Materialize rescans are not proof a hash/merge join is cheaper; actual work and join feasibility still matter.
- Workers Planned/Launched comparison correctly requires both fields and launched < planned, and skips explicitly unexecuted nodes. It does not incorrectly treat missing launched counts in estimated plans as zero. Resource-limit diagnosis is appropriately tentative, though runtime eligibility and max_parallel_workers_per_gather can also matter.
- JSON view limits displayed source text to 200,000 UTF-16 characters plus an ellipsis (`QueryAnalyzer.tsx:132-145`); full-document serialization/copy/export remains intact. This limits DOM layout, not serialization cost. Node/recommendation paging remains bounded at 100 rows; no requirement to virtualize established here.
- Direct WeakMap uses result identity; variants separate engine and Explain/typed fallback. Pro gating runs before cache lookup. External tier-loss/regrant plus engine/kind-switch regression passes, as do both supplied cache tests. Running/error streams are not read from the done cache; same-count revision issue above remains. Stream LRU is capped at 16 entries; direct entries can be collected with their result objects. Strong stream entries retain up to 16 plans after result closure until eviction; no network/telemetry sink added.
- Suggested SQL has no execution handler. Full JSON copy intentionally preserves private content, as before; JSON/report exports retain explicit privacy confirmation and native local-file wrappers (`QueryAnalyzer.tsx:231-260`). Markdown now includes severity and suggested SQL; the P1 finding invalidates the claimed literal-free subset. No new raw-plan logging or telemetry found. Export/copy is not redaction.

### Independent verification and limits

- `npm run build`: **passed** (TypeScript plus production bundle). Warnings: browser-externalized node:zlib, mixed static/dynamic imports and large chunks.
- `npx vitest run`: **189 files / 1,359 tests passed**, including all four requested test files. Existing suite does not cover the demonstrated edge cases.
- External pure-function probes transpile the actual unmodified plan/findings modules into `/private/tmp/politedb-b3-nOxKeQ/`; reproduce literal leakage, malformed quoting, foreign-column/OR suggestions, missing nested-loop finding, non-bitmap recheck warning, inherited temporary-write warning and parallel JIT wording. These are synthetic fixtures checked against current official PostgreSQL docs/source, not live-server EXPLAIN captures.
- External component harness `/private/tmp/politedb-b3-nOxKeQ/cache.test.tsx`, external Vite config: **3 passed / 1 failed**. Both supplied cache tests and added tier/engine/fallback control pass; expected-failing same-count metadata regression reproduces finding 8. Harness explicitly cleans DOM before each test. Initial exploratory fixture used a nonempty arbitrary column name, which the existing parser accepts; final reproduction uses empty metadata and fails at the intended final analyzer assertion.

## Re-review: Query Analyzer batch 3 — 2026-10-23

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Branch confirmed: `feat/query-analyzer-polish`. Read `AGENTS.md` and `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` first, then RTK guidance and the previous batch-3 review. Scope: complete application/test diff against HEAD, including both untracked test files; `.claude/launch.json` excluded. Only this review section appended; no application source/test edits, staging or commits. Previous report preserved. Inputs in external probes are synthetic.

### Findings (P1/P2/P3)

**P1:** no remaining literal-leak reproduction or new P1 established.

1. **P2 — Predicate context is still inferred from parenthesis depth rather than validated.** `src/lib/query-analyzer/findings.ts:92-111`: depth <= 2 does not establish that a comparison is a top-level conjunct. Current-code probes return `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY ON "orders" (a);` for `COALESCE((a = 1), false)` and `bool_identity((a = 1))`, and `(a, b)` for `((a = 1) = (b = 2))`. The latter is equality between boolean expressions, not `a = 1 AND b = 2`; arbitrary boolean functions can invert or otherwise transform their argument. The supplied `coalesce` regression adds an extra outer pair of parentheses, which happens to push its comparison past the depth cutoff; removing that unnecessary wrapper bypasses the refusal. These suggestions reach the measured selective-scan recommendation and Markdown/clipboard paths. **Fix:** recognize the complete supported predicate grammar (conjunction plus supported column comparison/null-test leaves), including expression boundaries, rather than accepting every shallow opening parenthesis. Refuse unsupported surrounding expressions; add wrapper-free function/COALESCE and boolean-equality regressions. The early exit at line 117 should cap collected columns without ending validation of the remaining filter: a foreign qualifier after three accepted columns currently bypasses the documented whole-filter refusal too.

2. **P2 — SQL value keywords are emitted as column names.** `src/lib/query-analyzer/findings.ts:85-86,100-115`: every lexer `word` is assumed to be an identifier, but this lexer deliberately does not distinguish keywords. `(CURRENT_USER = owner)` produces `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY ON "orders" (CURRENT_USER);`; `(CURRENT_DATE > created_at)` produces an index on `CURRENT_DATE`. These are SQL value expressions, not columns of the scanned relation, so the generated column-list templates cannot be used as advertised. Quoting these words would instead reference nonexistent columns; raw relation-name escaping does not solve operand classification. **Fix:** reject keyword/value-expression operands within the supported grammar, or use parsing that establishes a column reference. Add SQL-value keyword/constant regressions alongside quoted columns such as `"current_user"` that really are identifiers.

**P3 — Safe but unnecessarily broad refusal of `IS NOT NULL`.** `src/lib/query-analyzer/findings.ts:72-77,108-110`: `(deleted_at IS NULL)` produces an index template, but `(deleted_at IS NOT NULL)` and `((id = 1) AND (deleted_at IS NOT NULL))` suppress it entirely because all `NOT` words are rejected. This preserves safety and is nonblocking; distinguish `IS [NOT] NULL` leaves from boolean negation if null-test suggestions are intended. Casted left operands such as `((status)::text = 'x'::text)` are also refused; keeping uncertain expression indexes out is acceptable. Literal-side `::text` remains supported. No new blocking copy-control/accessibility defect established.

### Previous finding dispositions

- **Literal leakage — RESOLVED for prior probes.** `findings.ts:66-78,85-86`: strings remain indivisible tokens; predicate-shaped literal contents and doubled quotes no longer become columns. Unterminated input is refused. The previous `secret_customer` and quoted-secret probes now emit only `note`. This is a literal-free suggestion subset, not redaction of the full plan/report.
- **Raw relation/schema quoting — RESOLVED.** `findings.ts:45-46,120-125`: every raw metadata identifier is wrapped and internal quotes doubled. Independent probes cover reserved words, embedded quotes, leading quotes and schemas. Quoted column tokens retain their SQL quoting. Keyword operand classification remains finding 2 above.
- **Predicate ownership / OR — PARTIALLY RESOLVED.** Prior foreign qualifier, OR, NOT and CASE probes are refused; ordinary own-alias conjunctions still work. Complete predicate-context validation remains finding 1 above.
- **Nested-loop InitPlan ordering — RESOLVED.** `findings.ts:240-248`: labelled Inner wins; positional fallback applies only when all direct children lack relationships. InitPlan-before-Outer/Inner probe now warns on the correct child; supplied unrelated-InitPlan regression passes.
- **Bitmap/recheck semantics — RESOLVED for reported cases.** `findings.ts:289-295`: only Bitmap Heap Scan with positive Lossy Heap Blocks triggers; non-bitmap GiST-style rechecks no longer warn. Recheck removals multiply by loops; BRIN exception is explicit. Exact-only bitmaps do not trigger. The index method is not inferred from a name.
- **Inclusive temporary writes — RESOLVED for reported ancestor duplication.** `findings.ts:222-234`: subtracts immediate child subtree counters; Aggregate over disk Sort with equal counters yields only the Sort warning. Block size is now qualified as usually 8 kB. Attribution still relies on complete, consistent input counters; no live PostgreSQL instrumentation matrix was run.
- **Parallel JIT share — RESOLVED.** `findings.ts:319-324`: any launched workers suppress the aggregate-work/wall-clock share heuristic. Parallel 800/500 probe emits none; serial 450/900 still warns. Missing timing remains harmless.
- **Stream cache revision — RESOLVED.** `SqlResultsPane.tsx:486,503,540`: active key, memo dependencies and baseline load key include rowsVersion. Previous same-count metadata failure now passes. Extra same-count row replacement with stable getter/columns passes. Cache remains gated by Pro/engine/result kind and done status.
- **Copy labels/status — RESOLVED in component coverage.** `QueryAnalyzer.tsx:78,96-97,565-568`: node-specific accessible name and live status announce successful copy. Clipboard rejection remains silent as before; actual native clipboard and assistive technology were not exercised.

### Whole-batch assessment and independent verification

- Reviewed severity ordering, all added heuristic branches, report SQL inclusion, copy-only control, JSON preview bound, direct WeakMap variants, stream LRU, lazy comparison loads and both untracked test files. Index Scan/Bitmap Heap Scan Filter fixtures do not acquire sequential-scan index recommendations. Existing estimate/selectivity, sort/hash, row-loop accounting and zero-loop guards remain intact. No new SQL execution, raw-plan logging, telemetry or export-confirmation bypass found. Full JSON copy/export still intentionally contains original plan data. Preview truncation bounds rendered text, not JSON serialization cost.
- `npm run build`: **passed**, TypeScript and production bundle. Existing browser-externalized node:zlib, mixed static/dynamic import and chunk-size warnings remain.
- `npx vitest run`: **189 files / 1,363 tests passed**. Supplied tests pass but do not cover the two remaining P2 cases.
- Reused external harness `/private/tmp/politedb-b3-nOxKeQ/cache.test.tsx`: **6 passed** after adding external-only eviction and stable-getter row-replacement checks. Previous same-count metadata probe now passes, as do tab reuse, late rows and tier/engine/fallback separation. Seventeen stream entries evict the least recently used entry; a recent entry stays cached and the evicted entry reparses on return. No repository test changed.
- External `/private/tmp/politedb-b3-nOxKeQ/recheck.mjs` uses freshly transpiled current findings/lexer modules and the prior unchanged plan module. Assertions pass for prior literal/quoting, OR/foreign qualifier, nested-loop, non-bitmap recheck, inclusive-temp and parallel-JIT probes. Printed expression/keyword outputs reproduce findings 1–2 and null-test/cast behavior above. These are synthetic source-level probes, not live-server captures. An initial esbuild-based probe setup could not resolve that package; switched to installed TypeScript transpilation without dependency changes.
- `npm run dev`: **blocked**, `listen EPERM ::1:1420`. No browser/native visual inspection, actual clipboard/screen-reader session, live PostgreSQL/version matrix or native performance verification. Rust checks not run because this batch changes only frontend code.

## Re-review 2: Query Analyzer batch 3 — 2026-10-23

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Branch: `feat/query-analyzer-polish`. Read `AGENTS.md`, `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`, RTK guidance and preceding review first. Scope: complete `git diff HEAD` plus both untracked application test files; `.claude/launch.json` excluded. Previous sections preserved. No application source/test modifications, staging or commits. Memory used only for architecture orientation; conclusions checked against current files.

### Findings (P1/P2/P3)

- **P1:** none established; prior literal-leak probes remain resolved.
- **P2 — Match complete supported operators before emitting a B-tree template.** `src/lib/query-analyzer/findings.ts:118-123` checks only the first punctuation token against `COMPARISON_START` (`:49`); `src/lib/sql/pgLexer.ts:124-126` emits punctuation one character at a time. Thus `<@` and `<<` pass as though they were ordinary `<` comparisons. Fresh current-code probes produce `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY ON "orders" (tags);` for `(tags <@ '{1,2}'::integer[])`, `(address)` for `(address << '10.0.0.0/8'::inet)`, and `(payload)` for `(payload <@ '{}'::jsonb)`. These are containment predicates, not B-tree ordering comparisons. The template at `findings.ts:176` always uses default B-tree, and all three reach the high-severity measured-scan finding at `:223-231`; copying this advice creates an index that does not provide the advertised predicate search. PostgreSQL documents ordinary B-tree search operators separately from containment methods: [Index Types](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/indexes-types.html), [Network operators](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/functions-net.html). **Fix:** consume the complete contiguous operator and allow only predicates supported by this generic template; refuse unknown/custom/containment operators unless choosing an appropriate method/operator class from sufficient evidence. Add regressions for `<@`, `<<`, `<<=`, `>>`, and preserved ordinary `<=`/`>=` comparisons. Apply explicit predicate validation to the `IS` branch too: it currently accepts `IS DISTINCT FROM` and `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` indiscriminately, rather than just the supported null tests. Pattern operators such as `(name !~~ '%abc%'::text)` also currently receive a generic B-tree template despite lacking an ordinary B-tree search condition. These are the same missing predicate-support boundary, not separate findings.
- **P3:** no additional actionable finding established. Conservative refusal of unsupported left-side expressions remains acceptable.

### Prior findings and edge-case dispositions

- **Previous P2 predicate context — RESOLVED for reported reproductions.** `findings.ts:129-149` validates a whole leaf or conjunction; wrapper-free `COALESCE((a = 1), false)`, `bool_identity((a = 1))`, and `((a = 1) = (b = 2))` now return undefined. Whole-filter validation continues beyond three columns: a fourth foreign qualifier or value keyword refuses the suggestion; four valid columns retain only the first three (`:169`). Own aliases, quoted columns, RHS functions/ANY/casts and ordinary conjunctions remain supported.
- **Previous P2 value keywords — RESOLVED for reported reproductions.** `findings.ts:54-70,104-106` rejects CURRENT_USER, CURRENT_DATE, CURRENT_SCHEMA and other listed SQL value operands; quoted `"current_user"` remains a column. Ordinary function calls are refused by the following-token check. No further standard parenthesis-free SQL value function missing from this list was established in this pass.
- **Previous P3 IS NOT NULL — RESOLVED.** Both `(deleted_at IS NOT NULL)` and the mixed id/null-test conjunction now produce the expected columns; IS NULL remains supported. This does not establish support for every other IS predicate.
- Literal-shaped strings/doubled quotes, unterminated input, raw relation/schema escaping, foreign qualifiers, OR/NOT/CASE, nested-loop labelled Inner after InitPlan, non-bitmap rechecks, inclusive temporary-write deduplication and parallel JIT suppression remain resolved in the reused probe suite.
- JSON `->` expressions, `@>` containment, left-side `::` casts, array subscripts and ROW comparisons are conservatively refused in independent controls. RHS casts remain supported. `<@` containment is the asymmetric unsafe acceptance above.

### Whole-batch review and verification
