## Review: ordered-list marker clipping — commit 0cd510e — 2026-10-23

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Reviewed `git show 0cd510e` (HEAD `0cd510e9c40668b78183ffefe2f83326e486169b`), parser/renderer, tests, both production consumers, and font/CSS context. Application source/tests unchanged; only this report appended. Untracked `.claude/launch.json` left untouched.

### Findings

- **P2 — Bound the ordered-list start before numeric conversion/rendering.** `src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.ts:446` converts unrestricted `\d+` into a Number; `src/components/ai-assistant/MarkdownText.tsx:65-71` calculates width from that Number, then passes it to the signed-long DOM `ol.start` property. Independent Preact/jsdom probe: `999999999999999999999. item` parses as `1e+21`, renders `start="-559939584"`, and receives `calc(5ch + 1rem)` despite its ten-character negative marker. A 309-digit all-nine prefix parses as `Infinity`, renders `start="0"`, and receives `calc(8ch + 1rem)`. Thus accepted input can silently change numbering and again under-allocate marker space. Earlier implementation had no start assignment, so these conversion/wrapping failures are introduced here. Define a supported decimal range (including room for subsequent items); reject/fall back to literal text outside it before lossy conversion, or use a representation/rendering strategy supporting larger numbers. Calculate width from the actual displayed sequence. Add regressions for oversized/overflowing prefixes and supported-range boundaries. Exact native WebView layout remains unverified; numeric DOM mismatch reproduced independently.

### Correctness and adjacent behavior

- Ordinary nonnegative starts: `start + items.length - 1` is the widest rendered counter within a supported exact range. Zero survives `?? 1`; `007.` correctly normalizes to numeric 7 with ordinary decimal markers. Subsequent source numbers remain intentionally ignored by native sequential numbering, as before. Blank-separated lists now preserve each block's own first number, consistent with the requested change.
- Two-digit list case passes; independent probes also cover zero, leading zeros, `9)` syntax and 99→100 width growth. Mixed ordered/unordered blocks retain separate tags; bullet padding/class remains unchanged. Indented continuation lines retain inline breaks. Native outside markers retain hanging alignment for visually wrapped text; actual wrapping geometry was not tested in jsdom.
- `tabular-nums` is inherited by list items/markers. For the configured Inter UI font, tabular figures make decimal digit advances uniform; `ch` measures the zero advance in the same font. The additional `1rem` reserves punctuation/gap. This is sound for ordinary Inter decimal markers; a proportional font lacking tabular figures would not guarantee every digit is as narrow as zero. No current font override establishing that regression was found. The utility also applies tabular figures to list body numbers, an intentional-looking small typography change. Font fallback/native marker rasterization was not visually verified.
- Production `MarkdownText` usage is limited to `AiAssistantMessageParts.tsx:227` (structured text parts) and `:521` (legacy assistant message text), including floating/sidebar variants. Both share this renderer; no other production parser consumers or persistence contracts found. Paragraphs, inline formatting, quotes, code and user-message paths are unchanged. No new HTML execution or remote-link behavior introduced.

### Verification

- `npm run test:run -- src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/MarkdownText.test.tsx`: **2 files / 12 tests passed**.
- `npm run test:run -- src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantMessageCard.test.tsx`: **1 file / 11 tests passed**.
- External `/private/tmp/politedb-0cd510e-review/edge.test.tsx`: **6 tests passed**, including four start/width cases, mixed lists/continuation and an assertion reproducing oversized-number mismatch (passing reproduction is not remediation). Captured DOM values: `/private/tmp/politedb-0cd510e-review/results.jsonl`. Initial harness resolution needed JSX/Preact aliases corrected; initial huge-number expectation assumed attribute parsing, then was corrected after observing property assignment.
- `npm run build`: **passed**; browser externalization, mixed static/dynamic imports and chunk-size warnings.
- `npm run dev`: **blocked**, `listen EPERM: operation not permitted ::1:1420`. No browser/native visual verification of marker clipping, font fallback or wrapped-line geometry. No Rust changes; Rust tests not required for this scope.
- Committed regression tests assert DOM/style strings only; they do not prove marker visibility. Add oversized-number coverage with the remediation; retain a real-browser visual check for 10/11 and 99/100 when available.


## Re-review: oversized ordered-list start — commit 0cd510e fix — 2026-10-23

**Verdict: VERIFIED WITH LIMITATIONS.** Reviewer: Codex. Scope: uncommitted `git diff -- src` fixing the preceding P2, plus parser/renderer and adjacent regression paths. No source/test edits, staging or commits. Existing report content and untracked `.claude/launch.json` preserved.

### Findings and fix disposition

- **P2 oversized ordered-list start — RESOLVED.** `src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.ts:358` anchors the complete marker and accepts only 1–9 ASCII digits before either delimiter. Ten-digit and larger prefixes cannot match a trailing nine-digit suffix; they reach paragraph text before `Number` conversion. Nine-digit values remain exact, finite and within the DOM signed-long range. This applies to both list entry and subsequent-item matching because both use `LIST_ITEM`.
- **Sequence bound — verified from an existing hard limit.** `MAX_MARKDOWN_CHARS = 200_000` and the early oversized-input fallback (`markdown.ts:37,374`) bound the number of list items to at most 200,000 (a conservative bound of one source character per item). Therefore even `999999999 + items.length - 1 <= 1000199998`, below 2147483647. This safety does not depend on an assumption about realistic list lengths. Oversized input itself bypasses list parsing.
- **Width and adjacent behavior — preserved.** Maximum accepted start with two items retains DOM `ol.start = 999999999` and allocates `calc(10ch + 1rem)` for the final counter 1000000000. Independent probes also pass for zero, leading zeros, `)` markers, 99→100, mixed bullets/ordered blocks and indented continuation text. An unindented oversized prefix between valid lists becomes a paragraph and splits the blocks; an indented oversized prefix remains literal continuation text in its current item, consistent with existing continuation handling. No new actionable regression established. Paragraph/inline rendering and HTML/link safety paths are unchanged.

### Independent verification and limitations

- `rtk npm run test:run -- src/lib/ai-assistant/markdown.test.ts src/components/ai-assistant/MarkdownText.test.tsx src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantMessageCard.test.tsx`: **3 files / 24 tests passed**, including added nine-digit boundary, ten-digit fallback and ten-character width assertions.
- `rtk npm exec -- vitest run --config /private/tmp/politedb-0cd510e-review/vitest.config.mjs /private/tmp/politedb-0cd510e-review/remediation.test.tsx`: **1 file / 10 tests passed**. External harness preserves the original reproduction file; verifies 10-, 21- and 309-digit literal fallback with both delimiters, exact boundary DOM start/width, block splitting/continuation, and preceding ordinary-list probes.
- `rtk npm run build`: **passed**; browser externalization, mixed static/dynamic import and chunk-size warnings remain.
- `rtk git diff --check`: **passed** before report append.
- No native WebView/browser visual session performed in this re-review. DOM numeric conversion and style allocation are verified; actual clipping, font fallback and wrapped-line geometry retain the preceding review's visual limitation. No Rust changes; Rust checks outside scope.

Working-tree observation: during this review HEAD advanced to `61b4bb5` (`fix(ai): keep ordered-list numbers to nine digits`), and the reviewed source diff became committed. Codex issued no commit command. Final working tree has only the report modified plus the existing untracked launch configuration.


## Review: AI chat UX batch 1 — working-tree diff — 2026-10-23

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Reviewed six-file uncommitted `git diff -- src` against HEAD `3687a1a4c1162eb3fc029d9efb1f518a45594510`, plus session persistence, submit/streaming, data-access, Insert, agent re-check and floating production integration. Followed `.agent/WORKFLOW.md`; current PLAN concerns separate MAS work, so this review uses the user's explicit batch requirements. No application source/test edits, staging or commits. Existing untracked `.claude/launch.json` untouched.

### Findings

- **P2 — Keep focus inside History while it remains open.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantChatArea.tsx:310-312` unconditionally focuses the prompt on every active-session change. In Chat History, confirm deletion of the active chat: `deleteSession` selects another session (or creates one), but History stays open (`:866-877`). The effect focuses the textarea behind the `aria-modal` dialog, including when focus was on its Delete button. Keyboard input can now reach the obscured composer; screen-reader focus leaves the active modal. Independent floating-component probe reproduces this with the real Dialog and deletion state transition. Defer session-change autofocus while History/another modal is open; restore the prompt after History closes on a real switch, and retain/move focus within History after deletion. Add regressions for deleting the active/last chat while History stays open and for an ordinary History switch that closes it.
- **P2 — Copied feedback is still shared, not independent per SQL part.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantMessageCard.tsx:87,108-114` stores one `copiedIndex`. Copy block A, then B within 1.5 seconds: A immediately loses its tick instead of retaining its own feedback interval. This falls short of the requested independent copied ticks even though it fixes all blocks showing a tick together. Use copy state keyed by part index (including -1), with per-index timer cleanup or generation tokens. Preserve a fresh interval when copying the same block again; the current index equality also lets an older timeout clear a newer copy of that index. Independent probes reproduce both behaviors. The repeated-same-block timeout issue existed in the old boolean implementation too; the new cross-block exclusivity is the remaining batch requirement gap. Add copy-A-then-B and repeated-copy timing regressions, not just one copy assertion.
- **P2 — Validate restored result indices before suppressing the fallback.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantMessageParts.tsx:498-501` uses array property lookup to decide whether the result belongs inside a part, whereas `:241` uses strict equality against a numeric map index. A persisted `resultPartIndex: "0"` resolves `parts["0"]` to SQL and suppresses the trailing result, but matches no numeric part index, so the result disappears entirely. `normalizeSession` spreads stored messages without validating this new field; the TypeScript annotation does not guard disk JSON. Independent malformed-session-shape render reproduces the missing result. Accept only an integer number in range identifying a SQL part, and use the same validated index for both placement decisions; otherwise render at the end. Add a storage/render regression for string and other invalid indices. Normal typed writers generate numbers; this finding concerns the explicitly requested invalid-index fallback, not ordinary saved sessions.

### Correctness and test assessment

- IME guard checks both `isComposing` and 229 before preventing Enter or submitting. Ordinary Enter sends only when idle and allowed; busy Enter neither sends nor cancels. Shift+Enter retains native multiline behavior. Textarea stays enabled. Submit clears the prompt only at request start; late completion does not clear the next draft. Stop still uses the existing cancel path.
- Completion autofocus preserves a focused control outside the composer and returns focus after Stop within the composer; independent probes pass. Ordinary chat-switch autofocus passes. The History deletion exception above is not covered by committed tests.
- SQL Run/Insert status and errors are indexed correctly; no Insert callback means no Insert button. Independent Insert and overlapping-run probes pass: one block's loading does not disable another, and the last completed successful query owns the single result and its index. Standard numeric indices survive storage loading, patching and persistence. Absent, -1, fractional and out-of-range numeric indices fall back once at the end; valid SQL indices render under their part. Legacy `message.sql` uses -1 for copy/Insert; existing migration converts old SQL to structured parts and retains untagged results at the end.
- Data-access updates preserve part order, so their state updates do not shift SQL indices. Permission-save/retry and stale-grant guards are unchanged. Local read-only classification, safe/production authentication, and the current-connection agent safety re-check remain in the same execution order. Existing tests cover auth-before-query, agent re-check refusal, grant failure, duplicate answers and retry. No new DB write, telemetry, or logging path found.
- New committed tests cover mount focus, both IME signals, busy drafting, denied menu/History deletion, accepted History deletion, one selected result, one copy and a scoped error. Gaps: completion/switch focus, modal focus after active deletion, independent copy intervals, Insert outcomes, pending runs on multiple parts, numeric index persistence, malformed-index fallback, and accepted menu deletion. Added external probes cover several gaps but are not repository regression tests. Panel tests mock MessageCard and Popover; card tests use the real floating card. DOM tests do not establish native IME sequencing, modal/window focus behavior or screen-reader usability.

### Independent verification

- `rtk npm run test:run -- src/components/ai-assistant src/lib/ai-assistant`: **26 files / 239 tests passed**, including streaming, session bounds/purge, data access and agent paths.
- `rtk npm run build`: **passed** (TypeScript and Vite). Warnings: browser externalization of node:zlib, mixed static/dynamic imports and chunk sizes.
- `rtk npm exec -- vitest run --config /private/tmp/ai-chat-batch1-review/vitest.config.mjs`: **14 tests passed**. This external harness asserts the observed defects as reproductions, not successful fixes; also verifies normal focus, Insert, concurrent result ownership, fallback and numeric persistence. Query/security/dialog I/O and Popover are mocked; real ChatArea, MessageCard, Dialog and session hook are exercised. Initial harness lacked the project's React/Preact integration for Popover and failed three cases with InvalidCharacterError; replacing only external Popover with the repository suite's mock pattern resolved the harness issue.
- `rtk npm run dev`: **blocked**, `listen EPERM: operation not permitted ::1:1420`. No browser/native visual, real OS confirmation, live database/provider or screen-reader verification. No Rust changes; Rust checks outside this scope.
- `rtk git diff --check`: **passed** before report append. Only this review report written in the repository.

The completion effect at `:322-334` also skips while either dialog is open. Normal numeric placement, session load/update/persistence and last-completed concurrent query ownership still pass.

No new logging, telemetry, database-write or safety-policy bypass introduced by these fixes. Existing authentication, read-only and agent safety ordering remains unchanged.

## Review: AI chat UX batch 2 — working-tree diff — 2026-10-23

**Verdict: CHANGES REQUIRED.** Reviewer: Codex. Scope: four tracked modified files from `git diff` against HEAD `6873a8455197faa65af18f09128847be3c88cccb`, plus untracked `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantChatArea.test.tsx`. Floating chat is the only production presentation; panel-only behavior was not treated as a release requirement. Followed `.agent/WORKFLOW.md` and the user's explicit batch requirements; current PLAN covers separate MAS work. No application source/test edits, staging or commits. Existing untracked `.claude/launch.json` untouched.

### Finding

- **P2 — Preserve keyboard focus on the relocated scroll button.** `src/components/ai-assistant/AiAssistantChatArea.tsx:721-731` moves “Scroll to latest message” inside `composerRef`, but the completion effect at `:335-345` still treats every descendant as eligible for prompt autofocus. Reproduction: while an answer streams and the user is away from the latest message, focus the scroll button without activating it; finish the answer with `showScrollToBottom` still true. Focus moves from the still-mounted button to the textarea. Previously the button was outside this ref and retained focus. A subsequent Enter can send a prepared draft instead of activating the intended scroll control. Keep the new positioning wrapper, but exclude this button from completion autofocus (or narrow the focus region to the actual composer controls). Add a floating regression asserting the button retains focus across `submitting: true -> false`; preserve existing Stop-to-prompt and outside-control behavior. Independent DOM probe reproduces the focus transfer and confirms the button remains connected. This is a regression from this batch, distinct from the resolved History focus finding.

### Correctness, regressions and accessibility

- History removes only `min-h-200`; its max-height, fixed header and nested scrolling structure remain. The title popup sorts a copied session array by descending `updatedAt`, groups adjacent days through `sessionDayLabel`, and applies a bounded scroll area. No mutation of authoritative session state. Ordinary title-list switching closes the popup, calls the correct session ID and returns focus to the prompt in an independent probe.
- Empty status-row markup is removed; `Loading model...` renders only during a submitting `loading_model` state. Prompt layout effect resets height before measuring `scrollHeight`, including after clearing/replacing text; `max-h-50` and vertical overflow provide the cap. The relative composer wrapper and `bottom-full` anchor place the button above that wrapper independent of prompt height. Actual pixel geometry remains unverified as noted below.
- Model picker removes the permanent New badge. Auto explanation matches the explicit Auto handler selecting the enabled default provider and its default model (with first-enabled fallback). Copy chat retains transcript serialization and handles clipboard rejection or missing clipboard access with a visible `role="status"` message. Existing whole-chat timeout behavior remains: repeated copies can let an older timeout dismiss newer feedback early; timer replacement/cleanup would be a useful follow-up, not a new blocking finding here.
- No-provider empty state opens AI settings; enabled-provider suggestions only replace/focus the draft and never invoke submit. Connection-specific suggestions depend on the runtime connection. The @ hint depends on available context. Context removal uses an X icon and explicit accessible button name; independent probe verifies the correct context callback. Error and legacy text use `wrap-break-word` while retaining preformatted whitespace and width constraints.
- Existing request, stream, cancel, persistence and session-state paths are unchanged. Batch 1 remediation probes still pass for modal focus deferral, active/last History deletion, ordinary switching, Stop completion, outside-composer focus preservation, independent copy ticks and invalid result-index fallback. No new query execution, database-write, logging or telemetry path introduced. The shared Dialog's existing incomplete focus trapping remains outside this batch.

### Test quality

The eight new tests cover basic day order, settings entry, suggestion fill/no-send, conditional @ hint, clipboard success/failure, loading status, measured prompt height and scroll-button DOM placement. They mock Popover and message rendering. The height and placement assertions verify an assigned style/class and ancestry, not max-height scrolling, resize behavior, clipping or actual separation from the composer. The grouping test uses real wall-clock time and global text ordering; it never asserts that the initial Today lookup succeeded, and does not test multiple chats within a day, an exact date heading or a year boundary. Prefer fixed local dates and assertions scoped to the open chat list. Missing repository coverage includes the focus regression above, title-list switch, accessible context removal, model-picker explanation/badge removal, History height and textarea growth/shrink/cap. Independent probes cover switch/context behavior but do not replace committed regressions.

### Independent verification and limitations
